# The full depth — digest of the readings

**Only One research** · October 1, 2026 · early draft 0.1 · Result [032](../../CONTENTS.md#032) · manuscript 1 of 17 · [Cite](CITATION.bib) · [Quote check](../../verification/catalogue.md#the-full-depth-digest-of-the-readings-october-1-2026)

---
## AB1 — Ayin Beis I

Hemshech Ayin Beis, lines 1-1950 (Shavuos 5672 to Shavuos 5674). Its main addition to the depth: under every light of the soul it finds one plain point, and it shows that the deepest point is the lowest and easiest to reach. (1) Will is under love. Love has reasons and limits; the soul's will for G-d has none, not even pleasure. It is the bond, like a father's will for his son. It stays when love is gone and rules over every pleasure. (2) What fits you does not change you. Love that settles in the heart quenches thirst and leaves nature as it was. Love too big to hold changes what you are. (3) You are room given. True loftiness makes room for the smallest thing; the worlds exist only because He gives them place, and 'nothing else' is lived in the something, not by escaping it. (4) The subtlest ego is felt humility. Real bittul does not know it is batel. (5) The narrow place reaches highest. Pain and bitterness reach above pleasure. Teshuvah touches His delight in the soul itself and brings a new will. Exile is love in the shape of anger. (6) The return is required. Running up without coming back (Nadav and Avihu) is a sin. The Essence is found in Torah after prayer and in mitzvos, and by giving to others. Everything lands in plain, whole (tamim) action that every Jew can reach.

## Depths

- **AB1:AB1-X1 The wanting under the love** (extends D8): Under every love of G-d there is a will that has no reason and needs no pleasure: the soul's bond, like a father's will for his son. It remains when love is gone, and it is what lets a plain Jew give his life. [stated] | Inside: A flame rising by itself; a pull with 'no request at all'; a father's longing for a son. [L485, L1639] | Lands: In plain deeds done with no taste: chukim, and holding firm when tested. [L1645] | Q: ואהבה עצמיות היא בחי' רצון והוא מה שרוצה בדבר שהוא עצמו ומהותו — essential love is really will (/home/user/Research/Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:223)
- **AB1:AB1-X2 Wanting rules pleasure** (extends D8): Pleasure is deeper, but will carries the self outward, so will rules pleasure. No pile of pleasures can change a Jew's will for G-d, and even someone sunk in pleasure truly wants G-d. [stated] | Inside: 'Many waters cannot quench'; the man at a celebration praising the G-d-fearing. [L1649-1650] | Lands: Fasting or effort for teshuvah becomes sweet; the old pleasures become repulsive. [L1650] | Q: וזהו הטעם מה שהרצון שולט על התענוג ואין התענוג שולט על הרצון — will rules pleasure (/home/user/Research/Chassidus-txt/R. - **AB1:AB1-X3 Room given** (extends D10): You do not exist as a 'something' beside G-d. You exist only because He gives you room. Real loftiness gives room to the smallest thing without that thing becoming anything on its own. [stated] | Inside: A king who stoops to the lowliest; 'I dwell on high and with the crushed'. ## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- AB1:AB1-C1 [Why can a simple Jew give up his life for G-d when his love of G-d is small?] (M1) Love that grows out of understanding always has a measure. It goes as far as the reason goes, so it can never demand that you give your life. [stated] → (M2) Under love there is a wanting. A father wants his son, not because the son is good for him, but because the son is his own self. [stated] → (M12) So the wanting stays even when the love is gone. When the son disappoints and no love shows, the father still wants him. 'Either way, they are My children.' [stated] → (M8) Your soul has the same wanting toward G-d. When the soul's core shows, it is pulled to Him like a flame rising by itself, asking for nothing in return. [stated] → (M15) That is why giving one's life happens with no feeling of sweetness at all. If it ran on pleasure, it could fail. [stated] → (M14) What holds it is one thing only: it is impossible for him to be separate from G-d. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: A wanting with no reason and no pleasure behind it: the soul's own bond with G-d's Essence. Nothing underneath it is a 'why'. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: It comes back down as plain deed: the same will is what keeps the mitzvos that have no reason (chukim), and what a person keeps even when he feels nothing. [stated L1645; the link to today's dry mitzvah is ours] - AB1:AB1-C2 [Pleasure is deeper than will. So why does will win?] (M2) Will runs out toward something. Pleasure stays hidden inside you. [stated] → (M5) The deepest pleasure is not in what you own, and not even in your good qualities. It is simply being glad in yourself. [stated] → (M1) Yet pleasure cannot change what you want. A great mind loves ideas, but he wants only holy ones, and no clever idea will make him want the opposite. [stated] → (M9) So no pile of pleasures can put out a Jew's want for G-d. 'Many waters cannot quench the love.' [stated] → (M9) Even a man sunk in pleasures, at a happy moment, will tell you 'I want this too, I just have obstacles.' It is not a lie. The want is really in him. [stated] → (M3) Will can even turn pain into pleasure: a fast hurts, but someone who badly wants to fix himself enjoys it. [stated] → (M4) Why? When you want, your self itself goes out. When you enjoy, only a glow of the pleasure comes out. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: Will carries the self itself into the world, so the will toward G-d cannot be bought off by any pleasure. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: A baal teshuvah comes to despise the old pleasures completely, because the want has taken them over. The person far away is told: your want is real; act from it. [stated L1650; the advice is ours] - AB1:AB1-C3 [Why does real love in prayer leave a person's nature the same?] (M2) Love that comes from contemplation fits inside the heart. It quenches the thirst, the way a drink of water cools the fire inside you. [stated] → (M4) But that is not yet true service. It has no end-point; it is a step, not the goal. [stated] → (M7) The real closeness comes afterward, in doing Torah and mitzvos all day. [stated] → (M10) Even so, for most of us the body's heart closes around this love like a jug around water. Even the 'good' we feel in G-dliness is mixed with a physical good. So our nature stays. [stated] → (M5) Love that does not fit, that never quenches the thirst, changes your very nature: from cruel to merciful, from coarse to humble. [stated] → (M16) Think of your will: it can put your foot into icy water against all your nature, because will is not held in one organ. [stated] → (M10) And a teacher who gives what is beyond the student's head: the student grasps only that it is precious, and that is what refines his mind itself. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: What fits you leaves you as you are; what is too big for you changes what you are. [stated, phrased by us] ⇒ RETURN: So do not look for love you can hold. Pray toward what you cannot hold, then carry it into the day's mitzvos, where it settles. [ours, following L1794-1805] - AB1:AB1-C4 [Why could the sefiros of Tohu not live together?] (M1) Two great ministers, each very big in his own eyes, can never truly unite. Each fills the whole room. [stated] → (M10) Two humble poor people, even with opposite natures, will love each other and give way to each other. [stated] → (M1) Yet true greatness is drawn to the lowly. A king has more mercy than his ministers, because before him everyone is small. [stated] → (M4) The difference is how you see the other. If you see him as truly nothing, you are drawn to him. If you see him as 'something', you cannot bear him. [stated] → (M11) The root of it is in the lofty one: he feels himself as 'something'. [stated] → (M5) Real loftiness gives room to everything, the smallest thing as much as the largest. [stated] → (M15) That is how you exist. The worlds are not 'something' from their own side. They are here only because the Infinite gives them room. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: You are not a 'something' next to G-d; you are room He gives. [stated, phrased by us] ⇒ RETURN: Like King David: full royal power and, at once, 'I am poor and needy'. In practice: give room to others the way you are given room. [stated L1912; practice is ours] - AB1:AB1-C5 [Why did the sefiros of Tohu refuse even to give, when giving makes one great?] (M1) 'What's mine is mine and what's yours is yours' is the trait of Sodom: each one complete, needing no one. That was Tohu. [stated] → (M2) In Tikkun each quality feels it is not whole alone: even kindness feels that kindness alone is not complete. [stated] → (M1) But why should anyone give? To teach, a teacher must first hide his own mind completely, to find the small piece that fits the student. [stated] → (M14) Because by giving, the giver gains. You can understand an idea in detail and still feel it is 'not comfortable yet'. That means there is more depth you have not reached. [stated] → (M15) Speech, the lowest power, has its root above the mind. That is why teaching brings out ideas you never had. [stated] → (M9) The student who asks hard questions makes his teacher wiser. [stated] → (M4) The giver grows only when he gives by setting himself down. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: Giving is laying yourself down to what G-d intends; through the receiver, light without limit comes to the giver. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: 'I am G-d your G-d who took you out of Egypt': out of limits, through light that settles in vessels. Teach what you know, and you will be given more. [stated L1950; advice ours] - AB1:AB1-C6 [Can a person really know G-d is the only reality?] (M10) Whatever picture of 'nothing' or 'pure light' you make in your mind is really a something. [stated] → (M3) What we can grasp is that nothing makes itself. Every thing needs a G-dly power making it and giving it life. [stated] → (M16) You can know this and still be far from it. Then it does nothing to you. [stated] → (M16) By thinking it through deeply, the knowing comes near. You feel it, and it begins to remove your sense of self. [stated] → (M15) Down here this is most certain, because you watch things fall apart every day. What decays cannot exist by itself. [stated] → (M7) The example from the physical world is not a mere picture. The example IS the thing it shows. [stated] → (M7) When the animal soul is broken open by this certainty, like a nutshell, the fruit shows: the G-dly soul sees G-d's essence. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: The soul's own seeing of G-d, more than it had in Gan Eden, opened by the body's plain certainty. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: In the future the body itself will see. For now: look at one decaying thing today and ask what holds it up while it lasts. [stated L1850; the exercise is ours] - AB1:AB1-C7 [I understand it all. Why do I feel nothing?] (M3) Think: all the worlds are only a glow, and they change nothing in G-d. [stated] → (M8) This should make you vanish like a small child before a great adult, who simply loses himself. [stated] → (M1) But 'you, sons of Jacob, have not ended': no longing, only coldness. [stated] → (M11) The reason is a heart turned to stone, which nothing moves. [stated] → (M11) Even when you see the infinite power in creation, with real clarity, your eyes still show only the physical thing, and you follow it. [stated] → (M11) The hardest root is the 'taste' you have for worldly things. It is the source of every strong desire. [stated] → (M9) The remedy is steady Torah with real effort, like water dripping on a stone until it makes a hole. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: Coldness is the hardest kelipah (Amalek, 'who cooled you'). Its root is the taste for the world. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: Don't wait to feel. Learn every day with toil. The drops work even when you feel nothing; and the work of prayer at the soul's depth removes the taste itself. [stated L1481, L1751] - AB1:AB1-C8 [If I feel very humble before G-d, is that the real thing?] (M2) True self-nullification does not feel that it is nullified at all. [stated] → (M16) Before a great king, if someone asks 'what happened to you?', you would answer: 'I don't know anything.' [stated] → (M4) One step lower: you feel so nullified that you have no place for yourself. It is real, yet there is still a someone there. [stated] → (M11) Lower: it feels good to you that you are humble. [stated] → (M11) Lower still: you want your humility to be seen, even 'for the sake of heaven'. [stated] → (M11) At the bottom is the plain ego: 'a man wise in his own eyes', who does a kindness and says 'see how much good I did you.' [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: The highest ego is felt humility. Real bittul is too absorbed in the King to notice itself. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: Do not measure your humility. Turn to the One before whom you stand; and do kindness without telling anyone what you did. [ours, from L1744-1749] - AB1:AB1-C9 [Nadav and Avihu died in the highest longing. Why was that a sin?] (M1) It seems strange: their death came from the highest service, the soul's expiry in longing. [stated] → (M2) The purpose of creation is 'He made it to be lived in': after the running up must come the return. [stated] → (M6) Where is G-d's Essence found when you run to it? In Torah. That is why 'these words' come right after 'with all your might'. [stated] → (M11) Their sin was longing with no return: no hold on the inner self, so the soul left and the body stayed. [stated] → (M15) Pinchas fixed it: he risked his body, but as a mitzvah, to do G-d's will. That kept him held. [stated] → (M7) So after prayer, learning Torah is not to light up your own soul. It is to bind yourself to the Infinite that shines in the Torah. [stated] → (M5) And higher: not even to quench the thirst of prayer, but to bring G-d's Essence down below. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: The Essence is not found by leaving; it is found in Torah, below. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: Pray with all your might; then sit and learn, as a servant bringing G-d down, not as a seeker still thirsty. [stated L1443-1447] - AB1:AB1-C10 [Why does an insight feel perfectly right, and then fall apart when I try to explain it?] (M9) At the moment an idea 'falls' into your mind, it sits right and feels very good. [stated] → (M1) Then you start to work out the details, and it seems not to make sense yet. [stated] → (M2) What you had at first was not an explanation. It was a deeper 'settled sense' of the thing, where the essence of the idea was felt. [stated] → (M9) The advice: tie yourself back to that first moment, to the 'lie' of the matter. Then the details will follow. [stated] → (M5) Great sages have room in their minds for things above understanding, not only by faith, because of this settled sense. [stated] → (M4) In Chochmah the Infinite shines as the only true 'something', and all that we grasp is 'nothing' next to it. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: Beneath every understanding is a felt 'it is so' that the explanation cannot hold. In Chochmah that 'so' is G-d as the only real. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: In contemplation, when the details scatter you, go back to the one point and let it settle. Do not chase every detail. [stated L292] - AB1:AB1-C11 [How can all my different good deeds come from one place?] (M16) A painter paints on white, because white has no color of its own, so every color shows true on it. [stated] → (M3) In service, the base of everything is the point of binding yourself to G-d with all your heart. [stated] → (M10) If your mitzvos grow out of understanding and feelings, something of 'what I get from it' is mixed in. [stated] → (M4) Only when they come from the point of binding alone are all things done as they truly are. [stated] → (M13) To that point of the heart, understanding G-d and giving charity are the same. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: One colorless point of the heart, bound to G-d, under every action. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: Each morning in prayer, set that one point; then let study, charity and refusing evil all branch from it. [stated L920] - AB1:AB1-C12 [If everything is batel, why is 'there is nothing else' said about this solid world?] (M16) Your will is not a separate 'thing' in you the way your intellect is. It is only your soul leaning toward something. [stated] → (M3) Stop wanting, and nothing remains. A thought you had stays in memory; a want leaves nothing. [stated] → (M6) The world comes from G-d's will. If the will withdrew, there would be absolute nothing. [stated] → (M4) This is why 'there is nothing else' is said about the solid world itself, the yesh. [stated] → (M5) Creation is an existence that is not really an existence, just as the Essence exists without being 'an existence'. [stated] → (M15) So the deepest 'nothing else' is possible only where there is a 'something'. [stated] → (M8) That is why your soul, once made a separate 'something' below, can set its whole self down in a way it could not above. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: Your being a 'someone' is the very place where 'there is nothing but Him' can be lived. [stated, phrased by us] ⇒ RETURN: Do not try to stop being someone. Lay down the someone you are, here, in this day. [ours from L1458] - AB1:AB1-C13 [What are the levels of serving G-d, from the lowest?] (M8) Still: you keep mitzvos like a servant who would rather be free, only because you must. The forbidden is not unthinkable to you; you simply hold back. [stated] → (M8) Growing: you feel a little life, knowing you are doing what the King wants. A commoner sent by a king is glad. [stated] → (M8) Animal: love and fear in the open heart, from deep contemplation. Here there is real life in the mitzvos. [stated] → (M5) Speaking: the mind itself, past all feeling, bound and cleaving to G-d. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: Pure cleaving of the mind to G-d, like the chariot. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: Start where you are: even 'still' service counts; but a king's servant should at least be glad. [stated L1154; framing ours] - AB1:AB1-C14 [How do you build love and fear of G-d step by step?] (M3) Begin with logic: a 'something' must come from 'nothing'; there cannot be an endless chain of somethings. [stated] → (M3) And every thing is made of parts, so it cannot exist on its own. [stated] → (M16) Just this brings you close, as if engraved from the outside. [stated] → (M9) Then feel it from your own body: you know a soul gives you life. The world is alive, so G-dliness is its life. [stated] → (M12) You may feel bitter that the world hides it, but service must be in joy. Keep the bitterness for before prayer, at midnight. [stated] → (M5) After long work you see that all this life is only a glow, nothing next to His Essence. Then you long to be absorbed in Him. [stated] → (M15) Highest: the Essence is found below exactly as above. From this comes an inner awe. [stated] → (M11) If you contemplate well and feel nothing, the missing piece is not the proof. It is the feeling of G-dliness itself. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: Awe at the Essence that is here below as above. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: Before you contemplate, lean toward it and give yourself to it; the G-dly soul's pull ('a tsi tsu Elokus') does the rest. [stated L1192-1193] - AB1:AB1-C15 [If G-d's will does not change, how does teshuvah erase sins?] (M1) It looks as if forgiveness means G-d changed His mind. [stated] → (M5) No. Teshuvah reaches His essential delight, above the will that the mitzvos express. [stated] → (M2) There can be delight without will: 'Only in your fathers did G-d delight.' He delights in you yourself, not only in what you do. [stated] → (M6) When that delight comes, the will comes again, new, and fills every gap. [stated] → (M15) Pain and bitterness reach higher than pleasure, even higher than essential pleasure. [stated] → (M16) Like jumping a huge ditch: only someone running for his life finds that power. [stated] → (M11) The 'not-good' of having sinned is what reveals the soul's whole self. [stated] → (M4) So in this teshuvah even sins done on purpose become merits. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: The soul's whole self, reached from the narrow place, touches His essential delight in it. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: Do not run from the bad feeling about yourself; cry from it ('their heart cried out'), then return to Torah and mitzvos with a new will. [stated L1585-1586] - AB1:AB1-C16 [Where do my ideas come from?] (M2) Your wisdom is not your deepest self. It has a cause, and the cause is your deepest self. [stated] → (M9) That is why 'a person learns only where his heart desires'. The main thing is the pleasure. [stated] → (M10) You can think against what you want, but you cannot think against your 'geshmak'. [stated] → (M16) Think of a man who loves the idea of a house: out of that love he invents every room and stone. [stated] → (M4) And he enjoys the house only if it is built exactly as he planned. This proves the plan IS the pleasure, in another form. [stated] → (M9) That is why an idea grows old and dry: it has moved away from the pleasure. Will does not grow old; it only hides and returns. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: Thought is your delight in another garment; delight is your very self. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: If you have no taste for learning, learn anyway by giving yourself to it (kabbalas ol); that too opens the mind. [stated L1655, L1825] - AB1:AB1-C17 [If G-d loves us essentially, why such a long and dark exile?] (M16) A father sees his son fall in the mud and gets very angry. [stated] → (M14) If he did not love him, he would not care, just as he does not get angry at his friend's son. [stated] → (M4) This anger is not a cover over love. It is love itself, in the shape of severity. [stated] → (M6) When the son accepts it with love and mends, the inner love shows openly. [stated] → (M15) So it is with the exile: our dirt touches G-d's inner love, and that is why the darkness is so great. [stated] → (M9) Your part: stay strong in Torah and mitzvos through every obstacle. [stated] → (M5) And more: let the body and its drive turn so that 'my heart and my flesh sing'. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: The darkness is love that cannot be shown any other way; through it the hidden Wisdom itself will be taught. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: When things are dark, receive it as the father's anger: accept, mend, and keep doing the mitzvos. [stated L1832-1835] - AB1:AB1-C18 [Why does a test feel so strong?] (M2) The vessel for G-dliness is bittul. Only what gives itself up can hold Him. [stated] → (M11) The other side is the opposite: 'I am, and there is nothing else', like Pharaoh. [stated] → (M3) Such a thing is not truly alive even while it exists. It only floats on light held over it from outside. [stated] → (M1) A test looks heavy and hard to stand against. [stated] → (M4) But stand against it, and it vanishes completely, as if it never was. [stated] → (M10) Ordinary physical heaviness is different: it needs work day after day and never fully goes. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: A test is an absence made to look like a wall. [stated, phrased by us] ⇒ RETURN: When a test comes, stand once, firmly. It is lighter than it looks. [stated L1719-1720] - AB1:AB1-C19 [Isn't the deep service only for great souls?] (M2) All year the work is in mind and heart. On Rosh Hashanah the work is in the soul's very essence. [stated] → (M1) In understanding, people differ: no two minds are alike. [stated] → (M4) But at the soul's depth everyone is equal. That is why 'all the world will say' the psalm 'from the depths'. [stated] → (M15) Setting your whole self down before G-d, like a servant, is in every Jew, and it is easier than service through understanding. [stated] → (M8) It is to lay yourself wholly away to G-d. [stated] → (M9) So each person can reach the highest level in his service. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: At the bottom of every soul is the same point, and it is open to everyone. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: When you cannot understand, do not wait. Lay yourself down, and guard your eyes, ears and mouth as its first sign. [stated L412-413] - AB1:AB1-C20 [What makes a mind truly wise?] (M2) Wisdom has two opposite powers: rising into wonder, and coming down to explain. [stated] → (M7) Only someone who knows the core of an idea can hide it inside a parable. [stated] → (M12) The sharp questioner goes deep and stays tangled; the steady one is clear but shallow. True wisdom has both lines. [stated] → (M17) Like a hand: as strongly as it lifts a weight, so strongly can it throw it. [stated] → (M8) A student sitting before his teacher shrinks into one point to receive, and later becomes a flowing spring. [stated] → (M15) Moshe was heavy of speech; at Sinai, in the deepest bittul, he was healed. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: The depth you reach decides how far down you can bring it. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: On Rosh Hashanah and the Ten Days, work from the depth of the soul; that depth is what builds the kingship below. [stated L1626] - AB1:AB1-C21 [What does it mean to be 'whole' with G-d?] (M1) The physical mind says: your business is your source, think hard, use tricks. [stated] → (M2) Wholeness is the core point of the soul's will, in bittul above reason. [stated] → (M8) In the mind: you think only good; the other side never occurs to you. [stated] → (M8) So the whole person never complains, because he does not think another way. [stated] → (M8) In the heart: earnestness. What matters, matters; what doesn't, doesn't. [stated] → (M15) In deed: it is not even a test, because any other way does not exist for him. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: A will so simple that there is no second option. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: In business hours and at prayer time, trust; when a deal arises at prayer time, let it go. [stated L623] - AB1:AB1-C22 [Why is fear of G-d so much harder than love?] (M1) The soul below is far from its source and needs great effort to come to fear. [stated] → (M10) Love is someone who loves: it keeps a self. [stated] → (M2) Fear needs more than understanding. It needs some recognition, some nearness to the thing itself. [stated] → (M9) Hence 'you toiled in fear': it takes great work, in the mind and in refining the body. [stated] → (M15) Yet the soul below gains: it is called 'bride' (kallah), from 'yearning to the end'. Only below is there such burning thirst. [stated] → (M4) Its bond with G-d below has an advantage over its oneness above. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: The distance itself makes the burning thirst and the full self-surrender. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: Toil for fear: contemplate until there is some recognition, and refine the body so it can carry it. [stated L1455] - AB1:AB1-C23 [What does it mean when an idea is 'deep'?] (M9) Some depth you reach only with time: 'after forty years a person understands his teacher.' [stated] → (M9) Before an idea comes, you sometimes feel its 'rightness' and see sparks; the darkness 'becomes light'. [stated] → (M2) You can only call something 'deep' if you already touch its edge. [stated] → (M9) When a new idea falls in, you feel it came from somewhere. [stated] → (M5) But what is hidden in itself keeps hiding deeper, just as light keeps spreading. [stated] → (M15) That is why Moshiach comes 'when no one is thinking of it': the most hidden looks like its opposite. [stated] ⇒ BOTTOM: There is a hiddenness that is not a lack of our vessels but G-d's own; it shows as its opposite. [stated] ⇒ RETURN: Through the teshuvah of Yom Kippur from the heart's depth, that hidden is drawn into the sukkah. Do not judge by how dark it looks. [stated L1692-1694]

## AB2 — Ayin Beis II

Hemshech Ayin Beis, lines 1951-3900 (the Rebbe Rashab's maamarim from Shavuos 5674 to Shavuos 5675, 1914-15), read through line by line. What it adds to 'Go deeper' is mostly the inside of the climb: how to tell a real step from a false one, and how a person actually moves from knowing to seeing to being nothing. (1) Knowing is not yet seeing: understanding passes when you stop thinking; seeing stays, and you get there by pressing the same idea until it becomes recognition, starting from the physical sky and earth (C01, C18). (2) False and true arousal: a longing to rise with no accounting, a storm over sins that leaves no mark, a cry that is only self-feeling, a ratzo with no bittul (Nadav and Avihu), anger after fiery prayer; and in each case the text's true form (C02, C03, C14, C15). (3) The stillness under love: love has a lover; 'with You there is something' versus 'even with You, nothing'; the calm mind that is moved most deeply where it does not show; a fire too deep to see (C09, C11, C16). (4) The bottoms the text finds: the will that never sleeps, seen in a simple Jew and even in joy; the test that has no being; the mind's blank before a new insight; the soul's taste for G-dliness that clings without understanding; the name that calls the self from under its powers (C04, C05, C08, C10, C17). (5) The returns: Modeh Ani as setting yourself down, a second page of learning, charity beyond your will, eating to learn and pray, praying for the Shechinah's lack, being after prayer as in prayer (C06, C12, C20, avodah). (6) The lowest has the plainest mercy: the 'heel' soul for whom contemplation stays outside has a wonderful rise through honest bitterness and self-pity, because it is the nature of the exalted to go to the low (C13).

## Depths

- **AB2:AB2-D1 'With You there is something' and 'even with You, nothing'** (extends D3): There are two kinds of 'only G-d'. In the first, nothing exists apart from Him, but with Him there is something: a light that feels it is His. In the second, even with Him there is nothing: the light as it is inside Him is not a light at all. Each is real; the second is deeper. | Inside: The text's own Yiddish: 'mit dir iz faran a zach', with You there is a thing. The seraphim quench their thirst in the very wish to be included. | Lands: In prayer, it is the step from Shema's love to the Amidah's standing silence (2822). In the day it means: you exist, as His. | Q: דאין זולתך הוא שזולתך אין שום מציאות כלל אבל אתך יש מציאות (מיט דיר איז פאראן א זאך) — 'There is none but You': apart from You no existence at all, but with You there is existence (with You there is a thing). (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:2294)
- **AB2:AB2-D2 The test is not there** (extends D10): The thing that pulls you from G-d has no existence of its own. Only the Infinite can make a nothing look real, even very real. When you stand, it is cancelled and leaves no mark. G-d's purpose was never in the thing; it was in you, standing. | Inside: It looks like a wall; it is a picture of a wall. The text: G-dliness 'iz a zach', is a thing; what is apart from it is no thing at all. | Lands: The very next temptation. Permitted things, used for a G-dly aim, are real; the test-thing is not (2732). And it still takes 'great strength of soul' (3540). | Q: והוא רק כח הא"ס להעמיד דבר שאינו מציאות כלל רק דבר נדמה בבחי' דמיון בלבד שיהי' נראה למציאות — Only the power of the Infinite can hold up a thing that is no existence at all, only a seeming, so that it looks like existence. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:2731)
- **AB2:AB2-D3 Love still has a lover** (extends D2): Love is not the deepest closeness, because in love there is still someone who loves. Wisdom's bittul is not bowing before something greater; it is nullity itself, with no one standing apart to feel it. Even fear and love at their deepest are not 'emotions'. | Inside: 'A still small voice' (2822). Like the man of real mind: not moved where no need, moved most deeply where needed, and none of it shows (3832). | Lands: The Amidah, where legs and body stand as one: the bittul reaches deed (2822). In the day: a calm that is not coldness. | Q: ה"ז בבחי' קול דממה דקה בבחי' ביטול שלמע' מאהבה כו', דאהבה אינו ביטל דיש מי שאוהב — This is a 'still small voice', a bittul above love; for love is not bittul, since there is one who loves. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:2822)
- **AB2:AB2-D4 The blank before the new** (extends D5): After deep effort the mind goes stunned and empty, as if it has stopped. This blank is not failure. It is the mind emptying before its source, and right after it a new insight falls in. To receive a higher light, the vessel must be emptied first. | Inside: 'Es vert im lichtig', it begins to get light; 'es hert zich rechtkeit', rightness is heard before it is understood (3529). Then astonishment, 'like a complete stopping of the mind' (3530). | Lands: In study and in contemplation before prayer. Also in life: the pause between mind and heart where feeling goes quiet before it becomes love (3511, 3475). | Q: והשתוממות זאת היא ממוצע שעי"ז בא על ההשגה הנפלאה — This astonishment is a go-between through which he comes to the wondrous grasp. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:3530)
- **AB2:AB2-D5 The will that never sleeps** (extends D8): Under the love that comes and goes with thinking, there is a will in the soul's essence for G-d that never stops, like a parent's love for a child. Even a simple Jew far from inner work has it. It surfaces at times, not only in distress but in joy, and when it surfaces it is wholly true. | Inside: 'Ernstkeit', seriousness: a heart faithful to G-d (3018). True 'because it never breaks off' (3017). | Lands: Into deed: never turning good into bad, whoever pushes (3018). | Q: משא"כ הרצון שהיא אהבה ישנה תמיד גם כשאינו מתעורר, כמו אהבת האב והבן — But the will, which is a love that is there always even when not aroused, like the love of father and son. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:3008)
- **AB2:AB2-D6 The lowest gets the plainest mercy** (extends D9): There is a mercy that comes from knowing and feeling, and a simpler mercy above all reasons: it is the nature of the high to be drawn to the low. So the soul that is farthest, 'the heel', for whom contemplation stays on the surface, has a wonderful rise: when it lowers itself and pities itself, it wakes that plain mercy. | Inside: The desert: no seed, no water, and the strongest thirst. 'Would that in the holy place I had such love' (3891). | Lands: Self-rebuke that is honest, and being careful even in permitted things (3888); the ascent goes in order: first mercy on the body's coarseness, then on the soul's lowness (3893). | Q: רק מפני שטבע המרומם להיות נמשך אל השפל כו', וע"כ עיקר הרחמים הוא דוקא על השפל במדרי' — Only because it is the nature of the exalted to be drawn to the lowly; so the main mercy is on the one who is low. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:3887)
- **AB2:AB2-D7 Called by name** (extends D6): A name is not felt even by its owner, yet it reaches deeper than all your powers: calling a fainted man's name brings him back, and calling a man 'wise' or 'kind' wakes power from his hidden source. It works because, under everything, he knows what he is. The soul's depth answers being called. | Inside: 'Penei elai', turn to me: the whole self turns to the caller (3801). The river that stopped, dug again at the depth of the spring (2535). | Lands: Teshuvah from the depths (2535); on Rosh Hashanah, calling 'HaMelech' (2536). With people: praise them into their goodness (ours, from 3802). | Q: וכמו בהתעלפות ר"ל שיכולים להשיב נפשו ע"י שקוראין אותו בשמו — As in fainting, where they can bring back his soul by calling him by his name. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:2534)
- **AB2:AB2-D8 What is plain, reversed** (extends D13): Today the world is plain to us and G-d is news we have to learn. Grasp that comes as news can always be set another way. When the infinite light shines, it reverses: G-d is plain, the world is the novelty, and there is no room for another view. In the Temple a person felt G-d and did not feel himself. In the future grasping G-d will be natural. | Inside: 'Es leigt zich op der inyan zeyer gut', it settles very well, yet another setting stays possible (3674). In the Temple, 'the feeling of G-dliness and not the feeling of himself at all' (3677). | Lands: Now, by toil; the text says the seeing that is given 'comes through toil and great preparation' (3683). It lands in the plainness of a Jew's simple faith. | Q: אבל כאשר תפיסת האלקו' הוא בפשיטות אז אין מקום להנחה אחרת — When the grasp of G-dliness is plain, there is no room for another setting. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:3674)
- **AB2:AB2-D9 The child who says 'Abba'** (extends D7): A baby who has tasted grain calls 'Abba'. He knows nothing about his father, not why or how he is his father. Yet he recognizes him, from the essence, and is drawn after him with great longing. Matzah is the soul's recognition of the Essence in that way: knowing without knowing what. | Inside: 'Great longing' (3667). Matzah is both 'food of healing' for the ego and 'food of faith' (3669). | Lands: On Pesach night; and every morning in Modeh Ani, which is the same general recognition (2495). | Q: שהרי אינו יודע איך שהוא אביו ואין בזה שום התגלות רק שהוא בהכרה שמכיר שהוא אביו ונמשך אחריו בגעגועים גדולים — He does not know how he is his father, and there is no revelation in it; only he recognizes that this is his father and is drawn after him with great longing. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:3667)
- **AB2:AB2-D10 Both true: it does not touch Him, and He wants it** (extends new): On the outside of His light, nothing we do makes a difference to Him. In His inner Essence is rooted the intent that there be service. Both are true and do not contradict. So too forgiveness: it comes only from the Essence, where sin leaves no mark at all, and yet it comes only through teshuvah. | Inside: 'Mah ichpas lei', what does He care, and 'specifically slaughter it properly' (3632): two truths in one breath. | Lands: In the details of mitzvos as Torah gives them (3632). In teshuvah that knows it is forgiven from where sin cannot reach, and still does the work (3634). | Q: ומ"מ לא איכפת לי' ושניהם אמת ואינם סותרים זל"ז — And still it does not matter to Him; both are true and do not contradict. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:3633)
- **AB2:AB2-D11 A fire too deep to see** (extends D2): Felt wind, noise and fire in prayer are still 'not G-d', because they are felt. Inside the point of the heart there is a fire so deep it does not show, a trembling no one else notices, and a life that is not excitement: you simply live in it. | Inside: Yiddish: the heart 'smolders inside, very deep' (3275); 'er lebt in dem', he lives in it (3277). 'All the people in the camp trembled' versus 'they moved and stood far off' (3277). | Lands: In Shema and the Amidah; then in the day, as ordinary aliveness. | Q: שלא נראה וניכר מגודל עומקו כו' (בל"א דיא הארץ קלייעט אינוועניק טיף זייער — Unseen and unnoticed because of its great depth (the heart smolders inside, very deep). (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:3275)
- **AB2:AB2-D12 A taste without understanding** (extends new): What words cannot say, song says, the way a kiss says love. The song of the point is one sudden movement holding all tunes. Deepest of all, in everyone: you hear a G-dly idea you do not understand and your soul clings anyway, because it simply has a taste for G-dliness. | Inside: Rabbi Akiva singing 'every kind of tune' in prayer; Moshe's voice, above the Levites' ordered song (3292-3293). | Lands: In the listening itself; and in niggun during prayer. Still tied to learning: only 'when he hears some G-dly understanding' (3294). | Q: ופי' בכל מיני ניגון היינו כמו תנועה אחת הכוללת כל מיני ניגון פרטי — 'With every kind of tune' means like one movement that includes every particular tune. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:3293)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- AB2:AB2-C01 [You have understood that G-d makes the world from nothing. You were moved while you thought about it. An hour later it is gone. Why?] (M1) Understanding, even good understanding, shines only while you are thinking. When you stop, it leaves, and the love that came from it leaves with it. → (M9) Moshe already knew about the golden calf. G-d told him plainly. He broke the tablets only when he saw it. Seeing does something that knowing, even sure knowing, does not. → (M9) Think of something beautiful you have seen. It stays in front of your eyes. A thing you only heard about you picture for a moment, and then it fades. → (M15) So the place to start seeing is low and close: the real sky and the real earth. Look at them until you see that they exist only from G-d's power. That plain looking makes a deeper bittul than lofty ideas you only hear about. → (M16) How do you get from understanding to seeing? Not by a new idea. By pressing your mind hard into the same idea until it becomes recognition. Then it stays and does not leave. → (M12) Even seeing can still be a rush. You are thrilled because it is new to you, and it is new because you are still someone apart. Moshe saw face to face and stayed calm, because he was not a separate self; his seeing settled into doing the mitzvos. ⇒ BOTTOM: Seeing that stays: the truth of the matter standing before you always, like a beautiful thing you once saw (3816-3817); and under it, Moshe's calm seeing that is no longer an event (3820). ⇒ RETURN: Down to the sky over your head and the ground under your feet (3717), and to doing the mitzvos in deed, which is where settled seeing lives (3820). - AB2:AB2-C02 [You feel a strong pull to G-d: 'It is not good for me down here. I want to throw it all away.' Is that teshuvah?] (M1) Ask yourself: what exactly is not good? A longing to rise with no accounting of what is wrong is not yet a turn to G-d. → (M2) If you counted, you would find two things. If it is the narrowness of the soul down here, then work to understand as well as you can here, because the deepest drawing of Torah happens only below. If it is the hiding, then be bitter about it and fix your ways. → (M4) The second false arousal is a storm over your sins. You want to throw everything away. But it is an outburst, not an inner hearing, and afterwards not even a mark of it is left. → (M9) Real teshuvah has to reach the animal soul too. Picture a channel of water that was dammed. When it breaks through, it breaks the dam and carries the dam along with it. → (M10) Only the animal soul is allowed to feel that it can find no comfort at all, because it is the guilty one. The G-dly soul's pain is greater, but it cannot fall into that kind of collapse; that would be like the rot of sadness. → (M7) So contemplate to quiet the excitement, not to stir it. Hold your powers back so the feeling goes inward, where even your natural self can feel it. → (M15) Where does real comfort come from? Not from fainting toward G-d. From a strong decision in Torah, mitzvos and G-d's ways. Exactly there you find rest for your soul. ⇒ BOTTOM: The animal soul's own 'no comfort at all', which breaks it in its essence (2465); the G-dly soul is kept from that collapse. ⇒ RETURN: A plain, strong resolution about tomorrow's Torah and mitzvos, which is where the soul rests (2475). - AB2:AB2-C03 [You feel dry. Torah has no taste, prayer has no life, and you cry out about it. Surely that cry is holy?] (M3) There is a real cry that comes from spiritual distress: no life in Torah or prayer, and you beg to have your G-dly soul back. → (M4) But if the cry is only 'it hurts me', and you do not feel what you are far from, it is still cold. It is feeling yourself without feeling G-d. → (M2) You can tell by the result. Such a cry leaves you stuck in sadness, with no joy, or close to despair. Joy is the sign of G-dly life. → (M11) Why does it happen? A person can serve with his open mind and heart while his soul's very point is wrapped inside other wants, without his knowing it. → (M5) The fire that is real comes from thinking about how great G-d is until there is one want only, like a wife's desire for her husband. A love like that has to be true, or it is no love at all. → (M15) That fire eats the other fire. Each time you strengthen the holy fire, it cools and burns away the natural heat. At first it only forces it down; deeper, it turns the darkness itself to light. ⇒ BOTTOM: The soul's own point found wrapped in other wills (3328): the dryness is not the bottom, the covering is. ⇒ RETURN: Back to the next contemplation of His greatness, and to the small daily act of strengthening the holy fire (3329). - AB2:AB2-C04 [Something is pulling at you that you know is wrong. It feels huge and real. Is it?] (M3) Every Jew holds a simple faith: there is nothing outside G-d. Nothing at all stands apart from Him. → (M4) Then what is the thing that pulls you away from Him? Because it is cut off from His oneness, it has no real existence. Only the infinite power can hold up something that is not real, so that it looks real, and even very real. → (M2) That is why the Torah says 'G-d is testing you'. The test is a big thing to you, but the thing itself is nothing; it was set up only to be a test. When you stand firm, it is cancelled and leaves no mark. → (M7) Where is G-d's purpose, then? Not in the thing. In you, the one who stands and does not do it. → (M13) G-dliness is a real thing. Whatever is cut off from G-dliness is not a thing at all. Permitted things, used for a G-dly purpose, are real. → (M12) But knowing this takes a G-dly feeling. Without it, you will take the test for a real wall. With it, you know it is only a test and you stand. Even so, it is not easy; it takes great strength of soul. ⇒ BOTTOM: G-dliness 'is a thing' and nothing else is (2732): the pull has no being of its own. ⇒ RETURN: To the next real moment of pull: stand, and it leaves no trace (2731); the purpose was you, standing (2732). - AB2:AB2-C05 [You love G-d when you think about Him. When you stop thinking, where is the love?] (M2) There are two kinds of love. A love that comes from thinking exists only while you think. A love that is part of you, like a parent and child, is always there, even when it is not showing. → (M4) Your soul has that second kind toward G-d: a will to its source that never stops. It does not show because a person, as he lives, is mind and heart, and the work is with them. → (M9) You can see it in plain people. A simple Jew who has nothing to do with the work of the heart still wants G-d, from the point of his heart. → (M15) Even someone sunk deep can have it surface, and not only in trouble. It can come out exactly in a happy moment, when he finds himself close to G-d-fearing people. Then it is true through and through. → (M11) Another door is distance. When you honestly see that you are not what you should be, and it truly touches your heart, the will rises with force to leave the physical and be drawn to Him. → (M8) When this will comes out, it reaches all your powers. In the heart it is a quiet seriousness, 'ernstkeit': it leans only toward G-d. In deed it means never turning good into bad, whoever pushes you. ⇒ BOTTOM: A will in the soul's essence that has no hiding and never stops (3008), true 'because it never breaks off' (3017). ⇒ RETURN: Into a heart that leans one way and hands that do not turn good into bad (3018). - AB2:AB2-C06 [You wake up groggy. Your mind is thick and your soul feels tiny. Is this the worst moment to start serving G-d?] (M3) When you wake, your heart and mind are thick and coarse, and your soul is as small as a newborn baby. → (M15) Exactly then, when you are most physical and smallest, you need the higher light most. That is why the morning blessings come right then. → (M15) Being small helps. A newborn does not know what he understands and what he only accepts. So 'Modeh ani lefanecha', 'I thank You', is a general yes that gives over your whole self. → (M13) This is how true servants of G-d begin: with Modeh Ani. → (M16) Then look at what is before you. Things done only for lust are like a body with no life, something disgusting. Deepen your thought about it until it is recognition, like seeing. → (M12) The order is: the night before, an accounting and a broken heart; then, the moment you wake, Modeh Ani as setting yourself aside. Then light is felt in the morning blessings. ⇒ BOTTOM: A newborn's yes that does not yet know what it understands (2495-2496): total giving-over because nothing is sorted yet. ⇒ RETURN: Modeh Ani said as setting yourself down, then the morning blessings with light (2954). - AB2:AB2-C07 [You sit before a teacher who has something deep to give you. You burn to get it. How do you actually receive?] (M9) An empty cup holds; a full one does not. A student who is already wise in his own eyes cannot receive his teacher's mind. → (M4) Even working it out for yourself while he speaks is being full. You become your own teacher at that moment, and you lose what he is giving. → (M10) Surprising: even your burning thirst to hear gets in the way. Thirst before the lesson draws the teacher out. During it, thirst confuses you. You must be cool and given over. → (M9) You can feel it in yourself. While you are taking in a deep idea, if someone asks you a question you know, you cannot answer. You are busy swallowing. → (M15) That holding back is not a lack. It is what makes the idea yours. Once it is yours, it comes out in your own way of thinking, and every receiver becomes a giver. ⇒ BOTTOM: The receiver's stillness, holding himself back even from answering (3504-3505). ⇒ RETURN: He gives, in his own words, to the next person (3505). The text applies the same pause to the gap between mind and heart (3511) and to all screens above. - AB2:AB2-C08 [You have been working on a deep idea for a long time. Suddenly your mind goes blank. Did you fail?] (M3) Everything in the mind hangs on the will. If you want to think something through, you will. If you do not want it, you will not understand a thing. → (M8) Before understanding comes, there are stages. First it starts to get light in your mind. Then you feel that it is right before you know how or why. → (M4) Now the blank. After deep effort you go stunned. It is as if your mind has stopped; there is no light of understanding at all. → (M9) That blank is the bridge. Everyone who learns knows it: right after it, a new insight falls into your mind, and it reaches the depth. → (M6) Why? The light is coming from the source of the mind, which is bigger than your mind. To receive it, your mind must be emptied first. Rabbi Zeira fasted a hundred fasts to forget the Babylonian Talmud so he could take in the Talmud of the Land of Israel. ⇒ BOTTOM: The mind's own stopping (3530): its emptiness before what is above it. ⇒ RETURN: A new insight in plain words, 'it is like this and this' (3530); and the same emptying before every higher step, as souls in Gan Eden let go of their old delight to rise (3532). - AB2:AB2-C09 [In prayer you feel wind, noise and fire: lifting, trembling, burning. Is that the deepest prayer?] (M2) There is a noise in the soul that comes from meeting something brand-new, something you never imagined. What you are used to does not shake you. → (M5) Fire is higher. When you grasp His greatness fully, the soul catches fire, then thirsts, then grows lovesick, until it is ready to give itself in Shema. → (M4) But the verse says 'G-d is not in the wind, not in the noise, not in the fire'. All three, when they are felt, are still you. Deeper, there is a cry of the heart with no sound. → (M7) Inside the point of the heart the fire burns so deep it cannot be seen. The Yiddish says it: the heart 'smolders inside, very deep'. → (M7) Inner trembling is the same. 'The people saw and moved and stood far off' is fear on the outside. 'All the people in the camp trembled' is a shaking inside that no one else can see. → (M15) And the inner wind is not excitement at all. It is that you live in the matter. Like a man who is simply alive: alive in everything, not worked up. ⇒ BOTTOM: A fire too deep to be seen and a trembling no one notices (3275-3277). ⇒ RETURN: Living in the matter, as a living man is alive in whatever he does, not only in prayer (3277). - AB2:AB2-C10 [You have learned a G-dly idea in detail. Where is the idea itself?] (M11) Details can carry you away from the point. If you lose the point, you can go wrong. You must guard the very point of the idea all the time. → (M9) A great sage hears a few short words and knows what it is and what is meant. Long explanation adds nothing for him. → (M10) At this level you hold the whole matter in the point, details shining in it. If you go into the details, you are torn from the essence of the matter. → (M7) What cannot be said comes out in song. Inner pleasure and inner love, which words cannot carry, come out in a tune, the way a kiss does. → (M13) The song from the point is not an arranged tune. It is one sudden movement. Rabbi Akiva sang 'with every kind of tune' in prayer: one movement holding all tunes. → (M15) Deeper still, and it is in everyone: you hear a G-dly idea, you do not understand it at all, and your soul still clings to it. That is because your soul simply has a taste for G-dliness. ⇒ BOTTOM: A taste for G-dliness in the soul's essence that clings without understanding (3294). ⇒ RETURN: But only 'when he hears some G-dly understanding' (3294): back to learning, held at its point (3281). - AB2:AB2-C11 [You feel close to G-d and love Him. Is love the end of the road?] (M14) Why does a flame rise? Because it feels its source. A thing that feels where it comes from is pulled back to it. A thing that does not feel its source falls. → (M9) A candle in daylight does not stop being a flame. It simply does not give light. It has no place. → (M10) A small sage before a great one 'loses himself entirely'. Yet he is still a sage; before someone smaller he is somebody again. → (M5) There are two depths. 'There is none but You': without You there is nothing, but with You there is something. And 'There is nothing besides You': even with You, nothing. → (M4) So love is not the end. Love is not bittul, because there is someone who loves. → (M2) Wisdom's bittul is not bowing before something bigger. It is bittul itself. Wisdom is not love and clinging; it is simply being nothing. ⇒ BOTTOM: 'Even with You, nothing' (2294): the light as it is inside the Essence, no longer a light at all. ⇒ RETURN: The seraphim quench their thirst in their very wish to be included (2295); and wisdom's quiet nullity is the 'still small voice' of the Amidah, where the legs stand with the body (2822). - AB2:AB2-C12 [G-d is perfect and lacks nothing. So what is your service for?] (M1) All service fills some lack, like a servant cooking for his master. But G-d is the perfection of everything; nothing is missing. And the Sages ask: what does He care how an animal is slaughtered? → (M3) The world itself came from His holding Himself back. The tzimtzum is like G-d setting aside His own will. Now He waits for us: you set aside your will. → (M13) That means every power: your mind, your love, your anger and pride, your thoughts, even about permitted business, your speech, your eyes. 'Make yourself holy in what is permitted to you.' → (M4) Even a mitzvah done to draw down light can be a business deal. Do it as a servant, to do His will. Learn the hundred-and-first time. Read two pages if you usually read one. → (M15) As you set your will aside below, He sets His aside above, holds back His great light and comes down to clothe Himself in the worlds. → (M12) So both are true. On the outside, it makes no difference to Him. On the inside, it must be slaughtered properly. His inner intent is that there be service, and it does not grip Him at all. ⇒ BOTTOM: An intent rooted in the Essence that 'it should be service' (3632), and service that by its nature is 'in the Essence of the one served' because it is not for oneself (3635). ⇒ RETURN: Down to a second page of learning and a coin more than you wanted to give (3610-3611). - AB2:AB2-C13 [You try to contemplate and nothing happens. The understanding stays on the surface. You feel like the lowest of souls.] (M2) There really are souls like this, the 'heel' souls. The snake is wrapped around the heel: they are tied to the body's wants, and contemplation does not help them separate. → (M7) Even what they understand stays outside. It does not enter the mind or the heart. It is only surface knowledge. → (M15) Yet exactly because they are so far, they have a wonderful rise. When such a person lowers himself, bitter over how small he is, and has mercy on his own soul, he wakes a higher mercy. → (M14) Why? There is a mercy that comes from knowing and feeling, and a plain mercy above all that, which is just the nature of the high to go down to the low. So mercy falls most on the lowest. → (M10) Thirst is greater than hunger, and the desert makes the strongest thirst because nothing is revealed there. David wished: if only I had such love in the holy place. → (M12) And the bitter itself can be tasted as sweet. If you say even of the hard things 'this too is for good', there are no longer two kinds of flow. Then the place where all is good is revealed. ⇒ BOTTOM: The simple mercy of the Exalted for the lowest (3887), reached from the bottom of the heel. ⇒ RETURN: Honest self-rebuke, being careful even in what is permitted (3888), and 'gam zu letovah' said over the next hard thing (3884). - AB2:AB2-C14 [You reached a moment of burning closeness. You never want to come back from it. Is that the goal?] (M2) Aharon's sons truly saw the Infinite and were burning to dissolve into it. The intent above is that after running comes returning. They did not want to return. → (M1) But the verse also blames the coming close itself. How can drawing near be a sin? Because their running did not start from setting themselves aside. → (M3) The rule: if the run begins from bittul, from putting yourself down, then even at its height the return will come by itself. → (M2) That is what 'strange fire' means. Fire is running toward G-d. It is strange only when it is not in bittul. → (M11) A return without bittul is also a trap: wanting the light for yourself because it feels good to you. That is self-love. → (M15) So on Yom Kippur the High Priest goes into the Holy of Holies and comes out. They wanted only to go in. The cloud of incense is the bond; the point is that it be drawn down. ⇒ BOTTOM: A ratzo that begins in setting yourself down, so the shov is already inside it (3790). ⇒ RETURN: Coming out of the Holy of Holies into the day; the light is for drawing down, not for keeping (3791-3792). - AB2:AB2-C15 [You prayed with fire. An hour later you are furious at people who break G-d's will. Isn't that zeal for G-d?] (M3) Prayer with burning longing is a great pleasure. It comes from the pleasure of understanding a G-dly matter well. → (M11) When the burning stops, some time after prayer, you can come to anger at those who break His will. Whatever you see makes you angry. → (M4) If your service had kindness inside its fire, there would be no anger, only pain in your soul that something is not good. → (M12) Even when it is for the sake of Heaven, it is still harsh severity, and harm can come from it. → (M5) The text says this plainly elsewhere: anger at evil is not yet the true good. Anger is not needed even against evil. Only the pain that evil exists. → (M15) Your temperament is not erased. A kindly nature serves calmly, with love like water, and fixes things by drawing them close; a fiery nature serves with fire and pushes away. Serving through your own nature is what refines it. ⇒ BOTTOM: Pain that evil exists, without anger (2051, 3480). ⇒ RETURN: Back into the same temperament, now serving G-d, and being refined by it (3363). - AB2:AB2-C16 [A wise, calm person is not moved by much. Is he cold? Is that calm just ego?] (M2) Mind and emotions are two different things. The mind is bittul. Pride comes from foolishness, the opposite of wisdom. → (M4) The strength of the mind is that it does not get carried away. 'Er farlirt zich nit': he does not lose himself. That is settledness, which is truth, not ego. → (M7) Where there is no need to be moved, he is not moved at all. Where there is need, he is moved more inwardly than anyone, only it does not show. → (M10) Emotions are a 'something': there is one who loves and one who fears. Love and fear that are not a 'something', like essential love or inner fear, are not emotions at all. → (M9) A small mind needs to pour out. 'More than the calf wants to suck, the cow wants to nurse.' A great mind holds itself back. → (M15) This is also how a person grows. A child's feelings are strong and loud. As the mind grows, the feelings weaken and grow calm, like old wine that has settled. ⇒ BOTTOM: An inward stirring that does not show (3832), and love that is not an emotion because no 'one who loves' stands out in it (3832). ⇒ RETURN: Ordinary calm and holding back, as the mind grows (2101, 3833). - AB2:AB2-C17 [Your name is just a label others use. What could a name have to do with your depths?] (M3) A name is not felt even by its owner. Light shows its source; a name shows nothing. Life comes to you through it, and you do not notice. → (M9) Yet call someone who has fainted by his name, and he can come back. A name calls the life up from its root. → (M6) So teshuvah must come from the depth, like a river that stopped flowing: you have to dig at the depth of the spring. → (M15) Call a person 'wise', and even when no wisdom is showing, new ideas come to him from his hidden power. Praise a man as kind when he has no wish to give, and the power of kindness wakes in him, as if from his very self. → (M14) Why does it work? Because deep down he knows what he is capable of. Without that inner self-knowing, praise would reveal nothing. → (M15) Your name is not something you need. You feel no use for it, except that through it you turn to whoever calls you. ⇒ BOTTOM: A self-knowing beneath your powers, reached only by being called (3803). ⇒ RETURN: Turning when called (3801). On Rosh Hashanah we call out 'HaMelech' (2536); in the day, praise others into their goodness (3802). - AB2:AB2-C18 [Is G-d really here, in this ordinary room, the way He is 'above'?] (M4) Yes. The Essence of the Infinite is below exactly as above, because nothing hides anything from the Essence. → (M14) Then why do you not feel it? Because if creatures felt it even a little, there would be no world at all. Its not being felt is His strength. → (M16) The aim is not more light. The aim is that our knowing becomes like His knowing: that before Him all is as nothing, and we know it too. → (M8) In practice: think about it until it lives in your mind and you feel it as truly as if your eyes saw it. → (M11) Notice what understanding is like. You did not know before, so the idea comes as news. Even when it settles 'very well', the earlier hiding leaves room for another way to see it. → (M10) When the infinite light shines, it is the other way: G-d is felt plainly, and the world is the news. Then there is no room for another view. Today, the world is plain to us and G-d is the news. → (M15) It has happened. In the Temple a person felt G-dliness and did not feel himself at all. And in the future, grasping G-d will be natural, the way grasping the world is natural now. ⇒ BOTTOM: The reversal of what is plain: G-d plain and the world the novelty (3674), as when one in the Temple 'felt G-dliness and not himself at all' (3677). ⇒ RETURN: Back to this room, thinking until it is 'as if seen' (3628); and to mitzvos in physical things, where the Essence is drawn (3545). - AB2:AB2-C19 [After you climbed higher, it went dark. You saw more before. Did you fall?] (M3) Before dawn the dark gets stronger. That is the 'darkness of morning'. So too before the great day, in the heels of Moshiach, the dark grows. → (M9) And look at dawn's light: it is stronger than the sun at midday. The sun's light is only a spread-out glow. Dawn is the essential light, hidden. → (M5) Light that comes after darkness is mightier than the light before it, because it has to break through. → (M15) You do not see with the white of the eye, only with the black. The extra light comes from the dark. → (M10) The Baal Shem Tov's mashal: a spiral staircase around a pillar with a carving at the top. On the first step you see it. On the second you are higher and closer, but behind the pillar, and you do not see it. On the third you see it again. ⇒ BOTTOM: The dark step behind the pillar, which is higher than the bright one below it (2890-2891). ⇒ RETURN: Keep climbing the next step; the light after the dark is stronger (2231). - AB2:AB2-C20 [You pray hard for what you lack. Is that the best prayer?] (M6) When a person lacks something, the lack is first in the Shechinah. That is why he lacks. → (M7) So do not pray about your own lack; pray about the lack in the Shechinah. When it is filled above, it is filled below too. → (M9) This is Moshe's 'prayer of the rich'. He said: I do not ask for myself. A country is in ruins, and it is Yours. Decree that it be built. → (M5) That is the level of a chassid, above a tzaddik and above the upright: he does nothing for himself at all. → (M15) And what chassidus does is make your natural powers into G-dly powers. → (M12) So eat to learn and pray with the strength of that food. When that is truly your aim, light is added at once, as 'wine and fragrance open the mind'. ⇒ BOTTOM: Prayer that is not for oneself, because the lack is His (3554, 3079). ⇒ RETURN: Natural powers turned G-dly: eating to learn and pray (3082, 3084).

## AB3 — Ayin Beis III

Hemshech Ayin Beis 3901-5750 shows how the Essence itself (not only its light) comes into the plain world, the body and the dark: the made thing proves the True Being; love is still 'someone who loves'; nearness feels like sin; hoda'ah where the mind touches and bows; darkness that turns itself (black flame, incense, the animal soul's own cry); the desert thirst found only below; and Kel De'os, where 'only He' and 'the world bows' are one. Practice: pray as you are, outshine stray thoughts, let the natural mind agree, contemplate until it is sweet, avoid sadness.

## Depths

- **AB3:AB3-D01 The made thing proves the Essence** (extends D10): The solid, ordinary world is not further from G-d than spiritual light. A real 'something' from nothing could only come from the One whose being is from Himself, so His very power is 'carried down' into it. Light only shows that there is a source; the thing shows the Essence. (stated) | Inside: The rooster that would vanish if its root showed; the river that fails; the tree whose being is the power joining its elements (5612). | Lands: In the plainest object, now. (Ours: say 'Creator' over one ordinary thing.) | Q: ונמצא דהיש הנברא דוקא מכריח את בחי' יש האמיתי — It is precisely the created thing that proves the True Being. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:4454)
- **AB3:AB3-D02 Love is still someone who loves** (extends D2): Even the highest love from understanding — loving G-d 'because He is your life' — still has a self in it: I want what is good for me. Full bittul is either the yechida's love that has no self, or 'complete fear' that comes from seeing. That is why true servants feel like sinners on Rosh Hashanah: their love was felt as theirs. (stated) | Inside: A coarse feeling after a warm prayer (4125); the 'set-aside' person who does not feel his own excitement (4126). | Lands: After prayer; on Rosh Hashanah; in the Amida's 'forgive us'. | Q: ה"ה בבחי' יש מי שאוהב, שעז"נ לאה' את ה"א כי הוא חייך — It is still 'someone who loves': love G-d for He is your life. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:4124)
- **AB3:AB3-D03 Nearness feels like sin** (extends new): The closer you stand before the King, the bigger your smallest flaw becomes — not in fact but in your soul. That is why 'forgive us' comes right after the Shema of all your heart. In the future, with deeper bittul, the righteous will feel even more like sinners. Their 'darkness' is only light counted as dark beside more light. (stated) | Inside: 'Around Him it storms greatly' — strict to a hair's breadth (5220-5221). | Lands: In the Amida, at 'selach lanu'. | Q: אחר שקרוב אל אור פני מלך נעשה בנפשו חוטא גדול יותר — Since he is near the King's light he becomes a greater sinner in his soul. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:5222)
- **AB3:AB3-D04 Hoda'ah: the mind touches and bows** (extends D5): There are three contemplations. Seeing G-d as the life within (heart fills). Seeing the endless power in worlds (lovesick, the deeper the sicker). And the Essence itself: the heart is too small, the mind cannot bear it; understanding cancels itself and only admission remains. This is not 'not knowing' but knowing that touches and bows, and it nullifies the whole self. (stated) | Inside: Lovesick; 'the heart too small, the mind cannot bear'; the spirit in the soul that is never filled (4655). | Lands: In the Amida (Yehuda = admission) after the Shema (5428-5429). | Q: אדרבה שם הוא ביטול ההשגה שמתבטל בהשגתו והוא רק בבחי' הודאה לבד — There understanding cancels itself; only admission. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:5427)
- **AB3:AB3-D05 Darkness that turns itself** (extends D9): Beyond light chasing darkness (the sun) and light coming into darkness' place (the tzaddik's way) is darkness turning by itself: the black flame that consumes the wick, the animal soul's own cry, R. Elazar ben Durdaya's tears. It comes from the hidden Essence, deeper than revealed light, and reaches higher than the Thirteen Attributes. (stated) | Inside: Black flame; incense; poison turned medicine; the lower waters weeping; the groan of the slain (5157). | Lands: In the body's own cry; in teshuvah from the tight place. | Q: נהורא אוכמא אינו מאיר רק ששורף ומכלה את הפתילה — The black flame does not shine; it burns up the wick. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:5180)
- **AB3:AB3-D06 The desert: distance makes the thirst** (extends D8): Above, the soul has complete fear. Only below, in a body and a dry world, does it become a 'kallah' with a longing that consumes. Starting from the pain of this world's emptiness, it sees that all worlds and lights are a desert before Him, and only He lives. That thirst brings hatred of evil and turns the animal soul over. (stated) | Inside: Black but beautiful; 'my soul thirsts... in a dry land'; hungry and thirsty at once (5679). | Lands: Weekdays lovesick; Shabbos the sun shines for the poor (5691). | Q: דבארץ צי' בלי מים שאין האור והגילוי שם דוקא נעשה הצמאון ביותר — In a dry land without water the thirst is greatest. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:5674)
- **AB3:AB3-D07 Making Him King** (extends D11): Kindness can be felt alone; kingship cannot. It wakes only when people give up their will completely. Adam crowned Him; each Rosh Hashanah we do. Yet our act 'takes up space' only by His kindness. (stated) | Inside: The coronation; the people bowing. | Lands: Rosh Hashanah; Shema's kabbalas ol. | Q: שהעם מראה הביטול ביותר אל המלך שמתבטלים אליו בכל רצונם לגמרי ועי"ז דוקא מתעורר בו כח המלוכה — Only the people's full bittul wakes the king's power. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:5301)
- **AB3:AB3-D08 Make the natural mind agree** (extends D13): The animal soul is not changed by the G-dly soul's understanding or love alone. The G-dly soul must work to explain G-dliness in the animal soul's own common sense until it agrees. Then the physical loses its taste. (stated) | Inside: Oil drawn up the wick into the flame (5694). | Lands: In the morning's contemplation; in the day's pulls. | Q: כ"א שמשתדל ומתעסק עם השכל הטבעי דנה"ב שיבין ג"כ את הענין ויונח אצלו בטוב — He works with the natural mind so it too understands. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:5701)
- **AB3:AB3-D09 Two knowings, one** (extends D4): From above, nothing exists but Him. From below, things exist and bow by choice. Both are true and one in the Essence, since the hiding is His power. Shema joins them: 'one in one'. (stated) | Inside: Fish in the sea (5746); 'I am first and last' (5739). | Lands: Shema. | Q: הנה בעצמות א"ס ב' הדעות אחת הן ששניהם אמת — In the Essence the two views are one. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:5738)
- **AB3:AB3-D10 Cold understanding and the honey** (extends D12): Understanding without pleasure does not move the heart. Every Jew has pleasure in G-dliness; go deep until the idea lives in you; then love Him like your own life, a honey that turns you into itself. (stated) | Inside: Honey that turns all into honey; the forest dripping honey (5656). | Lands: Shema; Shabbos. | Q: והסבה לזה הוא מפני שההתבוננות היא בקרירות שאין בו חיות ועונג בהענין המושג — The contemplation is cold. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Ayin Beis (Lubavitch and Rostov, 1912-1916).txt:5647)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- AB3:AB3-C01 [Is the world around me real, or is only G-d real?] (M2) A rooster in the yard is not just a far-off 'echo' of an angel. No number of steps down from heaven could ever produce one solid thing; it has to be made from nothing. → (M10) Something that can stop existing is not truly real, even while it is here. → (M5) Only One is real in a way that no one made: He simply is what He is. → (M14) That is why the only true name we can give Him is 'Creator'. It does not describe Him; it points at the One whose being is from Himself. → (M15) So the solid world does not hide Him. It is the proof of Him: the made thing forces you to the True Being. → (M16) The Essence's own power is carried into the ordinary thing in front of you, more than into any spiritual light. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the plain, solid thing is where the Essence's own power is most present (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: look at one ordinary object and say 'Creator' over it; its being solid is His being, carried down (stated: 'carried down'; the practice is ours). - AB3:AB3-C02 [Some mornings I wake up broken and dull for no reason. Is my prayer wasted?] (M9) Sometimes a person wakes up broken-hearted and low for no reason at all, and can only pray with bitterness. → (M3) Absence always comes before being. Every new thing starts with something missing. → (M2) A seed only grows once it rots in the ground. → (M12) The breaking and the joy hang on each other: the more the heart is broken over being far, the greater the joy that comes. → (M4) Being pushed away lifts you higher than being drawn close. → (M15) This is what makes a person a 'walker'. Souls above only stand; down here, through distance, you move. → (M7) An empty vessel holds. The broken heart is the vessel G-dliness fills. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the empty, broken morning is itself the vessel (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: pray that morning as it is, in bitterness, without forcing joy; joy comes after, measured by the breaking (stated). - AB3:AB3-C03 [I love G-d. Isn't that the goal?] (M8) Even great love, 'with all your heart and soul', is still 'someone who loves'. You love Him because He is your life. → (M11) So inside the love there is a feeling of yourself: your own soul is precious to you and you want what is good for you. → (M11) That is why, after a warm prayer, a coarse feeling can come. → (M4) Someone with real bittul — 'a person who is put away' — does not feel even his own excitement. → (M5) Higher is the love of yechida, 'with all your might': a bond with the Infinite itself, like a son's. → (M15) So on Rosh Hashanah even true servants feel like sinners, because their service was felt. → (M5) Even the kernel is only a shell to what is higher. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the love that has no 'I' in it, the soul's own bond (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: feel bitter not over sins but over how much of yourself was in your love (stated, RH). - AB3:AB3-C04 [If G-d hides, is He really hidden?] (M3) A teacher who wants to teach must first hide his own light, as if he never understood. → (M2) He must cut the giving short to fit the student's vessel. → (M7) He even holds back his own love and insight at their source, so they do not pour out. → (M4) Sometimes the opposite: the teacher pours out light too deep, and the student falls in his own eyes, and that makes him a vessel. → (M10) But the hiding is only for the student. The teacher sees the whole depth in every simple word. → (M6) For the giver nothing is hidden at all. → (M13) The hiding is His own power. An essence does not hide an essence. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: there is no place empty of Him; the hiding is Him (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: the same as the burning bush: in the lowest thorn bush, He is there (stated, 5538). - AB3:AB3-C05 [How can the bad in me become good, not just pushed down?] (M9) When a stray thought falls on you suddenly, do not lose heart; strengthen your love for G-d. → (M10) But wrestling with it is like wrestling with a filthy man: you get dirty too. That is the first, weak sweetening. → (M5) A second sweetening: a much higher light shines, and the accuser simply becomes a defender. → (M2) Like those sentenced to death who meet the king: they are pardoned without any trial. → (M15) The third sweetening is deepest: the animal soul itself wakes up, finds no comfort, and is disgusted with its life. → (M2) That is the incense: the sharpest, bitterest spices become the strongest, sweetest smell. → (M7) Like poison that becomes medicine for a dying man: the bad itself turns into life. → (M15) The groan of the body from below reaches the Essence: 'the lower waters weep'. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the animal soul's own cry, from its own darkness (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: do not run from the crude pain of the body-self; let it cry; that crying reaches higher than a polite teshuvah (stated in principle; phrasing ours). - AB3:AB3-C06 [Is light chasing darkness away the deepest thing?] (M10) The sun only pushes darkness away. When it sets, the dark is back. → (M4) A black flame in a candle does not light the room. It eats the wick. The darkness itself is used up. → (M9) Rabbi Elazar ben Durdaya, seeing that it depended only on him, cried out and his soul left him.
