That is darkness turning itself. → (M6) Then the harsh powers are sweetened in their root, by themselves, not by kindness from outside. → (M5) In the future the night itself will shine, not by any sun. → (M10) Like time itself without time, as space under the Ark took no space. → (M13) 'Eternal light' means not light on the world, but the world itself is light. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the dark itself as light; time itself as timeless (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: right now, by a turn from the bottom of the dark, not by waiting for a 'sun' (stated through R. Elazar). - AB3:AB3-C07 [Why does G-d's anger, or my own intensity, matter?] (M3) Kindness is like water: cool. A cow is gentle, but slow and cold. → (M9) Teaching from the cold mind does not move anyone. → (M7) Words from the fire of the heart go into the other's heart. → (M5) And then the speaker himself hears new things he never knew. → (M13) Strength breaks into drops, like rain; each drop is full of life. → (M4) A king's anger is more life, not less: the more life in the blood, the more anger. → (M6) So when he is appeased, more life flows than before. → (M15) That is why teshuvah from distress draws a light higher than the Thirteen Attributes of Mercy. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: even His anger is His life; the far place holds more (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: speak and pray from the heart's warmth, not cold accuracy (stated). - AB3:AB3-C08 [What kind of mercy can reach me, if G-d is so far above?] (M2) One kind of mercy comes from feeling the other's lack, up close. → (M10) The higher one is, the more mercy: to the king even the minister is low. → (M4) But even that mercy still has some feeling for the people; the king still relates to them. → (M5) The Essence is 'the only King', with nothing beside Him; there is no one to pity. → (M15) And there we ask: in Your great, essential mercy, have mercy on us. → (M9) It is reached by teshuvah from the tight place. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: mercy from the Essence, where no one is beside Him (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: say 'in Your great mercy' in morning prayer as asking the Essence itself (stated). - AB3:AB3-C09 [How can a righteous person do teshuvah if he did not sin?] (M1) Because of their height, the righteous count themselves as real sinners. → (M2) An animal that dirties the king's palace is no surprise; a man who does it deserves death. → (M15) So right after the Shema of all your heart, standing before the King, we say 'forgive us, our Father, for we have sinned'. → (M16) The closer to the light of the King's face, the greater a sinner one becomes in his soul. → (M10) Every service is not true at all compared to His truth. → (M5) In Moshiach's days, with deeper bittul, the righteous will feel even more as sinners. → (M13) Not real darkness: light that counts as dark beside a higher light. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: nearness feels like sin (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: say 'selach lanu' at the height of the Amida as nearness, not as despair (stated). - AB3:AB3-C10 [Why does G-d need me to accept Him as King?] (M9) A kind man feels kindness even alone; Avraham was pained to have no one to give to. → (M10) Kingship is different: it comes only through others. → (M3) Only when the people give up their will completely does the king's power wake. → (M10) And only over those apart from him, not over children or friends. → (M6) Above, too: G-d's kingship was not revealed until Adam accepted His yoke. → (M16) So every Rosh Hashanah: 'say kingship verses before Me so you make Me King'. → (M10) But who are we that our act should count? It counts only by His higher kindness. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the King's kingship waits for a small person's bittul, and even that is His gift (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: accept His kingship in words, each morning and each Rosh Hashanah (stated). - AB3:AB3-C11 [Why would the deepest Torah be in stories and simple acts?] (M1) A story like 'Timna was a concubine' is as holy as 'Hear O Israel'. → (M4) Because its light is so high, it can only come as a plain story. → (M2) The mitzvos in the written Torah are just the bare deed, with no reasons. → (M3) The outside of something high reaches lower than the inside of something low. → (M2) That is why Eliyahu can be a messenger everywhere. → (M15) So the mitzvos are the same for every Jew, but Torah learning has levels. → (M15) What went lowest has the highest root. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the bare deed, the same in every hand (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: do the mitzvah plainly; its plainness is where the highest is (stated). - AB3:AB3-C12 [Why do I have to struggle with questions? Why not just see?] (M3) The wise man sees the truth at once; Binah is the seal that makes it true for others. → (M10) Only the one who invented the idea sees why it must be so. → (M4) While you work through questions, the truth gets darker. → (M15) But out of that darkness the light is found, like a new birth. → (M9) The Talmud sages toiled with mesirus nefesh, because Torah truth touched their very soul. → (M5) So they reached a higher source than the clear Mishnah. → (M13) Like Avraham's test: G-d 'knew', but now it became true. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the dark of questions is the door to a higher light (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: learn with toil, caring about the truth (stated). - AB3:AB3-C13 [What are the levels of contemplating G-d's unity?] (M8) First: feel the life in your own body; so G-d is the life of everything. → (M5) Second: look at the endless power that keeps worlds existing. This brings a lovesick longing. → (M4) The deeper you go, the sicker with love. → (M12) His heart aches, because all his days he will never grasp the Infinite, while his eyes see it fills everything. → (M5) Third: about the Essence itself, the heart is too small and the mind cannot bear it. → (M10) There, understanding cancels itself; all that is left is to bow and admit. → (M15) Because the soul cannot bear it in mind or heart, it is nullified from all its being. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: hoda'ah — the mind touches and bows (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: Shema (seeing and hearing), then the Amida (bowing) (stated). - AB3:AB3-C14 [How do I 'lengthen' the word Echad?] (M3) Speech is only a glow of the soul: speak much or not at all, the soul is not less. → (M9) Deep in thought you do not feel the words at all. → (M7) The letters are fixed in the soul's essence: a mute father has a speaking child. → (M12) One lost in a deep idea speaks about it without even feeling it. → (M11) So letters, and things, exist only because light is hidden. → (M6) And since for Him the hiding hides nothing, all is nullified, now as before creation. → (M9) In every unity, you unite your own soul, to be nullified in the One. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: Shmo hagadol in the Essence — and your soul inside it (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: say Amen yehei shmei rabbah with all your strength (stated). - AB3:AB3-C15 [If everything is G-d, why does the world feel solid?] (M3) A tree is made of the four elements, but what makes it a tree is the power that joins them. → (M14) A thing made from nothing needs the making power always; stop it and the thing is gone. → (M11) The 'something' does not feel this: it feels itself real. → (M15) In the future it will be seen, and the body will be G-dliness. → (M15) Then the soul may even get life from the body. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the body itself, Infinite light (stated, future). ⇒ RETURN: Return: now, in Shema, think it, though the body does not feel it (stated). - AB3:AB3-C16 [Does my mood matter to what flows from above?] (M3) The lower world receives the way it stands: shining face below, shining face above. → (M11) If it stands in sadness, it is given judgment. → (M9) From here you see how bad sadness is. → (M1) Our service reaches only Malchus, yet it changes what flows from above it. → (M16) 'Open for Me like the eye of a needle, and I will open like a hall.' → (M7) Your opening by itself brings His. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: Atika's mercy opens to a small opening (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: guard against sadness; stand with a shining face (stated). - AB3:AB3-C17 [Is praising G-d the highest thing?] (M3) Angels come from a 'kiss' (outside), souls from deep union (essence). → (M2) Angels sing to express how much they long. → (M2) A man inside the king's palace who sees the glory does not praise. He is nullified. → (M10) Only when he goes out does he tell the praises. → (M4) Angels only know that 'there is One who is holy'. → (M15) Jewish souls, when they love, are included in their source. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: silence inside the palace (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: Pesukei and Yotzer (praise) lead to Shema (being absorbed) (stated). - AB3:AB3-C18 [I understand it well, but my heart doesn't move. Why?] (M1) Sometimes you grasp a G-dly idea well, even your mind is moved, but your heart doesn't love. → (M11) Because the contemplation is cold, without life or pleasure. → (M8) Every Jew has pleasure in G-dliness, even simple people. → (M9) You can see it from the pain at being far. → (M17) Go deep until the idea lives in you. → (M2) Joy in the heart changes you, the way a miser becomes generous at a joyful time. → (M8) Then you love Him as you love your own life. → (M15) Like honey that turns everything into honey. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: love as pleasure, being absorbed (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: Shabbos eating, too, becomes G-dly pleasure (stated 5655). - AB3:AB3-C19 [Why must a soul come down into a body and a dark world?] (M2) The eye sees only through the black. → (M10) Above, the soul has full fear, because love is still 'someone who loves'. → (M11) Love wants for itself. → (M4) Down here she becomes a 'bride' — kallah, from 'kelos', longing to the end. → (M15) In a dry land without water, the thirst is greatest. → (M5) Then see: all worlds are a desert before Him. → (M16) The longing begins from the distance of this lower world. → (M7) And the more the thirst, the more you hate the bad. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: thirst in the desert (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: Shabbos and Torah quench it (stated). - AB3:AB3-C20 [What does it mean that G-d is 'living'?] (M2) The body lives only from the soul. → (M5) The soul lives forever only because it is bound to Him. → (M5) Even His light: if He willed not to shine, there would be no light. → (M13) So all worlds and lights are like stone before Him. → (M9) 'He is your life' means: you have no life but Him. → (M15) The thirst is quenched in Torah and mitzvos, which are His essence. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: only the Essence lives by itself (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: mitzvos (stated). - AB3:AB3-C21 [Why doesn't my animal side change when I pray?] (M3) The animal soul has a mind, but only for physical things. → (M12) Opposites joined. → (M9) Work so the natural mind itself understands. → (M10) Otherwise it does not move. → (M11) At most it is weakened. → (M15) When it understands, it loses its taste for physical things. → (M13) Each power refined adds much light. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the oil burns into the flame (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: explain G-dliness to your own common sense (stated). - AB3:AB3-C22 [Is the world nothing, or is it something that bows to Him?] (M3) Higher knowing: above is real, below nothing. → (M2) Lower knowing: the world is real, but bows by choice — kabbalas ol. → (M12) In the Essence both are one and both are true. → (M6) Because the hiding is His power as much as the showing. → (M13) 'I am first and I am last' — both equal. → (M15) So the two unities of Shema must become 'one in one'. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: one in one (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return: Shema's first verse holding both (stated).

## AR — the Alter Rebbe

The Alter Rebbe's maamarim go deeper by being honest about the self. Even the greatest lover of G-d is 'something, not nothing' (Tanya 35); even Rabbi Akiva's death on the word 'One' was a creature's yielding, 'still far from G-dliness' until a light from above took it in (TO Vayeshev); and yielding itself can turn into pride (LT Behar). So the bottom is not a feeling and not an achievement. It is a deed of His will, where no one stands apart, and a gift received, like Shabbos, that we only guard. Under love there is a bond with no reason. The Torah gives love a reason, 'He is your life', and gives self-giving none, because self-giving binds to Him as He is, before He is anyone's Life: 'even had He not created the worlds, all would be the same before Him' (TO Esther). This point is equal in every Jew and surfaces even as a sinner's regret (LT Bamidbar). From His side the bond is a covenant He chose against reason (LT Nitzavim). Knowing Him is sensing that He is, the way you sense your own soul without grasping what it is; feeling follows attention, so the command is to keep Him in mind as you keep yourself (LT Vaeschanan 6). The Essence was never hidden, only the light: 'even little children know there is a G-d' (TO Vayera). The turn back is driven by compassion for one's own soul, for the distance itself, even without sin. If you feel nothing, the numbness is the sign of being cut off and a reason for greater mercy; we say 'Your great mercy' because only He knows how far it is (LT Shir HaShirim, Re'eh, Pinchas, Shabbos Shuva). The low holds the high. Earth grows something from nothing, which the heavens cannot (Iggeres HaKodesh 20); the feet heal the head (TO Bereshis); hidden and revealed are equal before the Essence, so the highest light dresses in nature, like a groom sleeping in a tannery for his bride (TO Esther); a test holds G-d's power, like the top stone of a wall that falls farthest (LT Re'eh); the bare power of desire is holy at its root (LT Chukas); the beginner's ego is chaff that shields the kernel (TO Esther supplements). For the guide: never demand that the depth be felt. Union does not depend on feeling ('I was like an animal with You, and I am always with You', Tanya 46; LT Vayikra 5). In the surrounding light reached by bowing, 'He sees no wrong' (TO Bereshis 1). The running and returning is His rhythm too; even His giving pulses, so that vessels can exist (TO Bereshis 2). How these texts climb (stated markers): a kasha on a word or a clash of verses; 'the matter is'; a mashal from the person's own body or soul ('from my flesh I see G-d'); 'but in truth'; the mashal pushed until it breaks; 'higher still'; and then 'from this', down to the lips, the hand, the next mitzvah, the bow at Modim.

## Depths

- **AR:AR-D1 Even the lover is still someone** (extends D2 and D12: sharpens D2 (the self cannot make itself vanish) by saying where it does vanish: in His will done by the body.): The deepest love of G-d still has a lover in it: someone who loves and fears Him. That someone is 'something, not nothing', and the Presence rests only on what has fully yielded. The deed of a mitzvah is different: it is His will with nothing hiding it, so in the act itself there is no separate one. The descent does not end in a feeling of love; it ends in a deed where no one is standing apart. (stated: Tanya 35; ours: the placement after D2.) | Inside: A wick that needs oil: the soul alone does not keep the flame; deeds are the oil (Tanya 35, the Zohar's picture). | Lands: The hand giving a coin, the mouth saying a blessing, the feet walking to a mitzvah, now. | Q: ואם כן, זה האוהב, שהוא יש ולא אפס – לית מחשבה דיליה תפיסא ביה כלל — This lover, who is something and not nothing: his thought does not grasp Him at all. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Tanya (Liozna, 1786-1796).txt:476)
- **AR:AR-D2 Your yielding is not yet the nothing** (extends D2 (no one left to watch): confirms and sharpens it with the Akiva case and the warning that bittul can itself become a 'yesh'.): Turning the 'something' into 'nothing' is the aim of every mitzvah, but a creature's yielding is still a creature yielding: 'still he is a something, only he nullifies himself'. Even Rabbi Akiva's death on 'One' was 'still far from G-dliness' until a light from the true One took it in. The true nothing is a gift, like Shabbos, to be guarded, not made; and the worst trap is pride in one's own yielding. (stated: LT Behar; TO Vayeshev.) | Inside: Being mute: like the sheaves, like a fetus that eats what its mother eats; you stop speaking and giving for a while (TO Vayeshev). | Lands: The bow at Modim; Shabbos received, not produced. | Q: אמנם ביטול זה אינו אמיתי שהרי מ״מ יש הוא אלא שמבטל א״ע — This nullification is not true, for he is still a something, only he nullifies himself. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Likkutei Torah (Liozna and Liadi, 1796-1812).txt:997)
- **AR:AR-D3 Bound to Him before He is your Life** (extends D8 (what you cannot let go): extends D8 by locating the bond above Him-as-Life-giver, not inside love.): The Torah gives a reason for love, 'He is your life', and none for giving up life. Because 'He is your life' describes Him as Life-giver to the worlds, and 'that is not the main thing of G-dliness'; even had He created nothing, all would be the same to Him. Self-giving comes from refusing to be cut off from Him as He is, above being Life of life. That is why it has no reason, and why it is in every Jew, even the lightest. (stated: TO Esther 12; LT Vayikra 5.) | Inside: A candle in a torch: the soul's essence above the mind is nullified in His Essence (LT Vayikra 5, line 238). | Lands: The daily Shema 'with self-giving at One'; refusing one small separation today because it is a separation. | Q: ואין זה טעם על מסירת נפש כיון שהטעם הוא כי הוא חייך והרי מסירת נפש הוא להשליך חייו מנגד — This is no reason for self-sacrifice: the reason is 'He is your life', and self-sacrifice is casting life away. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Torah Ohr (Liozna and Liadi, c. 1796-1812).txt:2631)
- **AR:AR-D4 The Essence was never hidden** (extends D13 (plainness is deepest) and D7 (simple faith): supplies the reason plainness reaches: the Essence is revealed, the light is what is hidden.): The great contraction hid the light, not the Luminary. 'In the Luminary, the Infinite Himself, contraction and hiding do not apply; on the contrary, it is revealed; so even children know there is a G-d', without grasping how. The space is not truly empty; He fills it. What a child knows simply is the deepest, not the least. (stated: TO Vayera.) | Inside: A child's certainty with no proof. | Lands: Saying 'Modeh ani' like a child, meaning it plainly. | Q: ואדרבה המאור הוא בהתגלות ולכן אפילו תנוקות יודעים שיש שם אלוה מצוי — On the contrary, the Luminary is revealed; therefore even little children know there is a G-d. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Torah Ohr (Liozna and Liadi, c. 1796-1812).txt:315)
- **AR:AR-D5 Knowing He is, as you know you are** (extends D5 (where knowing ends): corrects a possible reading of D5. The end of grasping is not the end of knowing; a sensing of existence remains and is commanded.): Da'as is not grasping what He is; no thought does that. It is sensing that He is, the way you sense your own soul: you cannot say what it is, yet you feel it fully and need no faith or proof. And feeling follows attention: you are moved by your own affairs because you never take your mind off yourself. 'Know the G-d of your father' means: never take your mind off Him. Faith alone stays above, like the thief who prays at the tunnel and breaks in anyway. (stated: LT Vaeschanan 6.) | Inside: Not a vision, not a concept: the quiet certainty of being alive. | Lands: Shiviti: G-d before me always, through the day, as one carries oneself. | Q: ומ״מ מציאותן יודע ומרגיש ממש אף שאינו משיג מהותן ואין צריך על זה אמונה וראיה. כ״א נרגש ממש — He knows and truly feels their existence though he does not grasp their essence; he needs no faith or proof; it is truly felt. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Likkutei Torah (Liozna and Liadi, 1796-1812).txt:2910)
- **AR:AR-D6 Compassion for your own soul** (extends New: between D8 (the bond) and D9 (the turn). The turn is driven by compassion, not by insight.): Under the bottom there is a weeping that is not about sin. Even without sinning, being in a body is 'utter distance' from Him, and all paradise is one ray. The return is compassion for one's own soul. If you feel nothing, that numbness is the sign of being cut off, a reason for 'even greater mercy'; and since only He knows the size of the distance, we say 'in Your great mercy'. One thought of return reconnects; a captive prince is still a prince. (stated: LT Shir 4, Re'eh, Pinchas, Shabbos Shuva, Rosh Hashanah, Shir 41.) | Inside: Tears in the inner room; the shofar's sobbing; 'the lower waters weep: we want to be before the King'. | Lands: Rosh Hashanah weeping; 'ahavah rabbah... b'rachamecha harabim' every morning; after the tears, the bread of Torah. | Q: אף שלא חטא אעפ״כ הוא נתגשם ונפרד ממהותו ועצמותו בתכלית הריחוק — Even though he did not sin, he became physical and separated from His Essence in utter distance. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Likkutei Torah (Liozna and Liadi, 1796-1812).txt:4369)
- **AR:AR-D7 Hidden and revealed are the same to Him** (extends D4 (neither something nor nothing) and D10 (the world given back as His): adds that the hidden itself is His chosen dwelling for the highest.): Every world is hidden to the one below and revealed to the one above; that chain is the chain of worlds. Before His Essence there is no above and below. When the Essence showed itself at the Sea, hidden and revealed became equal and the sea became dry land. On Purim, when His Essence shines, Haman and Mordechai are equal, darkness like light; and the highest light, having nowhere to dress, dresses in the lowest: nature, a story with no Name, a groom in a tannery. (stated: TO Beshalach, Esther.) | Inside: The sea turning to dry land; the joy that goes past knowing. | Lands: Seeing Hashgacha in an ordinary day; the Purim meal's joy. | Q: שההעלם נעשה גילוי ע"י גילוי עצמותו שהעלם וגילוי שוין ממש — Hiddenness became revelation through the revelation of His Essence, for which hidden and revealed are truly equal. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Torah Ohr (Liozna and Liadi, c. 1796-1812).txt:1662)
- **AR:AR-D8 The bare desire is holy at its root** (extends D10 and D11 (the world and the self given back): the self returns with its desire included, not removed.): A desire has clothes (the object, the thoughts and words about getting it) and a body: the bare power of wanting. The power itself 'is not sin or evil'; it can want good as it now wants bad, and its root is in high holiness. Burn the clothes and keep the ash: then the same power turns to love Him like one's own life. G-d can be found even 'from there'. (stated: LT Chukas.) | Inside: Ash: what is left when the smoke is gone is the wood's own earth. | Lands: Closing the eyes from a sight; turning the energy of craving into prayer. | Q: מ״מ אין מהות כח הזה חטא ורע מצד עצם עצמותו שהרי עצם מהותו הוא רק כח המתאוה והרי הוא יכול להיות כח המתאוה לטוב — The essence of this power is not sin or evil in itself; it is only the power to desire, and it can desire good. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Likkutei Torah (Liozna and Liadi, 1796-1812).txt:2117)
- **AR:AR-D9 Practice without breath is a womb** (extends D12 (the deed in the hand): a caution. The deed is the deepest place, but the deed without breath is still a womb, not the bottom.): A false bottom: the mitzvos done, the heart cold. The text calls it exile: like a fetus with every limb, fed through the navel, with no breath; 'a learned human command, physical doing alone and coldly'. A love that rises only in prayer and then passes is 'the lip of truth', the hem: the beginning of truth, and it leaves a mark. Neither is rejected; both are named as stages, and the way on is compassion, then joy, like a newborn who cries first and then gets milk. (stated: TO Vaera; LT Behar 1.) | Inside: A fetus that eats what its mother eats; a newborn's first cry. | Lands: Keep the rote mitzvah; add the cry; after prayer, notice the trace that remains. | Q: בבחי' עשיה גשמית בלבד ובקרירות כו'. וזהו עיקר הגלות ברוחניות — Physical doing alone and coldly; this is the main spiritual exile. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Torah Ohr (Liozna and Liadi, c. 1796-1812).txt:1420)
- **AR:AR-D10 The test holds His power** (extends D10 (the world given back as His) and M11: extends 'the world is His' to the obstacle itself.): A test is a hiding of His face, and 'in the hiding itself there is G-dliness'. Shimi's curse came with G-d's breath in his mouth. When you stand firm you break the shell and find the fruit: a power of G-d from very high, because, as with a falling wall, the highest stone falls farthest. The beginner's ego, too, is a husk with a purpose: like chaff, it shields the kernel from heat and rot until it falls away. (stated: LT Re'eh 3; TO Esther supplements.) | Inside: Breaking a shell; a stone falling from the top of a wall. | Lands: Not answering an insult; not giving in today. | Q: הנה בחינת הנסיונות הם הסתר פנים ובהסתר עצמו יש אלהות — Tests are a hiding of the face, and in the hiding itself there is G-dliness. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Likkutei Torah (Liozna and Liadi, 1796-1812).txt:3154)
- **AR:AR-D11 Even His giving pulses** (extends D14 (no bottom: begin again): gives D14 a body. The rhythm is His before it is ours.): Running and returning is not only ours. The heart beats, the breath goes out and in; the giving from the Infinite itself 'reaches and does not reach', spreading and withdrawing, or the vessels would dissolve. In His giving the spreading comes first; in ours the running comes first. 'If your heart runs, return to the One': 'against your will you live', to bring His life down. And the ash stays on the water: the mark of the running remains in the return. (stated: TO Bereshis 2; Tanya 50; LT Chukas.) | Inside: A pulse; breath; a flame that rises and falls. | Lands: After intense prayer, going straight to learning and to a mitzvah; not staying in the high. | Q: כי מיד שנמשך ומתפשט צ"ל הסתלקות שאל"כ יתבטלו הכלים מהיות בחי' כלים — For as soon as it spreads there must be withdrawal, or the vessels would cease to be vessels. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Torah Ohr (Liozna and Liadi, c. 1796-1812).txt:37)
- **AR:AR-D12 He bound Himself to you** (extends D8 (what you cannot let go): adds the other side. The bond also holds from His side, by covenant beyond reason.): From His side there is no likeness at all; a king of trees and stones is no king. Yet He 'compelled Himself, as it were, to bind our souls to Him', like friends who make a covenant because they fear the reason for love may end, so that love lasts even when reason says it should stop. From our side the answer is a voice without words, the shofar, and the bitterness of distance, which on a long scale gives the weight. (stated: LT Nitzavim.) | Inside: The shofar's plain blast; two friends passing between the halves. | Lands: Rosh Hashanah; any moment of feeling unworthy: answer it with the covenant. | Q: שאף שע״פ הטעם והדעת היה צריך לפסוק האהבה או לגרום איזה שנאה אעפ״כ מחמת הכריתות ברית מוכרח להיות אהבתם לעד קיימת — Even when by reason the love should stop or turn to hatred, because of the covenant their love must last forever. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Likkutei Torah (Liozna and Liadi, 1796-1812).txt:3516)
- **AR:AR-D13 The union you do not feel** (extends D7 and D12: removes the demand that the depth be felt.): Union does not depend on feeling it. A plain person doing a mitzvah has G-d resting on him, 'only his soul does not feel it'. 'I was like an animal with You, and I am always with You.' One who learns a law for its own sake has his essence included in the Infinite 'even though he does not feel this inclusion at all', even if a little pride creeps in; 'though he does not see, his root sees'. (stated: Tanya 46; LT Vayikra 5.) | Inside: Coarse matter as a screen over eyes that are still open behind it. | Lands: Standing up for anyone doing a mitzvah, even an unlearned person (Tanya 46). | Q: רק שאין נפשו מרגשת, מפני מסך החומר הגופני — Only his soul does not feel it, because of the screen of bodily matter. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Tanya (Liozna, 1786-1796).txt:666)
- **AR:AR-D14 No checking in the surrounding light** (extends D2 and D13: a relief for the self that keeps auditing its own depth.): In the inner light G-d 'tests hearts', checking if the longing is real. In the surrounding light, reached by bowing, 'He sees no wrong': only 'G-d his G-d is with him', drawn on its own, always. The one who has nothing, not even his own Torah and mitzvos, is the one He looks at; 'Blessed are You' means 'be blessed from Yourself'. (stated: TO Bereshis 1.) | Inside: Bowing to a king you cannot come near, so that he comes to you. | Lands: The bows of the Amidah; praying when you feel insincere. | Q: משא"כ בבחינת מקיף לא הביט און וגו' רק ה' אלהיו עמו שנמשך מאליו תמיד — In the surrounding light 'He sees no wrong'; only 'G-d his G-d is with him', drawn on its own, always. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shneur Zalman of Liadi (Alter Rebbe) (1745-1812)/Torah Ohr (Liozna and Liadi, c. 1796-1812).txt:14)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- AR:AR-C01 [He made the worlds, so something must have changed. It did not.] (M1) The world was made, yet nothing changed in Him. He is exactly who He was before there was a world. → (M9) Look at yourself speaking. One word you say is nothing next to your power to speak, less next to your thinking, and nothing at all next to you. → (M8) Go further in: before any words, there is just a wanting in the heart, with no letters in it at all. → (M10) Here the comparison breaks. Your word leaves you and lives on its own. His word never leaves Him, because there is nothing outside Him for it to go to. → (M11) So why does the world feel separate? Because the hiding is there for us, so we can receive. To us it looks like something apart from Him. → (M4) And the hiding itself is not a wall between you and Him. It is like a snail's shell, grown from the snail. Darkness and light are the same to Him. → (M2) That is why, before Him, everything is truly as nothing. ⇒ BOTTOM: Your own wanting-before-words, your word that is still inside you: that is how the world sits inside Him, never having left. ⇒ RETURN: Chapter 23 brings it down: a limb moves the instant you will it, with no order given; a mitzvah done by the body is that kind of limb for His will, and a thought of Torah is His will itself in your mind (lines 305, 309). - AR:AR-C02 [What is the root of being far from G-d?] (M2) The root of all idol worship is not denying G-d. It is feeling yourself as a thing standing on your own. → (M2) Holiness only rests on what yields to Him. So the feeling 'I am my own' is the one thing that blocks Him. → (M11) But even a plain, weak person would give up life rather than bow to an idol. Something in them refuses to be cut off. A 'spirit of folly' only covers it. → (M4) Even while a person sins, the soul is still faithful to Him. It is in exile, not gone. → (M5) This refusal to be cut off has no reason in it. It is not something you figured out. It is the nature of a G-dly soul. → (M5) It is above time. That is why no one will bow even for a moment and repent later: the light in the soul does not live in moments. → (M15) And it is in your hand, at every moment, to push away the folly and the forgetting and remember that love. ⇒ BOTTOM: A refusal to be cut off from Him, with no reason in it, above time, in every Jew. ⇒ RETURN: If you would accept death rather than be cut off for a moment, then the small daily things (resisting a desire, getting up to pray, giving charity) are much easier, and in them the inner will of G-d is fully present with nothing hiding it (lines 340-341). Remember this in the Shema every day (line 348). - AR:AR-C03 [Even a complete tzaddik who loves G-d: is he one with Him?] (M1) The Zohar says the Divine light on a person's head needs oil, which is good deeds. Why isn't the soul enough? The soul is a piece of G-d. → (M4) Because even a perfect tzaddik who serves with love and delight is still someone: one who fears G-d and loves Him. → (M2) G-d's presence rests only where something has completely yielded. Anything that is still 'someone' cannot hold it. → (M10) So the lover, who is 'something and not nothing', cannot grasp Him by his thought. → (M6) A mitzvah is different. It is His own will, with no hiding at all. So the life in the deed is not a separate thing. It is one with Him. → (M15) And when the body does the mitzvah, the power of the body in that act is joined to Him completely. → (M11) Even the animal soul, which moves the hand, turns from bad to good in that moment. ⇒ BOTTOM: The deepest self-forgetting is not a feeling of love, which always has a lover in it. It is a deed of His will, where no one is standing apart. ⇒ RETURN: Do the mitzvah with the body: give with the hand, speak with the mouth, walk with the feet. The deed carries the oneness your love cannot (lines 479-482). - AR:AR-C04 [Knowing G-d is everywhere. Isn't knowing it enough?] (M16) Knowing about G-d is not yet knowing G-d. Real knowing is fixing your mind on Him firmly, the way your mind holds a thing your eyes are looking at. → (M3) It may be hard. Dig as for buried treasure; the awe is hidden in every heart. → (M9) How does He see you? Like you feel every part of your body, even heat in a toenail, from inside. → (M10) But the comparison breaks: your soul is hurt by your body. He is not changed by anything in the world. → (M7) You do not fear a king's body; asleep he frightens no one. You fear his life inside. So when your eyes see heaven and earth, see His life inside them. → (M16) The main thing is habit: training the mind until it is fixed that everything you see is the King's outer garment. 'Faith' means training, like a craftsman training his hands. → (M9) Wisdom is seeing how each thing is born from nothing. And do not leave yourself out: your own body and soul are also nullified in His word. ⇒ BOTTOM: You yourself, held in His word like a ray in the sun. ⇒ RETURN: If you think about this for a long while each day, the awe stays fixed for the whole day; then even a light reminder at any moment keeps you from what is wrong (line 617). - AR:AR-C05 [How do you wake love in a heart of stone?] (M9) Love wakes love. As a face is reflected in water, a person who sees another truly loves him will love back. → (M3) Now picture a great king who comes down with all his ministers to a lowly man lying on a dunghill, lifts him, and brings him into his innermost room. Even a heart of stone would melt. → (M6) He did more. Every world and angel is nothing before Him, and He Himself came down into Egypt to lift us out. → (M12) And if you feel nothing? Asaf said: I am like an animal with You, I do not know; and I am always with You. Coarse matter does not stop the union. → (M14) He set aside His endless light and hid it in contractions, all out of love for the lowly person. So a person should leave everything for Him. → (M15) And it must come out of your lips, not stay a thought, so it reaches the life in your blood and the whole world. → (M15) When the heart runs toward Him, wanting to leave the body, tell it: against your will you live, to bring His life down here. ⇒ BOTTOM: Seeing that He came down to the dunghill for you, and that you are always with Him even when you feel like an animal. ⇒ RETURN: Return to the body, to speech, to the day, because the run upward is for the sake of the home below (line 708). - AR:AR-C06 [What stands between me and cleaving to Him?] (M15) Every morning we say: You breathed this soul into me and You will take it. So from now I hand it back to You. → (M13) Learning and praying are this giving-over: like the soul after seventy years, thinking only His words. → (M9) Each day you leave Egypt: the G-dly soul leaving the prison of the body, to be included in His oneness. → (M6) What Avraham earned step by step, we were given as an inheritance. Giving the Torah is like giving us Himself. → (M2) So nothing holds you back from cleaving to Him except the wanting. The moment you want it and say 'G-d is One', your soul is included. → (M12) And if your love seems imagined, do not worry. It is true in itself, a hidden love; you bring it out to turn it into deeds. → (M16) Make it a habit, with mouth and heart together; habit becomes nature. ⇒ BOTTOM: The only thing between you and Him is your wanting, and the wanting is already given to you. ⇒ RETURN: Before prayer, before learning, before each hour of study, give the soul back with 'Elokai neshamah', and let the love go into a deed (lines 600-601, 644). - AR:AR-C07 [Who can make something from nothing?] (M4) From His side the created thing is nothing. That is true 'before Him', as He knows it from above. → (M12) But from below, the created thing really is separate. There is no ratio at all between it and the power making it. That is why it is called something from nothing. → (M14) Only He can do this, because His being comes from Himself, with no cause before Him. So only He can make being where there was nothing. → (M14) Why make the creature separate at all? So He can be King over separate beings who do His commandments. → (M15) The lowest element shows this power most: the earth keeps growing plants from nothing, year after year, something the heavens cannot do. → (M5) Because just beneath the lowest point the Infinite shines with almost nothing between. → (M15) So the deeds done in the physical world are the purpose of the soul's coming down. ⇒ BOTTOM: Being that has no cause, the only power that can make 'something'; and it shows itself most in the dust. ⇒ RETURN: Your feeling of being a separate someone is the very thing only the Essence could give, and it is given so you can freely do His will in the physical world (lines 1268, 1286). - AR:AR-C08 [Who are you under your mind and feelings?] (M8) Your soul in itself is a simple light. Your thinking and your feelings are not you. They are like jewelry the soul wears. → (M9) Proof: mind and feelings change. A child loves small things; an old man's mind changes. The essence of the soul does not change at all. → (M6) Above: before creation there was 'He and His Name'. His Name is His light, like sunlight; He Himself is the sun. → (M4) The great contraction hid only the light, folding it back into the sun. The sun itself was never hidden. That is why even small children know there is a G-d, without understanding how. → (M4) So the 'empty space' was never truly empty. He fills it all. → (M3) How does the higher reach the lower? Like a teacher's parable: the whole of his wisdom is inside it, though the student sees only a story. → (M12) And the new understanding the student gets was already there all along, hidden in the parable. ⇒ BOTTOM: The Essence was never hidden: even a child knows He is there. What is hidden is only the light, the 'how'. ⇒ RETURN: Your changing moods and thoughts are ornaments of something that does not change; and the world is a parable with the whole of the Teacher inside it. - AR:AR-C09 [My day is full of worry about making a living. Does that put out the love?] (M2) 'Many waters' are the worries of making a living. They cannot put out the love; that love is the soul's nature, like a flame that rises by itself. → (M4) On the contrary: through those waters the soul can reach higher than before it came down. → (M4) Busy people think they cannot pray like full-time scholars. It is the reverse: light is greater coming out of darkness. → (M6) In prayer, think: the grass grows by its star, the star by angels, all from His kingship, which is only a ray. Then the soul burns to leave the darkness and wants Him, not even paradise. → (M5) Even all the wisdom and reasons of Torah, the 'wealth of the house', are scorned next to wanting Him Himself. → (M15) How do you reach the One beyond reason? Through the physical mitzvos, His will above reason. A very deep thing can only be pointed at with a finger. → (M10) Like someone who loves a great sage's inner self: he can only hug him by his body. So you hold His Essence through the mitzvos, His limbs. ⇒ BOTTOM: Wanting Him Himself, beyond paradise and beyond understanding, born out of a day of worry. ⇒ RETURN: 'Today' specifically, while the soul is in a body (line 211): the busy person's prayer and the physical mitzvah are where the Essence is held. - AR:AR-C10 [What if I can't think deep thoughts in prayer?] (M3) Sea creatures live hidden inside the sea and die out of it. Land creatures look like they stand on their own. → (M7) The Levites served with song. A melody moves the soul by its movements alone, not by any idea in it. → (M9) That is why even an old tune stirs you. → (M7) This is a stirring of the soul from itself, not from grasping, and it reaches the point of the heart, not just around it. → (M15) So the verses before Shema are a kind of melody. If you say them and they do not touch the point of your heart, it is not yet song. → (M10) Words are like a horse: the rider steers it, but it carries him further than he could walk. Speaking the prayer lifts you beyond what your mind can reach. → (M7) What G-d wants is not the strength of the horse but those who yield, who are moved by Him Himself, waiting for His kindness and not for their own thirst. ⇒ BOTTOM: A stirring of the heart's point itself, with no idea in it, for One who is beyond every idea. ⇒ RETURN: Sing the verses; let the words carry you; one who cannot contemplate should go deep in the words alone (line 1648). - AR:AR-C11 [Is He 'only His glory' here, or truly here?] (M1) One verse says His glory fills the earth; another says 'I fill heaven and earth'. Which is it? → (M12) Both are true. In truth He Himself fills it; He comes into this low world through His glory, the Torah. → (M5) All worlds, layer on layer, are hidden and revealed relative to each other. But before His Essence there is no above or below. → (M12) At the Sea His Essence was revealed, and so hidden and revealed became equal: the sea turned into dry land. → (M10) How do we draw Him down? You cannot grab a person's soul, but if you take him by the hand and pull, the soul comes along. → (M15) Charity is His right arm; overcoming a desire is His left; Torah is taking the whole body. → (M5) And the very top cannot be awakened from below at all. 'G-d will fight for you, and you be still.' ⇒ BOTTOM: The Essence, for which hidden and revealed are the same, reached at the end only by stillness. ⇒ RETURN: Take Him by the arm: give to the poor, overcome one desire, learn (lines 1665-1666). - AR:AR-C12 [I keep mitzvos but feel cold. What is wrong?] (M3) A baby in the womb has every limb, but its head lies between its knees and it eats only through the navel. It has no breath until it is born. → (M7) That is exile inside a person: the world looks like a thing on its own, and mitzvos feed only the body of the service, not the heart and mind; done by rote, coldly. → (M1) Shema says 'One', not 'Only One'. 'One' means He is one inside the many directions of the world. His Essence is the same below as above. → (M12) Even in our exile He did not leave us; He gave us the power to draw His oneness down. → (M13) The order of the heart: first anger at the bad in you; then compassion on your own soul in its exile; then joy. Like a newborn, who first cries and then gets milk. → (M5) Higher still is Moshe: 'what are we?'. In Torah he felt no love and no delight, did not feel himself at all, and only said what the King says. → (M15) A person learning a law is like one repeating the King's word; he is not speaking his own words at all. ⇒ BOTTOM: A Torah where you are not there at all: only the King's word in your mouth. ⇒ RETURN: The cold, rote mitzvah is a womb, not the end: cry for your soul, then let the joy come; and learn a law as one repeating the King's words (lines 1428, 1432). - AR:AR-C13 [How does a heart full of other wants come to love Him like its own life?] (M2) In every Jew is a point of the heart, a flame that rises by itself. It is the soul's essence, a real part of G-d, and it is the same in everyone. → (M9) When it is revealed, you love G-d the way a person loves his own life. That love comes from the essence of the soul. → (M1) But how can a heart that lived for bodily pleasures turn over completely? → (M11) Through the 'back': forcing the heart toward Him even when there is no delight. Love that rules from the mind, not yet love that fills the heart. → (M12) Even ordinary people who only subdue can truly say: in our heart there is only One. → (M11) Amalek is a hard neck: knowing the truth and doing the opposite anyway, for no reason. It cuts the link between the head and the heart. → (M5) This war is G-d's, not yours to win. Your part is the effort of contemplating as much as you can, and asking for mercy. ⇒ BOTTOM: A point in the heart that loves Him like life, equal in all; the forcing from the 'back' is the road to it. ⇒ RETURN: Contemplate as far as you can, force the heart, ask for mercy in every blessing of the Amidah (line 2235). - AR:AR-C14 [Why does the Torah give a reason for love but none for giving up your life?] (M3) The Torah is a parable of the Ancient One, G-d as He is beyond all worlds. You can only reach a teaching through its parable. → (M1) The Torah gives a reason to love G-d: 'He is your life'. But it gives no reason for giving up your life. Why? → (M4) Because 'He is your life' is about Him as the One who gives life to the worlds. And that is not the main thing about G-d. → (M5) Even had He never created anything, it would all be the same to Him. → (M2) Self-sacrifice comes from not wanting to be cut off from Him as He is, above being 'the Life of life'. That is why it has no reason; and it is in every Jew. → (M12) So the joy of Purim is beyond knowing: when His Essence shines, cursed Haman and blessed Mordechai are equal, darkness like light. → (M15) Why was the Purim miracle dressed in nature, with no Name of G-d in the Megillah? Because a light so high has nowhere to dress except the lowest place. Like a groom who sleeps in a tannery for his bride. ⇒ BOTTOM: A bond with Him as He is, prior to being your Life; it has no reason because nothing is beneath it. ⇒ RETURN: That is why the hidden, natural, ordinary day can carry the highest light: it has nowhere else to dress (lines 2638, 2647). - AR:AR-C15 [My wish to be close to G-d has a lot of 'me' in it. Is that bad?] (M3) A wheat kernel grows inside chaff. The chaff takes the sun's heat and the rain's damp so the kernel does not burn or rot. In fact the growing comes through the chaff. → (M11) Anyone who begins to serve G-d in prayer needs a little ego: 'I must be a servant of G-d; I don't want to be far'. It is real wanting, and it still has 'me' in it. → (M4) In truth, if he were really joined to G-d he would need no reasons at all. There would be no one owing and nothing to pay, only one simple One. → (M12) Even the most heartfelt moment in prayer is 'physical doing' next to the Infinite. And still: set your heart; to the Infinite, spiritual and physical are equal. → (M14) At the start it must be this way, with the ego, or he would never begin. And only the 'I don't want to be far' gives the strength to fight desires. → (M5) When the fruit is grown, the chaff falls away. All the reasons and understandings he had become chaff next to cleaving to His Essence. → (M10) True self-giving has no reward in it. Giving your life to gain something is not self-giving, only a bigger wish. ⇒ BOTTOM: One simple heart, like Avraham's 'faithful heart': no claims, no reasons, because there was never anything separate. ⇒ RETURN: Keep the husk while you grow; do not throw away the wish to be close because it has 'me' in it (lines 2954, 2964). - AR:AR-C16 [Why did He make a world that hides Him?] (M2) If His light shone as it is, nothing could exist as a separate thing; everything would be nullified. → (M14) He wants a 'something' that then yields. That is His pleasure. → (M3) A mirror works because silver covers the back of the glass. The blocking makes the light come back, so you see what is behind you. A thick cover would show nothing. → (M5) So the hiding makes a returning light that reaches higher than the source of the light that came down. → (M11) 'It is not good for man to be alone' means: if everything were revealed and nullified, no return. The 'helper against him' is the body and animal soul that hide, and from them comes the return. → (M15) Serving G-d is like tanning hides: working the skin that covers until it becomes useful. ⇒ BOTTOM: The covering, when it is thin enough, is what lets the light come back to Him higher than it came. ⇒ RETURN: Your body and your animal soul are the silver on the mirror: work them, do not try to get rid of them (lines 109, 112). - AR:AR-C17 [What does a low deed do for a high soul?] (M9) The feet carry the head where it wants to go. Doctors let blood from the foot to heal the head. In that way the foot is the head. → (M15) So by fixing his deeds, the lowest part, a person's mind and heart become a thousand times purer. → (M1) Some people's heart wakes in prayer and then the love passes and they go their way. That is because the feelings are separate from the mind. → (M7) The Infinite cannot fit inside the heart, only around it. That surrounding comes through bowing in prayer, through yielding. → (M10) He looks at the poor and broken in spirit, who has nothing of his own, not even his Torah and mitzvos. Like bowing to a king you cannot come near, so he comes to you. → (M2) 'Blessed are You' means: be blessed from Yourself. → (M12) In the inner light He tests the heart to see if the longing is real. In the surrounding light He does not look at faults at all. He is simply with you, always. ⇒ BOTTOM: A Presence that surrounds you without checking whether your longing is sincere enough. ⇒ RETURN: Bow, say 'Baruch atah', fix one deed: the feet heal the head (lines 8, 13). - AR:AR-C18 [By midday I have forgotten my morning prayer. What now?] (M1) You accept G-d's kingship in the Shema, but where can that Oneness live? In the Torah you learn right after. → (M9) Then the day comes: work, worry, the body. You forget all your contemplation. → (M4) So He gave mitzvos for the whole day. They do not need you to perfect them; their power comes from themselves, not from your service. → (M7) Your part: think how far you are from G-d because of the whole day, let your heart break, and still want to come close, and do the mitzvah in front of you. → (M10) That is 'living water': water pressed through the earth comes out clearer. Your small service, squeezed through a day of matter, is sweet. → (M16) You rely on the decision you already made in your heart to serve Him truly. And the little bit is very sweet to you, because you know how far you are. → (M15) The Arizal rejoiced greatly in doing mitzvos, not in his own service. Real humility is seeing that your friend is closer to G-d. ⇒ BOTTOM: A hungry soul to whom every bitter thing is sweet: the smallest deed tastes of Him. ⇒ RETURN: When you have forgotten, do the next mitzvah anyway, knowing you are far: that is the living water (lines 461-463). - AR:AR-C19 [Is there a love deeper than the love I understand?] (M2) One kind of will is born from understanding. The deeper you grasp, the more you want. The heart can hold it, because the mind rules the heart. → (M10) So it is small or great as your mind is small or great. → (M5) But there is a higher will at the root of the soul, shining below as the 'yechidah'. It comes from seeing that nothing compares to Him, and it makes the soul want to leave its sheath. → (M7) The outer heart cannot hold this love. It is too high. So the soul's powers go still, as if they were not; this is 'with all your might'. It shines equally in every Jew. → (M11) What stops it? Only that it does not dress in the body; it stands around it. → (M9) That is why even the wicked are full of regrets: it comes from the yechidah. → (M13) Three wills: do His will as if it were yours; then His will in you; then cancel your will. The last is 'with all your might'. ⇒ BOTTOM: A will at the root of the soul that the heart cannot feel and the body cannot hold, equal in all, surfacing even as a sinner's regret. ⇒ RETURN: Torah connects it to the powers that live in the body; hold it and do not let go until you bring it home (lines 1146-1148). - AR:AR-C20 [How can I 'know' G-d if no thought can grasp Him?] (M1) The verse commands us to know Him. But no thought grasps Him. How can that be? → (M2) Knowing here means sensing that something is, not grasping what it is. → (M9) You know this from yourself. You cannot grasp what your own soul is, yet you feel it is there, fully. You need no faith or proof for that. → (M9) And notice: you feel your own matters strongly because you never take your attention off yourself. About a friend, even a dear one, you care less, because your mind leaves him. → (M3) Even natural feelings depend on attention. A soldier who stops thinking about his fear stops being afraid. → (M16) So 'know the G-d of your father' means: do not take your mind off Him, as you never take it off yourself. Then love and awe follow on their own. → (M10) Faith alone stays above, surrounding. A thief prays to G-d at the mouth of the tunnel, and still breaks in. ⇒ BOTTOM: Sensing that He is, the way you sense that you are: no grasp of what, no doubt of that. ⇒ RETURN: Learning Torah grasps the essence of His will dressed in physical things; it feeds faith and makes it knowing, as grain gives a child the mind to say 'Abba' (line 2939). - AR:AR-C21 [If I yield myself completely, have I arrived?] (M13) The purpose of every mitzvah is to turn the 'something' into 'nothing'. Tefillin: 'One' written on physical parchment. → (M4) But this yielding is not yet the true one: he is still someone, nullifying himself. → (M5) Even Rabbi Akiva, whose soul left him on the word 'One', was a creature giving himself, still far from G-dliness, until a light from the true One took it in. → (M10) Yielding makes you mute, like the 'sheaves' that cannot speak; like a fetus, which only eats what its mother eats. You stop being a giver to others for a while. → (M4) The true 'nothing' is given. Shabbos is a gift; our part is to guard it from spoiling, from lies and pride. → (M11) Guard especially that the yielding itself does not become a 'something': 'I nullified myself by my own power'. It is all from G-d. → (M15) And yet every Jew yields a little, as he can, by subduing or by transforming. That is his share. One who does not bow at Modim has none. ⇒ BOTTOM: The true nothing is not an achievement but a gift; the creature's best yielding is only the vessel for it. ⇒ RETURN: Bow at Modim; do the small yielding of today; guard it from becoming pride (lines 997, 1007). - AR:AR-C22 [Why should I weep in prayer if I have not sinned?] (M5) The whole World to Come is one ray, one 'yud', next to Him. And the soul came down from far above that. → (M9) So even without sin, being in a body is a great distance from Him. Think: where did you come from, where are you going. Have mercy on your soul. → (M9) Compassion opens a closed heart. We see it with a cruel man: stir his pity, and his heart opens. → (M10) And if you feel nothing? A toenail that hurts is felt in the brain, unless it is cut off. Not feeling the pain is itself the sign of being cut off, and a reason for even greater mercy. → (M4) Each of us thinks our own share of bodily things is just right. Before G-d it is a huge descent. Since we cannot feel it, we say: 'in Your great mercy'. → (M5) 'Your' mercy, because only You know how great the pity is; our souls, being limited, cannot measure it. → (M15) That is the weeping of Rosh Hashanah: it is for the soul, that came down from its place and became physical. → (M12) And one thought of return reconnects even what was cut off. A captive prince is still a prince; with one move he turns back. ⇒ BOTTOM: Compassion for your own soul, for the distance itself, measured by the One who alone knows its size. ⇒ RETURN: After the crying comes the joy and the bread: cry in the inner room, then 'put out the bread', learn, do (Behar 1, line 974). - AR:AR-C23 [My love in prayer is real, but it fades after. Is it true love?] (M9) True love is like brother and sister: fixed in nature, needing no arousal, never stopping. → (M4) Love you wake in prayer and that then passes is the 'lip of truth', the hem of the garment: the beginning of truth, not yet its point. → (M12) Still, something stays after prayer: when you remember G-d, a willing spirit stirs by itself. → (M11) What blocks it from the heart's center is desire, rooted in pride. You must break the wall, like making a hole in it. → (M5) 'Open for Me a hole like a needle's eye and I will open for you like the great hall.' One who comes to purify himself is helped. → (M10) Torah must be 'baked' by the fire of prayer. Unbaked bread lies in the stomach undigested; Torah without that fire stays one thing and your soul another. → (M15) Weep in the inner room, then hold back and say: 'put out the bread'. Both, together. ⇒ BOTTOM: A love you no longer need to wake, as natural as a sibling's; reached by breaking the wall a needle's width. ⇒ RETURN: Pray first, then learn right after, so the Torah is baked into the soul (lines 969, 976). - AR:AR-C24 [What do I do with my strong desires?] (M3) When wood burns, fire, air and water go up in smoke. What remains is ash, the earth that was its essence. → (M7) A desire has a form: the thing desired, the thoughts and words about getting it. Those are its clothes. → (M4) But the power to desire, in itself, is not sin and not evil. It could want good just as it now wants bad. → (M11) Its root is high, in holiness. So in return it can turn completely, and love G-d like one loves his own life. → (M15) So burn the clothes: do not think it, speak it or do it; close your eyes. Then only the bare power remains, the ash, and it can turn to love of G-d. → (M12) You can find G-d even 'from there', the deepest place, because there is no place empty of Him, by crying out from the depth of the heart. → (M15) Then living water is poured on the ash: Torah and mitzvos. The ash stays on the water: the mark of the running stays inside the return. ⇒ BOTTOM: The bare power of wanting, stripped of its objects, which was always holy at its root. ⇒ RETURN: Living water into a vessel: learn and do, with the fire of the running still showing (line 2121). - AR:AR-C25 [Why would He stay bound to me when I am so far?] (M13) All Jews are one body. Even the 'head' is incomplete without the 'foot'. So be humble before everyone. → (M1) Why do friends make a covenant? Because they fear the reason for their love may end. → (M5) A covenant binds love above reason: even when reason says the love should end, it stays. It covers all faults. → (M10) He is not a king to us by nature; a king of trees and stones is no king. He bound Himself to us by covenant, as a faithful friend, though by reason it is not worth it to Him. → (M7) So the shofar: a cry from the heart's inner point, so deep no words can hold it, only a voice. → (M11) And the distance itself gives weight. On a long scale, the far end decides. Bitterness at how far you are draws the love beyond reason. ⇒ BOTTOM: A covenant He chose against reason; met from our side by a voice with no words. ⇒ RETURN: Rosh Hashanah: kingship, remembrance, shofar; the year begins from the bond, not from merit (line 3525). - AR:AR-C26 [Does learning with my mind touch the part of me above my mind?] (M9) Your intellect is not your soul. It changes: sometimes clear, sometimes foolish. A baby has the whole soul and a tiny mind. → (M9) As the Maharal said: can you grasp your own soul? How much less its Creator. → (M2) Self-sacrifice comes from that essence above the mind, which is nullified in Him like a candle in a torch. That is why the Torah gives no reason for it. → (M6) When you understand a law of Torah, your mind unites with His wisdom; and since your soul is in your mind and He is in His wisdom, your essence is included in Him. → (M12) Even if you do not feel it, and even if a little pride creeps in when you understand well: since you mean it for His sake, your essence is included. You do not see it; your root sees it. → (M5) Reading the written Torah counts even if you do not understand the words; its letters carry what is above wisdom. → (M15) The start of service is 'poor bread': faith and subduing, with no taste of understanding. Be like a simple dove that does what it is told. ⇒ BOTTOM: An essence above your mind, joined to Him while you learn, whether or not you feel it. ⇒ RETURN: Pray first ('self-giving at One'), then learn a law with your mind; and read the verses even where you do not understand (lines 248, 255). - AR:AR-C27 [Is my yielding my work or His gift?] (M3) Chametz rises and has taste; matzah does not rise and has no taste. In service, matzah is 'cancel your will'. → (M11) The first matzah, made by us, needs bitter herbs: bitterness at how far you are from Him. Mercy belongs only to the one who is low. → (M5) That matzah needs guarding; it might still rise. The second matzah, after midnight, came from His revealing Himself. It cannot rise at all. → (M4) They were too low to leave Egypt on their own. Only His revealing took them out. → (M4) Even our arousal from below needs a light from above first: thoughts of return that come to us. That is why the blessing comes before the mitzvah. → (M7) What comes from that place is not grasped. It flashes in the mind like lightning, beyond what the mind can hold; and one is stunned for a while. ⇒ BOTTOM: A yielding that cannot rise because it was given, not made. ⇒ RETURN: Say the blessing before the mitzvah, eat the poor bread, and guard the matzah you made (lines 2852, 4534). - AR:AR-C28 [Is the thing testing me also from Him?] (M2) He did not 'create' the world the way a worker makes a thing. 'He commanded and they were created', as if by itself. → (M5) Physical things cannot come from spiritual ones by any chain of steps; only His power can do it. So even a stone holds His letters. → (M11) A test is a hiding of His face; and in the hiding itself there is G-dliness. → (M6) David said of Shimi: 'G-d told him to curse'. The thought fell into Shimi's mind from G-d; without His breath in him he could not speak a word. → (M15) When you stand the test you break the shell and find the fruit inside: the hidden power of G-d. → (M10) Like a falling wall: the highest stone falls farthest. A power that reaches so low must come from very high, so a test can bring a person to real knowing. ⇒ BOTTOM: Inside the hiding, a power of G-d from very high, waiting to be found by not giving in. ⇒ RETURN: When the test comes, stand; when the insult comes, hear 'G-d told him'; the fruit is inside (line 3154). - AR:AR-C29 [Is the hiding a black stain on His light?] (M3) Engraved letters are one with the stone, yet even they darken the bright gem a little. → (M10) Ink on parchment only darkens the parchment so the parchment can show a letter. A letter not surrounded by blank parchment on all sides is invalid. → (M6) In the meaning: the parchment itself, the Infinite, reveals the letter. Through all the contractions He Himself is shown below. → (M9) Like your thoughts: the letters you think in are not separate. It is your mind itself thinking in them. → (M12) So 'I am black and beautiful': black outside like the tents of Kedar, inside like Shlomo's curtains. → (M5) The hidden love is 'black' because its source is so bright it cannot be seen, like a son from his father's mind who by nature will not be separated. ⇒ BOTTOM: The blackness is too much light; the letter is the parchment showing itself. ⇒ RETURN: From the 'black' of exile comes song; the hidden love can reach even one pushed to the edge of the heavens (line 4391). - AR:AR-C30 [I say Shema twice a day. Why don't I love?] (M1) Everyone says Shema twice a day and still does not reach 'and you shall love'. Why? → (M1) 'Choose life'. But who does not choose life? Someone who loves life loves pleasures. → (M2) There are upper waters, the delight in G-d, and lower waters, the leftover pleasures of this world. Choose the upper life. → (M14) Before the Temple was destroyed, contemplation alone brought love. Now it comes only through accepting His kingship: 'Baruch shem'. → (M15) Service means doing the opposite of what the heart craves. What is not giving up your will is not called service at all. Learning the hundred-and-first time; the extra coin. → (M10) Nothing changes from one 'something' to another except by losing its form. A seed sprouts only after it rots. → (M15) Then comes love 'with all your might', too big for any vessel; its only vessel is the speech of halacha. ⇒ BOTTOM: A love with no vessel, reached through the seed's rotting: one small daily giving-up. ⇒ RETURN: Do one thing today against your habit (the hundred-and-first review, the extra coin), and speak words of halacha (line 3010).

## MH — the Maharash and the Rayatz

The Rebbe Maharash and the Rebbe Rayatz take 'there is nothing but Him' and ask what kind of 'nothing' it is, where it is true, and who can reach it. Their answers move the depth downward and inward: the strongest 'nothing else' is true of the lowest things (MH-D2); 'nothing' also names the soul we live from and cannot see (MH-D1); the soul is a part of G-d, not a ray, and its distance makes its pull stronger (MH-D3); under the highest name of the soul is a spark that is G-dliness itself (MH-D4); the one who feels nothing has a road of his own, the cry (MH-D5); only below, in physical deeds, does the soul 'walk' in the Infinite (MH-D8, MH-D9); and the plain child's 'Abba' carries all knowing (MH-D10). They also guard the way: feeling love in prayer can still be 'completely outside' (MH-D12).

## Depths

- **MH:MH-D1 Two nothings: the ray that changes nothing, and the soul you cannot see** (extends D3, D4 (sharpens: two meanings of 'nothing', one about Him, one about our knowing); D5): 'Nothing' (ayin) means two different things, and Chassidus needs both. Looking from Him down, the world comes from a ray so small it takes up no room at all, so nothing in Him changed when the world began. Looking from us up, 'nothing' means what we cannot grasp, like our own soul: closer to us than anything, sure beyond doubt, and never seen. The descent's 'even with Him, nothing' (D3) is the first; the second is new and gentler: the 'nothing' you live from is the most real thing in you, only unseen. | Inside: The Maharash's images: the line in the middle of an aleph joining two yuds; a deep spring dripping between the great abyss and the rivers; a seed that rots before it sprouts (TS 3454, 3462-3463). | Lands: In the body: at the sea, 'the hidden nothing of the soul shone in their bodies', and each pointed with a finger, 'this is my G-d' (TS 3467). In the present moment: the life in this breath. | Q: ורק מפני שאין אנו יודעים מה הוא לכן אנו קוראי' אותו אין — Only because we do not know what it is, we call it 'nothing'. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shmuel Schneersohn (Rebbe Maharash) (1834-1882)/Toras Shmuel (Lubavitch, 1867-1881).txt:3458)
- **MH:MH-D2 'Nothing else' is true only down here** (extends D3 and D10 (corrects the idea that the deepest unity is found higher; the stronger 'nothing else' belongs below)): There are two sentences: 'all counts as nothing before Him' and 'there is nothing else besides Him'. The first is like a candle at noon: still there, just taking no room. It only works for things of the same kind, like a small man next to a great one. The second, stronger sentence is true only of the lowest created things, because they have no being of their own at all: their whole being is the Essence. So the deepest unity is not found by leaving the world but in the plainest physical thing. | Inside: The ofanim's great noise; a riddle suddenly solved; a person who cries 'gevald, it is not as you see' (SHM 246). | Lands: On the lowest, plainest object in front of you, and on the empty space between things: 'nothing else, even in the empty space of the world' (SHM 245). | Q: והמשל בזה כמו שרגא בטיהרא — The parable is a lamp at noon. (Chassidus-txt/R. Yosef Yitzchak Schneersohn (Rebbe Rayatz) (1880-1950)/Sefer HaMaamarim (Riga and Otwock, 1929-1933).txt:244)
- **MH:MH-D3 A part, not a ray: the farther, the stronger the pull** (extends D8 (the bond: adds that separation strengthens it), D9): A ray rises toward its source only while it is attached; cut off, it has no pull and even falls. The soul is not a ray but a part of the Essence. A part that is separated longs to return more, not less. So distance is not the opposite of the bond; it is where the bond is felt most. And the Essence in the soul is hidden, which is exactly why it can live in a body. | Inside: The spark running fast to the torch; malchus 'never silent'; the father's love 'as you are and where you are'. | Lands: In the ache of distance itself, now; then in one simple good deed, which is 'oil' for the lamp (SHM 1476). | Q: אבל הנשמה היא חלק מן העצם — But the soul is a part of the essence. (Chassidus-txt/R. Yosef Yitzchak Schneersohn (Rebbe Rayatz) (1880-1950)/Sefer HaMaamarim (Riga and Otwock, 1929-1933).txt:1457)
- **MH:MH-D4 Under yechidah: a spark that is G-dliness itself** (extends D6 (names the point under the names), D8): DESCENT reaches 'a point under all your names' (D6). The Rebbe Rayatz names it: even yechidah, the highest name of the soul, is only a name. Under it is a tiny spark that is actual G-dliness, from 'the lowest level in the Creator', clothed in a created spark called yechidah. That is why thoughts of teshuvah come suddenly, and why teshuvah reaches where Torah does not. | Inside: Daniel's companions who saw nothing yet trembled, because 'their mazal saw' (SHM 415). | Lands: In teshuvah right now: a sudden turning, then breaking one's self-satisfaction ('zich tzu brechen', SHM 429). | Q: דגם בחי' יחידה היא רק שם למדרי' שבנשמה ואינה עצם הנשמה — Even yechidah is only a name for a level in the soul, not the essence of the soul. (Chassidus-txt/R. Yosef Yitzchak Schneersohn (Rebbe Rayatz) (1880-1950)/Sefer HaMaamarim (Riga and Otwock, 1929-1933).txt:415)
- **MH:MH-D5 The cry of one who feels nothing** (extends D7 and D2 (new: the route for the one who feels nothing)): There are two roads to the soul's essence. Lofty souls climb through understanding, step by step. In our time most cannot. For them the road is the cry from the depth of the heart, out of bitterness at how far they are, even when they have 'no feeling at all for the Infinite'. The text says this leap is the truest drawing of the Essence. The heart route in DESCENT is not second best; here it is first. | Inside: The shepherd boy's rooster-cry on Yom Kippur that broke the decree (KTH 28-36); the son crying 'Abba' moving the father's whole being (SHM 1334). | Lands: In the throat and chest, in the cry; then in one deed done the Torah's way. | Q: והגם שמצד עצם מהותו אינו בערך זה כלל, ואין לו כלל הרגש בענין דא"ס — Though by nature not fit for it, and with no feeling at all for the Infinite. (Chassidus-txt/R. Yosef Yitzchak Schneersohn (Rebbe Rayatz) (1880-1950)/Sefer HaMaamarim (Riga and Otwock, 1929-1933).txt:1417)
- **MH:MH-D6 The laying-down: knowing that settles** (extends D5 (where knowing ends: adds the settled knowing between understanding and will)): There is understanding, which explains and can be argued away, and there is a deeper knowing that 'lays itself down' in you: 'it is so'. No clever objection moves it. When the clear point fades into details you feel you no longer know; the advice is to carry yourself back to that settled place. Even this, though, is still knowing, and the Essence is held only by the heart's will. | Inside: 'Es leigt zich op': something lays itself down; a sage who knows there is depth before knowing what it is (TS 12382). | Lands: In quiet: the body stops arguing; then the will turns to Him. | Q: עס לייגט זעך בא אים אפ אז עס איז אזיי — It lays itself down in him that it is so. (Chassidus-txt/R. Yosef Yitzchak Schneersohn (Rebbe Rayatz) (1880-1950)/Sefer HaMaamarim (Riga and Otwock, 1929-1933).txt:1323)
- **MH:MH-D7 Only when you look: nature sunk like a thing under water** (extends D10 (the world given back: adds how it is seen, by looking)): Below, what we see first is the world's solidity, and G-dliness is the surprise. There is a difference between what shows itself and what you see when you look. 'Nature' (teva) means sunk: the light is covered like a thing under water, yet fully there. Its unchanging course every day is itself the sign of the Infinite, since everything limited changes. Believing is not enough; the thief believes. You must look until you get life from it. | Inside: Things under water; 'that is how the world runs' said coldly and dryly; a thief praying at the tunnel. | Lands: In the eyes, on this ordinary object, now. | Q: איז אז מ'קוקט זיך צו זעט מען טאקע דעם אמת שהעיקר הוא האלקות — When one looks, one truly sees the truth: that the main thing is G-dliness. (Chassidus-txt/R. Yosef Yitzchak Schneersohn (Rebbe Rayatz) (1880-1950)/Sefer HaMaamarim (Riga and Otwock, 1929-1933).txt:1162)
- **MH:MH-D8 Standers and walkers** (extends D12 (the deed: why the deed moves where nothing above moves)): Above, souls and angels 'stand': each stays on its level, because everything above, even the highest surrounding light, is measured. Only the Essence is without measure, and only the soul below, through physical mitzvos, 'walks' in it. So the descent is not a fall from a high place to a lower one; it is the only way to move in the truly Infinite. | Inside: Michael always in love, Gavriel always in awe; Eliyahu 'before whom I stood'. | Lands: In the hand that does a physical mitzvah; in the sukkah, sitting in the shade of the covering. | Q: דוקא למטה נעשה הנשמה בבחי' מהלך — Only below does the soul become a walker. (Chassidus-txt/R. Yosef Yitzchak Schneersohn (Rebbe Rayatz) (1880-1950)/Sefer HaMaamarim (Riga and Otwock, 1929-1933).txt:1150)
- **MH:MH-D9 Physical and spiritual are equal to Him, and the body is fed** (extends D12, D13): To the Essence there is no higher and lower; physical and spiritual are equal. More: for G-dliness to rest in physical things takes the Essence itself. Souls above know only that G-d exists; only by coming into a body and doing physical mitzvos do they come to His essence. On Pesach the body's physical life is made from food with G-d's essence in it. | Inside: Matzah, 'the food of faith' and 'the food of healing'; tzitzis wool; an orchard apple whose very taste changed through tending (VK 43221). | Lands: In eating, in the body's strength, in a mitzvah of the hands. | Q: ובאמת לגבי בחי' מהות האלקות גשמיות ורוחניות שווין — Relative to the essence of G-dliness, physical and spiritual are equal. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shmuel Schneersohn (Rebbe Maharash) (1834-1882)/Toras Shmuel (scans, OCR).txt:38595)
- **MH:MH-D10 The child's 'Abba'** (extends D13 (plainness) and D7): A baby who tastes grain says 'Abba' and clings with longing, without knowing why. The grown-up who understands also only says 'Abba'. All the adult's knowing is clothed in the child's word. So the small, plain knowing of faith carries the whole. And the cry 'Abba' is what moves the Father's whole being. | Inside: A baby reaching for his father; a halachah a child learns that holds all the adult's reasoning; a map where a dot is a great city (TS 720). | Lands: In a whispered word, now. | Q: הרי שמתלבש כל ידיעת הגדול בדעת הקטן — All the grown-up's knowing is clothed in the child's knowing. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shmuel Schneersohn (Rebbe Maharash) (1834-1882)/Toras Shmuel (Lubavitch, 1867-1881).txt:717)
- **MH:MH-D11 Bowing makes room, and the world comes home brighter** (extends D9 and D10 (the return: adds that the return shows in room for others)): In the Temple people stood packed, yet when they bowed there was room for all. A 'something' never makes room for another 'something'; the proud cannot bear another. When you bow and feel that only He is real, there is room. And a Jew who went home with that feeling found that all ordinary things looked brighter and cleaner, and he heard G-d's power in each thing. | Inside: The crowded courtyard and the spacious bow; a house left open on the way to Jerusalem with no worry (SHM 1558). | Lands: In the bow (the body bends); in how you treat the person next to you; in how plain things look afterward. | Q: וויילע ער איז א גס הרוח, א מציאות דבר בעיני עצמו ומשו"ז אינו יכול לסבול את זולתו — Because he is proud, a something in his own eyes, he cannot bear another. (Chassidus-txt/R. Yosef Yitzchak Schneersohn (Rebbe Rayatz) (1880-1950)/Sefer HaMaamarim (Riga and Otwock, 1929-1933).txt:1561)
- **MH:MH-D12 Still outside: the guard against holy self-love** (extends D2 (its counterfeit) and D1 (guards)): A person can contemplate well and feel love or awe in prayer and still be 'completely outside', if he has not cleaned his mind and traits of self-love. The worst part of the illness is not knowing you are sick. The Rebbe Maharash said this was the Alter Rebbe's innovation: knowing that you lack, and that the lack is an illness. A vessel used with ego that thought its ego was light must be burned clean. | Inside: The brilliant young man who studied Chassidus for hours and asked 'Rebbe, what am I lacking?'; the spit that must be burned until sparks fly (KTH 173-179). | Lands: In conduct after prayer: honesty, good traits with people. | Q: הנה בכל זה הנה הוא עדיין מבחוץ לגמרי — With all this he is still completely outside. (Chassidus-txt/R. Yosef Yitzchak Schneersohn (Rebbe Rayatz) (1880-1950)/Kuntres Toras HaChassidus (scans, OCR).txt:172)
- **MH:MH-D13 The well: the will the soul never had above** (extends D8 and D11 (new: the body as what creates the deepest will)): The Maharash says the soul above did not have the simple will of the heart at all. A well's water comes from the salty sea; passing through the thick earth it becomes sweet and living. So the soul breaks through the body's darkness and comes to want only G-d. The body is not only the place of the test; it is the earth that makes the water sweet. | Inside: Digging the well: heavy-headedness, humility, a bedtime reckoning of 'to whom am I giving my soul' (TS 7790). | Lands: In the body's weight and in bedtime Shema. | Q: כמו"כ הנשמה כשהיתה למעלה קודם בואה לגוף לא הי' לה בחי' רעו"ד — The soul above, before coming into the body, did not have the heart's will. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shmuel Schneersohn (Rebbe Maharash) (1834-1882)/Toras Shmuel (Lubavitch, 1867-1881).txt:7787)
- **MH:MH-D14 Night longing** (extends D9 (new: darkness as a stronger thirst), D14): No one longs for light at noon. In darkness you long most. So the yearning of exile, and of a dark time in a person's life, is stronger than the yearning of times when G-d shone openly; and the plain mitzvos done then are a gift.
