Moshe himself felt small before the simple Jews of the last generation who pray with warmth and plain faith. | Inside: A forest at night with wild animals; a hungry man with nothing to eat; Moshe falling low in his own eyes (SHM 1567-1569). | Lands: In the dark hour itself, with one plain act. | Q: און עס ביינקט זעך אים ניט נאך אור, וויילע עס איז דאך ליכטיג — He does not long for light, since it is light. (Chassidus-txt/R. Yosef Yitzchak Schneersohn (Rebbe Rayatz) (1880-1950)/Sefer HaMaamarim (Riga and Otwock, 1929-1933).txt:1565)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- MH:MH-C01 ['There is nothing but Him' is usually said as one sentence. This maamar splits it in two and shows that the stronger half is true only down here.] (M1) If He is the only true 'something', why do we call creation 'something from nothing'? It sounds backwards. → (M2) His greatness shows most where He makes something totally new: in the lowest world that comes from nothing. → (M3) There are two kinds of 'nothing before Him'. One says: it counts for nothing. The other says: there is nothing else at all. → (M10) 'Counts for nothing' is like a candle at noon. It still burns; it just takes up no room next to the sun. → (M10) But even 'takes up no room' only works between things of the same kind. You cannot say a fly 'takes up no room' next to a man. It is not even in the running. → (M4) So 'there is nothing else' is true only of the created things down here. Their whole being is the Essence itself. → (M16) When you really see this, it is like solving a riddle. You want to shout: it is not the way it looks! → (M16) This has to become yours in prayer. Then the idea is no longer far from you; it becomes your own matter. ⇒ BOTTOM: 'There is nothing else besides Him' is true right here, even in the empty space the world stands in: the lowest things are nothing but His own being. ⇒ RETURN: The return is prayer that makes the knowing near, and the slow work on one's own goat, ox and sheep (the stubborn, the kicking and the soft traits) so the world becomes His home. - MH:MH-C02 [Why do simple people who do not understand the words reach what the wise cannot?] (M9) A simple Jew who does not even know what the Psalm means can pour out his soul before G-d. → (M1) So why is the highest power, wisdom, completed by the lowest power, deed? → (M5) You can know G-d by what He is not. But toward His very Essence even 'not' does not work. There is no ladder from here to there. → (M7) The deepest kind of knowing is not figuring out. It is when something settles in you: 'it is so.' → (M16) When the clear point fades into details, go back and carry yourself to that settled place again. → (M5) No thought grasps Him. The heart's simple will does. → (M10) It is like a son and father. When the son cries 'Abba!', the father is moved in his whole being, more than by any stranger. → (M11) That bond is always there. It wakes when you remember 'You are our Father', or when you feel far away. ⇒ BOTTOM: The bond of a child to his Father, which needs no reason and is never cut, is what grasps the Essence that no thought can hold. ⇒ RETURN: So the goal of wisdom is teshuvah and good deeds: the plain cry and the plain act are where the Essence is held (line 1335). - MH:MH-C03 [How can a person be a home for G-d, when the heavens cannot hold Him?] (M1) The heavens cannot hold Him, yet He says He dwells inside each person, in every place and time. → (M3) Think of a coal. There is the flame you see, and there is fire hidden inside the coal, called flame only because it could become one. → (M5) Deeper than that is flint. The fire is not in the stone at all, not even hidden. Water and fire do nothing to it. → (M8) In your own soul there are powers so deep they are not even 'intellect' yet. Whatever is inside the essence is like the essence. → (M5) Higher still, before anything began, the 'ten sefiros' were only like a name. A name is not even hidden in you; it becomes true when someone calls you by it. → (M7) Prayer means to move from your place and leave all your own matters, and be drawn to the Infinite. → (M11) If you cannot climb by understanding, there is another road: the cry of bitterness over how far you are. It reveals the soul's essence even when you feel nothing of the Infinite. → (M15) And what is 'holy to Me'? The doing. Even ordinary things done the Torah's way, not the cold way of the world. ⇒ BOTTOM: The Essence is grasped not by knowing but by the soul's leap out of itself in prayer, open even to the one who feels nothing. ⇒ RETURN: The leap comes back as deed: the plain act, done the Torah's way, is what He calls holy, and makes each person a home for Him. - MH:MH-C04 [From the love that comes from thinking about G-d's life in all things, down to the love that has no reason at all.] (M6) Think it through: the life in each thing is what matters, and every creature trails after its life back to the first Source. → (M9) Do not only understand it about the world. You have to feel it in yourself. → (M15) And aim at one trait you are fixing now; otherwise after prayer, back in the world, it is all forgotten. → (M7) Higher love: the heart gives way to the mind, and even makes room for its own opposite. → (M5) Highest: a pull with no reason and no goal. The soul is drawn to the Essence by itself, because it is its root. → (M10) Like a flame above the wick, it wants to rise even if it goes out and nothing of it is left. → (M12) That pull is never lost, like a seal pressed into wax. 'My image stays in You, so Your image stays in me forever.' → (M14) The soul did not come down for itself. It came to teach the animal soul, in words it can grasp, so its hardness cracks like a nutshell. ⇒ BOTTOM: A bond of the soul's essence with the Essence that is always there, like a seal's imprint, wanting to rise even at the cost of itself. ⇒ RETURN: The prayer is that we not err: not to float off into 'wonder' far from our work, and not to fall in spirit (line 153). The work is the refining of the animal soul, one trait at a time. - MH:MH-C05 [Why does the soul long for G-d even when it is far? Because it is not a ray of Him but a part of Him.] (M1) A lamp is a ray, so how can it be 'constant'? If it is a lamp it is not constant; if it is constant it is not a lamp. → (M6) A ray rises toward its source only because it is still attached. Cut it off, and it has no pull upward. That is why the world falls downward. → (M4) But the soul is not a ray. It is a part of the Essence itself. → (M11) And because it is separated, its pull to return is stronger, not weaker. → (M12) The Essence in the soul is hidden. That is its advantage: only a hidden essence can enter a body that is nothing like it. → (M16) Before the light goes out, a love from above comes to wake it: 'I love you as you are and where you are.' → (M5) 'One' says the worlds take no place before Him. 'Alone' is beyond even that: you cannot even say they take no place. → (M5) Then a love is given from above: you sense the Infinite, knowing what cannot be known, and you simply melt into it. → (M15) And this bond is the same in every Jew. The good in the simplest person is the oil for the light. ⇒ BOTTOM: The soul is a part, not a ray: its bond is its own, hidden, and strongest where it is most cut off. ⇒ RETURN: It comes back as the simplest good of the simplest person, gathered into light: Tehillim said with feeling, any good deed, 'precious above very much' (line 1480). - MH:MH-C06 [Why send a soul down from a high roof into a deep pit? Because only below can it walk into the Infinite.] (M3) Above, the plain everyday thing is G-dliness and 'existence' is the surprise. Below it is reversed: the plain thing is the world, and G-dliness is the surprise. → (M14) Above, souls and angels 'stand'. Only below does the soul become one who walks. → (M5) Why? Every level above, even the light that surrounds the worlds, is measured. Walking means going into the truly Infinite, and that is only the Essence. → (M7) There is a difference between seeing and 'being seen'. At first glance the world shows only itself. → (M4) The truth is that nature itself is G-dliness. The very sameness of the sky every day shows the power of the Infinite, because anything limited must change. → (M16) But it does not show itself. Only when you look do you see it. 'Teva' means sunk: hidden like a thing under water, yet fully there. → (M1) Believing is not yet light. A thief prays to G-d at the tunnel and still steals. You need to think it through until you get life from it. → (M15) Then the sukkah teaches the end: you can 'sit' in what surrounds, by drawing the Essence into physical things. Mind work is not enough; the heart has to work. ⇒ BOTTOM: Only the Essence is unmeasured, and only the soul below, walking through physical deeds, moves in it. ⇒ RETURN: Look, and see the plain world as the sunk light; then sit in the sukkah of the day: physical things made into vessels, with joy. - MH:MH-C07 [A maamar said in Yiddish at the meal of 13 Tammuz, the anniversary of his release from prison: what an offering is, and what the Temple taught about room.] (M9) An offering means coming closer. 'From yourselves' you can come closer to G-d. → (M2) What do you come close to? To feeling that apart from Him there is no existence at all, and every existence is G-dliness itself. → (M14) Why He wanted a home in this world we do not know. We only know that He wanted it, and gave us the power. → (M10) In the Temple, people stood packed tight, yet when they bowed there was room. A 'something' never makes room for another 'something'. → (M15) Then a Jew went home from Jerusalem, and all ordinary things looked brighter and cleaner. He could hear G-d's power in each thing. → (M11) In exile it is night. But no one longs for light at noon; in the dark you long for it most. → (M13) Moshe saw the simple Jews at the end of exile, praying with chassidic warmth and plain faith, and he felt himself small before them. → (M2) Because every Jew has a point that neither wants nor is able to be separate from G-d. ⇒ BOTTOM: A point in each person that cannot be separated from G-d, which is the same truth as 'every existence is G-dliness'. ⇒ RETURN: Coming home with it: things look brighter, and the plain deed in the dark (learning, mitzvos, kindness to one another) is the gift, the offering. - MH:MH-C08 [Where do sudden thoughts of teshuvah come from? From a part of the soul higher than anything we name.] (M9) Sometimes thoughts of return come suddenly, with no preparation at all. → (M5) Even 'yechidah', the highest of the soul's five names, is only a name. The soul's essence is above it. → (M5) That essence is a tiny spark that is G-dliness itself, from the lowest level of the Creator, dressed in a created spark we call yechidah. → (M6) That is why teshuvah reaches where Torah does not: it touches the Essence, and from there every gap is filled. → (M11) The block: someone keeps mitzvos coldly and is pleased with himself. He does not know he needs to return. → (M15) The way out is to break yourself, and then return. Nothing stands before teshuvah. → (M7) Another block: you understand that nature is G-dliness, even feel a flash, and nothing is born from it. You grasped the idea, not the G-dliness in it. → (M16) The remedy is to stay with it: contemplate it many times, at length, until it takes hold. ⇒ BOTTOM: A spark that is G-dliness itself, above every name of the soul, from which teshuvah comes and to which it returns. ⇒ RETURN: It returns as lingering contemplation that takes hold, and as breaking self-satisfaction so that plain, careful mitzvos come alive. - MH:MH-C09 [Is the plain physical world the lowest place, or where His greatness shows most?] (M1) King David praises G-d for physical things: feeding every creature, high mountains for the ibex. 'You became great when You made Your world.' → (M9) Our feelings mostly feel ourselves. How strong a feeling is depends on how dear we are in our own eyes. → (M6) Physical from spiritual is the greatest 'something from nothing'. Even the sweetness of a fruit, at its root, is the sweetness of G-d. → (M4) But calling the creature 'something' compared to its Maker is against the truth. → (M2) So 'from nothing' means: from the light, which is 'nothing' because it is only a ray, not the Essence. → (M5) The Essence is beyond limit in the truest way: beyond limit and beyond limitlessness too. → (M12) The true unity, which we bring, is that nature itself is above nature. ⇒ BOTTOM: The Essence beyond both limit and limitlessness shows itself in the plainest physical world, which is His greatness and not a 'something' beside Him. ⇒ RETURN: The hidings can cause mistakes, so 'happy is the gever', the one who overcomes (line 912): taste the good and keep working. - MH:MH-C10 [How can we 'know' a G-d no mind holds? By what is near, then by what is only a ray, then by what does not shine at all.] (M9) In your own soul, will is the one power that shows your essence. It is not dressed in any one organ; it commands them all. → (M6) Understand one thing from inside another: once you grasp the light in the world, you see it is only a ray, so there must be an Essence above. → (M2) His 'ability' is not a skill He has. He can, because He is alive in His very being. → (M12) So He holds opposites in one act: striking Egypt and healing Israel in the same moment. → (M10) We know He is beyond place, but we do not know what 'no place' is. → (M4) Even 'light' is a borrowed word. The very essence of light does not shine, like elemental fire, which is dark. → (M5) So we pray: 'In Your light we will see light.' What we call light is dark; we ask to see what truly is light. ⇒ BOTTOM: An Essence whose light does not shine, known only as what is beyond every light we can name. ⇒ RETURN: The giving of Torah was on a mountain and from fire: always long to rise higher, burn with yearning, and keep refining yourself (line 1032). - MH:MH-C11 [Between the true Something and the created something there must be a 'nothing'. But what is a nothing that connects?] (M1) Between the true Being and the created world stands 'nothing'. But how can nothing connect two things? → (M4) Seen from above, 'nothing' means a ray that takes up no room at all. So nothing changed: You are the same before and after creation. → (M9) Seen from below, 'nothing' means what cannot be grasped. The nearest example is your own soul: nothing is closer to your body, and your mind cannot grasp it at all. → (M2) We know it is there without any doubt. Only because we do not know what it is, we call it 'nothing'. → (M10) A seed must rot before it sprouts. To receive the power of growth it must lose its own 'something' entirely. → (M16) If a person saw with the eyes of his soul that his very life is G-dliness, he would want nothing else. → (M15) At the splitting of the sea, the hidden 'nothing' of the soul shone in their bodies, and each one could point: 'This is my G-d.' ⇒ BOTTOM: Two 'nothings' meet: the ray that changes nothing in Him, and the soul's life that we know is there and cannot grasp. ⇒ RETURN: It lands in the body: the hidden life shining in flesh, 'this is my G-d', and the seed's willingness to undo itself so that something can grow. - MH:MH-C12 [Why does the first commandment mention leaving Egypt and not the creation of heaven and earth?] (M1) Creation from nothing is a bigger miracle than the plagues. So why does 'I am Hashem' mention Egypt, not creation? → (M2) Because creation comes only from His 'name', the way a person's name is for others, not for himself. → (M5) 'I' means His very self, and from there comes leaving every limit, even the limits of the holy worlds. → (M9) Inside you too there is an Egypt: the narrow throat between mind and heart. What you understand reaches the heart only a tenth. → (M4) Creation from nothing even the wise of all nations accept. Faith is only for what is above all that. → (M10) A baby who tastes grain says 'Abba' and clings with longing, not knowing why. Yet all the grown-up's knowledge is inside that word. → (M15) Why matzah and not bread? Leaven is puffing up; matzah is flat, humble. Like Avraham, dust and ashes, who thought everyone was better and gave everything away. ⇒ BOTTOM: Faith in the 'I' beyond all worlds, held the way a child holds 'Abba': small, and carrying the whole. ⇒ RETURN: It is eaten: seven days of matzah into the seven traits, like light food for one recovering, until bittul enters every feeling (line 729). - MH:MH-C13 [Why does the soul need to go into a body at all? Because only there can it want G-d with a simple will.] (M2) A 'home below' means His very self lives here, like a man living in his house, not just His glow. → (M11) Thought cannot grasp Him. A simple will can: 'whom do I have in heaven?' How do you come to it? From the straits. → (M4) Before it came down, the soul did not have that will at all. → (M10) Like a well: its water comes from the salty sea, and passing through the thick earth it becomes sweet, living water. → (M16) You must dig the well. At bedtime you say 'into Your hand I give my soul'. Think: to whom are you handing it, and how stained is it? → (M12) Love alone stops after prayer and can even turn into silliness. It needs awe to sink in, and the steady third way to hold. → (M15) Then the will is carved in you, like engraved letters that are part of the stone, not ink that can be wiped off. ⇒ BOTTOM: A simple will for G-d alone that the soul never had above, made sweet by passing through the body's darkness. ⇒ RETURN: From that will, Torah and then mitzvos draw the Infinite down into counted, limited worlds: the home below. - MH:MH-C14 [There is a point in the heart that is the soul pouring itself into its Father's lap. How is it hidden, and how is it found?] (M9) Saying words aloud gives a new depth that silent thinking could not reach. → (M8) The inner heart is the soul's 'yechidah' pouring itself into its Father's bosom. → (M11) It is hidden in sackcloth: the worry of making a living wraps around it. → (M15) When you fight and win the small war inside, the King's daughter is given to you from above. → (M5) A glow of the point can change your thoughts and words. To change what your mind and feelings are, you need the essence of the point itself. → (M16) So you have to search for it: 'Seek My face', My inwardness. → (M13) And it is in every Jew, even one with no understanding at all. ⇒ BOTTOM: The heart's point: the soul pouring itself into the Father's lap, present in every Jew. ⇒ RETURN: The voice of words said aloud wakes it (line 11460); winning one small inner battle brings it out; then even the essence of one's traits can turn. - MH:MH-C15 [How can mitzvos measured in inches hold the One without measure?] (M9) A wise person can know there is a deep point in something before he knows what it is, because he knows his own power. → (M1) But mitzvos are all measured: seven handbreadths, four strings, a tenth. How can they hold the Infinite? → (M4) Even 'with all your very-much', the love that leaves the body, is still yours: very much for you, not truly without limit. → (M14) To refine something, you must go down into the place of what you refine. → (M10) A mitzvah is a lamp. Light a thousand candles from one flame and it is not lessened. → (M12) The flame is the same when you light it and when you put it out; it only lacks something to hold. 'I have not changed.' ⇒ BOTTOM: The unlimited 'very much above', which the soul's own 'very much' cannot reach, held in a measured mitzvah like a flame in a lamp. ⇒ RETURN: Do the measured mitzvah exactly; it is the lamp. On Rosh Hashanah return the year's life as a clean deposit (line 12396). - MH:MH-C16 [Two kinds of giving oneself over, and two kinds of being 'nothing'. Not everyone can do the first, and the second is not less.] (M15) Eating to learn and pray raises the food to its high root and adds wisdom. Eating for appetite brings it down. → (M5) In 'One' you give yourself over completely: every want melts into the Infinite. → (M16) But not every person can come to that. → (M12) So the Sages added 'Va'ed', the same letters as 'Echad', shifted: you stay a someone, and you bow that someone. → (M3) One kind of nothingness: you know you are something, and you set it aside. The other: you are not anything at all, only 'what'. → (M10) A mirror image disappears when you step away. A statue stays. The aim is to be made 'in His image', not only His reflection. → (M15) And He is your shadow: whatever you do with your hand, He does opposite you. ⇒ BOTTOM: Total self-giving into the One, and, for whoever cannot, the bowing of a self that stays. ⇒ RETURN: Both land in the body: how you eat, how you bind tefillin at thirteen, how carefully you act, mirrored above like a shadow. - MH:MH-C17 [Why come down from the upper Garden of Eden only to rise to the lower one?] (M3) A sage's casual talk needs study: in his small words his great wisdom hides. → (M7) The prophet saw the essence through an image. The sage knows only that it exists, though he speaks of higher levels. → (M9) Even the head, the best part of you, which speaks and thinks, never grasps what your soul is. It knows only that it is there. → (M4) Souls above, higher than angels, also know only G-d's existence. Only by coming down into a body do they come to His essence. → (M12) To the Essence, physical and spiritual are equal. In fact, for G-dliness to rest in physical things takes the Essence itself. → (M15) When you eat matzah, the life of your physical body is made from food with G-d's essence in it. → (M10) Faith is the feet. The feet carry the head. One who hops between two opinions is lame: sometimes lifted, sometimes low. ⇒ BOTTOM: The Essence, to which high and low are equal, is reached only in the low: in the body, through the physical mitzvah. ⇒ RETURN: Strengthen the feet: learn the Oral Torah, eat the matzah, do the mitzvah properly, 'it is not grasped by reason, but it is so' (line 38652). - MH:MH-C18 [Two names, two 'nothings', and the light you need for a dark cellar.] (M2) The world is made 'by itself', as if on its own. The Maker hides from what He makes. → (M6) Before the world was made there was 'He and His name alone': His glow still inside Him, like sunlight inside the sun. → (M10) A candle is enough for a small, clean room. In a cellar with thick air the flame cannot hold the wick; you need a torch. → (M11) So the darkest place calls for the highest light, from the One who hides; and in the end even the hiding will shine. → (M4) Higher and lower apply only to light. To the Essence there is no higher and no lower. → (M16) Seeing your own soul is higher than visions; the Arizal merited it through the joy of the mitzvah, not the mitzvah alone. ⇒ BOTTOM: Where the Essence is, there is no up or down: the darkest cellar is where its torch belongs. ⇒ RETURN: Light the lamp at the door to the street; do the mitzvah with joy. - MH:MH-C19 [What Chassidus is, by its own account: not more ideas, but life that turns matter into a vessel; and a sharp guard against feeling holy while still 'outside'.] (M13) There are three wisdoms: to despise the coarse (mussar), to know the worth of the spiritual (inquiry), and to make form master matter (Chassidus). → (M17) Learn them in order, from below: the mussar inside Chassidus, then its inquiry, then its life, which is its essence. → (M11) The worst sickness is self-love, and its bitter drop is that the sick one does not know he is sick. → (M2) The Rebbe Maharash said the Alter Rebbe's innovation was this: that a person know he lacks something, and that the lack is an illness. → (M4) Even if you contemplate well and feel love or awe in prayer, if you have not cleaned your mind and traits you are still completely outside. → (M10) A vessel used with ego, which thought its ego was light, has to be burned clean until sparks fly. → (M15) Prayer is the vessel that carries the light into deed: into conduct and good traits through the day. ⇒ BOTTOM: The Alter Rebbe's own prayer: 'the coming-into-being of the physical is from the Essence' (line 46): the plain world as the Essence's work. ⇒ RETURN: Know your illness; burn out the self that thinks it is light; let prayer carry it into the day. - MH:MH-C20 [Stories the Rebbeim told about what moves heaven, and why the Alter Rebbe became a Chassid.] (M9) A shepherd boy who could not pray cried out like a rooster, 'G-d, have mercy!', and that simple cry broke the decree. → (M15) The Alter Rebbe prayed with the lectern: 'the coming-into-being of physical things is from the Essence'. → (M17) The order of our knowing goes backward: from spreading to point, from existence to essence. So explaining begins with the deed. → (M12) A person, with a physical brain, grasps G-dliness more than the angels, and in that he is higher than the highest. → (M5) The Maggid taught: 'I, who I am', hidden even from the highest, made this earth and man on it. Avraham hosting guests is higher than the kindness of Atzilus. → (M16) The Alter Rebbe said what made him a Chassid was the Maggid's wonderful calm. → (M10) A person is like a gem in the mud. The comparison breaks: unlike building a house, our materials come mixed and wrapped in dirty garments. ⇒ BOTTOM: 'I, who I am' wants the plain world; the physical comes from the Essence. ⇒ RETURN: Settled calm, a cleaned-out gem, and the simple cry: these, not ecstasy alone, are the way. - MH:MH-C21 [Why is one hour of teshuvah and good deeds here finer than all of the World to Come?] (M9) No one has ever seen his own soul. We know the deed, not the doer. → (M7) In the worlds, G-d is known only as existing, and so it is dark. In the Garden of Eden His essence is grasped. → (M1) But how can a created soul receive such revelation? Only through the garments made of mitzvos done here. → (M10) The deeper an idea, the more it needs a parable. So the parable comes from higher than the idea itself. → (M15) So the garment, the deed down here, is higher than the light up there. And joy in serving is greatest of all. ⇒ BOTTOM: The deed below is a garment whose root is higher than any revelation above. ⇒ RETURN: Use the hour: teshuvah, a good deed, done with joy.

## MR1 — the Mitteler Rebbe's core books

The Mitteler Rebbe writes for people who do not feel. His books say so. Derech Chaim was written because of 'the great poverty and pressure of the hour,' for people who 'cannot free even one hour,' so that 'every man will find rest for his soul according to his place.' Kuntres HaHitpa'alut is a long diagnosis of real versus imitation arousal. What his core works add to 'Go deeper' is a map of the person who is numb, busy, sad, or far, and of the exact ways the texts walk that person down to the point that does not change.

(1) Numbness is sleep, not death (MR1-C01). The rope's top end is tied above. Yom Kippur and Ne'ilah show it: even 'the lightest of the light' weep with no preparation. (2) Sadness and bitterness are different things (MR1-C02, MR1-E2). Sadness 'is not from G-dliness at all, only that his soul is not whole in some completeness that would honor his name.' The 'no hope' is the evil inclination's plan. (3) The root of falling is casting off the yoke, not desire. The fix is one push at a time, and each push counts as teshuvah in deed (C04). (4) Under all earned feeling is a Jewishness 'like physical nature.' A child of six won't break Shabbos, and simple people are often more careful than scholars. Here the highest and lowest souls are equal (C05, C15, E13). (5) The last generation, 'unable to budge the body,' is honestly named. Its plain deed under the yoke is 'what gives it.' Mashiach comes 'only from the power of the deed' (C06, E5). (6) Love with no limit is born from what you cannot bear: the drowning friend, the soldier surrounded. 'Pharaoh brought them close.' Trouble 'is only a cause' that uncovers what was always there (C08, E3). (7) Feeling is the wing, not the flight. Seeking the feeling is a common mistake, and so is banning it (C09). (8) Silence before the King: noise measures distance, and the Amidah is whispered by one too near to speak (C10). The shofar is the stages of grief (C11). (9) Darkness comes before light, and only a source beyond light can turn darkness itself (C12, E6). The candle needs the oil of 'nishtkeit,' or the flame jumps off (C13). (10) Before love there is a 'want of the want,' and it is in every Jew (C14, E1). (11) A father's essential mercy is not used up and doesn't wait to be called (C22, E8). The dirt under the palace floor was in the King's first will (C23, E12). (12) Tears are bread: pain and delight weigh the same, and every tear adds up. Even one mishnah guards you all day (C18, E10).

How to use it with DESCENT (ours). E3 sharpens D8 (what you cannot let go): the unbounded love shows exactly at the point of what you cannot bear. E5 extends D12 (deed): the poorest generation's plain deed is the cornerstone. E7 extends D5 (where knowing ends): acknowledging beyond understanding, where the simple man's thanks to the scholar holds more bittul. E9 extends D13 (plainness): broken vessels are His dwelling. E12 extends D1: the smallest detail is equally willed. E13 extends D7: in simple will every Jew is 'one man.' E1, E2, E4, E6, E8, E10, E11 are new: the want of the want, sadness versus bitterness, the image that stays, darkness that turns, mercy that is Himself, pain and delight as one weight, joy at completion.

Guards (ours, from the texts' own balances). Despair is named as the inclination's tactic, never as humility. Pain over thoughts or missing good 'has no advantage at all.' Feeling is neither the goal nor forbidden. Lowliness is 'nishtkeit' that lets you receive, not 'who am I,' which the texts call false humility. Mesirus nefesh is described as a hidden nature that shows in a test, not as something to seek.

## Depths

- **MR1:MR1-E1 The want of the want** (extends new): Before any love or fear can light, there has to be a small longing to have them, a 'will for a will.' The text says this longing is in every Jew, whoever he is. Without it even calm love goes cold and dead. It is something you can ask for: 'Unite our heart.' | Inside: A small warm point, not yet a flame. It feels like a lean toward, not a thing you have. | Lands: You stop waiting to feel before you begin. The beginning is already here. | Q: ונק' רצון לרצון היינו שרוצה שיהי' לו רצון ואהבה אמתית לה' — it is called a will for a will — he wants to have a will and true love for G-d (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Orah (Lubavitch, c. 1822).txt:197)
- **MR1:MR1-E2 Sadness is about me** (extends new): The text separates two things that feel alike. G-dly bitterness comes from feeling a lot of G-d, and it makes a person refuse even small wrongs. Sadness (atzvus) comes from 'his soul not being whole in some completeness that would honor his name.' The first is the ash kept by the altar. The second is the leftover ash carried outside the camp. Sadness leads step by step to 'why bother,' to coldness, to the permitted, to the forbidden. Its message of 'no hope' is the evil inclination's plan. | Inside: A quiet sorting: this ache is His, that one is mine. | Lands: The despair loses its authority. You can feel bitterness without falling. | Q: ועצבות זו אינו מצד אלקות כלל רק מאשר אין נפשו שלם באיזה שלימות שיכבד שמו — this sadness is not from G-dliness at all, only that his soul is not whole in some completeness that would honor his name (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Derech Chaim (scans, OCR).txt:381)
- **MR1:MR1-E3 Love born from what you cannot bear** (extends D8): There is a love with no limit that doesn't come from reasons. It comes from the opposite, from something you can't bear: a friend drowning, being surrounded with no way out. 'Pharaoh brought them close.' The text says hardship 'is only a cause' that uncovers a love that was always in the soul by nature. Once a person gives up on his own escape, the narrowness turns over, and he walks on calmly, without grief. Like a circle, the lowest point touches the highest. | Inside: Fear turning into steadiness. A love bigger than its occasion. | Lands: Suffering is not proof He left. It may be the place where the hidden love surfaces. | Q: אין זה רק סיבה להביא מן העלם לגילוי אה"ר זו שהיא טבעיות ועצמות בנפשו — this is only a cause to bring from hiding to revealing the great love that is natural and essential in his soul (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Pirush HaMilos (Lubavitch, c. 1826).txt:504)
- **MR1:MR1-E4 The image that stays** (extends new): All day the soul's powers 'go here and there' after the body's needs and making a living, and still its image stays engraved above, like lovers whose images are cut into each other's hearts. Love from above is a hug from the outside. Even if the heart wants to pull away, the hug doesn't let it. A sleeping man isn't dead. When woken he is alive at once, as before. Busyness, numbness, and exile are sleep, a knot to be untied, not a death. | Inside: Relief. You were not dropped. | Lands: You come back without needing to rebuild. Returning is waking, not starting from zero. | Q: אע"ג דאזיל הכא והכא — even though he goes here and there (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Imrei Binah (Lubavitch, 1821).txt:568)
- **MR1:MR1-E5 The deed of a generation with nothing left** (extends D12): The last generation is honestly described: learners no better than plain folk, prayers that move no one, souls that can't budge the body. And yet, 'that is exactly what gives it.' What's left is the bare deed under the yoke, and that is 'higher than the beginning of thought.' Mashiach comes only from the strength of that deed. The choice that comes from feeling changes. The yoke doesn't, 'as if it were natural and essential.' | Inside: Steadiness without drama. | Lands: You stop needing to be spiritual to matter. | Q: וזהו ענין ביאת משיח ב"ד שאינו בא רק מכח של המעשה בעול תו"מ בפ"מ לבד — Mashiach comes only from the power of the deed, under the yoke, in actual practice alone (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Orah (Lubavitch, c. 1822).txt:225)
- **MR1:MR1-E6 Darkness that turns** (extends new): Light pushes darkness away but can't turn it into light. Only a source beyond light can do that, 'He made darkness His hiding place.' So there are two kinds of repair. One overpowers bitterness with sweetness, and the bitterness as such stays bad. The other makes the bad itself, as it is, into good: 'willful sins become merits — the willful sins as they are.' That second repair belongs to teshuvah from the depth, and the prosecutor becomes the defender. | Inside: The dark thing stops being only a stain. It becomes a door. | Lands: You no longer need to hide your past from your service. | Q: היינו שנדחה החשך מפני האור אבל לא שיהפך החשך עצמו לאור — meaning the darkness is pushed away before the light, but not that the darkness itself turns into light (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Orah (Lubavitch, c. 1822).txt:397)
- **MR1:MR1-E7 Acknowledging what you cannot understand** (extends D5): Blessing draws down what can be known. Acknowledging (hoda'ah) reaches the Essence that can't be known: 'Your Name is good, and to You it is fitting to give thanks.' It's like admitting your friend is right against your own view, or seeing a miracle with your own eyes and having to admit it. The Jews of Purim are called 'Yehudim,' from hoda'ah, because their self-sacrifice was above reason. A simple person who acknowledges a great scholar he can't follow has more bittul than the wise man's own thanks. | Inside: A bow without a reason, and quiet. | Lands: Not understanding becomes a way of being with Him, not a wall. | Q: הטוב שמך ולך נאה להודות — 'Your Name is good, and to You it is fitting to give thanks' (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Orah (Lubavitch, c. 1822).txt:475)
- **MR1:MR1-E8 Mercy that is Himself** (extends new): Measured mercy can be used up. Essential mercy is a father's toward a darling child, 'My insides stir for him.' Even if the son sins until he is loathed, it prevails, 'as one always has mercy on himself.' Above patience, which is strength holding anger back and can run out, is 'He does not stir up all His wrath' to begin with. Such a father doesn't wait to be called: 'before they call I will answer,' because 'in his distress he is distressed.' | Inside: Being held without having earned it. | Lands: You stop calculating whether you've used up your chances. | Q: דגם אם ירבה לפשוע עד שימאס וישנאנו בתכלית יגברו רחמים העצמיי' — even if he sins until He loathes and utterly hates him, the essential mercy will prevail (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Imrei Binah (Lubavitch, 1821).txt:498)
- **MR1:MR1-E9 Broken vessels are His home** (extends D13): The higher a king truly is, the more he raises the lowly. So 'the dwelling of the Holy One is in broken vessels.' 'Let my soul be like dust to all; open my heart to Your Torah': the heart is opened from above only when the soul is dust. G-d dwells in a truly broken heart more than He shows Himself to those near. 'Near is G-d to the broken-hearted.' The text sees this in the cry many look down on: a plain, sudden cry of a man who sees he can't hear. | Inside: Low and warm at once. | Lands: Brokenness stops being proof you're far. | Q: דיורא דקב"ה באנון מאנין תבירין — the dwelling of the Holy One is in broken vessels (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Orah (Lubavitch, c. 1822).txt:462)
- **MR1:MR1-E10 Pain and delight weigh the same** (extends new): Pleasure and pain are 'one power split into two equal lines.' How much you ache at a loss shows exactly how great the delight was. That's why tears come from the root of delight, when the mind is pressed beyond what it can hold. That's why 'my tears were bread,' and why 'every single tear joins into the account of the bond.' Real teshuvah carries bitterness and joy in one weight, like a son crying and laughing when he sees his father. | Inside: Ache and sweetness in the same place. | Lands: Pain becomes a measure of love, not a verdict. | Q: הרי כח אחד הוא ממש, רק שנחלק לב' קווין שוין ממש ושקולין — it is truly one power, only divided into two lines exactly equal and balanced (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/The Gate of Unity (Lubavitch, pub. 1820).txt:228)
- **MR1:MR1-E11 Joy at completion, beyond knowing** (extends new): Joy comes at the end, like the king's feast when the building is done. On Purim the bride rejoiced openly because acceptance was completed by deed. Joy reveals hidden things, as wine does. The highest joy is above knowing. It isn't confusion about Haman and Mordechai. It is joy from His essence, before which dark and light are the same. That's why weekday work doesn't block it. | Inside: Open, unguarded gladness. | Lands: Joy stops depending on understanding. | Q: עד"מ מלך שעושה משתה וסעודה להיות הכל שמחין בשמחה בהתגלות לפי שכבר גמר הדבר — like a king who makes a feast so all rejoice openly, because the matter is completed (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Orah (Lubavitch, c. 1822).txt:501)
- **MR1:MR1-E12 The dirt under the floor was wanted** (extends D1): A king who decides to build a palace wants the dirt under the floor and the plaster on the beams in his first, simple will, as much as the walls. A king's command is the same for a great task and a small one. So creation, high and low, physical and spiritual, comes from one will equally. Providence reaches 'down to the very last detail.' When the King 'wakes' from exile's sleep, He notices even the fly in the house. | Inside: Dignity in the tiny. | Lands: Nothing in your life is outside the plan. | Q: כמו העפר שבקרקע שתחת הרצפה או העפר שע"ג הקורה — like the dirt in the ground under the floor, or the plaster on the beam (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Orah (Lubavitch, c. 1822).txt:417)
- **MR1:MR1-E13 One man in the simple will** (extends D7): In understanding no two Jews are alike. In the simple point of will they are 'as one man,' like soldiers going to war. That's why at Sinai and on Purim the verbs are singular. Thought can't take hold of Him, but the heart's simple will can. Here high souls and the lowest souls of the last generation are exactly equal. A man whole in deed reaches the level of the greatest martyrs. Lowliness 'equals and makes equal small and great.' | Inside: Belonging without competition. | Lands: Comparison stops. You are not behind anyone in the place that matters most. | Q: כמו עד"מ העם היוצאים למלחמה שיחדיו יהיו כאיש א' במס"נ ואין הפרש כלל בין א' לזולתו — like a people going out to war, together as one man in self-sacrifice, with no difference at all between one and another (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Orah (Lubavitch, c. 1822).txt:474)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- MR1:MR1-C01 ["I pray and feel nothing. I haven't done anything terrible, but inside I'm empty."] (M1) You don't need big sins to feel dead inside. The text says plainly that a person with no grave sins can be as empty as someone who sinned a lot. His prayer comes from his lips while his heart is somewhere else. → (M2) Usually it comes slowly, from being worn down. Hard work and money worries wear on you until you feel nothing, as if you had never tasted fear of G-d. → (M4) But nothing in your real self has died. It is like sleep. A sleeping man isn't dead, and when you wake him he is alive at once, just as before. → (M3) Your soul is like a rope. The top end is tied above, and the bottom end is in your body. When the top end is pulled, the bottom moves by itself. That is why a person sometimes suddenly feels like coming back, without having prepared at all. → (M12) That is why, even after long numbness, a trace is usually left. When a time of favor comes, it wakes. → (M15) And look at what actually happens. On Yom Kippur even the lightest of the light cry, with no preparation and no connection to what they've done all year. → (M15) At Ne'ilah, the closing prayer, even someone who has thrown off the whole yoke and is lost in every grave sin weeps and truly returns. It happens at that hour. → (M16) So never say it is hopeless. It is the very nature of your G-dly soul to be moved again, each person by his own measure. ⇒ BOTTOM: The numbness is sleep, not death. The rope's top end is still tied. ⇒ RETURN: Keep doing the deeds and don't give up on yourself. Expect to be woken: at Yom Kippur, at a time of trouble, or with no warning at all. - MR1:MR1-C02 ["Every time I look honestly at myself I fall into a pit. I see I'm full of bad, I feel hopeless, and then I get worse."] (M1) This is a known cycle. A man sees himself full of bad. Now and then it stirs him and he falls into gloom. He sees no hope, so he goes back to doing worse. → (M11) The text names where it comes from. The voice saying 'no hope' is the evil inclination's plan to knock you down completely. → (M10) Here's the line he draws. Pain over a bad deed you actually did is accepted. Extra pain over thoughts, or over the good you lack, adds nothing. That sadness comes from the body. → (M2) Look at what the sadness is really about. It isn't about G-d. It's that you aren't the complete person you want to be honored as. → (M11) Follow it and you can see where it goes. 'Why bother, I can't do it anyway' turns into coldness, then into giving in on what's permitted, then into what's forbidden. Sadness is the doorstep into the rooms of the other side. → (M4) Real bitterness is different. It comes from feeling a lot of G-d, and it makes you hate even the small wrongs you never noticed before. Like fire in a refiner's pot: the more fire, the finer the silver. → (M12) Real return holds both. Bitterness at having been far, and great joy at coming back. If there is no joy, it isn't real teshuvah yet. It only lasts until the next test. ⇒ BOTTOM: Sadness is about me and my honor. Bitterness is about distance from Him. ⇒ RETURN: Once you notice the pain, go straight back to strengthening yourself. Take on something for the future, and let the joy of coming back carry it. - MR1:MR1-C03 ["How can I cry over what I did and be happy at the same time? Isn't that two opposite things?"] (M1) He asks the question himself: how can the deepest bitterness and the greatest joy go together? → (M6) Picture a spark and a big torch. Far from the torch, the spark slowly goes out. As it comes closer it grows brighter, until it joins the flame. → (M7) So every step you walk away from where you were is also a step closer. That's why the verse says he 'walks along weeping.' The walking and the crying are the same thing. → (M9) It is like a son who was far from his father for a long time. He walks crying to find him. When he sees him, he cries and is glad at once. → (M16) And G-d isn't waiting at the top. 'You will find Him' means He finds you where you are, at the bottom step. → (M15) Even someone in the lowest pit is taken back while still unclean, if he turns with a broken heart. Menashe was answered through a tunnel dug in heaven. → (M5) In the end the poorest rejoice the most. The joy of someone who was far is bigger, so big his heart can't hold it and he cries for joy, like Yosef when he saw Binyamin. ⇒ BOTTOM: Crying and gladness are one walk toward one Father. ⇒ RETURN: Walk, even if you cry as you go. The joy is the sign you are getting closer. Don't wait to feel clean before you come. - MR1:MR1-C04 ["I keep regretting, fasting, promising — and falling again in the same place."] (M1) If you keep regretting and falling back, you haven't touched the root. The verse mocks 'fools who fast over their sins' while still living however they please. Constant regret doesn't fix that. → (M11) The root is casting off the yoke: going after whatever your heart wants, as a matter of course, without stopping to ask if it is against His will. → (M2) Taking the yoke on is very concrete. When something bad comes into your heart, you push it away right then, because you decided that firmly. → (M4) And each push counts. Every time you push a thought away, that is teshuvah in actual deed, even though all you did was not follow your heart. → (M10) Don't measure it by how you feel. A flare-up of feeling changes from hour to hour. Someone suddenly on fire with teshuvah falls the other way soon after. Plain wholeheartedness doesn't flare, but it is true. → (M5) That's why turning in deed has a higher root than turning in will or thought. The end is wedged into the beginning. → (M3) There's a sign it's working. Your heart is in your own hands, and you can drop a thought the moment it comes. 'Let the willful not rule me, then I will be whole.' ⇒ BOTTOM: 'Tam' is simple wholeness, a point of will beyond reason. It doesn't flicker. ⇒ RETURN: Push one thought away at a time, and count each push as a deed of teshuvah. Fasting helps only after the root is fixed. - MR1:MR1-C05 ["In prayer I feel real fear of G-d, and an hour later I'm angry, stingy, cutting corners in business. Is my fear fake?"] (M1) You aren't the only one. Everyone sees these two opposites in himself every day. In prayer, love and fear come. A moment later you're empty and can't stand up to one bad trait. → (M2) There are two kinds of fear. The first is earned by effort and contemplation. It rises and falls with your days. The second is like nature, in every Jew, and it never changes. → (M15) Proof: tell a child of six or seven to break Shabbos or eat something unkosher and he'll risk his life. An orphan with no one to teach him keeps it. Women guard it even more. → (M15) Many simple people who are whole in what they do are much more careful than people of learning and Torah. → (M2) He says it in Yiddish so you can't miss it: in the point of his heart, in his being a Jew, he is very strong, like a physical nature. → (M5) And this plainness is not a lower level. A man whole in his deeds reaches the level of the great tzaddikim who gave their lives. → (M13) At this point the high souls and the low souls of the last generation are exactly equal. Every generation stood firm the same way, in every persecution. → (M9) You can see it in joy too. Jews are very happy when they do a mitzvah, women, children, and simple people too. That joy doesn't come from understanding the reasons. ⇒ BOTTOM: A Jewishness that is like physical nature. You don't earn it and you can't lose it. ⇒ RETURN: Lean on the deed and the plain point. The earned feelings come and go. This one holds. - MR1:MR1-C06 ["I learn, I pray, I even get inspired — and my body doesn't budge. Nothing changes in my habits."] (M1) He says it right out: the very low souls of the generation before Mashiach can be moved by closeness to G-d, and still have no strength to change the body's nature at all. → (M2) Once, everyone who studied Torah was also a complete tzaddik. Now the Torah's light seems unable to bring even its learners back. They count as plain people. → (M10) It is like someone who fell into a very deep pit. Light doesn't reach him, voices don't reach him, ropes don't reach him. → (M4) All that's left is the deed done under the yoke, without inner feeling. But in truth that plain deed is higher than all, higher than the beginning of thought. Mashiach comes only by the strength of that deed. → (M3) Here is why it holds. A choice made from feeling changes, good one day and bad the next. A servant's work under the yoke never changes, because no feeling is in it. → (M15) So the very thing that seems like poverty is the opening. The last generation, missing all inner light, left with only the deed and the joy of a mitzvah from the deed itself: 'that is exactly what gives it.' → (M5) For souls like these, the way is teshuvah, which turns the darkness itself into light. 'And G-d will light up my darkness.' ⇒ BOTTOM: The plain deed of a generation with nothing left is 'the stone the builders rejected,' and it becomes the cornerstone. ⇒ RETURN: Do the mitzvah plainly, without waiting to feel. Let the joy come from having done His will. - MR1:MR1-C07 ["My whole head is in making a living. Am I still connected at all?"] (M1) Prayer and inspiration fix the bad traits only for a while. Once you're back in the world's business it gets dark again. Anyone very busy knows this. → (M2) But being caught isn't the same as being bad. The soul tied up in earning a living, mind and heart, is like a captive. There's no evil mixed into the spark. It's only held. → (M8) There are three states: bound, fallen, bent. The fallen one has only lost his footing, in his doing, and he can get up right away. 'Seven times the righteous fall and rise.' The bent one is still standing on his feet. Only his head is bowed with worries for the body. → (M16) And the bound one doesn't need to be rebuilt. He only needs the knot untied. → (M4) Even while your powers go here and there all day after the body's needs, your image stays engraved above, like two lovers whose images are cut into each other's hearts. → (M16) Love from above can pull even a heart that has turned away, in the noise of making a living and even in its impurity, suddenly, with no preparation. ⇒ BOTTOM: The spark is held, not spoiled. The image stays. ⇒ RETURN: Treat each turning-back as untying one knot. It takes a moment, not a rebuild. - MR1:MR1-C08 ["I only really turned to G-d when things got terrible. Is love born from suffering second-rate?"] (M1) Look at how people act. A man runs into a burning house to save his money, though his life is worth more to him than money. → (M2) There are two loves. One is measured, like gratitude for a favor. The other has no limit, and it comes from something you cannot bear, like seeing your friend drowning. → (M9) Or a soldier surrounded on every side. Once he gives up on saving himself, his heart flips. The great fear leaves him and he turns to fight with joy and courage. → (M14) That is 'Pharaoh brought them close.' The chase brought out a love they had never felt. Today it's the same: 'In your distress they will find you.' Menashe came back through his suffering. → (M4) But it isn't new. The trouble only brings out a love that was already natural and essential in your soul. → (M12) While you still fight the narrowness you haven't let go. Once you give up on yourself, you go on calmly, with no grief at all. → (M15) It's like a circle. The lowest point touches the highest. The animal soul's endless love of its own life, once broken, sits right next to the G-dly soul's endless love. ⇒ BOTTOM: The love was always there. What you couldn't bear only uncovered it. ⇒ RETURN: When it's narrow, say 'To You, G-d, I lift my soul.' Don't wait for the narrowness to end. - MR1:MR1-C09 ["I want to feel something when I pray. If I don't feel it, I've failed."] (M1) Many people make the feeling the goal. They work to produce love and fear, and when they get enough of it they are content, as if they arrived. → (M10) A bird's wing isn't the point. Flying is the point, and the wing is how it happens. Love is the wing. The point is the closeness it carries you to. → (M3) Here is how to tell the difference. A feeling that's really for G-d doesn't let you rest. It leaves you thirstier, and you go to drink from Torah after prayer. → (M11) A feeling that feels itself can flip into anger. Fervor in prayer that stands out to the person himself turns into wanting to hit whoever touches his honor. → (M9) Real feeling is like clapping your hands at good news. It's felt, but you don't know you're doing it. Effortful feeling is waited for, and if it doesn't come you're upset. → (M12) But don't swing to the other side. Banning all felt feeling is also a mistake. Even love that enjoys itself leads to a firm resolve to do good all day. So what if it isn't perfect. → (M2) The true sign comes afterward: real lowliness, not humility worked out by counting your faults. Insults about your 'level' don't reach you, because you have nothing of your own there. ⇒ BOTTOM: Nothing of my own. Feeling is the wing, and closeness is the flight. ⇒ RETURN: Take what feeling comes and don't rest on it. Go drink Torah after prayer. Watch for anger. It shows when a feeling was really about you. - MR1:MR1-C10 ["Why is the Amidah whispered? Wouldn't crying out show more feeling?"] (M10) Noise comes from distance. The far-off angels sing with great noise, the nearer ones with less. The closer you are, the less your bittul shows. → (M9) Think of someone stuck on a hard halachic question. He goes silent until the truth comes. Then his lips move in a whisper, and he doesn't even feel what he says. → (M2) Or someone standing very near the king. He is so nullified that if the king asked him something he'd stay silent like a stone. He'd only whisper, and not know what he's saying. → (M4) That's why the Sages say whoever raises his voice in the Amidah is 'of little faith.' The loudness shows a small mind far away. Rava stood like a still stone. → (M5) There are two kinds of nullifying. One is understanding how something comes from nothing, so the something lets go. The other is like standing before the king: from the king himself comes a bittul where you are nothing at all. → (M7) That's why 'Modim,' we thank You, is acknowledgment beyond understanding. It's like admitting your friend is right even against your own view. You bow with your body. Whoever doesn't bow at Modim, the Sages say, won't rise at the resurrection. → (M3) So the opening line of the Amidah is a confession of this: 'G-d, open my lips,' because there's no word on my tongue. The lips open only by the King's own strength. ⇒ BOTTOM: Standing like a stone and whispering because you are near, not because you are empty. ⇒ RETURN: Whisper the Amidah and bow in your body. Let 'open my lips' be true. Ask, don't perform. - MR1:MR1-C11 ["What am I supposed to do while the shofar blows?"] (M1) The shofar alone doesn't do it. Without teshuvah from the depth of the heart, the blasts don't help. You fulfill the mitzvah of hearing, but the sin isn't forgiven. → (M16) You don't need grave sins to cry. Anyone can be bitter about how far he's drifted while chasing this world. 'Return, wayward children.' → (M8) The sounds are the stages of real grief. First, pain so deep you can't even cry, you're like a stone, the way a person goes still at trouble with his son. That's the tekiah. → (M8) Then a great sigh that breaks the body, the shevarim. Then short, broken sobs, the teruah. It's like laughter, which also comes in short bursts when joy is too much to hold. → (M9) This is 'their heart cried out.' It's like someone who hears bad news that touches his very life. All his life gathers into one point of the heart. → (M12) Sometimes the crying has no sound at all. The heart can't hold it, like a man led out to be killed or mourning his only son. He's silent and weeps inside. In Yiddish: the heart cries by itself, without choosing to. → (M15) 'From the depths I called You.' There are two depths. The depth of bad is a heart widened by pleasures. The depth of good is a heart broken to pieces, in Yiddish 'tzubrochenkeit.' From the second depth the cry rises like a well of living water. ⇒ BOTTOM: The plain cry from the heart's point, without words. ⇒ RETURN: During the blasts, let the heart do what the shofar does: be still, then sigh, then sob. Don't make speeches. - MR1:MR1-C12 ["Why does everything good I've ever reached come only after I was about to give up?"] (M3) That's the order of everything: 'first darkness, then light.' You can't come to real joy in serving G-d unless the opposite comes first, deep bitterness and lowliness. The deeper the bitterness, the brighter the joy after. → (M9) Look at a learner. He doesn't reach the depth until he has worked so hard he almost despairs, worried enough to get sick. 'No one stands on a halachah until he has stumbled in it.' → (M9) It's the same in business and farming. Only after someone gives up on his capital does the big profit come. The farmer 'walks weeping, carrying the seed,' and comes back singing with sheaves. → (M10) Torah learning works this way too. 'He seated me in darkness' is the Babylonian Talmud. Every answer gets knocked down by a question, darkness on darkness, until a far greater light of truth comes out. → (M16) Being far makes the longing stronger. Someone who lives inside the king's palace doesn't long much. Someone sent far away into a dry, dark land, remembering the king's glory, longs with his whole soul. → (M15) Even the black part of a flame, the part that seems not to give light, has a higher root than the bright part. A put-out wick placed under a burning flame pulls the flame down to itself. → (M5) And in the end the darkness itself will shine like light. 'Night shines like day.' ⇒ BOTTOM: Darkness isn't the opposite of the path. It is the root that comes before the light. ⇒ RETURN: When you're near despair, keep walking with the seed and keep asking the hard question. Say 'My soul longed for You at night.' - MR1:MR1-C13 ["I get inspired, really on fire — and in a minute it's gone."] (M2) A candle has a wick, a dark flame that slowly eats the wick, a white light above it, and oil that holds the flame on the wick. Without oil the flame jumps off or eats the wick all at once. → (M9) We see it: whenever someone burns with closeness to G-d, his natural mind and heart catch fire too. But often the light jumps away in a moment. That's from missing oil. → (M2) The oil is bittul, the nothingness of 'what are we.' It works two ways. It suddenly humbles the coarse heart into a broken heart that comes back. And it brings real lowliness, where you feel no merit in anything you do. In Yiddish: nishtkeit, he is nothing in essence. → (M10) And when the heart is like stone and doesn't move at all? That's why the Chanukah light goes outside the door. It reaches someone standing outside in the night who can't get to the palace. → (M14) Even the blows of life serve this. When the body doesn't let the soul's light rise, it gets struck in children, health, and livelihood, so the soul's light shines in it with a thought of teshuvah from a broken heart. → (M15) Strange but true: the oil follows the wick, and the light burns only on the wick. So the wick, the body and the animal soul, has a quality higher than the oil. Its slow burning is what pulls down the white light. → (M3) Only when the soul is like dust does the heart open from above. 'Let my soul be like dust to all; open my heart to Your Torah.' These go together. ⇒ BOTTOM: Oil, the nothingness, is what holds the flame to the wick. ⇒ RETURN: Don't chase a bigger flame. Bring oil: a thought of teshuvah with a broken heart, then pour yourself out in prayer. - MR1:MR1-C14 ["I work hard at contemplating, and still nothing comes. Not love, not fear, not even regret."] (M1) Sometimes the soul flares up by itself with no effort. Sometimes, with great effort, nothing shows in the heart at all. That's because the body screens it, not because the soul is missing. → (M3) What's missing first is smaller than love. It's a little longing to have love. The text calls it 'a will for a will': you want to want G-d for real. → (M15) And this longing is in every Jew, whoever he is: the wish to be near G-d always. → (M11) Without it even a cool, steady love goes dead. The text uses the Yiddish words kaltkeit and toitkeit, coldness and deadness. Praise from the lips alone is called 'the dead do not praise G-d.' → (M2) That's why the whole menorah was gold, even the lamp of cool love. Every good trait needs a spark of fire mixed in, or it won't last. → (M16) So ask for the wanting itself. That's what we say every day: 'Unite our heart to love and fear Your Name.' ⇒ BOTTOM: The want of the want. It is in everyone and can be asked for. ⇒ RETURN: Before trying to feel, ask to want. Say 'unite my heart' slowly, as an actual request. - MR1:MR1-C15 ["I've never been tested. How would I know whether I'd really stand?"] (M1) Even at Sinai, 'we will do' was only said in words and accepted in the heart. It hadn't yet come to an actual deed. → (M2) In Haman's year everyone, from great to small, women and children, decided they wouldn't convert even if they were all killed. Not one stray thought came into their hearts. → (M14) Giving your life isn't written in the Torah as a command, because it's an inheritance, by nature, from the Patriarchs. → (M13) In understanding, no two Jews are alike. Each grasps G-d in his own way, like commentators on one line of Gemara. In the simple point of will, all of them become like one man, like soldiers going to war. → (M5) Thought can't take hold of Him at all. But the heart's simple will can. → (M12) It took two things together: giving their lives, and being utterly low, like dust. The lowliness made small and great equal. → (M15) That's how the plain deed becomes a crown. Precious stones are just minerals, yet set in a crown they become the king's glory. ⇒ BOTTOM: The simple will, where every Jew is one man and G-d is grasped. ⇒ RETURN: You don't need a test to know it's there. Act from it in small things today, plainly and without bargaining. - MR1:MR1-C16 ["Why does Purim ask for joy 'until you don't know'? Isn't joy supposed to come from understanding?"] (M3) Real joy comes when a thing is finished. A king throws a feast when the building is done or the war is won. Until then he may be glad inside, but he doesn't celebrate. → (M2) Joy opens what's hidden. Everyone sees that a happy person reveals all his secrets. Wine does the same. → (M5) Purim's joy is above knowing. That doesn't mean you'd confuse cursed Haman with blessed Mordechai. It means the joy comes from G-d Himself, before whom darkness and light are the same. → (M10) That's why work is allowed on Purim. Weekday work blocks joy that comes from knowing, but nothing blocks joy that comes from His essence. → (M8) There are two feelings. Joy from understanding is felt. Delight from deeper wisdom is so deep you don't feel it. In Yiddish, 'it moves you from your place,' kvelling. → (M9) At night comes the song. Joy belongs to the morning, and song to the evening after the day's work, like a man singing when he finishes his work well. ⇒ BOTTOM: Joy that comes from His essence, where nothing can stop it. ⇒ RETURN: Finish something today for His sake. Let the joy come at the end and come out loud. - MR1:MR1-C17 ["I want to come back, but I don't feel moved. Can teshuvah start without feeling?"] (M3) Yes. The first teshuvah is just changing your choice in actual deed. You make a very strong decision in your heart to leave the bad ways. → (M16) And you aren't alone in it. G-d, who tests hearts, knows the strong resolve and helps you keep walking the good way, even to conquer your inclination. → (M10) But don't confess while you're still doing it. That's like immersing with an impure creature in your hand. Better to turn in deed without confessing than the reverse. → (M16) A humble, detailed confession wakes great mercy. 'Whoever confesses and leaves is shown mercy.' Like an orphan whose mother and father left him, and G-d gathers him in. → (M8) The next level is teshuvah in the heart, with feeling. Even simple people who aren't learned have this in their own measure, like crying bitterly during the confession. → (M12) Feeling may stop. Resolve doesn't. It can happen that the heart's arousal ends while the resolve never ends. → (M15) Even the deepest damage, where the soul's root seems cut, has hope on Yom Kippur. 'I, I am He who wipes away your transgressions.' Even what died at the root will live, through teshuvah from the depth of depths. ⇒ BOTTOM: The resolve, which G-d sees and helps, under all the feelings. ⇒ RETURN: Decide one thing firmly today. Confess it at bedtime with 'Into Your hand I entrust my spirit.' Let the feelings come when they come. - MR1:MR1-C18 ["I cry over how far I am, and then I feel I should stop crying and do something useful."] (M9) 'My tears were bread for me day and night.' How can tears be bread? Someone grieving over losing what he loves wants to cry. It's good for him. Stopping him hurts him almost to fainting. → (M3) Pain and delight weigh the same. How much you ache over losing G-d's closeness shows exactly how great the delight was. → (M5) So each tear isn't lost. Every tear adds to the measure of your bond, and fixes what casting off the yoke damaged. → (M16) Then comes the comfort. 'This is my comfort in my poverty, that Your word gave me life.' Every Jew has a letter in the Torah. Even one chapter, one mishnah, or one verse of Shema guards you all day. → (M15) This goes against what you'd expect. Learning Torah mostly comes not from delight in prayer but from its opposite: feeling poor and empty. An empty vessel holds a lot. Someone full of his own spiritual feelings doesn't feel he needs Torah. → (M6) 'The gates of tears are never locked.' Those tears open the way to learning, as comfort, not as pride. ⇒ BOTTOM: The poor soul's bread: the tear, and the letter of Torah that is his. ⇒ RETURN: Cry if it comes. Then open a book and learn even one mishnah as your bread. - MR1:MR1-C19 ["My friend hears a Chassidus talk and is on fire. I hear the same words and feel nothing. What's wrong with me?"] (M1) Everyone sees it: one person is moved when he hears, another not at all. It isn't because one idea is deeper. Many deeper ideas move no one. → (M10) And it isn't piety either. A person can fear G-d in his heart and be whole in his service and still have no 'sense of hearing' for this at all. → (M15) So where does it come from? From the soul itself. That's why even a lowly person with little understanding is deeply moved when he hears about G-d filling and surrounding all worlds. It comes from his soul's root, like a spark drawn to the flame. → (M4) The busy merchant may hear only a cold thought, far away, that's gone an hour after prayer. Even so, what is left in him is the very nature of his Jewishness. → (M15) And when he sees he can't hear, he comes to plain teshuvah: bitterness, remembering what he did as a youth, a sudden cry with no contemplation at all. That's 'their heart cried out.' Many look down on that cry. They're blind. → (M11) The deepest reason some can't hear is that there is no broken heart. A man completely 'whole in himself,' in Yiddish 'gantz zeier bay zich,' hears Chassidus like something outside him. → (M9) So don't copy your friend. Everyone should do what he truly is, in his own nature, not imitated. ⇒ BOTTOM: The hearing comes from the soul itself, and the broken heart is what lets it in. ⇒ RETURN: Stop measuring yourself against him. Let your own cry come, and keep it. - MR1:MR1-C20 ["At 'Shema... Echad' I'm told to be ready to give my life. That feels like play-acting."] (M9) There's a love you don't decide on, like your own will to live. You can't split 'wanting to live' from 'not wanting to die.' They're one thing. The inner heart loves G-d that way. → (M15) This love is hidden and doesn't show except in a test. Then even the lowest Jew gives his life, because the spark breaks into the open. → (M14) That's why the Torah never commands giving your life. It's the soul's nature, so it comes by itself. Only 'love G-d... with all your soul' is a command, and that's for the body's side. → (M7) So the real work in this world is to bring that hidden heart into the open, so its darkness lights up. 'G-d will light my darkness.' → (M5) 'Because he desired Me.' 'Me' means His very self, and 'desire' means the whole soul pulled, until there's no other want. A father who sees his son in trouble doesn't wait to be called. His son's trouble is his own. → (M12) Rabbi Akiva longed for the actual deed: 'When will it come to my hand?' The soul giving itself in a body has an advantage, because G-d 'took counsel' only with souls in bodies, not with souls by themselves. → (M16) And that readiness isn't for heroes. It's in everyone's power: to have the heart and natural soul truly ready. ⇒ BOTTOM: The inner heart's love of Him is like the love of being alive. It doesn't need a command. ⇒ RETURN: At 'Echad,' don't act anything out. Let the inner heart be what it is, and then do one thing with your body that matches. - MR1:MR1-C21 ["Deep contemplation of sefiros is for scholars. I'm a simple person with no time."] (M11) Don't say 'who am I to reach deep contemplation.' That's false humility, and it's the evil inclination's way of pushing you away. Even someone who can only read must work at this by his own measure. → (M3) And it starts simple: think about how heaven and earth and everything in them come into being from nothing. 'Lift up your eyes on high and see who created these.' → (M11) Without this simple foundation, all the deep study doesn't settle in you at all. → (M7) He means seeing with your physical eyes. 'Lift up your eyes,' with physical sight, at things made into full physical existence. → (M10) By the count of levels it's very far from top to bottom. But in truth it's very near, with no break and nothing hiding in between. → (M15) When you see it like this, even your flesh heart burns. For that hour your heart becomes completely pure, wanting only Him. → (M13) The purpose of every contemplation is to include everything in the One, until it's fixed in your soul that His providence reaches the very last detail. ⇒ BOTTOM: One look at the plain world, as coming from nothing, holds all the levels. ⇒ RETURN: Each day, for a minute, look up at the sky or at your hand and say 'who created these.' Let it be seeing, not a thought about seeing. - MR1:MR1-C22 ["I've sinned too many times. Surely His patience has run out."] (M10) There are two kinds of mercy. Measured mercy can be used up when sin goes past the measure. Essential mercy is like a father's for his darling child. Even if the son sins until the father is disgusted with him, the essential mercy wins, as a man always has mercy on himself. → (M5) Patience is strength. It holds back anger, and it can run out. Higher than that, 'He does not stir up all His wrath' to begin with. That comes from mercy that is His essence: 'My insides stir for him.' → (M4) Even punishment heals. When the sinner was lashed they recited 'He is merciful, He atones,' and after the lashing 'he is your brother' again. The blow takes away the death. → (M15) The higher He is, the more He has mercy. And the lower the light comes, the more inward it is. 'I and not an angel.' → (M16) An answer can come two ways. One is weighed by merit. The other comes at once, with no weighing at all. 'The prayer of a poor man' is heard the moment he cries. 'Before they call, I will answer.' → (M13) The merit of the fathers can run out. His essential kindnesses never end. That's why time itself can't hold enough thanks. ⇒ BOTTOM: A father's mercy that is himself, and doesn't wait to be called. ⇒ RETURN: Cry out like the poor man, without lining up your merits. Then thank Him, knowing you can never thank enough. - MR1:MR1-C23 ["Why would G-d care about my little life? I'm a fly in the palace."] (M12) Seen from His height, nothing below takes up any room, like a gecko in a king's palace. Yet the same verse says He 'lowers Himself to see.' He pitied Nineveh's animals and said of the Egyptians: 'My handiwork is drowning in the sea.' → (M2) He knows and feels His creatures' pain, because they are His own handiwork. → (M9) Picture a king asleep. He doesn't feel a fly. Awake, he notices every fly and gecko in the house. Exile is called sleep. 'Awake, why do You sleep, G-d.' When He wakes, nothing is too small. → (M10) A king's command is the same for a big job or a small one. It's all one command. That's how He makes the world, equally high and low. → (M13) When a king decides to build a palace, the dirt under the floor and the plaster on the beam are in his first wish just as much as the walls. Without them the palace isn't finished. → (M15) And the lower the light reaches, the more inward a part of Him comes. When He went down into Egypt it was 'I, not an angel.' ⇒ BOTTOM: Nothing is too small for His first will. The dirt under the floor was wanted. ⇒ RETURN: Do the small thing in front of you as part of His palace. Don't wait for something big. - MR1:MR1-C24 ["I know I'm low. So why try? Who am I to reach anything?"] (M11) Lowness can stop you. If your heart doesn't rise a little, you can't serve G-d at all, because you keep saying 'who am I.' So the heart needs strengthening. → (M3) The Sages measured it: a scholar needs 'an eighth of an eighth' of pride, one part in sixty-four, so his heart can rise and join his mind. More than that is coarse. Less, and the heart never reaches the mind. → (M10) Real lowliness is what lets you receive. A student who makes himself into one small point to take in his teacher gets more, and later can give more. → (M9) Look at a king of flesh and blood. The more exalted he is, the more he raises the lowly and pities them. That's the nature of true height. → (M15) So G-d's dwelling is specifically in broken vessels, the poor and crushed in spirit. → (M2) This lowliness is inherited from Avraham, who made himself dust. It's what opens the heart's intent in a mitzvah, to do only His will. → (M5) And the plainest humility, total nothingness in Yiddish, 'nishtkeit,' draws down the true Nothing, higher than all. ⇒ BOTTOM: 'Nishtkeit,' nothing of my own. It is the vessel, not the excuse. ⇒ RETURN: Say 'I am nothing' only so you can receive. Then lift your heart one sixty-fourth and go serve. - MR1:MR1-C25 ["These ideas are too deep for me. Can a simple person really touch them?"] (M2) The deeper a wisdom is, the less it can be held as it is. It needs a parable, a 'foreign garment,' to come into grasp at all. Shlomo spoke three thousand parables. → (M9) A great teacher wraps the deep idea in parable after parable until even a small student can get it. What the student holds is the very same point, the way you hold a person by his coat. You're still holding him. → (M10) Only the very greatest sage can do this, bringing it down even to a little child. Someone less wise knows the idea only as it is and can't explain it to the small. → (M16) A student may not reach his teacher's mind for forty years by words. Through a parable he gets it at once. Even a small child receives, in his measure, all the depth of the idea. → (M15) And further than parable is a gesture of the hand. It's a plain physical motion, yet it holds the deepest point. The mitzvos are like that gesture. → (M15) The rule: whatever comes down into the most physical form, that's exactly where the depth and source are. ⇒ BOTTOM: The deepest point comes to you dressed in the plainest garment, and you are really holding it. ⇒ RETURN: Don't wait to be a scholar. Take the parable and the mitzvah as the real thing, because they are. - MR1:MR1-C26 ["All I do is small things: a coin to a poor man, a quick mitzvah without much thought."] (M2) 'With all your might' means with your money, because some people hold their money dearer than their body. Charity from your own toil has great power, even more than giving your body. → (M13) Each penny to a poor man joins the others into a great sum, like armor made of many small scales. → (M3) Rava would give a penny to a poor man and then pray. The charity comes first, and then the shining face in prayer. → (M9) A poor man rejoices more over the penny that saves his life than a rich man over his whole fortune. That's how one hour of teshuvah buys a world. → (M15) Even 'this coin is for charity so my son will live' counts as complete righteousness, but only in a Jew. The lowliness underneath it is about doing His will. → (M4) All Israel has a share in the World to Come through the body of the mitzvos as done, even without intent. → (M5) The deed makes a delight before Him, 'that I spoke and My will was done.' It's a pleasure that comes only from doing. ⇒ BOTTOM: 'I spoke and My will was done.' The plain deed is His delight. ⇒ RETURN: Give the coin, then pray. Count every small mitzvah as a scale in the armor. - MR1:MR1-C27 ["My mind won't stop. Bad thoughts, irritation, money, honor. I can control what I do but not what I think."] (M2) That's real. The heart can hold back anger. Thought can't, and you can't take your mind off something you desire. Thought is rooted in the soul's very essence. → (M11) The stray thoughts come from habit. Whatever you've poured your inner heart into, honor and money and the like, keeps thinking itself even when you don't want it, like dreams of what you thought by day. → (M2) Even permitted thoughts and words of business, and all the idle words of a lifetime, build a wall around the soul so it can't rise. → (M16) What tears down that wall is tears. 'Let tears run down like a river, wall of the daughter of Zion.' → (M8) Purity of thought is a moment-by-moment job: despise each foreign wish that comes up and push it away, each moment again. ⇒ BOTTOM: Thought is rooted in the essence. That's why it is hard, and why it matters. ⇒ RETURN: Push away, cry if you can, and feed the mind with Torah so new habits form. - MR1:MR1-C28 ["I feel so far from G-d. Doesn't that mean I can't love Him?"] (M10) Love is felt most from far away. A father close to his son doesn't feel the full strength of his love. When the son is far, the longing burns.
