'Yaakov's spirit revived' at the news of Yosef. → (M15) That's why Israel's love in exile is called 'flashes of fire,' more than in the Temple days. → (M12) Fear works the opposite way. It grows from nearness. Someone close to the king trembles until his knees knock and he becomes like a stone, with no feeling of himself. → (M15) And the deeper you understand that He is beyond grasp, the more the light comes with force. So His light is strong exactly below, in the distance. ⇒ BOTTOM: Distance is what shows how much you love. ⇒ RETURN: Use the feeling of being far as fuel. Say 'my soul thirsts for You in a dry land' and mean the dry land.

## MR2 — Toras Chaim

The Mitteler Rebbe's Toras Chaim (on Bereishis and Shemos) climbs slowly and at great length: each maamar lays foundation upon foundation until the reader stands inside the idea. What it adds to 'Go deeper': (1) two bittuls, not one: setting yourself aside versus finding you were never apart (the one who stands before the King always, 'like a silent stone'), and each bittul is a 'something' before the next. (2) The soul's core is under even its strongest will and pleasure (the miser who gives freely at his only son's wedding), and the yechida is already embraced with G-d, so that self-sacrifice still means 'someone is there'. (3) The yechida shows in the body before the mind: sudden clapping, a plain scream, a simple tune, the mother's hug; and it runs backward: 'the voice wakes the intention' even when you feel nothing. (4) Every ascent is for descent: the mute sheaf, the baby hidden to be born with its own eyes, 'thirst that reaches water and does not drink is not thirst', 'against your will you live'. (5) The highest lives only in the lowest: the seal shows in dark wax, His simple will rests in parchment, His essential delight is reached only by the finished deed ('very good'), and the plain still yes of the yoke rises above love, like dust from which everything grows. (6) Teshuva and joy: joy from opposite to opposite, 'nishkeit' lowliness in the marketplace, the sudden leap of Pesach that must become the step-by-step counting.

## Depths

- **MR2:MR2-D1 The bittul that was never a 'something'** (extends D2 (sharpens it: the deeper stillness is not 'self-loss' but never having been apart)): There is a quieter depth than letting go of yourself: finding that you never stood apart from Him. One person comes in to the king and bows. Another stands before the king always and does not feel himself at all. | Inside: A stone standing before the king; the person who does not feel himself; the bow pulled back toward oneself so the arrow flies high. | Lands: In ordinary work: Joseph at his accounts. Not in a special hour. | Q: ביטול העצמי דבחי' אין ומה עצמו שנק' ביטול בעצם ולא שנעשה בטל מן הישות כי לא היה בבחי' יש כלל מעולם — Not that he became nullified from being something, for he was never a something at all. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:1570)
- **MR2:MR2-D2 Under even your strongest wanting** (extends D8 (goes under 'what you cannot let go': even the deepest will is a power, not the self)): The soul's core is deeper than even its deepest will and pleasure. Will and pleasure are its powers. The self is a simple light that can turn its own nature around in one moment. | Inside: A miser turned generous at his son's wedding; pain taken as pleasure; loving an enemy. | Lands: An act against your own nature: a generous gift, forgiving someone. | Q: אין הנפש בבחי' מציאות דמזלא בבחי' מהות רצון או עונג רק כמו אור היולי ופשוט — The soul is not a will or a pleasure; it is like a simple light. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:3305)
- **MR2:MR2-D3 Already embraced** (extends D8 (the bond at the bottom is already an embrace; corrects any idea that the bottom must be reached by sacrifice)): In every Jewish soul the yechida is embraced with G-d always, only hidden. A soul that knows it needs no self-sacrifice; giving yourself up still means someone is there to be given up. | Inside: 'Praise G-d, my soul'; a spark drawn into a flame without choosing; a father delighting in his son's faithfulness. | Lands: Deeds and tests reveal it; the Father delights. | Q: בשרש נש"י בכ"א ביחידה שבו חבוקה ודבוקה בעצמיות אלקות בהתקשרות עצמי תמיד רק שהוא בהעלם גדול — Embraced and joined with G-d in an essential bond always, only very hidden. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:5192)
- **MR2:MR2-D4 Knowing the core by what it cannot bear** (extends D5 (extends 'where knowing ends')): We know a person's essence better by what he cannot bear than by what he loves. So too, knowing that we do not know G-d is real knowledge. | Inside: The 'mah pishpashta' of a mind that has gone past its grasp. | Lands: Keep one 'do not' today, for His sake. | Q: אחר שיודע מה שאינו יודע — He knows what he does not know. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:5359)
- **MR2:MR2-D5 The sudden whole-self: hands, scream, simple song** (extends new (a body-route to the yechida); joins D6): The yechida shows in the body: sudden clapping at great news, a plain scream in danger, a simple tune that comes out by itself. And it works backwards: raise your voice, even without feeling, and it can wake the core. | Inside: Clapping, dancing, wringing hands; a scream; a father's kiss; a mother hugging her baby. | Lands: In hands, feet, throat. Out loud. | Q: כי הרי מיד שיבא השמחה בלבו פתאום תומ"י יתנענעו הידים בסיפוק ממילא ומאליהן או בריקוד ברגל — The hands clap by themselves. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:2581)
- **MR2:MR2-D6 Untouched by what you did** (extends D6 (sharpens it for someone weighed down by guilt)): No wrong a person did reaches the yechida. 'You are all beautiful, my beloved' even if she limps. | Inside: A one-day-old baby; even light-headed people breaking their hearts without knowing why. | Lands: The first small mitzvah, in order. | Q: כולך יפה רעייתי כו' אע"פ שהיא חיגרת או סומא — You are all beautiful ... even though she is lame or blind. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:2584)
- **MR2:MR2-D7 Thirst that drinks** (extends D9 (gives the turn its reason: unturned longing is not real longing)): The soul's run toward G-d is natural, but He wants the return. A thirst that reaches water and does not drink is not real thirst. | Inside: Thirst and cold water; holding a king's garment. | Lands: Learning after prayer; a mitzvah after longing. | Q: כמי שאינו חפץ רק להיות בצמאון וכשמגיע למים אינו שותה שאינו צמאון אמיתי כלל — When he reaches water he does not drink; that is not real thirst. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:4816)
- **MR2:MR2-D8 The highest lives only in the lowest** (extends D12, D13): What is very high can only show itself in what is lowest, like a seal that shows its letters in dark wax, not in the bright gem. His simple will rests in physical parchment. | Inside: Wax and seal; an adult lowering his hands below a child to lift him. | Lands: The physical mitzvah object and act. | Q: כל דבר גב[ו]ה מאד נעלה א"א לו להתגלות כ"א בהיותר נמוך במדריגה — Anything very high can only be revealed in what is lowest. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:2211)
- **MR2:MR2-D9 Joy from the opposite** (extends D9, D14 (for one who fell)): The greatest joy comes from opposite to opposite. G-d's delight in the low and hard is like a weak princess revived by burnt scrapings. | Inside: From death to life; the burnt scrapings; the princess. | Lands: A hard mitzvah done with joy. | Q: עיקר השמחה כשבאה מהיפוך להיפוך כמו ממות לחיים ומאפילה לאור גדול — The main joy is when it comes from opposite to opposite. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:4455)
- **MR2:MR2-D10 Changing a will at its root** (extends new): To change a desire, don't fight it on its level. Let it rise into the core of the self, and it comes back down in new 'letters'. | Inside: Raising the staff before stretching it over the sea. | Lands: A new act. | Q: צריך שיתבטל ויעלה הרצון או השכל הקדום למעלה לבחי' יחידה שבנפש ואז יורד הרצון ויבא בגילוי באופן אחר — The old will must rise to the yechida, then comes down revealed another way. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:2831)
- **MR2:MR2-D11 Nishkeit: not being by yourself** (extends D2 (guard: lowliness is not self-hatred)): A lowliness deeper than guilt: simply not being anyone in your own eyes. Not from bitterness. It breaks pride in the marketplace. | Inside: The ground everyone walks on; Pharaoh falling before Moshe. | Lands: In the market, at work. | Q: בל"א נישקייט בעצם ער איז ג ר נישט באיי זיך בתכלית — 'Nishkeit' in essence: he is not by himself at all. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:4459)
- **MR2:MR2-D12 The many are the One's strength** (extends D10): Many things do not mean separation. The greater the oneness, the more it branches. | Inside: River from Eden; a lump cut into pieces. | Lands: Looking at the world. | Q: לפי עוצם כח האח"פ כך יוגדל כח התחלקות שלו — According to the strength of the oneness, so great its power to divide. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Toras Chaim (Lubavitch, c. 1820-1827).txt:1551)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- MR2:MR2-C01 [You have learned to let yourself go quiet before G-d. The text asks: is that the deepest giving-way there is?] (M2) There are two kinds of giving yourself up. In one, you are something, and you set yourself aside. In the other, you never stood apart as something in the first place. → (M9) Picture two people and a king. One walks in and bows; he stops being important for that moment. The other stands before the king all the time; he does not feel himself at all, and stands there like a quiet stone. → (M15) So the deeper bittul does not look dramatic. Joseph sat over account books and was not separated for a moment. The quiet is in the person, not in a special hour. → (M7) How does one get there? Not by thinking more, but by holding on. The text uses the Yiddish word for sticking (klepn): the deep learner sticks to the subject with his whole will, and that is how Rabbi Akiva, who started unlearned, became a vessel for all of Torah. → (M9) Real depth of mind works the same way. The deep thinker climbs until he goes blank and empty; it feels as if his mind has stopped. Right then a new, deeper idea comes. → (M10) And the lower you go, the higher you are sent. A broken heart that comes from plain, essential lowliness, without drama, is like a bow drawn back: the further back toward yourself, the higher the arrow flies. → (M5) Even this has no end. A man who bows to an officer must forget that bow when he stands before the general; that first bow is now 'something' compared to the second. Before the king he forgets both. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bittul that was never a 'something': not losing a self, but finding you never stood apart. ⇒ RETURN: Back to the account books: the person standing before the King always is the one who can sit at his work and not be separated. - MR2:MR2-C02 [Why are the brothers' acts of gathering G-d's sparks called 'binding sheaves'?] (M13) In a field each stalk of wheat drew its food from the ground on its own. At harvest you tie them into one sheaf, and now they are one body. That is what a life of mitzvos and prayer does: it makes oneness out of scattering. → (M13) When Jews pray with their whole selves, the many become one, like at Sinai 'as one man', and like the words 'bless us, our Father, all of us as one'. → (M9) But the word for sheaf also means 'mute'. Why? Think of a teacher working out a hard idea for himself. His lips move silently; for that moment he cannot speak at all. His speech is held inside his thinking. → (M8) That is what rising into your source feels like. You don't talk; you are held, like a baby inside its mother. That is why the Amidah opens, 'G-d, open my lips': on our own we are mute. → (M15) But the silence is not the goal. A baby is hidden nine months so it can be born with eyes and ears of its own. Every going-up is for a coming-down with more light. → (M5) Even exile is this kind of hiddenness, a pregnancy. And there is no last stage: even in the World to Come the righteous 'have no rest', going from strength to strength. ⇒ BOTTOM: Silent inclusion in the Source, like a child in the womb. ⇒ RETURN: Birth: the ascent exists to come back down with eyes of one's own. - MR2:MR2-C03 [In the dream, the sheaves bow to Joseph. What is a bow?] (M2) There are two bows. A servant falls at his master's feet. And a king bends his head down toward the one asking him for something. → (M9) In the Amidah we bow at 'Blessed'. That is our bow: we let go of our own existence before the living G-d. → (M12) And that very bow makes the King bend down to us. They look like opposites, but they are one act: His bending only comes through our bowing. → (M10) A seed of wheat must rot in the ground before it can grow many seeds. The giving-way comes first; the gift grows from it. ⇒ BOTTOM: Our bow and His bending are one act. ⇒ RETURN: Bow at 'Baruch', straighten at the Name: you stand up again carrying what He gave. - MR2:MR2-C04 [Which came first, heaven or earth? The sages argue. The text uses the argument to ask: what is the point of all G-d's giving?] (M15) A kindness that stays in your heart and your words, and never becomes an act, is not called kindness at all. → (M12) Will and deed need each other. A will with no deed is nothing; a deed with no will is nothing. The foot carries out what the head wants, and the head's wish is only complete through the foot. → (M9) A giver's joy depends on the gift being received. A kind man gives to a poor man and is happy only when he sees it really helps him. The cow wants to nurse more than the calf wants to drink. → (M4) But the receiver does not create the goodness. A truly good person (a 'guter') gives his whole self because that is his nature; the receiver only brings it out. → (M5) Now the deepest point: G-d's own delight was not reached until the work was finished and He saw it was 'very good'. Even though it was already in His hidden will, it did not reach His very self until the end. → (M15) Like a builder who finishes a house, and his whole soul delights in it. The finished, plain, physical thing is what touches His essence. ⇒ BOTTOM: His essential delight is reached only through the finished deed below. ⇒ RETURN: So finish the kindness: the act in the hand is what He was waiting for. - MR2:MR2-C05 [Why was Solomon's Temple built of stone, and why will the stone the builders rejected become the cornerstone?] (M1) Only earth makes something truly new. Water, air and fire don't; out of earth grows everything. The lowest thing has the most power. → (M2) Why? Because it is simple. When wood burns, three parts go and ash is left: the simple element. Because it is simple, everything can come from it. → (M7) Our service has the same layers. Love grows and spreads like a plant. Plain fear and acceptance of the yoke is like a stone: it doesn't spread, it just stays. → (M4) And yet in the end the plain acceptance rises above the love. Why? Because it is rooted in the highest fear: the one who suddenly comes before the king and is struck still like a stone, unable to speak. → (M15) That is why at Sinai saying 'we will do' was enough to bring the highest bittul. The plainest yes reaches the highest place. ⇒ BOTTOM: The plain, still yes of the yoke, rooted in the stillness before the King. ⇒ RETURN: Back to stone: build with what is plain. - MR2:MR2-C06 [Torah is called truth. Why then do the sages say 'the end of wisdom is teshuva'?] (M2) Teshuva is where light is tested and proven, by pulling it out of darkness. 'No one is as wise as the one who has been tested.' → (M5) There are two kinds of return. A lower one, from below, reaches only a garment of His hiding. A higher one, of people who also keep Torah, comes from above and turns the evil itself into good. → (M10) Rebbi, the greatest Torah scholar of his age, wept when a man earned his world in one hour of return. He envied him. → (M5) This return reaches the place where darkness and light are the same to Him. That is why Yom Kippur can turn even sins into merits. ⇒ BOTTOM: Before the Essence, darkness and light are equal; so even the dark can become light. ⇒ RETURN: Vidui: the plain words 'we have sinned' wake the root. - MR2:MR2-C07 [What happens when something touches the very essence of the soul, the yechida? The text studies it in the body.] (M9) When someone hears wonderful news that touches the core of who they are, their hands clap by themselves, at once, before any decision. → (M7) No thought or will moves the hands then. The joy is too big for mind and heart to hold, so it jumps straight to the hands, or to dancing feet. Grief does the same: the mourner wrings his hands. → (M5) Prayer has the same two levels. One goes step by step, mind then heart. The other is a stirring of the whole self that comes all at once, past even the longing to melt into G-d. In it even light-headed people break their hearts truly, without knowing where it came from. → (M11) This point is never damaged by what a person did wrong. 'You are all beautiful, My beloved', even if she limps or is blind. → (M9) The voice is the same. When something touches a person's very life, he screams a plain scream, suddenly, without choosing. And when joy reaches the essence, a simple tune comes out by itself. → (M15) And it works the other way too. 'The voice wakes the intention.' Even if you feel nothing at all, start to say the words out loud, even just on the outside, and it can wake the depths of the soul. ⇒ BOTTOM: The yechida: a whole-self stirring, above order, untouched by sin. ⇒ RETURN: It lands in the hands, the feet, the throat. Raise your voice. - MR2:MR2-C08 [Bread teaches a baby to say 'Abba'. Matza has no taste. How can it be the 'bread of faith'?] (M4) The lack of taste is exactly its greatness. Taste belongs to understanding. Matza is above taste. → (M9) It is like a small child who knows his father and runs after him calling 'Abba', with no reasons. That is simple faith. Matza is only flour and water, as they are; nothing puffed up. → (M2) Pesach is a leap. A person who has been tied to every bad desire can, suddenly, have a thought of return from the depth of his heart. → (M10) Then everything moves at once, heart and mind together, with no order. The reasons are forgotten. He becomes new, like a baby born today. A small thought, like 'G-d made the world' or 'He was kind to me', moves him more than a great thinker is moved by deep ideas. He becomes new, like a baby born today. → (M15) But it does not last in that form. When the first strong stirring runs out, he must serve step by step. That is the counting of the Omer after Pesach: one day, one trait at a time. ⇒ BOTTOM: The leap from the essence, clothed in plain faith. ⇒ RETURN: Count: one day, one trait, in order. - MR2:MR2-C09 [If loving G-d 'with all your might' means a love with no limit and no stop, how can a person ever stop praying?] (M3) 'Infinite' is said only of light. You can say the sun's rays spread without end; you cannot say that of the sun itself. G-d Himself is above even 'infinite'. → (M5) 'With all your might' is like gold, above the silver of love. It is a stirring so total that a person cannot rest. There is no love in it and no will, only being shaken, and he does not know what it is, like someone who sees a great king. → (M1) So the question: then a person should pray all day! And if he stops to do mitzvos, isn't that a fall? → (M4) The answer: a mitzvah is a limb of the King Himself. When you do it, you are already tied to His very self. The joy comes by itself. → (M9) Like someone already tasting sweet food: he is no longer thirsty for it. The longing in prayer was for something far. The mitzvah is having it. → (M15) So when the burning of prayer stops for the mitzvah, it is not a fall. It is the real rising of that longing. ⇒ BOTTOM: A mitzvah is a limb of His essence. ⇒ RETURN: Stop burning; put on the tefillin with joy. - MR2:MR2-C10 [How can wool threads and leather lift a G-dly soul to His essence?] (M12) G-d is 'first and last'. The first and the last are equally far from Him, the way the sky surrounds the earth equally on top and bottom. → (M10) So He can lift the lowest. A grown-up who lifts a child must first put his hands below the child. → (M2) And the highest thing can only show itself in the lowest. A very deep idea that cannot be explained can only come out through a parable, something completely different from it. → (M10) A seal cut into a brilliant gem: the letters can't be seen for all the brightness. Press it into dark wax and the letters show. The darkness of the wax is what reveals them. → (M5) So His simple will rests in the physical parchment of tefillin, higher than even the Infinite light. → (M15) That is why 'the reward of a mitzvah is the mitzvah'. The reward is the act itself. ⇒ BOTTOM: First and last are equally far; so the lowest can hold the highest. ⇒ RETURN: The parchment, the threads, the act. - MR2:MR2-C11 [What is the 'point of the heart', and how is it reached?] (M7) One man earns money by his brains, another by plowing. Both are only the clothes of what he really wants: the profit. → (M8) And under that, he just wants to live. 'Let me make a living, whatever way.' That wanting sits in the point of the heart, above mind and work. → (M7) In serving G-d too, thinking about His greatness is the clothing. Under it is a simple wanting toward G-d Himself. → (M14) That a person of flesh can have a heart-point aimed at G-d Himself is a wonder. It was given at the splitting of the sea, to be fixed in Jewish souls for all generations. → (M15) And the joy in a mitzvah after prayer grows as much as the thirst. 'Cold water to a tired soul.' ⇒ BOTTOM: The point of the heart: wanting Him, not His gifts. ⇒ RETURN: Joy in the mitzvah measured by the thirst before. - MR2:MR2-C12 [Why did Moshe have to raise the staff before he stretched it over the sea?] (M5) To change what is, you must go above the place where both the old and the new have their root. → (M9) Our own will works this way. When a will or a thought shows up in the mind, the text calls it 'letters', from the word for arriving: it has come home from far away, from the core of the soul. → (M15) So to really change a desire or an opinion, you don't fight it on its own level. You let the old will go up, dissolve into the core of the self, the yechida. → (M15) Only then 'stretch out your hand over the sea': come back down, in measure, into the real situation, and act in the new way. The raising is not the end; the stretching over the actual water is. ⇒ BOTTOM: The old will dissolved in the yechida. ⇒ RETURN: Stretch out the hand: act from the new will. - MR2:MR2-C13 [The soul's core is called yechida, 'single', because it leans no way. But it holds love and fear at full strength. How is that single?] (M12) At the core, love and fear are not two. Wanting to live and not wanting to die are one thing in you. → (M2) This simple core is where simple faith lives. From it comes the strength to give up one's life, and the power of even a wicked person to turn completely around. → (M5) But the soul itself is deeper than even its strongest will and pleasure. Will and pleasure are its powers, not its self. The self is a simple light. → (M9) The proof: a person can take pain as pleasure, and love his enemy. Think of a miser who would die over a penny. → (M10) At his only son's wedding he becomes a different man and spends freely on everyone. Joy lifted him to the core, which is above his nature. → (M15) His mind, his heart, his whole nature can flip in one moment. That is how deep the self is under its habits. ⇒ BOTTOM: The self is under even its deepest wants. ⇒ RETURN: It shows in an act against your nature: the generous spending, the forgiven enemy. - MR2:MR2-C14 [What does contemplation (hisbonenus) actually do, and where should it end?] (M17) The G-dly soul must go deep, at length, until the idea is close even to a human mind. → (M10) Contemplation is not a reason that forces a feeling. It is seeing. A son who thinks about his father far away is moved; seeing him is more. → (M6) Then the soul runs toward its Source by itself, because it is a piece of G-d. Turn your heart to Him, not His traits. → (M4) But the run upward is not what He most wants. 'Against your will you live.' In Torah and mitzvos you take hold of the King by His clothing. → (M15) So after prayer comes learning: 'All who are thirsty, come to the water.' → (M10) If you are thirsty and reach water but don't drink, it was never real thirst. ⇒ BOTTOM: The soul's own run to its Source, then the return. ⇒ RETURN: Learn after praying; drink. - MR2:MR2-C15 [In the Shema we give our life. Is that giving born from understanding, or deeper?] (M2) Understanding G-d's oneness can give birth to a will so deep it would give up life. → (M10) Like a child who wants something beyond his reasons because a great teacher explained it; the small vessel overflows. → (M4) But under it is a bond that is always there, hidden: every Jew's yechida is embraced with G-d. → (M5) A soul that knows this bond needs no self-sacrifice at all; it is already embraced. → (M4) Even self-sacrifice is still 'something nullifying itself': someone is still there. → (M15) Yet Rabbi Akiva longed for the deed: 'when will it come to my hand to fulfill it?' What is already true inside becomes real in the act. → (M14) Tests add nothing new. They are for the Father's delight in seeing His child hold on. ⇒ BOTTOM: The always-there, hidden embrace of the yechida. ⇒ RETURN: The deed reveals it; the Father delights. - MR2:MR2-C16 [One who has sunk the deepest: what can lift him? And how can teshuva (bitterness) and mitzvos (joy) go together?] (M11) Despair is the danger: sadness turned into giving up leads to worse falls. → (M5) Only a cry from the very depths, a pain that reaches the essence, lifts one who fell deepest. → (M12) Then the joy that comes after is the greatest, because it comes from the opposite, like from death to life. → (M16) When he comes to a mitzvah, he thinks: through this G-d comes close to my lowly soul and shines even in my darkness. → (M7) So he is happy in the mitzvah, not in himself. A tzaddik may enjoy his own closeness to G-d; the one who returns has nothing of his own to enjoy, so his joy is all G-d's. → (M5) There is a deeper lowliness: not from a bitter self-accounting but from simply not being anyone in his own eyes, 'nishkeit'. → (M15) This plain, essential nothingness is what breaks hardness and pride during the day, walking in the market, outside the hour of prayer. Pharaoh fell before Moshe for this reason. ⇒ BOTTOM: Essential lowliness: not being by yourself. ⇒ RETURN: The marketplace and the mitzvah. - MR2:MR2-C17 [The sages give a strange parable: a king's daughter who smelled the scrapings of the pot. Why would G-d want service that is hard and against our nature?] (M3) There are two kinds of pleasure. One is plain sweetness, good in itself. The other is a pleasure mixed with its opposite. → (M10) A princess used to fine food sometimes grows weak and cannot enjoy it. Then the sharp smell of the burnt bits stuck to the bottom of the pot revives her more than any delicacy. It is beneath her, so it is brought to her quietly. → (M11) The scrapings are the lowest leftovers of G-d's flow: the plain, hard, physical world. And G-d's delight is in exactly that. → (M15) So joy can live even in a mitzvah done against your whole nature, when the work is very hard for you. That joy reaches higher than the joy of a mitzvah that comes easily. ⇒ BOTTOM: His deepest pleasure is in what came from its opposite. ⇒ RETURN: Do the hard mitzvah; the joy in it is real. - MR2:MR2-C18 [How can G-d, who is above all place, live in a tent of wood and cloth?] (M4) When the texts say G-d 'surrounds' the worlds, it does not mean He is far above. He is here below exactly as above, hidden inside every detail. → (M10) A name shows how. A name is not the person, but call him by name and his whole self turns to you, even moves from place to place. → (M1) Now the kasha: if before Him above and below are exactly equal, how can He 'dwell' in one special place? → (M12) The answer turns the kasha around. Because heaven and earth are both nothing to Him, He can choose to rest His very self in the lowest place. → (M15) That is why a soul in a body, learning and doing good, gains more than it had in Gan Eden. ⇒ BOTTOM: Above and below are equal before Him; so He can rest in the lowest. ⇒ RETURN: Your body, this room, this deed: His chosen home. - MR2:MR2-C19 [Why would 'do not' commandments be higher than 'do' commandments? They only stop something; they show nothing.] (M1) The kasha: next to all of holiness, the forces of evil are like a lizard in the king's palace. Why should holding back from them be so high? → (M5) There are two ways to know. One is to grasp. The other is to know that you cannot grasp, and that too is knowledge. Before you reach it, you think you understand. → (M9) Here is how you know a person's very self: by what he absolutely cannot bear, more than by what he loves. → (M4) What he loves shows one small part. What he cannot stand shows his core. → (M15) So keeping G-d's 'do nots' touches His essence more than the 'do's, which reveal only His will. ⇒ BOTTOM: Knowing by negation: you touch the core by what it cannot bear. ⇒ RETURN: Hold back from one thing today, for His sake. - MR2:MR2-C20 [If G-d is one, why is the world so many?] (M1) The worlds do not come from G-d like an effect from a cause, because a cause has some likeness to its effect, and nothing is like Him. → (M9) Look in yourself. When you want to build a house, the finished house is already in your wanting, all at once. → (M8) And the will itself, when it rises into the soul's core, stops being felt as 'a will' at all. → (M2) A simple thing does not divide. It divides only when it goes out and mixes with other things. → (M12) And the greater the oneness, the more it can branch. Solomon's great wisdom came out in three thousand parables. The many show the strength of the One. → (M15) Cut a lump into many pieces: no piece is a new thing. And the four rivers would dry up if the one river stopped for a moment. ⇒ BOTTOM: The many show the One's strength. ⇒ RETURN: Look at the many things in front of you as one river's flow. - MR2:MR2-C21 [Prayer is in the heart. Why must the body bend?] (M15) Bowing at Modim, bending the spine, looks like the most outward thing. Yet it is what readies the bones for resurrection. → (M5) This body bow is higher than the bow at 'Baruch', which comes from understanding. Rav Sheshes thanked his head for bowing on its own. → (M10) Even Moshe, whose body did not hide G-d from him, fell to the ground in prayer. → (M5) And in the time to come the body itself, as a solid 'something', will receive life from G-d with no bittul at all. ⇒ BOTTOM: The plain body, bowed, carries the deepest bittul. ⇒ RETURN: Bow at Modim with the spine. - MR2:MR2-C22 [Can opposites that are strong stay strong and still be one?] (M4) Our feelings are rooted deeper than our thoughts. The mind can rule the heart only because the mind is humble enough to let G-d's light in. → (M5) The strong traits of Tohu stood closer to G-d's essence than the gentle traits of Tikkun. → (M12) So they stay fully strong and are also included in each other. That looks like a contradiction. → (M10) Two students: one forbids, one permits. When they stand before their teacher, whose mind is far above theirs, both are folded into one. → (M15) Yet Tikkun has an advantage: it builds 'a person', a whole working life. ⇒ BOTTOM: Opposites held as one by a light above both. ⇒ RETURN: Bring the conflict before the Teacher. - MR2:MR2-C23 [How are the forces that hide G-d removed? By fighting them?] (M5) The greater a person truly is, the lower he can stoop. A great sage explains to a child better than a small sage, because to him the child's small mind is no threat. → (M10) Students far down the line can be confused by any question. But when they all hear the idea again from the teacher himself, the confusions fall away by themselves. → (M4) So evil does not have to be beaten in a war. When His goodness is revealed, it melts like wax near fire. The fire does nothing to the wax. → (M15) That is why at the Exodus it was 'I, and not an angel'. Only He Himself, not any messenger, makes the darkness vanish just by being there. ⇒ BOTTOM: Revelation alone dissolves concealment. ⇒ RETURN: Instead of fighting a dark mood, turn to Him; let it melt.

## MR3 — the Mitteler Rebbe's maamarim

The Mitteler Rebbe adds four things to the full depth. (1) The deepest point is a PLACE and a REST, not an experience: the yechida rests in the four cubits, in plain bodily Shabbos rest, where great and simple are equal; the true Being rests only in plain being. (2) The bottom is a CIRCLE: pushed or lowered to the bottom, you are at the top; stepping back from the King is nearer than nearness; a son's cry from afar turns the Father's face; the treasure of the heart opens only when one feels 'as dead'. (3) He gives exact TESTS against fake depth: the wish 'to feel that I live' is the root that holds desire and even spiritual fervor; pain at a loss measures the hold; an insult reveals false humility; real nothingness is 'not made'. The soul's true life is the feeling of not-being. (4) The RETURN is concrete: He holds the world by calling its name and we call His Name; exile is His sleep, and deed and speech (not intention alone) wake Him; joy 'only because it is His will' reaches the Owner of the will; prayer (Yiddish 5587) runs from 'no one there to want', through falling on the face like a dead man, to being lifted by Him and carrying the King's print into the day. Faith from far, and the child's plain 'Abba', hold more than sight and understanding.

## Depths

- **MR3:MR3-D01 The love inside the blow** (extends D8 (the bond that cannot be let go), seen from His side.): His love for you is not based on anything, so nothing you did and nothing that happened can change it. Even the exile, even the blow, comes from that love. | Inside: A son being rebuked who knows, even while crying, that he is his father's son. | Lands: In the body, in the hard moment itself; then in the deed of keeping the mitzvah anyway. | Q: גם בשעת ההכאה האהבה עצמיו' לא ניזז מאתו — Even at the time of the blow, the essential love has not moved away from him. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Maamarei Admur HaEmtzai (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:268)
- **MR3:MR3-D02 The farthest place holds the deepest point** (extends D12 and D13 (the deed and plainness), sharpened: the place, not only the act.): Your deepest point does not live in your mind or heart. It rests in your four cubits, in plain rest, in the most solid 'something'. The true Being rests only in plain being. | Inside: Sitting still at the Shabbos table, with nothing to achieve, equal with everyone. | Lands: In the body at rest; in 'let no man leave his place'. | Q: כ"א דייקא בד' אמות הרחוקים בתכלית בריחוק אשר אין ריחוק יותר ממנו שם דוקא ישכון הארת בחי' יחידה — Only in the four cubits, the farthest of all, there the light of the yechida rests. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Maamarei Admur HaEmtzai (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:1598)
- **MR3:MR3-D03 Called by name** (extends D11 (the self as the one addressed): it is mutual; He turns when called.): He holds the world by calling it, the way a name turns a whole person. And we reach Him the same way: by calling His Name. | Inside: Turning toward someone who calls you; a cup lifted by its handle. | Lands: In speech: the Name said in each blessing. | Q: כי יותר בקל יפנה ע"י קריאת שמו משיאחזנו בגופו — He turns more easily by being called by his name than if someone took hold of his body. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Maamarei Admur HaEmtzai (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:2389)
- **MR3:MR3-D04 His sleep** (extends new): G-d's silence in exile is not absence. It is His withdrawal into His own Essence, like sleep, where the whole world is like a fly on a sleeper's coat. What wakes Him toward us is the deed and the spoken word. | Inside: A sleeper who does not feel the fly; then 'Hashem awoke like a sleeper.' | Lands: In the deed and speech, not intention alone. | Q: וזהו עיקר ענין הגלות לפי שבזמן הגלות הוא בחי' שינה לפני המקום — In the time of exile it is a state of sleep before the Omnipresent. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Maamarei Admur HaEmtzai (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:2416)
- **MR3:MR3-D05 The bottom of the circle is its top** (extends D9 (the bottom sends you back), sharpened: the bottom is not left behind; it is the top.): In a circle there is no up or down. When you are pushed, or lower yourself, all the way to the bottom, you are level with the highest point. Real lowliness has no measure, because it is simple. | Inside: A servant walking behind his master; an empty cup. | Lands: In the body: heaviness of head before prayer; then walking after Him all day. | Q: שאין בעיגול מצד עצמו בחי' מעלה ומטה כלל — A circle in itself has no up and down at all. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Teshuvah (Lubavitch, c. 1817-1826).txt:129)
- **MR3:MR3-D06 Faith from far away** (extends D7 (simple faith), deepened: faith is stronger than seeing, not a substitute for it.): A spark near a flame is pulled to it; no wonder. Faith pulls the soul from far away, as from Egypt. That is a deeper bond than seeing, and it holds everything up when all the light of love and awe has run out. | Inside: Walking after Him in the desert, in a land not sown. | Lands: In plain faithfulness on dry days; in eating matzah, bread without taste. | Q: אין זה חידוש כלל בהיות הניצוץ קרוב ורואה בעין שיומשך — It is no wonder that the spark, when close and seeing, is drawn. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Ner Mitzvah VeTorah Or (Lubavitch, c. 1820).txt:31)
- **MR3:MR3-D07 The child's 'Abba'** (extends D13 (plainness) and D7 (simple faith).): A small child's word 'Abba' holds more of the truth of his father than a grown man's understanding, the way a short law for a child holds all of the Talmud. Smallness is not lower. | Inside: A child asking: 'Abba, give me bread.' | Lands: In speech: 'Avinu Malkeinu', said simply. | Q: ומבקש לחם ומים אבא האכילני לחם — 'Abba, feed me bread.' (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Ner Mitzvah VeTorah Or (Lubavitch, c. 1820).txt:43)
- **MR3:MR3-D08 The wish to feel myself, and the life that is no-life** (extends D2 (no one left to watch), sharpened: names the hidden motive that keeps the watcher.): What holds a person is not desire but 'wanting to feel that I live'. Even in holiness this can creep in. The deepest life of the G-dly soul is the opposite: the feeling of not being, which is itself its life. | Inside: The red heifer burned to ash; a garment that shows the garment is gone. | Lands: In plain deeds after deep regret, done without self-watching. | Q: וראש דדעת דעמלק הוא בחי' תאות ותשוקת עצמיות הנפש להרגיש עצמותה — The soul's craving to feel its own self. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Maamarei Admur HaEmtzai (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:1849)
- **MR3:MR3-D09 Distance nearer than nearness** (extends D9 and D14 (the turn and no bottom).): Sometimes the most real moment is stepping back. When the King Himself shines, you cannot stand and you back away; each step back is a deeper giving up. And the son's bitter cry from far away is what turns the father's face. | Inside: Standing far off at Sinai; a son crying from a distance. | Lands: In tears and plain words, then the return to deeds. | Q: עד שכל פסיעה ופסיעה בפרט שנסוג אחור מורה ביטול העצמות ביותר — Each step that he retreats shows a greater nullification. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Teshuvah (Lubavitch, c. 1817-1826).txt:201)
- **MR3:MR3-D10 Pain as the test of what I still hold** (extends D8 (what you cannot let go), turned into a diagnostic.): Overcoming a desire in prayer does not remove it; it comes back. The honest test of what you still hold is how much its loss hurts. | Inside: Honey on a radish: sweet until the honey is gone. | Lands: In the day, when something is taken; bless on it. | Q: אבל מיד כאשר יסתלק אור וכח הרצון האלקי אז כח הרצון הזר חוזר וניעור כמו שהיה תחלה — The foreign will wakes again as before. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Teshuvah (Lubavitch, c. 1817-1826).txt:303)
- **MR3:MR3-D11 Joy only because it is His will** (extends D12 (the deed in the hand), with the key of joy.): The joy of doing a mitzvah 'only because I did His will', without needing to understand it, reaches past every screen to the One whose will it is. Joy breaks walls, like a king who leaves his palace at his feast. | Inside: A king in the street at his feast, freeing prisoners. | Lands: In the deed, with gladness. | Q: וכידוע בענין שמחה ש"מ שאינה רק מפני שעשה את רצונו ולא מצד בחי' ההשגה והטעם — Joy of a mitzvah is only because he did His will, not because of grasping the reason. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Maamarei Admur HaEmtzai (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:1107)
- **MR3:MR3-D12 The treasure that opens when you are 'dead'** (extends D8 (the bond), found from the bottom.): The heart's deepest point does not show in good prayer. It opens when you have fallen so far you feel cut off; then a will not to be cut off rises. The long exile is for this. | Inside: A man emptying his whole treasury to save his life. | Lands: In the moment of greatest distance, then deeds. | Q: עד שנפסק חבל התקשרות נשמתו וכמת יחשב אז יתמרמר ביותר — Until the cord is cut and he is counted as dead; then he becomes very bitter. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Sha'arei Teshuvah (Lubavitch, c. 1817-1826).txt:447)
- **MR3:MR3-D13 The real and the fake fervor** (extends new (a guardrail for the descent)): There is a true being-moved, which every Jew has, and a fake heat that 'serves oneself'. The test is not the high moment but the insult afterward and the day after. | Inside: Raw meat and cooked meat; blood heating versus a heart that has heard. | Lands: In the day after prayer. | Q: וזהו הנק' עובד א"ע — This is called serving oneself. (Chassidus-txt/R. Dovber Schneuri (Mitteler Rebbe) (1773-1827)/Maamarei Admur HaEmtzai (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:1655)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- MR3:MR3-C01 [G-d is good. But is goodness His nature, the way giving life is the soul's nature?] (M10) His goodness is not a nature He is stuck with. He is good because He can do anything, and so He can also be good. → (M5) Because His kindness comes from no limit, it has no limit either: it reaches the good and the bad equally. → (M8) In every Jew there is a point of Jewishness that no sin touches. Even someone who broke everything can come back from there. → (M4) That deepest point does not live in your head or your heart. It rests in your four cubits, the space around you, the place farthest from thought. → (M12) The rule behind it: the true Being rests only in plain, solid being. The most hidden place is the only place that can hold Him as He is. → (M15) So Shabbos rest of the body, plain rest, is where the yechida rests. There the great tzaddik and the simplest person are exactly equal. ⇒ BOTTOM: The deepest point of the person is not an experience but a place of rest: the plain body resting in His day. ⇒ RETURN: Down into Shabbos: stop working on yourself; sit, eat, rest; 'let no man leave his place' (1599). The rest itself is 'from You, from Your very Self' (1600), and it is where one would give his life for G-d. - MR3:MR3-C02 [Where does a new insight come from?] (M9) Notice: the delight of a new idea comes after the idea. Yet the delight is what produced it. → (M6) Every small delight you feel is a ray falling from a bottomless delight that you do not feel. → (M5) In the Essence itself there is no higher and lower at all. Head and end are equal. → (M3) To make a world of steps, He 'stepped back', the way a craftsman lets go of the whole picture in order to work on one part. → (M10) And the higher a thing is, the more it can only show itself through its outermost edge. → (M15) That is why the highest things come down lowest: the Torah is in physical matters, and the clouds of glory took the stones and arrows. ⇒ BOTTOM: The source of everything, delight itself, never shows; it shows only as the latest and lowest thing. ⇒ RETURN: So the outer and plain things in front of you (the deed, the physical Torah, the edge of a feeling) are where the highest has come down. Do not wait for the inside to shine first. - MR3:MR3-C03 [In exile we mostly just keep going, without feeling. Is that worth anything?] (M9) Love that comes from understanding fades; the doing goes on, carried by a small ray of the old love. → (M2) That is our state in exile: the 'small' state, just walking after G-d by doing, with mind and heart hidden inside the deed. → (M15) Bowing only your head seems like nothing. But bowing the head leads to lying flat on the ground, the real giving up of self. → (M10) When the teacher is beyond you completely, you stop measuring yourself at all. That bittul is deeper than the bittul of someone who understands. → (M10) A wheel has no top. What is up goes down and what is down comes up, and that is how the deepest surrender looks. → (M7) And the same part that rises is the part that comes back down, like a flame leaping from a coal and returning. → (M15) So the hidden glory itself will be seen below, 'below exactly as above'. ⇒ BOTTOM: The bottom is the wheel: a surrender with no head and foot, carried by the plain deed when feeling is gone. ⇒ RETURN: Back to the day: keep walking after Him in the small state; bow the head even without feeling; the deed carries the hidden love. - MR3:MR3-C04 [How does the Infinite reach a world that is nothing next to Him?] (M3) Ordinary causes make things that then stand on their own: cut the apple off and it stays an apple. → (M9) But G-d holds the world the way you hold a person by calling his name. → (M10) A name turns a person more easily than grabbing his body. → (M10) A name is like the handle of a cup: not the cup, but how it is picked up and brought to the mouth. → (M12) So the world is both: given up into Him and still really there, because He keeps calling. → (M8) The deepest delight is not pleasure in a thing. It is the self's delight in being itself, like a person's love of his own life. → (M14) Above, there is nothing besides Him to enjoy. So His delight is the King's delight in Himself, and the world comes from that. ⇒ BOTTOM: The world exists because He keeps saying its name, and His saying comes from His delight in Himself. ⇒ RETURN: Prayer: in each blessing we call Him by His Name, and 'why does Israel cry out and not get answered? Because they do not call by the Name' (2390). - MR3:MR3-C05 [Why is G-d silent in exile?] (M8) A person can be so absorbed in himself that he does not turn when called. Sleep is the fullest form of this. → (M4) Exile is His sleep. → (M10) A sleeper does not feel a fly walking on his clothes or even on his face. → (M11) His silence is His strength: 'these are His mighty deeds, that nations dance in His palace and He is quiet.' → (M15) What wakes Him toward us is not mainly intention. It is the deed and the spoken word. → (M12) Intention alone can even get in the way of the drawing-down. → (M15) In prayer the two meet: bowing at 'Blessed' is the running up, standing straight at the Name is the coming back down. ⇒ BOTTOM: The bottom is His sleep: a silence that is not absence but depth. ⇒ RETURN: Wake Him with the hand and the mouth: the mitzvah done, the word said. 'And Hashem awoke like a sleeper' (2418). - MR3:MR3-C06 [Why pray at all, if there are 613 commandments?] (M10) Prayer is like the spine: not one of the limbs, yet it holds them all together. → (M1) Prayer can also dry up, the same today as yesterday and as tomorrow. That is a spine drying out. → (M2) Begin from Him alone: there is nothing to want outside Him, and no one there to want. → (M9) That small knowing is like a man who is tied up and still has to do business. → (M16) And it is like a child proud to say 'that is my father' without understanding who his father is. → (M8) Then the songs of praise: like a man with boils going to see a great king. He wants nothing for himself; he only goes to see the king, and loses himself. → (M13) At Shema it all comes together at once, like a long Talmud discussion that suddenly lights up. ⇒ BOTTOM: The bottom of prayer: 'nobody there to want', the Infinite alone as before creation. ⇒ RETURN: See next chain: the return through the Amidah, falling on the face and getting up again. - MR3:MR3-C07 [After 'He alone', what happens to the world and to me?] (M3) There are two kinds of work: tilling and tanning. → (M16) A Jew can feel in his own heart that there is nothing but Him, just as before creation. → (M11) When we forget, the life-force goes silent, like a sheep being sheared. We keep living as if we were on our own. → (M11) And yet the vessels and the hiding are also Him. The hiding itself is G-dliness, for the good of the creatures. → (M8) After the Amidah, fall on your face like one who wants nothing, like a dead man, 'like a lion lying on its prey'. → (M15) But staying down is also a lack. You must be lifted again, and what lifts you is His understanding, not yourself. → (M15) Leaving the king's house, keep the impression of the king himself; it guards you all day. → (M15) Without prayer first, even Torah is not fully Torah. ⇒ BOTTOM: Falling on the face like a dead man, wanting nothing. ⇒ RETURN: Lifted again not by yourself; carry the print of the King into the day; 'today to do them' turns the physical thing into His dwelling. - MR3:MR3-C08 [Who wants the closeness more, G-d or us?] (M9) The giver wants to give more than the receiver wants to receive. → (M10) His love surrounds us like a hug: the hand around the back turns the other person to face you. → (M14) He wanted this world because our souls are rooted in His very Essence. → (M15) So the plain deed in the body moves Him more than love and awe, even love with self-sacrifice. ⇒ BOTTOM: His love comes from outside and turns us to face Him. ⇒ RETURN: The deed in the body is where His Essence is moved most. - MR3:MR3-C09 [Why does the Torah curse serving G-d without joy, even when the service is perfect?] (M9) Joy breaks the walls a person normally lives inside. → (M10) In his joy, the king even sets rebels free from prison. → (M9) Joy reaches the inner self: in joy a person tells secrets he would not tell even his dearest friend. → (M1) The kasha: perfect service with love and awe, but without joy, brings curses. 'This would make anyone's hair stand on end.' → (M2) Joy of a mitzvah is joy 'only because I did His will', not because I understood it. So it reaches the One whose will it is. → (M15) And one who is not happy himself cannot make another happy. ⇒ BOTTOM: Joy for no reason but His will, which reaches past every screen. ⇒ RETURN: Do the mitzvah glad that it is His will. That joy, not the depth of understanding, sweetens judgment below (1109). - MR3:MR3-C10 [What really holds a person to a desire?] (M2) What makes a desire stick is not the desire. It is the wish to feel alive in it. → (M9) That is why two people eat the same food and one is lost if he misses a meal while the other does not mind a fast. → (M5) The head of it all is the soul's craving to feel itself. → (M11) Gehinnom is not a punishment sent from outside. It is your own thoughts, the things you burned for, standing between you and the light. → (M11) So the worries and poverty of this world are a mercy: they make a person forget his pleasures here instead of there. → (M4) For the very root, the wish to feel oneself, there is no cleaning. There is only losing it, a despair until one prefers death to life. → (M10) That is the red heifer burned to ash. ⇒ BOTTOM: The bottom is the end of 'I want to feel myself': ash. ⇒ RETURN: See MR3-C11: from the ash, the deed that shines, and the life that is felt as no-life. - MR3:MR3-C11 [What is the higher Paradise, and why do we need deeds after teshuvah?] (M3) There are two Paradises. One is a ray of the Shechinah, for service felt by a 'me'. The other is a true 'nothing', for one whose only wish was to be included in his Source. → (M10) The sun through windows varies with each window. If the sun's ball itself came in, no window would change it. → (M12) Teshuvah without deeds is light without a vessel, like the sons of Aharon who ran and did not return. → (M12) How can 'nothing' be clothed? Because the feeling of having no life is itself the G-dly soul's life. → (M15) So the deed after that bitterness is a garment that shows that the garment is gone. ⇒ BOTTOM: Feeling oneself as nothing, which is the soul's true life. ⇒ RETURN: Good deeds 'shine' with it; 'not with himself at all' (1861) while doing them. - MR3:MR3-C12 [Can a person know G-d, or only that He exists?] (M10) From our own wisdom we learn only that there is wisdom above, not what it is. Like a blind man told about light. → (M7) Standing outside the palace and thinking about the king makes you humble by measure. Seeing the king himself undoes you. → (M4) Feeling is not lower than thinking. It comes from the soul's essence; the mind is only the pipe. → (M9) In great joy you clap your hands without choosing to. The essence of joy shows in your fingers. → (M8) Six hundred thousand left Egypt with no reason and no understanding. That was the soul's essence showing. → (M15) That is why Kiddush, the 'big' state, comes before matzah, the 'small'. Like a person returning from the depths, who first grasps beyond his level and only then grows step by step. ⇒ BOTTOM: The essence moving without reason, the way 600,000 walked out of Egypt. ⇒ RETURN: Start where you are; the big moment may come first; then go slowly, step by step. - MR3:MR3-C13 [If G-d's kindness turns to judgment, does He change?] (M7) Love that comes from reasons can change; love that is part of you cannot. → (M10) A father who strikes his son: even at the moment of the blow, the essential love has not moved. → (M11) So the destruction and the exile are from His essential love for Israel. ⇒ BOTTOM: A love that does not change because it is not based on anything. ⇒ RETURN: Torah after the argument does not change either: the mitzvah, once clear, is His unchanging will (265-66). - MR3:MR3-C14 [Who is closer: the one who never fell, or the one who came back?] (M9) A person pities his own handiwork like his own burning house, because some of his power is in it. → (M7) Mitzvos are 'the way of Hashem': a road the King walks on Himself. A home holds a person's very self, not just his traits. → (M16) Teshuvah is not only for sins. Anyone who slid into routine and comes back with his whole heart is a returner. → (M11) 'I will find You outside': the Essence is found specifically outside, by the one who returns. → (M10) Rabbi Akiva laughed at Rome's wealth: if the leftovers go to the servants like this, how great is the feast. → (M12) Return without deeds stirs the Essence but does not come down. Deeds without return have nothing to bring down. → (M10) Spiced wine is not good by nature; it was made good, and it is better than wine that was always good. ⇒ BOTTOM: He is found outside. ⇒ RETURN: Turn routine into return; then put it into deeds. - MR3:MR3-C15 [Where does a sudden wish to be close to G-d come from?] (M9) Sometimes a person suddenly wants to be a Jew, not for anything in particular, just to be one. → (M2) Even a man doing very well in business can suddenly be hit by a spirit of return. → (M14) That is what each day is for. 'If not, why should he live this day?' → (M15) The earth is never full of water: never think you have reached the end of the work. → (M10) G-d's 'stepping back' is like a scholar who stopped studying: ask him and he answers at once. ⇒ BOTTOM: A will with no object: simply to be His. ⇒ RETURN: Use the sudden stirring for something concrete today; never think you are done. - MR3:MR3-C16 [Why does contemplation sometimes make me worse, not better?] (M10) A sick man knows food is his life, but it does not go in; it even harms him. → (M8) Every Jew has 'der her', a sense that hears G-dliness and is moved, even when he cannot explain it. → (M2) So before prayer comes falling away from yourself. → (M16) Prayer speaks about low, visible things, so the contemplation will be near you. → (M5) Even then you grasp only that He is, not what He is. So after prayer comes Torah, which is His essence. ⇒ BOTTOM: Lovesick: knowing He is, unable to cleave. ⇒ RETURN: Order of the day: return, prayer, Torah, even a little. - MR3:MR3-C17 [Did my sins damage the real me?] (M10) The soul above is like a person; the soul in the body is like his portrait painted on a board. → (M9) Even a person who sins has things he simply cannot do, like eat bread on Pesach. → (M15) The lower waters cry: 'We want to be before the King too.' ⇒ BOTTOM: The root that nothing reached. ⇒ RETURN: Two self-sacrifices in Shema: the root's, and the soul in the body's 'love with all your heart'. - MR3:MR3-C18 [Is faith less than seeing?] (M1) It seems faith can at most be like seeing; one who sees has no need to believe. → (M1) Faith has no feeling in it at all, and still the simplest Jew will die for it. → (M12) The soul's 'what' is both its essence and its nothingness: it cleaves to Him by not feeling itself at all. → (M10) A spark near the flame is drawn to it; that is no wonder. Faith pulls the soul from far away. → (M5) When all the light of love and awe has run out, faith holds everything up. → (M10) A small child says 'Abba' without understanding how he was born. That word holds more of his father than a grown man's understanding. → (M15) So we call Him 'Avinu', our Father, like a child who asks 'Abba, give me bread'. ⇒ BOTTOM: Faith from far away, the child's 'Abba'. ⇒ RETURN: Eat the matzah, the bread without taste, to feed faith; say 'Avinu' plainly. - MR3:MR3-C19 [Why does G-d push us away before He brings us close?] (M10) To be filled, you must first be emptied. An empty cup holds. → (M10) A circle has no top or bottom. Pushed all the way down, you are at the top. → (M9) A student who gives up his whole self can receive his teacher's whole mind. → (M9) A teacher slaps a student who cannot get it, and at once his mind opens. → (M16) Better than being pushed is lowering yourself: a careful count where every hair looks like a mountain. → (M7) The servant who walks behind his master gives up more than the friend who walks beside him. → (M5) Nothing stands before real humility. → (M12) So Hillel could say at the celebration 'If I am here, all is here': not pride, but joy where small and great are equal. ⇒ BOTTOM: The bottom of the circle, which is its top. ⇒ RETURN: Stand to pray only 'with heaviness of head' (132); then walk after Him in the day. - MR3:MR3-C20 [Can I rejoice and tremble at the same moment?] (M10) At the king's wedding feast the lords rejoice with him, and his awe shines hidden in their hearts. → (M13) In prayer the bitterness at being far comes from the very same delight. → (M4) When the King Himself shines, the subject cannot stand; he steps back and stands far off. Each step back is a deeper giving-up. ⇒ BOTTOM: Stepping back from the King, which is nearer than nearness. ⇒ RETURN: Then he is absorbed 'in the King's body', and runs again (201). - MR3:MR3-C21 [What happens between the Amidah and falling on one's face?] (M10) First comes being stunned: a villager who sees the royal court loses his mind, but has grasped nothing. → (M6) Then he comes back to himself, sees the treasures, and cleaves. Like a seed that rots in the ground and then grows. → (M15) In the Amidah, 'open my lips': no word of my own. That is the first. Falling on the face, 'to You I lift my soul', is the second. ⇒ BOTTOM: Being emptied; then being included. ⇒ RETURN: Let the stunned moment come; do not rush to fill it. - MR3:MR3-C22 [When I overcome a desire, is it gone?] (M4) A smaller desire beaten by a bigger one is not gone; it is only hidden. → (M10) It is like honey sweetening a radish: take the honey away and the bitterness is back. → (M9) The test of what you still want is the pain when you lose it. → (M15) When it does not hurt at all: 'Hashem gave and Hashem took.' ⇒ BOTTOM: Giving the soul itself, not only the will. ⇒ RETURN: Watch what hurts you today; that is where your work is. - MR3:MR3-C23 [Why can't I feel my deepest point in prayer?] (M10) The deepest awe is a treasure. A man shows it to no guest, opens a little at his son's wedding, and spends it all only when enemies threaten his life. → (M11) It wakes when someone has fallen so far that the cord of his soul is cut and he is 'as dead'. Then he becomes bitter. → (M14) The long exile is not because of sin, but to wake this 'victory' in the heart. → (M10) A tree's root is hidden in the ground; the deeper and more covered it is, the stronger the tree. ⇒ BOTTOM: The treasure spent only to save one's life. ⇒ RETURN: If you feel 'dead', that is when the treasure opens. Let the bitterness turn to 'I will not be cut off'. - MR3:MR3-C24 [How does distance become nearness?] (M10) A father and son turned their backs and walked far apart. → (M11) ...the father, standing far off with his back turned, turns his face to him. → (M10) A covenant is one thing cut in two; the two are halves of one body. Even if one does ugly things, the other cannot take revenge. ⇒ BOTTOM: The covenant, which no deed can undo. ⇒ RETURN: From the far place, cry plainly. He turns. - MR3:MR3-C25 [How do I know if my excitement in prayer is real?] (M9) Every Jew, even of low level, is moved when he hears a true explanation of G-dliness. That is real. → (M1) There is also a fake fervor: shouting, heat, 'serving oneself'. → (M9) The test: when insulted, does it make you sick? → (M4) Real nothingness is not made by counting your faults; it simply is. → (M15) And the sign of the real thing: all day you are drawn to G-d in deed. ⇒ BOTTOM: Nothingness that is not manufactured. ⇒ RETURN: Judge the prayer by the day after it.

## RB — the Rebbe

The other Rebbeim teach that there is nothing but Him. The Lubavitcher Rebbe's maamarim keep asking one more question past that point, and his answers move the depth in five directions that DESCENT does not yet have.

**1. Past 'nullified' to 'no second'.** The Rebbe climbs past bitul itself. Speech yields to thought like a chariot to its rider; thought dissolves into the idea; but 'in your heart, only One' means there is no existence besides Him, not even one that is nullified to Him (RB-C05). Even the heart's essential love, the son's cry 'Father!', is still the person's own will. Accepting the yoke is truer and still does not grasp Him. He is grasped only by His own will, which is the commanded deed, and to Him being nullified and being a self are 'exactly equal' (RB-C06). This sharpens D2, D3 and D7.

**2. The Essence is in the lowest, because it has no opposite.** A revelation is one particular thing, so it pushes aside what is not it; the Essence is the essence of each thing, like oil that does not mix and yet soaks into everything (RB-C09). Showing and hiding are one in His 'ability' (RB-C11); the world feels self-standing because its root is the One whose being is from Himself, and the true Being shows in it, 'and in truth also now' (RB-C02). Why a world this low? Not for knowledge (higher worlds would do) but from a desire above reasons, which ran into creation like delight into the bones of the feet (RB-C01). The body is not 'even' precious, it is the main preciousness (RB-C24).

**3. The soul's point that nothing touches.** Chassidus is one point, the yechidah: above all details, not even their source. Modeh Ani is said with unwashed hands because no impurity reaches it; it thanks the Essence with no Name (RB-C07). Only the yechidah feels plainly that the world is being made now from nothing; the work is to recognize, not only understand (RB-C08). From it there was never a war (RB-C22), and in it all Jews are one point (RB-D16).

**4. The bottom keeps you standing.** Real contemplation of the One 'should' make the soul expire; it does not, because the Essence, for whom sea and land are equal, wants souls in bodies. The greater kindness at the Sea was walking on dry land inside it (RB-C15). Faith that is the self cannot deny; an ache for revealed G-dliness even in comfort is higher than self-sacrifice under decree, because it joins the everyday powers to the essence (RB-C16). Free choice, even to choose wrong, is the Essence's own 'not being compelled' (RB-C10).

**5. Plainness and 'only You'.** After all understanding, the height is to do the mitzvah plainly; a Jew's plainness is one with the Essence's simplicity; forgetting the language is a virtue (RB-C18). The clever one takes the King, not even the inner rooms of treasure; wanting Gan Eden for oneself treats a means as the end, the root of idolatry; the Alter Rebbe's 'I want nothing but You alone' is given as a power to every Jew (RB-C12).

The practical floor is constant: every climb ends in a plain physical deed, done for His will, today (avodah list). *Stated: each claim above, with the sources named. Ours: the five-way grouping and the links to DESCENT.*

## Depths

- **RB:RB-D01 Not even a yielding thing** (extends Sharpens D3 and D2 (stated).): 'There is nothing but Him' at its height is not 'everything bows to Him'. It is: there is no second existence at all, not even one that is nullified to Him. A bowing thing is still a thing. | Inside: Speech serving thought like a chariot its rider; letters swallowed in the idea; 'in your heart, only One'. | Lands: In the heart's inner point; and as strength in a body 'full of foreign wants' to overcome them. | Q: שאין שום מציאות מבלעדו, גם לא מציאות שבטל אליו — no existence without Him, not even one nullified to Him (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:1354)
- **RB:RB-D02 His ability: showing and hiding are one** (extends Extends D4 (neither something nor nothing) with 'ability' (stated).): Havaya (showing) and Elokim (hiding) are one, not first in the sefiros or even in His will, but in His ability: the ability to shine and the ability not to shine are one. That ability is nothing but that He is able. So darkness itself can be turned to light. | Inside: Two inclinations both loving; feet that lift the head in the dance. | Lands: In turning one dark thing in yourself rather than only adding light. | Q: דזה שביכלתו להאיר (השרש דהוי') ושביכלתו שלא להאיר (השרש דאלקים) הוא חד ממש — that He can shine and can not-shine are truly one (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:370)
- **RB:RB-D03 Held only by His own will** (extends Corrects and extends D7 (simple will and faith) and D12 (stated).): Below D7: even the heart's essential love, the son's cry, is still the person's will, an existence. Accepting the yoke is truer, and still does not grasp Him. He is grasped only by His own will: the commanded deed. Bitul matters only so the reaching is seen. | Inside: The son's cry; the servant who does not even feel he is yielding. | Lands: In the plain deed, done for His will alone. | Q: גם רצון זה הוא הרצון של האדם, מציאות — this desire too is the person's own, an existence (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:846)
- **RB:RB-D04 Recognize, don't only understand** (extends Extends D5 and D6 (stated).): The fact that the world is being made from nothing each moment is news to most of the soul, which lives in the worlds. Only the yechidah feels it plainly. The work is to recognize, not only understand, and recognition comes from the yechidah. | Inside: Waking as new creation; Reb Yekusiel refusing 'peasant years'. | Lands: At waking, before washing; in doing a mitzvah for no other purpose. | Q: נרגש אצלה בפשיטות שכל העולמות הם אין ואפס המוחלט — it feels plainly that all worlds are absolute nothing (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Inyanah Shel Toras HaChassidus (Brooklyn, New York, 1965).txt:80)
- **RB:RB-D05 Faith that is the self, and the ache of exile** (extends New; deepens D8 (the bond that cannot be let go) (stated).): Faith from the soul's 'seeing' is strong but caused; faith from the essence is the person, so he cannot deny. Higher still than self-sacrifice under decree: to be broken that G-d is not revealed, even in comfort, because it touches your being. This joins the everyday powers to the essence. | Inside: Olive oil crushed for the light; the 49th gate missing is sickness. | Lands: In the three daily prayers for return to Zion; in ordinary hours that stay steady 'like the lamp always'. | Q: שהאמונה באלקות היא העצם שלו, ולכן אי אפשר כלל שיכפור — faith in G-d is his essence, so he cannot deny (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:1728)
- **RB:RB-D06 Dry land inside the sea** (extends Extends D9 (the bottom sends you back) (stated).): The bottom does not drown you. Real contemplation of the One 'should' make the soul expire; it does not, because the Essence, for whom sea and land are equal, wants souls in bodies. Walking on dry ground inside the sea is a greater kindness than the splitting. | Inside: Fish covered by water; a maidservant pointing 'this'; Moses standing whole. | Lands: In the body that stays; in the mitzvah as a ship. | Q: שבנ"י לא נתבטלו מפני עוצם גילוי האור דעלמין סתימין — Israel were not undone by the intensity of the light (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:2472)
- **RB:RB-D07 The world's self-standing is His mark** (extends Extends D10 and ON-14 (stated).): The world feels it stands by itself because its root is in the Essence whose being is from itself; in the end its true root, the true Being, shows in it, 'and in truth also now'. | Inside: The Rashab: 'Don't look at the lump; the Infinite Essence brought that lump into being.' | Lands: In treating physical things as real and His, not illusions. | Q: ובאמת גם עכשיו הוא כן — and in truth it is so now as well (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:1290)
- **RB:RB-D08 The prayer of the rich** (extends New; extends D10 (stated).): From the Essence, the truth of His being is the truth of all beings, so nothing truly lacks. Then knowing G-d is your whole existence, and you pray for others from fullness, that they too feel they lack nothing. | Inside: A full barrel; a rich man asking for a ruined province to be rebuilt. | Lands: In prayer for others; then 'man of G-d': refine your traits all day. | Q: הכרת האדם באלקות היא באופן שזה (הכרת אלקות) היא כל מציאותו — recognition of G-d is his whole existence (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:2604)
- **RB:RB-D09 Your face in His water** (extends Extends D11 (called into being) (stated).): The image that appears in the Infinite by our ascent is not a separate thing in it; it is the water itself. A person's root is like a name: only the readiness to turn when called. | Inside: Water reflecting a face; a fainted man waking at his name. | Lands: In refining one trait so the reflection is clear. | Q: אינם דבר נבדל מאוא"ס אלא הוא אוא"ס עצמו — they are not separate from the Infinite; they are the Infinite itself (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:2302)
- **RB:RB-D10 The body is the main preciousness** (extends Extends D12 (stated).): The deepest cry comes out only through the 'stone' in a person: plain acceptance and the physical deed. 'You have no idea how precious to G-d is the body of a Jew': not 'even' the body, but mainly. | Inside: A cut horn sounding; a covenant of one flesh. | Lands: In the body, the deed, the 'four cubits'. | Q: אלא שעיקר היוקר הוא בהגוף — the main preciousness is in the body (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:94)
- **RB:RB-D11 Forgetting the language** (extends Extends D13 (stated).): Plainness of a Jew is one with the simplicity of the Essence. After all understanding, the height is to do the mitzvah plainly. Even fine intention can dim simple acceptance; forgetting the language is a virtue. | Inside: The prince who forgot his language and cried out. | Lands: In the plain deed and the plain word. | Q: והפשטות דישראל הוא חד עם פשיטות העצמות — Israel's plainness is one with the Essence's simplicity (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:117)
- **RB:RB-D12 Not even His gifts** (extends New: corrects any depth that ends in spiritual experience (stated).): Wanting revelations, even 'Your Garden of Eden', for yourself treats a means as the end, and that is the root of idolatry. The clever one wants only the King. Every Jew can wake at least a trace of 'only You'. | Inside: A palace of treasure rooms; the king's chamber. | Lands: In making all other wants 'without heart'. | Q: שעיקר הענין דעבודת כו"מ הוא שחושבים דבר שהוא רק אמצעי לעיקר — idolatry is thinking a mere means is the main thing (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:1903)
- **RB:RB-D13 New each day, before Sinai** (extends Extends D14 (no bottom: begin again) (stated).): Each day's Torah is incomparably higher, so yesterday's is as nothing; the wise son is always 'before Sinai', like a newborn. Even a slave to Pharaoh is taken out by 'G-d Himself in His glory', a leap from end to end. | Inside: A child at the moment of birth. | Lands: In learning and in the day's first deed. | Q: התומ"צ של הבן חכם הם בכל יום חדשים ממש — the wise son's Torah is new each day (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:1922)
- **RB:RB-D14 Peace where there was never war** (extends New; joins D8 (stated).): The yechidah has no opposite, so from it there was never room for foreign wants. Even someone low can draw this by thinking of it and deciding by it. Opponents are then seen as helpers. | Inside: The Tzemach Tzedek: 'you spread yourself over the whole study hall'. | Lands: In moments of inner conflict and of feeling stepped on. | Q: שמצד היחידה אין מקום מלכתחילה לרצונות זרים — from the yechidah no room from the start for foreign desires (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:679)
- **RB:RB-D15 Free choice is His ability** (extends New; corrects a reading of 'the turn' as mere surrender (stated).): The possibility of going against His will goes back to the first contraction, which shows He is not compelled to reveal Himself. A person's freedom, even to choose wrong, is the Essence's ability; serving by your own power reveals it. | Inside: The 101st review; hides softened by treading. | Lands: In the act against habit. | Q: שהעצמות אינו מוכרח ח"ו בענין הגילוי וביכלתו להיות (גם) בהעלם — the Essence is not compelled to reveal and can be hidden (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Sefer HaMaamarim Melukat (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:1391)
- **RB:RB-D16 One point in all Jews** (extends New (stated).): What is equal in every Jew, smallest to greatest, is the yechidah; when Jews gather as one, even the unlearned is 'pure', because impurity touches only the revealed powers. The white thread, the soul's simplicity, surrounds the whole person and runs through all his powers. | Inside: A white thread around the body; everyone in one sukkah. | Lands: In joining with others; in prayer with the community. | Q: ומחמת ה"יחידה" הרי כל ישראל הם כמו נקודה אחת ויחידה — because of the yechidah all Israel are like one single point (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneerson (Lubavitcher Rebbe) (1902-1994)/Likkutei Sichos (Brooklyn, New York, 1951-1992).txt:29930)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- RB:RB-C01 [Why is there a world at all, and why such a low one?] (M1) The usual reasons for creation are that G-d wants to be known and wants His powers seen. → (M4) But for knowing Him, the higher worlds would have been enough. Down here He is hardly felt at all, and in the physical world not even His existence is felt. → (M14) So the real reason is a desire, and a desire is above reasons. You cannot ask 'why' about a longing. → (M6) What He desires is a home, and a home is where a person lives with his whole self. Here, in the lowest place, the Essence itself wants to live. → (M10) That desire did not stay above. It ran down into the making of this world, the way real delight reaches even the bones of your feet. → (M15) So even before you do anything, this world already has a link to His Essence. It is ready to become His home from its own side. ⇒ BOTTOM: A desire above every reason, living in the Essence itself. ⇒ RETURN: The lowest world is not an accident or a punishment. It was made from His desire, so your plain work in it is the point (stated). - RB:RB-C02 [The world seems to stand by itself. Does that mean it hides G-d?] (M1) Light shows where it comes from. A created thing does the opposite: it hides its source and feels that it exists by itself. → (M11) That very feeling, 'I exist on my own', comes from its root: the Essence, Whose being is from Itself. → (M6) Only He can make something out of nothing at all. So whatever is solid and 'just there' carries His mark. → (M5) In the end, inside the created 'something' its true root will show: the true Something, Him. → (M16) And in truth it is so already now. → (M15) So the world is not a dream or a trick of the eyes.
