A thing cannot come from a thing forever. → (M12) But your being here also proves the Source is real. It truly is; it is not zero. → (M5) And the two nothings are not the same. The nothing that is lost in Him cannot be a source of anything. ⇒ BOTTOM: Two nothings: the light lost in Him, nameless; and the Source we cannot grasp, which is (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Back to your own existence: the fact that you are here, limited, is itself the proof that you come straight from Him (stated; the 'proof you can feel' framing is ours). - RM:RM-C06 [When I feel close to G-d I want to stay there forever. Why must I come back down?] (M9) Life inside you beats in two moves: out and back, like a heart. Light does the same. → (M6) Light runs upward because it is stuck to its source and can feel it. → (M4) Love that is only closeness is still just a feeling of Him. It does not quench the thirst, and it does not rest. → (M1) A love that only wants to dissolve has no way back. It wants to leave every vessel. → (M5) But a run made in surrender comes back. In His essence the purpose is the coming back. → (M13) Avraham's son nearly died and came back. The point of the Akeidah was the return. → (M15) Rabbi Akiva went in and came out whole. That came from giving up even himself. ⇒ BOTTOM: The deepest run already holds the return (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Come back to the world as one sent: Torah and mitzvos are the 'return' that fulfils the run (stated). - RM:RM-C07 [Part of me wants one thing, part wants the opposite. Can they ever really make peace?] (M1) Fire and water, as they are, cannot meet. → (M2) One kind of peace: each finds a little of the other inside itself, and softens. → (M10) A deeper peace: two great ministers who hate each other stand before the king. Both forget themselves and become one. → (M10) Two judges disagree. A third judge, who sees the matter from above knowing, makes both agree fully. → (M13) That is how at Sinai six hundred thousand different minds stood 'like one man'. ⇒ BOTTOM: Opposites are one only before what is above both (stated). ⇒ RETURN: In you: the run and the return can both stand at full strength, like R. Akiva, when you are set aside before Him (35502) (stated). - RM:RM-C08 [What does it mean that I am 'in His image'?] (M3) Ink letters show something by covering the page. The ink hides the parchment, and that is how you read. → (M7) Engraved letters are different. Nothing covers anything; the stone itself shows. → (M5) Deeper still: a form that is not a picture of the thing but the thing itself taking shape. → (M10) A statue has the person's real thickness. A drawing only looks like him. → (M9) A wise man knows his own mind before he has any idea. He knows he will understand. ⇒ BOTTOM: The soul is His essence taking shape, not a picture of Him (stated). ⇒ RETURN: This is why the soul can take in His essence through Torah and why Torah, too, is called 'in our image' (35535, 35547) (stated). - RM:RM-C09 [What keeps me alive inside? What am I really made of?] (M2) Every power you have lives on delight. Take away the delight and the power goes quiet. → (M9) Even a kind person gives because he delights in giving. → (M8) It is the soul itself that delights. When the soul is in pain, nothing gives pleasure. → (M5) Deepest is delight with no garment, in the thing itself, before any picture. → (M15) That is why on Yom Kippur we do not eat. Food is a felt pleasure, and Yom Kippur is a delight too deep to feel. → (M13) At that depth everyone is equal. On Simchas Torah plain people dance as much as scholars. ⇒ BOTTOM: Your life is His delight in you, below anything felt (stated, with 'His delight in you' ours). ⇒ RETURN: From there Torah 'brings back to good' even one who fell into darkness (35534) (stated). - RM:RM-C10 [My mind gets it, but my body and my wants don't care. What do I do with that part?] (M3) The G-dly soul can grasp that He is One after creation just as before, and melt into it. → (M1) But the animal soul cannot feel that at all. → (M16) Its path is 'baruch shem': the world is real, and G-d's life is inside each thing. → (M10) When the animal soul turns, that is something new. It is like bringing the king a talking bird. → (M14) A home is where a person lives with his whole self. That is what G-d wants below. → (M15) Holding back from what He doesn't want reaches Him most. Say: 'I would want it, but my Father said no.' → (M8) Doing this is how your own deepest self shows. ⇒ BOTTOM: Your soul's essence is found by working on your lowest wants (stated). ⇒ RETURN: The soul itself needs no fixing (35594); it came down for this (stated). - RM:RM-C11 [I feel nothing. Not even on the holiest days. Am I lost?] (M11) Falling happens when a 'spirit of foolishness' covers the truth. → (M1) Some feel nothing at all, even in the Ten Days of Return when G-d is near. → (M11) When no light moves him, life pushes him with its left hand: hardship. → (M4) His turning does not come from feeling G-d. It comes from the narrowness itself, like running from death. → (M8) His natural heart breaks into pieces. → (M5) A something that became nothing is higher than a nothing that never was something. ⇒ BOTTOM: The distance itself is a door; the yesh that breaks is the highest bittul (stated). ⇒ RETURN: He then becomes careful in every detail, with body and nature too (40393) (stated). - RM:RM-C12 [Does G-d still love me after what I did?] (M3) Love that has a reason can end when the reason ends. → (M5) Soul-love has no reason. 'He loved him like his own soul.' It does not end. → (M1) But even soul-love can go hidden over long years. → (M7) A covenant reaches a place where nothing can stand in the way. → (M5) Higher still, before His raw Essence, even the covenant 'is as if it was not'. So we ask Him to choose us anyway. → (M15) The shofar's plain voice wakes that choice. ⇒ BOTTOM: His love for you is His own self; even beyond it, He chooses (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Say 'He will choose for us' and blow (or hear) the shofar; then act from the bond, not the mood (stated). - RM:RM-C13 [Can the bitter things in me ever really change?] (M3) Bitter water can be thinned with sweet water, and the bitter is still there. Or the bitter itself can turn sweet, like the water Moshe sweetened with a tree. → (M4) Mercy is different from kindness. It agrees you don't deserve it, and gives anyway. → (M11) The force of your craving is not evil. That same force can go to G-d. → (M4) The 'feeling' of G-d at that depth is not a feeling of something outside you. → (M10) Like music so sweet you lose all your senses and forget yourself. → (M15) For most of us, during prayer the bad sleeps. Then we fix one thing at a time. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bitter can become sweet only from mercy above deserving (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Daily: one trait at a time, with the craving's strength turned to good (stated). - RM:RM-C14 [What is the deepest way to be connected to G-d?] (M3) Understanding G-d is real, but it grasps that He is, not what He is. → (M7) Feelings come from the 'so therefore' of the mind, not from the understanding itself. → (M9) A simple Jew who is glad after a mitzvah has love, even without feeling it. → (M13) Accepting Him as King, plainly, is the strongest of all. No wind can move it. → (M10) Seeing is beyond understanding. Seeing the king, a person drops his self at once and stands like stone. → (M5) The deepest is not giving up yourself. It is being tied. 'With one knot I am knotted to Him.' → (M15) And that knot shows in the plainest thing: every Jew will give his life to do a mitzvah. ⇒ BOTTOM: A knot, not a melting: the yechidah's bond (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Back to the plain deed: the mitzvah done, whatever you feel (stated). - RM:RM-C15 [Is my wanting G-d real, or just something I talked myself into?] (M2) There is a want for G-d in you with no cause at all. You do not need to think yourself into it. → (M11) It shows up by itself sometimes. Other times it shows when something tries to take you away. → (M8) It never changes and never splits. → (M12) Its anger at what hurts G-d is the same love, like a parent fighting for a child. → (M1) Choices made by understanding last only through prayer. After prayer the other side can come back. → (M5) Real self-sacrifice has no pleasure and no seeking in it. It is the bond alone. ⇒ BOTTOM: The bond with no feeling and no reason (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Both are asked of you: 'choose life' (deliberate choice) and 'with all your might' (the bond) (30411) (stated). - RM:RM-C16 [I serve G-d but I don't feel joy. Is that a problem?] (M9) Joy comes after you grasp something. Before you grasp it, you feel sad. → (M16) A flash of insight disappears unless you hold it and go into it. → (M5) Delight is deeper than joy. Joy jumps and shows; delight is hidden and at rest. → (M4) Doing a mitzvah for what it brings you is like business. It is not yet service. → (M10) A servant who gave himself away polishes the work because the master's pleasure is his pleasure. → (M15) So joy in a mitzvah is: 'His will got done.' ⇒ BOTTOM: Delight in His delight, with no 'me' in it (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Do the mitzvah; let the joy be that it was done (stated). - RM:RM-C17 [I believe, but it doesn't feel real. How do I make it real?] (M2) Simple faith is like seeing. It sits in you firmly. → (M17) Train yourself every day, little by little, until it is as if you see it. → (M9) Start with what you can see: the body is dying from birth; the soul is life. → (M9) Old age shows it: the body weakens, the mind of a wise man settles. → (M16) Then you see it with your own eyes: spirit is life, matter is death. → (M15) Then the physical stops pulling you. It becomes foreign. ⇒ BOTTOM: Faith becomes sight by daily practice (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Return from the day's distance in Mincha with extra strength: 'er reist zich op' (37081) (stated). - RM:RM-C18 [How do I know how deep I really am?] (M8) Your essence is not your mind. A wise man is called 'one who has a mind'. → (M9) Life is the same in a child and an adult. The essence does not grow. → (M3) There is a will that reason cannot explain. It can even do 'crazy' things. → (M12) It wants one of two things: to leave every vessel and be swallowed in Him, or that He show Himself in the world. → (M17) You reach it step by step, the way prayer is built. → (M1) Being good on the outside does not prove you reached it. Some outwardly good people find it hard to stir from inside. → (M13) Rebbi cried when he saw a sinner's one hour of total return. All his powers gathered into one will. ⇒ BOTTOM: No one can measure his own depth: 'I do not know my soul' (stated). ⇒ RETURN: So: 'be in your own eyes like a rasha' - not despair, but keep praying and gathering yourself (stated). - RM:RM-C19 [What does it mean that G-d is One? Only One?] (M17) Yisro said G-d is greater than all gods. → (M5) Naaman said there is no G-d in the land except Israel's. Rachav said He is G-d in heaven and earth. → (M5) Moshe said: 'there is none else' - even in the empty space of the world. → (M1) Why does Shema say 'one' and not 'single'? 'One' means one with all the worlds. → (M17) Four praises climb: none compares to You; none but You; nothing apart from You; and who is like You. → (M5) Not 'all things are one in Him'. He is single, the only one, carrying everything. → (M8) And the soul is rooted there: 'I am He and no other.' ⇒ BOTTOM: The Only One, who carries all (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Even in exile, when it is not felt, 'one must toil to grasp G-d is One'; it works a little bittul (27970) (stated). - RM:RM-C20 [Does G-d have to make the world? Is He bound by it?] (M3) Giving teaching ties up the teacher. Light does not tie up the sun. → (M10) But the sun must shine. G-d shines only because He wants to. → (M10) A soul in a body is tied to that body. G-d holds all and is held by none. → (M5) In His essence everything is 'able': able to shine, able not to. → (M14) So His choice of you is a real choice. Nothing forced it. ⇒ BOTTOM: His free choice (stated). ⇒ RETURN: Therefore we pray 'He will choose for us' and live as the chosen one (stated).

## SV1 — Samach Vav I

The first third of Samach Vav (Rosh Hashana to Sivan 5666) is the Rebbe Rashab's long argument about why there is a world and what reaches G-d's Essence. Read through, it does not climb only upward. It climbs to the Essence and then insists, again and again, that the Essence is found lowest: in the plain physical mitzvah, in teshuvah from the bottom, in the day after prayer. Seven things it adds to "Go deeper" (all stated):

1. Under every "why" is a desire, not a reason. G-d made this low world because He longed for it ("His legs" = "He longed"); "about a desire there is no question" (C01, D01). The mitzvos come from His inner will, with no reason at all, not even a hidden one; that is why the plain deed brings the Essence itself, unlimited (C06, D13).

2. Every step deeper costs letting go. Each higher level needs the last joy forgotten, "as if it never was"; nullification is the vessel for light. And the higher you climb, the more wondrous He becomes: "up to and not including" (C02, D02, D03).

3. Thought cannot grasp the Essence, not even by negation; the heart's bond does, like a son with a father: "when the son cries 'Abba', the father is moved in his whole being". That bond is a will that never changes, even in the lowest Jew; "the bad is added, the essence is good" (C09, C10, D04, D05).

4. The bottom is pain, and pain is power. Bitterness of distance reaches deeper than the delight of closeness, as a drawn bow or a pressed spring. Teshuvah leaps with abandon; the strongest pull comes from feeling no connection at all (C03, C04, D06).

5. The return is higher and harder than the ascent. "It is much easier to expire in love than to live below and keep the Torah properly." The return is rooted in the Essence; the soul shows it when it cannot rest until it does a G-dly deed (C15, D07).

6. The hiding is His, and it will shine. The world hides G-d like a sun's sheath; the work is to feel the G-dly life in each thing until the world becomes a parable that shows Him; and the hiding itself will shine, because "essence does not hide essence" (C08, C16, D08). G-d is not "nothing": He is the true Being, alone "even now"; the "nothing" is His light (C18, D11).

7. The law of gift and toil. Longing comes suddenly from above each morning and passes in moments; it opens the heart for your own work. The highest love is a gift given only after you have done all you can. Toil strikes the flint; obstacles in exile are the striking (C11, C13, C14, D09, D10). And the deepest delight is the one you do not feel (Yom Kippur, the mitzvah done dry) (C12, D12).

For the guide (ours): this slice gives the guide its strongest returns. Every chain here bottoms out in something a person can do this hour: an honest minute before prayer, a mitzvah done because He asked, the next ordinary duty after a high moment, mercy on one's own blocked soul.

## Depths

- **SV1:SV1-D01 No question about a desire** (extends Sharpens D11 and D12: the command and the deed rest on a desire with no reason, not on a plan.): Under every 'why' about the world and your life is not a reason but a longing in G-d Himself: He longed for this low world. A longing has no question. (stated: lines 207-213) | Inside: The text's own image is the Yiddish proverb, 'about a desire there is no question', and the Midrash reading 'shokav' as 'longing'. | Lands: Body and now: the deed in your hand is the thing longed for (stated: 'the end of the deed rose first in thought', line 375). | Q: שוקיו זה העולם שנשתוקק הקב"ה לבראותו — 'His legs': this is the world the Holy One longed to create (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:207)
- **SV1:SV1-D02 Let it go to go up** (extends Extends D9 and D14 (the return is deeper; each run begins again): the text adds that each new run requires forgetting the last one.): Every real step deeper costs forgetting the joy of the step before. The soul crossing from one Gan Eden to the next dips in a river of fire to forget its first delight. Nullification is the vessel for light. (stated) | Inside: Images: the river of fire, the pillar between the Gardens, Rabbi Zeira's hundred fasts, the rotting seed, astonishment 'for one hour'. | Lands: Now: when a good feeling fades, do not chase it; it is the price of the next step (ours). | Q: עד שישכח לגמרי בחי' העונג ההוא, והי' כלא הי' ממש — until he completely forgets that delight, and it is as if it never was (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:292)
- **SV1:SV1-D03 Up to and not including** (extends Sharpens D5 (where knowing ends): the end of knowing is reached only by going through knowing; it is earned, and it grows the more you learn.): With bounded things, each level leads into the next. With the Infinite, no level reaches the next, and none ever reaches the Essence. So the higher you go, the more wondrous He becomes; only one who studies the lofty things truly knows He cannot be known. (stated) | Inside: 'Up to and not including'; Gan Eden's ascents 'without end'; even the Patriarchs and Moshe 'did not reach'. | Lands: The mind, today: study the hard material; its end is wonder, not answers (ours). | Q: שלא תגיע לעולם לאורו האמיתי — that you will never reach His true light (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:358)
- **SV1:SV1-D04 Abba** (extends Extends D6 and D8: under every name is not only a point but a relationship of son to Father; the bottom bond is filial and can be woken.): Thought cannot grasp G-d's Essence, not even by negation. A Jew's bond with Him is like a son's bond with a father: with no reason, essence to essence. That bond grasps the Essence. It is always there; it wakes when you remember 'He is my Father', or when you are far and in pain. (stated: lines 876-917) | Inside: The son's cry, the father moved 'in all his essence'; 'for You are our Father'; the heart that grasps what the mind cannot. | Lands: Heart and mouth: one word, said from inside, and then one deed done simply because He asked (ours: the landing). | Q: אז עס איז גאר ניט דאס — for it is not that at all (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:876)
- **SV1:SV1-D05 The will that never changed** (extends Extends D8 (the bottom is a bond): the bond is a will that survives sin and pleasure; it cannot be lost.): Pleasure cannot change what a person truly wants. Every Jew's deepest will is to be close to G-d, and it never changes, even when he is sunk in pleasures. Even the lowest, at a happy moment, says he wants to be close, and it is not a lie. In a Jew, the bad is something added; the essence is good. (stated) | Inside: 'Many waters'; 'the lost ones in the land of Ashur will come'; the father who wants his straying son. | Lands: Now: speak the wish out loud; it is not hypocrisy (stated: 'it is no lie'). | Q: ואין זה שקר — and this is no lie (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:996)
- **SV1:SV1-D06 From the narrow place** (extends New: DESCENT has a turn and a return, but not a downward bottom of pain that becomes the strongest upward force; this extends D9.): The pain of being far reaches deeper into the soul than the joy of being close, because pressure makes force beyond measure. Teshuvah does not climb; it leaps, with abandon, from furthest to nearest. Its strongest pull comes from a despair that feels no connection at all. (stated) | Inside: Springs splitting rocks; the sling; the bow; the shofar's narrow end; leaping the river; the Raavad whose soul left in weeping. | Lands: Body: the tightness in the chest is the narrow place; that is where to call from (ours). | Q: ואיך שאין לו שום שייכות לאלקות כלל — and how he has no connection at all to G-dliness (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:482)
- **SV1:SV1-D07 Harder to live it than to die for it** (extends Extends D9 and D12: the return is not only deeper; it is harder, and its root is higher than the ascent.): It is much easier to burn out in love of G-d than to live below and keep Torah and mitzvos properly. The return to the world is rooted in G-d's own Essence, with no reason. A soul shows this root when it cannot rest until it does some G-dly act in speech or deed. (stated) | Inside: Rabbi Akiva who 'entered in peace and left in peace' versus Ben Azzai; the servant at the king's feast whose awe stays with him in joy. | Lands: Deed, now: the next mitzvah of the ordinary hour is the deeper devotion (stated: lines 2008-2010). | Q: דבאמת בנקל הרבה יותר להיות בבחינת כלות הנפש מלהיות למטה בקיום התורה ומצות כדבעי — in truth it is much easier to be in expiry of the soul than to be below keeping Torah and mitzvos properly (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:2016)
- **SV1:SV1-D08 The hiding itself will shine** (extends Extends D10 (the world given back as His): it is given back not only as kept, but as the very parable and as the shining of the hiding.): G-d hides behind His name Elokim like the sun in its sheath, so the world can exist. The soul's work is to feel the G-dly life in each thing and that it is the main thing; then the world stops hiding and becomes a parable that shows Him. And the hiding itself comes from the Essence: 'essence does not hide essence'; in the end the hiding will itself shine. (stated) | Inside: The sun's sheath; the tanned hide ('oved', like tanning); the cloud and darkness that lit up. | Lands: The world, today: what hides G-d is the material for seeing Him (ours: the landing). | Q: דבאמת העולם וכל הדברים שבו ה"ז משל על אלקות — in truth the world and everything in it is a parable for G-dliness (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:2938)
- **SV1:SV1-D09 The gift and the work** (extends New: DESCENT's D2 says the deeper stillness is given; the text adds the law of the gift: given suddenly, kept only by one's own toil, and the highest given only after it.): The longing for G-d comes from above, suddenly, each morning, in every soul. But it passes in moments. Its purpose is to open the heart so that you do your own work of thinking and loving. The highest love is a gift too, but it is given only after you have done all you can. (stated) | Inside: The father walking the child to school the first day with gifts; 'whoever toiled on Friday eats on Shabbos'; 'open for Me like the eye of a needle and I will open for you like the Temple hall'. | Lands: Now: the minute after a sudden spiritual stir is the minute to act (ours). | Q: שבכל בקר אור יש התעוררות פתאום בכל נשמה — that every morning at light there is a sudden awakening in every soul (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:1645)
- **SV1:SV1-D10 Strike the flint** (extends New: no D treats effort itself as the path to the Essence.): A quick mind brings out what already exists, like blowing on a coal; it runs out. Toil reaches what does not exist even to itself, like fire in a flint; it comes only by striking, and it never runs out. Exile's service is trials and obstacles, and that is how the deepest is reached. (stated) | Inside: Coal and flint; digging to living water; 'he who works his land will be sated with bread'; Avraham blessed 'with everything' through trials. | Lands: Today: the obstacle is not in the way; it is the way (ours). | Q: צור החלמיש א'"'א להוציא ממנו האש כ"א ע'י יגיעה והכאה — from a flint stone one cannot bring out fire except through toil and striking (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:1119)
- **SV1:SV1-D11 He is the true Being** (extends Sharpens D3 and D4: D4 says 'neither something nor nothing'; the text says the Essence is the true Something (yesh ha'amiti), and 'nothing' is His light.): 'Nothing' is too small a word for G-d. The Nothing from which the world comes is His light. He Himself is the true Being, alone, even now, not even 'surrounding' the worlds; 'Ein Sof' is the negation of every description, even of 'unlimited', and so everything is in Him. (stated: lines 2253-2302) | Inside: The sun's ray that is 'not even called light' within the sun; a person's name when no one calls him; the gem that shines. | Lands: Mind: rest on 'He is'; it is enough (ours). | Q: ובאמת אתה הוא לבדך ממש, בבחי' הבדלה לגמרי, גם עתה — and in truth You are alone, utterly apart, even now (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:2299)
- **SV1:SV1-D12 The delight you do not feel** (extends Extends D13 (plainness is deepest) and D7: the plainest, unfelt service touches the Essence's own delight.): There is a felt delight of the Essence (Shabbos: eat, it is all good) and an unfelt one (Yom Kippur: no food, affliction even of the soul). In the mitzvos now, only the will shows; the delight is hidden. A martyr goes from simple will, with a hidden taste he does not feel. (stated) | Inside: Shabbos and Yom Kippur; the martyr's hidden 'taste' ('a gishmak'); the son who suffers when his father suffers. | Lands: Body: the empty feeling is not absence (ours). | Q: שיבוטל ממנה ע'"י העינוי כל בחי' עונג מורגש — that through the affliction all felt delight is removed from it (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:1459)
- **SV1:SV1-D13 The will with no reason, in the deed** (extends Extends D12 (the deed in the hand): explains why the deed is deepest: the will in it has no reason, so nothing limits what it brings.): Creation comes from G-d's outer will, which has reasons. The mitzvos come from His inner will, with no reason at all. Their exact measures do not limit the light; they only say where His Essence is found. 'If He had willed chopping wood, we would chop wood.' (stated) | Inside: The merchant's hidden profit; the man who delights in his house for no reason; chopping wood. | Lands: Hand: the plain physical mitzvah, exactly as prescribed (stated: lines 822-849). | Q: שאין טעם לרצון כלל, אף לא טעם כמוס — that there is no reason for the will at all, not even a hidden reason (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:596)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- SV1:SV1-C01 [Why did G-d make a world at all? The usual answers: so that His powers would be complete, or so that He would be known.] (M1) The usual reason, that G-d made the world to complete His powers, cannot be the real reason. With Him, the moment He willed it, it already was. → (M1) 'So that He would be known' cannot be the reason either. Knowing Him happens fully in the high worlds. This low world hides Him, yet this world is the purpose. → (M2) The real reason is a longing. The Midrash reads 'His legs' (shokav) as 'the world He longed (nishtokek) to create'. → (M14) A longing has no 'why'. We do not know, and cannot know, a reason for it. → (M12) Because the longing comes from His very Essence, up and down mean nothing to it, and the main showing of it will be below. → (M5) The plain deed of a mitzvah was 'first in thought', before even the first stir of the will to create. Everything else came for its sake. → (M15) So when His will is done in deed, it is 'a pleasure to Me'. The pleasure of the Essence lives in the deed. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): a desire in the Essence with no reason, 'no question about a desire'. Nothing beneath it to ask about. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): the plain physical mitzvah, today, is the purpose itself; Rosh Hashana is 'the beginning of Your works' because man, who does mitzvos, was made then (line 386). Landing (ours): your small deed is not a step toward the point; it is the point. - SV1:SV1-C02 [Souls in Gan Eden climb from level to level. Why is there no rest, and how does one climb?] (M3) Every light has a head and a foot: strong near its source, weaker far away. Only the Essence is the same everywhere. → (M2) So the joy of Gan Eden, which is only a light, has endless levels. That is why 'the righteous have no rest, not in this world and not in the next'. → (M4) To rise to a higher level you must let go of the lower delight completely, as if it never was. → (M9) We see it in learning. Rabbi Zeira fasted a hundred fasts to forget the Babylonian Talmud so he could receive the Jerusalem one. A seed must rot before it sprouts. → (M2) Being nothing is itself the vessel for light. → (M5) The higher you climb, the more He is beyond you. Only someone who studies the lofty levels truly knows that He cannot be known; others hold it only by faith. → (M15) The Essence's own delight is reached not by climbing but by doing physical mitzvos. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): 'up to and not including': no level ever reaches the Essence; the climb is endless by nature. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): the essential delight comes into the plain physical mitzvah. Landing (ours): when you feel you have stopped growing, let the old good feeling go; and do the next mitzvah, which does what climbing cannot. - SV1:SV1-C03 [How does a person come to real closeness in prayer?] (M2) Prayer is coming close to G-d: either His light shines in you, or you burn to reach Him. → (M7) If the light itself shines in you, you feel joy and delight. If you only sense how far beyond He is, you run toward Him in thirst. → (M3) Both start with bitterness. 'One stands to pray only from heaviness of head': an honest reckoning of how far you are. → (M2) The deeper the bitterness, the more light comes after: 'the reward matches the pain'. → (M4) In real teshuvah a person cries until his whole form is undone and he becomes truly nothing. From that narrow place comes the wide answer. → (M9) The same is true in learning: a person reaches the depth of wisdom only after toiling until he almost gives up. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): becoming 'truly nothing' is what makes room for the light. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): then light shines in prayer, and wisdom comes 'with a shining face'. Landing (ours): before you pray, take one honest minute about where you really are; do not skip it to get to the good feeling. - SV1:SV1-C04 [What does teshuvah reach that a good life does not, and how?] (M2) Forgiveness can only come from above the whole order of worlds, because inside that order a flaw is real. → (M9) True teshuvah moves the very core of the soul from its place, like a deep pain shakes a person's whole being. → (M7) In that bitterness the pull toward G-d is really there, but he does not feel it; he feels only the pain of being far, like someone running away from death. → (M11) The strongest pull comes from a despair that does not sense G-d at all: 'how could someone like me have anything to do with Him?' → (M1) Where returners stand, the perfectly righteous cannot. A returner draws G-d 'with greater force'. → (M3) Anything pressed into a narrow place bursts out with force beyond its size: springs under the earth split rocks, a sling throws farther, a bow pulled back shoots far. → (M10) Teshuvah is a leap, not a climb. Like a man who must cross a wide river: if he jumps with total abandon, he jumps far beyond his strength. → (M15) Then he returns to Torah and good deeds, and they shine with a new light: 'Take words with you and return'. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the 'depth of depths' of the heart, reached by bitterness that touches 'the very point of his Jewishness'. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): Torah and good deeds after teshuvah light up from that depth. Landing (ours): the place you feel least connected is the springboard; do not measure the jump by your strength. - SV1:SV1-C05 [How much of G-d does our understanding actually hold?] (M9) When a new idea first strikes you, it is a flash of lightning: bright, but you cannot yet put it into words. → (M4) As the idea becomes clear understanding, the light inside it hides more. → (M9) The joy is greatest at the moment of discovery, like Rabbi Eliezer's face shining when he found a new teaching. Once it gets a little old, the joy stops. → (M5) So every understanding of G-d, even a great one, is only a glimmer; understanding always stands far from the essence. → (M12) When the essence itself shines, it shines in every power equally, mind and deed alike. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): no comprehension reaches the essence; even wisdom's sight sees only 'My back'. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): the essence, which shines equally, comes in the mitzvah deed (line 507: the will in mitzvos holds the essential delight). Landing (ours): do not chase the old flash; let it go and do the act it pointed to. - SV1:SV1-C06 [What is the difference between G-d's will in making the world and His will in the mitzvos?] (M9) Every person has an outer will and an inner will. A merchant's outer will is the travel and work; his inner will is the profit, and it does not show. → (M7) A man wants a house for good reasons. But deep inside he simply delights in this home, with no reason, and if every reason argued against it, his inner wanting would not weaken. → (M2) Inner will is not mixed with anything; it comes from the self alone, so it cannot be explained and has no limit. → (M6) G-d's will to create comes with a reason: 'because He desires kindness', the nature of the good to do good. That is His outer will. → (M5) The mitzvos come from a will with no reason at all, not even a hidden reason or a taste. → (M10) The mitzvos have exact measures, but the measure does not shrink the light. It only says where the Essence will be found. If He had willed chopping wood, we would have to chop wood. → (M15) So the deepest way to do a mitzvah is because He willed it, in acceptance of His yoke, and to find great life in His will being done. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): a will in the Essence with no reason, not even a hidden one. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): the plain physical mitzvah, done because He wills it. Landing (ours): next time you do a mitzvah you do not understand, notice that this is exactly where it reaches deepest. - SV1:SV1-C07 [Why is G-d's life in the world a 'running and returning', and what is above that?] (M9) The root of time is the running and returning of life. Like breath: each moment life comes from the soul, and at once goes back up, and new life comes. → (M2) Time itself is a break. If life flowed without stopping, there would be no 'an hour or two'. → (M2) You only run when you are far. A light runs to leave its vessel because it is distant from its source. → (M4) G-d's will never left Him, so it does not run. It 'reaches and does not reach'. Its rising and spreading are not opposites. → (M7) Rabbi Shimon bar Yochai said: 'In one knot I am tied, held to Him, aflame in Him'. His fire did not come from his own understanding but from the Source. → (M10) The will in the mitzvos is the Essence itself. Like water that flows down from high to low and does not change, it can come all the way down unchanged. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the Essence, where there is no running and no returning, 'a day that is all long'. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): through mitzvos the Essence comes into time and place, and saying 'Vayechulu' on Shabbos makes one G-d's partner. Landing (ours): your breath, right now, is the picture: it comes, it returns. The mitzvah is the water that does not go back up. - SV1:SV1-C08 [Do creatures really exist next to G-d?] (M10) A craftsman makes a vessel and walks away; it does not need him, and it even has a use he lacks. A ray of sunlight is different: it needs the sun every moment. → (M12) And the ray is stronger inside the sun itself, where it does not even count as light. So creatures need Him every moment, and add nothing to Him. → (M4) Even now, from His side, the contraction hides nothing. The worlds are truly one with Him, as before they were made. → (M5) Higher still: He Himself is not even 'surrounding' the worlds. You are alone, truly, even now, after the heavens were made. → (M4) He is not 'nothing'. He is the real Being, the true 'Yesh'. What we call the 'Nothing' that makes the world is His light. → (M9) From our side it looks reversed: we feel ourselves as real and the source as 'nothing'. That is why we do not even feel that our own life is G-dliness. → (M16) 'G-d's truth to the world' means that creatures will feel that there is G-dly life in them and that the G-dliness is the main thing. → (M15) A person who works on this finds the world no longer hides G-d. Through the world he sees G-dliness more tangibly, as a parable shows its meaning. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): He alone is, 'even now', and He is the true Being, not a nothing. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): the 'servant of Elokim' works with the physical, feeling the G-dly life in it, until the world becomes a parable that shows Him. Landing (ours): hold one object today and ask: what is the life in this, and is that the main thing? - SV1:SV1-C09 [If no thought can grasp G-d, what can?] (M1) No thought grasps the Essence, not even the highest kind of knowing by negation. Saying what He is not still does not say anything about Him, because it is not even that. → (M3) Outer love comes from thinking about G-d's greatness. Like a strongman known by the huge stones he wrestles with, G-d is known by the heavens. → (M9) Inner love is different: like a son's bond to his father, with no reason. A friend loves for good reasons and so reaches only what shows; a son is one essence with the father. → (M9) When the son cries 'Abba!', the father is moved in his whole being, as he is not moved by anyone else's cry. → (M16) This bond is always there, only sometimes hidden. It wakes when you remember 'He is my Father', or when you are far away and in distress. → (M11) The heart is lower than the mind, yet it grasps what the mind cannot, because the emotions are rooted in what is higher than mind. → (M15) After this inner love, the mitzvos are done simply because He said so, 'we will do and we will hear'. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the bond of essence to Essence, like son to father, which needs no reason and cannot be grasped by thought. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): mitzvos in acceptance of the yoke, 'we will do'. Landing (ours): say the word 'Father' slowly, once; then do one thing simply because He asked. - SV1:SV1-C10 [Which is deeper in a person: what he wants, or what he enjoys? And what does that say about our bond with G-d?] (M9) It looks like delight is deeper: we only want what we enjoy, and the more we enjoy it, the more we want it. → (M1) But it is also the reverse. In a time of distress a person is shut down in his wants and cannot enjoy even his only son. In joy he enjoys even silly things and even his enemy. → (M9) There is wanting with no pleasure at all. On Rosh Hashana every heart, even of the simplest people, is drawn to G-d for no reason, and that is why they feel how far they are. → (M9) A son is drawn to his father even if his father is a plain man who gives him no pleasure. That is will from the essence, not from pleasure. → (M11) Will is stronger than pleasure. Pleasure cannot change what a person truly wants. Even the lowest Jew, at a happy moment, says he wants to be close to G-d, and it is not a lie. → (M12) Will can turn pain into pleasure. That is how a Jew gives his life for G-d: not from the delight of love, but from the simple will 'that cannot be separated' from Him, 'for it is a stiff-necked people'. → (M4) Yet inside every true will there is a hidden delight. If the father suffers, the son hurts deep inside, so he must take pleasure in him. The martyr has a hidden 'taste' that for G-d's oneness it is worth it. → (M13) A real will wants the essence of the one it loves. A father wants his son even when the son is not going in the right way, and G-d wants Israel the same way, because in a Jew the bad is something added and the essence is good. → (M15) So in the mitzvos now, only the will is visible and the delight is hidden. We do them in hope, setting ourselves down before Him, and the hidden delight will be revealed. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): will and delight are one in the essence; the will shows, the delight hides. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): mitzvos done in acceptance of the yoke, in hope, without needing to feel their pleasure. Landing (ours): if you feel nothing in your mitzvos, that is not failure; the will is what shows now. - SV1:SV1-C11 [Why does toil in Torah reach deeper than a quick mind?] (M2) Real study means waiting with a matter: learning it again and again, finding it harder, and the deeper you go, the more wondrous it becomes. → (M9) It is like digging. A shallow dig finds collected water; a deep dig finds a living spring. → (M9) People with small minds who toil can climb higher than people with naturally good minds. → (M10) There are two hiddennesses. A flame inside a coal is real fire; blow and it comes out, but it runs out. A flint has no fire in it at all; you must strike it, but its sparks never end. → (M9) Before you understand, the light already starts to dawn. You do not yet know what, but you sense that it is right. → (M11) The Babylonian Talmud seats us 'in darkness': questions on top of questions hide every answer, until the thing is wholly wondrous. Then the truth shines, clearer than before. → (M15) Avraham was blessed 'with everything' through his trials. So too our exile's work is trials: obstacles to everything, even to doing mitzvos, and we must stand firm against every obstacle. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the 'essential hiddenness' of wisdom, which is G-d's own delight in Torah, 'only G-d understands its way'. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): toil in the plain matter, like a farmer clearing his field, 'he who works his land will be sated with bread' (line 1266). Landing (ours): the obstacle in front of you is the striker for the flint. - SV1:SV1-C12 [What will the joy of the future be, and can we taste it now?] (M2) Most spiritual joy is a joy in understanding: limited to how much each person grasps. → (M17) After many details, a person comes to the 'depth of what is grasped', the thing itself, very clear and very pure. Then song comes out of the heart by itself. → (M5) Higher is the 'simple song': the soul's own joy, not by its understanding, like the Song at the Sea, where each one pointed with a finger: 'This is my G-d'. → (M15) That simple joy comes into the body: 'my heart and my flesh sing'. David danced; dancing without song shows the whole essence is moved. → (M10) Rabbi Akiva wept when he heard the secrets of the Torah. Weeping comes when a felt joy is too much for the vessel. → (M5) Higher still is the delight that is never felt. That is Yom Kippur: no eating, and 'you shall afflict your souls', even the soul, until every felt pleasure is removed. → (M15) This last level is reached not by Torah and mitzvos but by teshuvah from the depth of the heart. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the Essence's own delight, never felt, 'shaashuim atzmiyim'. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): teshuvah; and after it, Torah and mitzvos shine from there (lines 1496). Landing (ours): on the day you feel nothing at all in prayer, it may be a Yom Kippur of the soul. - SV1:SV1-C13 [Where does the soul's sudden longing for G-d come from, and what is ours to do?] (M1) The soul does not long for G-d just by nature. Things fall back to their root by nature, but that is not longing. Longing needs to feel the source, and the soul down here does not. → (M9) Every morning there is a sudden awakening in every soul, to cling to G-d and not to chase emptiness. → (M2) Because it comes suddenly, without any preparation, it is clear proof it came from above. → (M1) But then is it our service at all? The arousal itself is only a preface. It passes in a short time, as if it never was. → (M11) At other times you cannot think about G-d at all: foreign thoughts, or old unrepaired things, dull the mind and heart. Then the advice is to have mercy on your own soul, and that opens it. → (M9) It is like the first day of school: the father walks the child and gives him many presents, so that later he will go by himself. → (M15) What remains from real inner work stays all day: to turn from what is bad and to do good, in deed. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the longing itself is a gift from above; the soul could not make it alone. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): your own toil in mind and heart, using the opened moment, then good deeds all day. Landing (ours): when a sudden pull to G-d comes, stop and use it within the minute; it will not wait. - SV1:SV1-C14 [How does prayer, step by step, lift the whole person?] (M9) Begin with 'fire from below': think how G-d makes everything new. At night your life withdraws; when you get up you are a new creature. → (M2) What is new excites us. Seeing renewal everywhere lifts the soul out of its heaviness. → (M15) Even if no flame comes down from above, you must still bring 'fire from an ordinary person': the Second Temple had no fire from above, and its offerings were still valid. → (M11) We recount how the angels praise G-d not because we do not know it, but for our animal soul, which comes from the same place as the angels. → (M7) On Shabbos the soul itself praises, with no heart of flesh involved, like a scent the soul enjoys and the body does not. It is quiet, like the candle's light, not noisy like the fire of offerings. → (M15) That higher love is a gift, but it comes only after you have done what you can. 'Whoever toiled on Friday eats on Shabbos.' ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): a love from above, 'service as a gift', where the soul is bound 'like a candle in a torch'. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): eating with intent (line 1917) and honest business continue the refining through the day. Landing (ours): tomorrow morning, before anything, notice: I woke up new. - SV1:SV1-C15 [Which is greater: the soul's burning to leave, or its coming back to live in the world?] (M2) The aim is both: after the burning love of prayer, to keep Torah and mitzvos all day. → (M10) Someone who hears of the king's greatness from far away gets very excited. Someone who stands before the king falls to the ground like a stone. → (M7) A minister who sees the king's glory delights and draws close. A servant who sees it shrinks back in awe. The servant's seeing reached his heart. → (M9) The return is rooted in G-d's own Essence, in a desire with no reason. In the soul it shows as this: a person cannot find rest unless he does some G-dly act in speech or deed. → (M4) It is much easier to burn out in love than to live below and keep the Torah properly. → (M5) Higher than the soul's flight is to be one with G-d while the world is still here: eating, drinking, dealing, and in all of it nullified to Him. → (M15) To break off from the highest love and come back down is very hard. It comes only from laying yourself down completely before His will. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the return is G-d's own desire, with no reason, 'He desired a dwelling below'. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): keeping Torah and mitzvos all day; and even forcing yourself without desire, which every Jew can do (line 2014). Landing (ours): when you feel like staying in the high moment, the mitzvah of the next hour is the greater devotion. - SV1:SV1-C16 [Does the world hide G-d, or show Him?] (M3) The sun has a sheath. Without it, its light would be too strong for the world. 'G-d (Havayah) is a sun and a shield (Elokim)'. → (M2) The name Elokim hides G-d so that a world can exist. That is why creatures feel separate. → (M7) The soul came down to be 'a servant of Elokim': to train itself to feel that in each physical thing there is G-dly life, and that the G-dliness is the main thing. → (M4) Then the world no longer hides G-d. Through the world he sees G-dliness more tangibly than before. → (M11) Higher still: the hiding itself will shine, like 'the cloud and the darkness, and it lit up', because all hiddenness comes from the Essence's own hiddenness. → (M5) And there is a level where time and place do not limit at all: the Ark in the Temple took up no space, and for the Baal Shem Tov time and place did not hide anything. → (M15) So: for the higher soul, rest comes at once; for the servant, six years of sowing, and then the Sabbath. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the root of all hiding is the Essence's own hiddenness, which will itself shine. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): the six days of sowing, refining the physical; then Shabbos. Landing (ours): the thing that hides G-d from you today is the material for seeing Him. - SV1:SV1-C17 [What is in every Jew, even one who knows nothing?] (M6) The highest level, Adam Kadmon, reaches its 'feet' all the way into the lowest world. Because of this, every Jew has a hidden love, even the simplest. → (M2) From that hidden love comes the power of every Jew to give his life for G-d, without any understanding, by faith alone, because he cannot be separated from G-d. → (M4) 'Though he does not see, his root sees.' The root of the soul truly sees, without any reasoning, and cannot move away. → (M15) This power shows especially here below. Above, the soul serves with mind and feeling; only in a body does the power of self-giving appear. → (M9) Like a father with an only son, whose picture is engraved on his heart and memory always; so Israel 'rose in His thought'. → (M16) Every Jew has the choice to bring this hidden love out of hiding. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the soul's root 'sees' G-d always and cannot separate; this is from the highest level reaching the lowest. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): bringing it out in deed, and at the extreme, in self-giving. Landing (ours): you do not need to feel it for it to be there. - SV1:SV1-C18 [What does 'Ein Sof', 'without end', really mean, and is G-d a light?] (M4) 'Without end' does not describe G-d as something very big. It removes every description: not limited and not unlimited, not 'is' and not 'is not', and so everything is in Him. → (M10) The sun shines because it must; it cannot choose not to. G-d's light shines only because He wills it, as He can tell the sun not to rise. → (M4) So inside G-d, the light is not a light at all. It is only His ability to shine. → (M10) G-d is not essentially light. A shining gem is a better picture than the sun: the stone is something itself, and shining is only something it does. Making worlds is not the main thing about G-d. → (M7) 'You are holy' means He is apart in Himself, not apart from something. His Name, the light, is apart from the worlds but still related to them. → (M9) His Name before anything existed is like a person's name when no one is calling him: there is only the person, and his ability to turn when called. → (M11) His ability includes the opposite: to hide, to limit. And the power to hide is the greater power. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the Essence, not even 'light', with only the ability to shine, eternal as Himself. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): the limit and the hiding are His strength; from them comes the line of light that makes and fills the worlds (lines 2513, 2553: 'He contracted His Presence between the poles of the Ark'). Landing (ours): when G-d seems hidden, you are looking at His greatest strength. - SV1:SV1-C19 [What is the difference between a servant who chops wood and one who sets jewels, and which am I?] (M9) There are two kinds of servants. One does rough work, like chopping wood, work the master would never do himself. The other does fine work, like boring pearls, that the master himself would do. → (M11) Keeping the world alive is the rough work; it needs a poor servant who works for pay. In a person, it is accepting the yoke by force, when he wants nothing and feels nothing. → (M6) Torah and mitzvos are the pearls on a thread: the 'voice of Yaakov' is the thread running from end to end, joining the farthest things to Him. → (M2) To string a pearl you must bore a hole. 'My heart is hollow within me': the Infinite rests only in one who is truly nothing before Him. → (M9) The soul longs to leave its sheath and pour itself into its Father's bosom. → (M15) Down here, honest business and eating for G-d's sake refine the scattered sparks; but to lift them all the way up needs real self-nullification, like Yosef. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the hollow heart, 'truly nothing', in which the Infinite rests. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): honest business, eating for heaven's sake, and accepting the yoke even when you feel nothing. Landing (ours): on a numb day you are the wood-chopper, and the world stands because of you. - SV1:SV1-C20 [Why is the mezuzah on the right and the Chanuka light on the left, and why does the mitzvah come before the Torah in 'a mitzvah is a candle and Torah is light'?] (M1) Torah is a great bonfire and a mitzvah only a single candle. Yet the verse puts the mitzvah first. → (M6) Because the light of Torah depends on the mitzvos, as fire depends on air. → (M3) The Greeks did not want our bodies; they wanted to make us forget Torah, so they made the oils impure. First the Chashmona'im won by giving their lives, and only then could the lights be lit. → (M15) When a person's soul is clouded by outside ideas, the light of Torah does not help him at first. What helps is a strong, concrete doing of mitzvos; then the Torah can light him. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the Supreme Will in the mitzvah is higher than the light of wisdom. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): strong action first, then learning lights up. Landing (ours): when ideas confuse you about G-d, do not first argue; first do a mitzvah firmly. - SV1:SV1-C21 [Is humility being crushed, or being lifted?] (M7) Some souls serve mainly through the soul itself; the body does not hide them. Others serve mainly by refining the body, and the body does hide. → (M9) Bitul that comes from the body is a broken heart with no height, like David, who always had a broken and crushed heart: 'for I am poor and needy'. → (M12) Bitul that comes from the soul has height in it: Moshe, Yosef the ruler, Rabbi Shimon 'I am a sign in the world'. It is not their own height, but G-d's height in them. → (M16) Many simple people have bitul in their very nature: their coarse hearts break easily, and they serve G-d truly with their bodies, in faith and simple hearts, without much understanding. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): two humilities, both true: the broken heart below and the lifted nothingness above. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): serving in plain deeds 'in faith and wholeheartedness'. Landing (ours): if your heart breaks easily, that is a root, not a weakness; and if you feel lifted, check that what is lifted is G-d. - SV1:SV1-C22 [What does 'Go to yourself' mean for a soul?] (M8) 'Go to you': go to the root of your soul. Not all of the soul comes into the body; only a little of it. Its main part stays above. → (M2) That root is the will of the heart, the soul's own bond with G-d. → (M3) To get there, first leave 'your land, your birthplace, your father's house': the heaviness of the animal soul, its mind, and its feelings. 'Forget your people and your father's house, and the King will desire your beauty.' → (M15) Then the mitzvos make G-d's Name great: 'I will make your name great'. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the root of the soul, its bond of will with the Infinite. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): the mitzvos then 'make the Name great'. Landing (ours): leaving your habits is not leaving yourself; it is going to yourself. - SV1:SV1-C23 [Why is the Torah given in the desert?] (M3) Before the light of Torah, there must be a rising up from below: prayer, the soul's thirst. → (M2) The desert is thirst. 'My soul thirsts for You, in a dry land.' → (M9) There is love like hunger for bread, and love like thirst, which is stronger. 'Hungry, also thirsty, their soul faints in them.' → (M15) After the thirst, the Torah lights up the person's face from his root, as a person's face on Shabbos is not like on a weekday. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom (stated): the soul's root in the inner Atik, which the Torah reveals in the face. ⇒ RETURN: Return (stated): 'lift the head': each person's root shows in him. Landing (ours): if you feel dry, you are in the place where Torah is given.

## SV2 — Samach Vav II

Hemshech Samach Vav, lines 3101-6200 (OCR), runs from Shavuos 5666 to Shemos 5668. It reads like one long argument about who reaches G-d's Essence and how. The answer keeps turning upside down. Feeling G-d in prayer is good. The child who runs after a hidden father is deeper. The plain servant who serves under the yoke, with no light, no knowing and no love, only because he was told to, is deeper still: he is 'not a being for himself', and his work counts as the Master's own hand. The 'no' reaches higher than the 'do'. Toil and even despair inside honest work act like a rotting seed. The lowest things (earth, the body, the plain mitzvah done with the hand) carry the power of the very first, because 'their end is wedged in their beginning'. Three corrections to the current depths. (1) Past 'all is as nothing before Him' there is 'there is none else'. In 'as nothing' someone is still there feeling 'He alone'. The world is real, not an illusion, but it is not true existence. The way down is the servant's self-giving and 'Modeh ani', not more thought. (2) Bittul has a sound. Noise means something still burns. Bittul made by thinking ends when the thinking stops. Essential bittul is silent. (3) Being hidden is not distance. G-d hides by His own will, and a willed hiding is not a hiding. His help is mostly a hand behind the child. The descent goes the same way. The soul's root shines only below, in a body lower than an animal's: the lever is gripped at its lowest end. The guide can take a person down by these steps: hiding, the narrow place, the 'no', the yoke, the 101st time, silence, none else. Then back up: the deed in the hand, 'Modeh ani', the next mitzvah. The insights are our plain English. Every argument, parable and order of steps is the text's own (stated). Links to D1-D14 and the guards are ours.

## Depths

- **SV2:SV2-D1 The hiding is the love** (extends Sharpens D9 (the bottom sends you back) and D14: the lowest point is the cause of the highest return; adds the stance for times when nothing is felt.): When you cannot feel G-d, that not-feeling is not real distance. It is the Father hiding to test the child, and His love is inside the hiding. The far point is the cause of the nearest point. | Inside: Running in the dark behind someone you cannot see; the double kiss when he turns (3261). | Lands: In the body: the next mitzvah done plainly. Now: this prayer, even if dry. | Q: שהיתה האהבה טמונה ומכוסה בהעלם בכוונת ההרחקה — The love had been buried and covered within the intention of the distancing. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:3261)
- **SV2:SV2-D2 The 'no' that reaches the self** (extends New. Deepens D8 (what you cannot let go): the bond shows most in refusal.): What you refuse touches your deepest self more than what you do. Holding back from what G-d does not want reaches His Essence, beyond all light, and nothing can block it, even in exile. | Inside: Strength without motion; a quiet, firm 'no'. | Lands: In the hands and mouth: not doing, not saying. Even in darkness. | Q: כי הלאו מגיע יותר בפנימיות ועצמות הנפש — The 'no' reaches more into the inwardness and essence of the soul. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:3378)
- **SV2:SV2-D3 Cannot be otherwise** (extends Sharpens D8 (the bottom is a bond): the bond's mark is 'cannot be otherwise', not intensity.): The deepest will in a Jew is not strong; it is single, and cannot be otherwise. A person gives his life not because he wants to die but because he simply cannot be separated. Many wants weaken each other; one simple will cannot be split. | Inside: Even while wanting to live; quiet, not dramatic. | Lands: The plain doing of a mitzvah, in which there are no levels. | Q: שאינו יכול להיות נפרד מאלקות בשום אופן — He cannot be separated from G-dliness in any way. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:3448)
- **SV2:SV2-D4 No one left to take credit** (extends Corrects D2 (no one left to watch): the self is not erased but given away; extends D11 (called by the command): kabbalas ol is the self's highest stance, not its lowest.): The plain servant under the yoke is not a separate thing; he is like his master's limb. His only joy is his master's joy, he does not care whose name is on the work, and his work counts as the master's own hand. That total self-giving reaches the Essence. Its bittul is a contraction, but it is not a goal of self-loss; it is service. | Inside: Contraction and steadiness; chopping wood the same as handing a cup. | Lands: The plain task in front of you, at work, at home, at night. | Q: כאלו אינו במציאות בעולם לעצמו כלל — As if he does not exist in the world for himself at all. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:3894)
- **SV2:SV2-D5 The 101st time** (extends New.): Serving G-d begins exactly where your nature ends. What you do by habit, however holy, is 'commandments of men learned by rote'. The one time beyond your nature is the service, even if your nature never changes. | Inside: Hard, against the grain; G-d sees the heart, not the outside. | Lands: One concrete act today beyond habit. | Q: שאינו דומה השונה פרקו מאה פעמים למאה פעמים ואחד — One who reviews a hundred times is not like one who reviews a hundred and one times. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:3929)
- **SV2:SV2-D6 Sweat, despair and the rotting seed** (extends New. Guard (ours): not for clinical depression; offer only as meaning inside honest toil, never as praise of despair.): Toil with no visible success is the seed rotting in the ground. The undoing of your 'something' becomes something new from nothing. Despair in the work, held inside the work, is the sowing in tears. | Inside: Weeping while sowing; the joy at the harvest is not yet visible. | Lands: Today's work, done honestly, and a prayer for a new will. | Q: כרקבון הזריעה — Like the rotting of the seed. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:4108)
- **SV2:SV2-D7 Past 'nothing but Him': there is none else** (extends Extends D3-D4 (even with Him, nothing; neither something nor nothing) and corrects any illusionism with D10 (the world given back).): 'All is as nothing before Him' still leaves someone feeling that He alone exists. 'There is none else' leaves no one at all: only the Essence. The world is real (not an illusion) but not true existence. The way there is not more thought but the servant's self-giving and the morning 'Modeh ani'. | Inside: No noise, no feeling of 'I am nullified'. | Lands: Waking up and saying 'Modeh ani'; the plain deed. | Q: העושה מעשה חייב, האוחז את העינים פטור — The one who does a deed is liable; the one who deceives the eyes is exempt. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:5175)
- **SV2:SV2-D8 Noise means something is still burning** (extends Extends D2 and D13 (plainness is deepest).): Excitement, noise and fire belong to bittul that still has a self. True bittul is silent, like standing so close to the king that you cannot speak. Bittul made by contemplation ends when the thinking stops; essential bittul stays. | Inside: Silent as a stone; speaking without knowing what you say. | Lands: The whisper of the Amidah; guarding speech and senses all day. | Q: וכמו עצים הנשרפים באש, כיז שלא נשרפו לגמרי ה"ה דולקים ברעש — As long as it is not completely burnt, it burns with noise. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:4493)
- **SV2:SV2-D9 The soul's root shows only below** (extends Extends D9-D10: the return is not only back to the world; the descent itself is where the root shows.): The soul's true root does not shine above; there it 'stands'. It shines only when it comes down into the lowest body, which is lower than an animal's. The descent is a leap that needs every hidden strength, and the body is the lowest end of the lever with which everything is lifted. | Inside: Fear that lifts a stone you could not lift; a strongman bringing out hidden strength. | Lands: The body and its daily needs, refined. | Q: זהו שנמשך ומאיר דוקא בירידתה למטה — It is drawn and shines specifically in its descent below. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:5881)
- **SV2:SV2-D10 The hidden hand** (extends Extends D14 (no bottom: begin again).): Most help from above is hidden: like a father behind a child, holding and pushing, while the child thinks he walks alone; like a teacher who leads from the side. Sometimes He lifts us on His shoulders. In exile He stands behind the wall watching. | Inside: A tug at the heart; being pushed from behind; seeing far from a giant's shoulders. | Lands: The Ten Days of Return, the morning stirring; the next step. | Q: ונדמה לתינוק שהולך מכח עצמו — It seems to the child that he walks by his own strength. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:4786)
- **SV2:SV2-D11 A willed hiding is not hiding** (extends Extends ON (the world hides because it is closest) and D4.): When something else hides a thing, the hiding is forced. G-d's hiding is His own will, and only the Infinite can hide itself; so He is hidden only toward the worlds, while fully present and the worlds still remain. Even sin only seems to hide, from our side. | Inside: Engraved letters: a little darker, nothing covering. | Lands: Before prayer: heaviness of head, bitterness and self-pity, then rising. | Q: והסתר רצוני אינו הסתר — A willed hiding is not a hiding. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:5684)
- **SV2:SV2-D12 Pain reaches deeper than pleasure** (extends New.): In learning and in judging what is right, the tight fear of missing the truth brings out stronger powers than enjoyment, and reaches above wisdom to the essence of the soul. Humility, not sharpness, makes a vessel for the Essence. | Inside: A strong not-good feeling; straining to hear. | Lands: Learning for practice; ruling honestly in daily choices. | Q: ער טראגט זעך צוא זייער שטארק, און הערט זעך צוא, ציא איז פארט דער אמת אזוי — He strains very hard and listens: is it after all the truth? (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:4716)
- **SV2:SV2-D13 Goodness waiting in every soul** (extends Extends D6 (under all your names) and D7.): Every person holds all the root powers, goodness included, because the soul is whole. They may not show; great occasions and joy wake them. Feelings cannot be forced, but you can bind yourself to the matter, and the will follows. | Inside: Even a miser's generosity at his only son's wedding. | Lands: Choosing to think about the matter now; making a joyful occasion. | Q: וכחות ההיולים העצמיים ישנם בכאו"א ממש — The essential root powers are in each and every one. (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:6083)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- SV2:SV2-C01 [When G-d feels far away in prayer, and nothing moves you.] (M9) A father hides from his small son to test him. A foolish child thinks the hiding is real. He cries, gives up, and soon forgets his father. → (M7) A wise child knows it is a test. He runs after his father on the paths his father walked, seeing only his back, never his face. → (M4) When the father sees how hard the child runs in the dark, he turns around with double love. The love was there all along, hidden inside the hiding. → (M11) So the walls you hit in prayer, when you cannot feel anything, are that hiding. The wise child knows it is not real distance. → (M16) Keep going even if your heart is not on fire. Think it through until it is clear that it should be so, and let at least your outer will move toward Him. → (M15) All day, hold on to Torah and mitzvos because they are His wisdom and will, even when you feel nothing. It is true even if you do not feel it. → (M12) Distance is how the deepest closeness comes. Like dancers who step apart and then come together closer than if they had never moved apart. ⇒ BOTTOM: The bottom: the love was inside the hiding. Not feeling Him is His way of drawing you closer (stated, 3261). ⇒ RETURN: Down: the father 'turns his face' as double kisses in prayer and as 'face-light' in Torah (3265); in the day it is plain study and mitzvos kept without feeling (3278). - SV2:SV2-C02 ['G-d has forgotten me.' Can He forget?] (M1) The verse has Zion say 'G-d forgot me.' But there is no forgetting before His throne. How can He forget? → (M2) There are two kinds of forgetting. One is just not remembering, because you did not tie your mind to it. The other is when something else pushes it out, the way hunger wipes out the memory of being full. → (M9) For us the active forgetter is chasing this world's pleasures. It wipes away every light and every G-dly feeling in the soul. → (M5) But forgetting belongs only to the light that enters the worlds. The light that surrounds them and is not dressed in them cannot forget. → (M8) That light shines in every Jew all the time, in the point of the heart above reason, even in exile, even in sleep. 'I sleep, but my heart is awake.' → (M10) 'My image stays in you forever.' Your feelings and thoughts may wander here and there. The deepest point of you is not dressed in anything, so it cannot be forgotten. → (M15) When the soul's essence shines, there is no weakness and no forgetting. The task is to lift your small, forgettable love into that remembering. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the point of the heart above reason, sealed in you, which cannot forget and cannot be forgotten. ⇒ RETURN: Return: in prayer, raise 'the kindness of your youth', the small love that can be forgotten, into steady remembering; in the day, guard against the pleasures that make you forget. - SV2:SV2-C03 [Why would a 'don't' reach higher than a 'do'?] (M1) The 365 prohibitions are tied to the higher letters of G-d's name. Why would 'don't' be higher than 'do'? → (M2) A 'do' builds a vessel and draws down light that already fits vessels. It is like a garment cut to your size. A 'don't' builds nothing. It touches what is beyond fitting, like a house that surrounds you from afar. → (M9) Think of a great king. Small matters do not touch him at all; his soul is too high to be caught in them. → (M7) But a thing he cannot stand moves his whole self. Pushing away what he hates reaches deeper into him than doing good does. → (M10) So by the 'no' you know Him more truly: not by seeing what He is, but by knowing what He is not and cannot stand. Knowing by negation still knows the essence. → (M13) That is why the prohibitions are kept by sitting still and not doing. The strength is in holding back. → (M15) Nothing can block the essence. That is why the prohibitions still apply fully in exile, while many positive mitzvos have fallen away. In the darkest time, your 'no' still reaches Him. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the essence that no light reaches is reached by refusal, by the strength to not do. ⇒ RETURN: Return: in exile and darkness the 'no's and the fences are what keep love above reason alive (3394); the deed is simply holding back from what He does not want. - SV2:SV2-C04 [Is my bond with G-d stronger than my wants?] (M1) The forces against holiness also have a fierce will, even fiercer. So why say they have no 'shadow of a shadow', no deepest level? → (M2) Because the holy will cannot be otherwise. Your soul cannot be separated from G-d in any way at all. → (M9) That is why a Jew gives his life when it comes to it, even though he wants to live. He does not choose death. He simply cannot do otherwise. → (M13) The other side has many wants, each strong. Many wants confuse and weaken each other. The soul's deepest will is one simple will. → (M15) You see this in the deed itself: in the plain doing of a mitzvah, all mitzvos are equal. That sameness is the one will showing. → (M5) Against this, nothing stands. Opposites exist only among revealed things, not against the essence. → (M16) This is why a sudden thought of return comes to a person and he does not know where from. It reaches everyone; whoever wants closeness at all, feels it. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: one simple will that cannot be otherwise; nothing stands against it. ⇒ RETURN: Return: it shows in the plain deed (all mitzvos equal there), and in the sudden stirrings of teshuvah and the morning wish to serve. - SV2:SV2-C05 [Where is G-d in my plain, ordinary body?] (M9) Decide to stretch out your hand. It moves at once, with no delay at all. So your will has no organ of its own that it must pass through. → (M1) Yet food strengthens your limbs and your mind, but it does not change your will at all. → (M12) And still, when someone does what you want, life floods your whole mind and heart; when they anger you, it drains. How can both be true? → (M10) Because the will holds the limbs the way a man holds his clothing. He moves it; it does not hold him. → (M13) It is the same will in the head and in the foot, equally. It does not divide itself among the limbs. → (M6) So it is above: He holds all the worlds and nothing holds Him. The same will moves the highest worlds and this one. → (M15) There is no place empty of Him, equally. Your plain foot is held as fully as your thought. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: a will that holds and is not held, the same everywhere. ⇒ RETURN: Return: every movement of the hand is a small proof; the deed is moved by the same will as the highest thought. - SV2:SV2-C06 [What does G-d want from me down here?] (M14) G-d wanted a home below: that His very essence be revealed here below, just as above. → (M4) But thought cannot grasp Him. He is grasped only by the heart's simple will: 'To You, G-d, I lift my soul.' → (M11) That simple will comes out of the narrow place: 'They cried out to G-d in their distress', from the squeeze of life and from honestly counting how far you are. → (M10) A well: sea water is salty, but when it breaks up through the earth it becomes living, sweet water. → (M7) Your soul above had no such simple will. It gets it only by breaking through the darkness of the body. Down here, you become a well. → (M16) Before sleep, as you say 'Into Your hand I entrust my spirit', ask: to Whom am I handing my soul? → (M15) And how can I hand over a soul full of wrongs? Let the trembling and regret become a cry. Then the well flows up, and the answer comes down. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the simple will that cries out from the narrow place: the soul's own spring. ⇒ RETURN: Return: each night before sleep, the accounting and the cry; each day, Torah as the channel for the answer. - SV2:SV2-C07 [I don't feel G-d. I only feel how far I am.] (M3) At the lowest point you are not moved by G-d at all, because you grasp nothing of Him. You are moved only by how far you are. → (M8) 'From afar G-d appeared to me.' That small, general knowing is already something. → (M9) Like a small child who knows his father from far off and knows to call him 'Abba'. → (M12) This smallness is for the sake of rising. Like dancers stepping away from each other: the distance leads to the closest coming-together. → (M5) Then the father turns: first kisses that surround you, and then in the Amidah his face lights you inside, until you are nullified in your very being. → (M10) Why can the deepest come to the lowest? A father can tell his son 'do this' without explaining. The son can do it without knowing why. → (M15) So the very essence of the thing reaches the lowest place, through the plain doing, not through understanding. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the point that is almost nothing, bitterness at distance and 'Abba' called from afar. ⇒ RETURN: Return: the 'no's kept without explanation lift you (3743); the essence reaches you through the command obeyed without reasons. - SV2:SV2-C08 [How do I come to want only Him?] (M4) The more something stands before Him, the more it is nothing. 'Before Him' is exactly what makes it nothing. → (M6) All the worlds come only from His kingship, His standing above others. That is only a glimmer of a glimmer; it does not touch His self. → (M17) This is what the morning prayers rehearse at length: that the source of all worlds, high and low, is only a glimmer. → (M10) Then a thirst comes, stronger than hunger. In thirst, every other want drops away; only water matters. → (M16) You do not have to grasp the Essence. You only need to see clearly that what you grasp is a glimmer, and the thirst for Him comes. → (M15) And when you walk this way, the Father turns His face to you. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: a thirst in which all other wants are void. ⇒ RETURN: Return: the prayer from Hodu to Shema is the place for this contemplation; Torah and mitzvos ('Yisrael') draw the face-light down. - SV2:SV2-C09 [What does real joy in G-d feel like from inside?] (M8) Some souls pray like on Shabbos: no struggle, just the soul's own stirring toward G-d, a spark drawn to the flame. → (M9) Watch a melody sung from deep in the heart: the words of the thought you began with fall away. The song is above understanding. → (M5) The sweetness of a song can make a person faint, as if his soul would leave. The soul wants to be included in its root. → (M4) The song does not make the stirring. The stirring makes the song. It comes by itself. → (M2) Like a son who serves his father out of love and feels no work at all. Work is only where there is no pleasure. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: love above understanding that sings by itself. ⇒ RETURN: Return (and correction, next chain): the text immediately ranks the toiling servant above this joyful son. - SV2:SV2-C10 [Is the plain, joyless doing of what G-d asks really the lowest level?] (M8) A son serves his father gladly. His father's will is simply his own will. → (M8) A plain servant gives himself away as a servant. It is as if he does not exist in the world for himself at all. → (M2) He does not ask why. 'This is how it has to be.' → (M4) The loving servant stands above the son, because he bound himself by his own choice; the son is pulled by his father's power. → (M4) Yet the plain servant has an advantage over both: real work is doing what goes against your own nature and will. Loving service is not really work. → (M13) And he is steady: chopping wood is the same to him as handing the king a fine cup. → (M12) The loving servant rises and falls: sometimes his love and bond are hidden. → (M15) So 'Walk after the L-rd your G-d', the lowest level, without light, knowing or love, only because commanded, comes first.
