From there you reach 'cleave to Him': your essence joined to His. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: a self-giving so complete that there is no one left to take credit or pleasure, and it reaches the Essence. ⇒ RETURN: Return: kabbalas ol all day, in study, prayer, business, eating, sleep (3923); the plain deed done 'because so it must be'. - SV2:SV2-C11 [How do I know if I am serving G-d or just my habits?] (M1) The Sages said: one who reviews his lesson 100 times is not like one who reviews it 101 times. If your nature is to learn 100 times, the 101st time, against your nature, is the one that is serving G-d. → (M9) A stingy man who loves his money more than his body, and gives charity beyond his nature, is serving G-d; a generous man giving what he likes giving is not yet. → (M12) Your nature may not change at all. Holding it back against yourself still gives great pleasure above. → (M7) Someone who prays and learns all day out of habit from childhood may look the same, but 'the commandment of men learned by rote' is not serving G-d. → (M7) Man sees only the eyes; G-d sees the heart, whether the yoke is in it. → (M5) Even the plain servant has joy: the master's joy is his, because he is not a separate thing; he is like one of his master's limbs. → (M16) So he does not care that his name will not be on the work. → (M10) Even a little bread earned by your own sweat satisfies more than a fortune handed to you. So G-d takes more pleasure in the toil done by your own strength: it is something new from nothing. → (M15) This is 'pleasure before Me, that I spoke and My will was done': the deed, without any intention, simply done. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the bare deed done against nature, as the master's own limb. ⇒ RETURN: Return: one act each day beyond your habit, in learning, giving, holding the tongue, holding back from what is permitted. - SV2:SV2-C12 [My work is hard and I see no success. Is it worth anything?] (M1) 'By the sweat of your brow you will eat bread' is called a curse. But the more you work the land, the more bread. What kind of curse is that? → (M2) There are two kinds of livelihood. One is fixed on Rosh Hashanah and flows down like manna. The other is renewed every moment, from nothing, through toil. → (M10) A seed grows only after it rots. Something new comes only when what was there is undone. → (M11) So the more you are sad and near despair in your toil, seeing no success, the more that undoing becomes the growth. 'Those who sow in tears reap in joy.' → (M5) A blessing only draws down what already exists in the root. A prayer asks for a new will, 'may it be Your will', something not in the order at all. → (M15) Not everyone can bless. Everyone can pray. → (M15) In business: deal honestly and trust G-d to send blessing, not your own cleverness and strength. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the 'yesh' undone in the toil, like a rotting seed; out of it comes something truly new. ⇒ RETURN: Return: work honestly, pray for a new will, give beyond habit; accept the sweat as the blessing. - SV2:SV2-C13 [Why should the lowest, plain deed matter most?] (M9) Look at the heavens: no new star has ever appeared. The heavens are high, but they never make anything new. → (M10) The earth makes things grow from almost nothing, but only after the seed rots and is undone in it. → (M4) Why does the lowest have this power? Because 'their end is wedged in their beginning'. The lowest holds the power of the very first. → (M9) A builder: all his wanting and planning is worth nothing until the house stands. → (M8) When the building is finished, the pleasure returns him to his very self, to before he even wanted. → (M14) G-d is 'first' not as a source of some things but as the Essence from which all exists. → (M15) That is why the practical mitzvos came down so low: because they come from the Essence. Mere lights could not descend that far. 'The thing is very near to you.' ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the end that was first in thought; the deed as the place of the Essence. ⇒ RETURN: Return: the mitzvah done with the hand, in this world, is 'very near'. - SV2:SV2-C14 [How does my animal side itself come to love G-d?] (M3) To refine the animal soul, the G-dly soul must dress itself in what the animal soul can understand: that there is G-dly life in every thing, 'from my flesh I see G-d'. Work until even your natural mind gets it. → (M4) When great light simply overwhelms the animal soul, it vanishes for a while, but it has not been refined. → (M11) Even the 'lower waters' weep at how far they have fallen. The sparks in things want to rise. → (M10) There are two stairways: a ladder you see yourself climb, and a hidden spiral stair where you do not feel yourself rising, and suddenly you are at the top. → (M5) When the animal soul turns to love G-d, it is that hidden stair: from the other side to holiness every step is a leap, not by stages. → (M15) Light from above makes lower things vanish; refinement from below makes them themselves vessels. That is the real home below: in the end even bodies will rise from the dust as vessels for G-d. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the lower thing itself turned, by a leap it does not feel. ⇒ RETURN: Return: contemplate in prayer at the level your animal side understands; eat what is needed for strength to serve. - SV2:SV2-C15 [If I am truly nullified, what does it look like?] (M9) A truly wise person is humble: 'every proud man is a fool'. He knows he has not reached the truth of anything yet, and he sees the good in others. → (M5) But the humility of wisdom comes from weakness: you sense something higher than you. The bittul of the crown comes from strength: it does not know itself at all. → (M10) Moshe was 'heavy of speech' because he was so nullified. Like one standing very close before a king: he cannot speak, he stands silent as a stone, and whispers without knowing what he says. → (M4) One who is crushed in essence (the text says in Yiddish 'broken in essence') is not like one who humbles himself by thinking. Bittul made by contemplation ends when the contemplation stops, and you go back to yourself. → (M10) Noise comes from what still exists. Wood crackles until it is fully burnt. The Shema is fire and noise; the Amidah is a still, small voice. → (M8) The flame of a candle pulls up from the wick to its source, though it would go out. That pull is wisdom. The crown is already included, like a silent stone. → (M5) Then: silence. 'Be silent before G-d.' Like the inanimate, with no spreading out and no selfhood at all. → (M15) In practice: conquer your will in every faculty, not by reasoning. Mind only on G-d and Torah, no foreign thought, no forbidden speech, eyes and ears closed to evil. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the silence of the crown that does not know itself. ⇒ RETURN: Return: guard thought, speech and senses all day, from bittul, not argument; let the Amidah be a whisper. - SV2:SV2-C16 [Is the world real, or an illusion?] (M2) Truth is what never stops. A river that runs dry is called a lie. → (M1) The world is not a lie. The Mishnah says: of two who 'pick' cucumbers by magic, the one who does a real act is liable; the one who only fools the eyes is exempt. If the world were only an appearance, there would be no reward, punishment or mitzvos. → (M12) The world really exists, but it is not true existence: it could stop if the Creator willed. → (M12) So there are two knowledges, and both are true: from the light that is the source of worlds, we are real; from His Essence, only He is real. → (M4) 'All is as nothing before Him' still has someone there, feeling that He alone is. → (M5) 'There is none else' means no existence at all, only the Essence. → (M15) You reach it by the servant's work: being no existence for yourself at all. → (M15) Only someone lowly to the very end receives it, and it starts with 'Modeh ani' the moment you wake: giving yourself with your whole self, acknowledging what you do not grasp. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: 'there is none else', no one even feeling 'He alone'. ⇒ RETURN: Return: the world is kept and real (no illusion); the practice is the servant's self-giving and the morning thanks. - SV2:SV2-C17 [Why does the struggle to know what is right reach so deep?] (M1) A great scholar knows the answer clearly. But when he must rule on a real case, doubts come: maybe it is not so. Contradictions appear. → (M9) He strains with all his might and listens: is this really the truth? (The text says it in Yiddish.) → (M5) That strain reaches the power of his very soul, above even the power of his wisdom. → (M7) You can study by taste, because you enjoy the matter. Or you can study from fear that you might miss the truth. → (M4) That fear is felt as a strong not-good-feeling, a tightness, and it brings out stronger powers than enjoyment. → (M2) For pain reaches deeper into the soul than pleasure. → (M13) That is why the law follows the humble school of Hillel: their humility made them vessels for the Essence. Sharpness reaches only wisdom. → (M15) And the simple, unlearned Jew reaches the same Essence by his plain service under the yoke. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the tightness of 'maybe I am wrong', held for the sake of truth, reaching above wisdom. ⇒ RETURN: Return: learn to know what to do; let fear of missing the truth drive the effort; honor the simple Jew's yoke as reaching the same place. - SV2:SV2-C18 [Am I doing this alone?] (M10) A small child walks with his father behind him, holding and pushing. The child thinks he walks by himself. → (M9) So the morning stirring to serve G-d, and the heavenly calls, are felt only as a pull of the heart (Yiddish: 'it gives a tug at the heart'). → (M11) Sometimes the light compels: you cannot be without some service; you are forced to serve G-d somehow. → (M5) Sometimes the Father lifts the child onto His shoulders, like a dwarf on a giant's shoulders who sees far. That was the desert and Sinai. → (M11) In exile He is hidden, but 'He stands behind our wall, watching through the windows', raising the fallen without their knowing. → (M16) In the Ten Days of Return, every Jew finds teshuvah easier: the flame is near, so the spark wakes by itself. → (M10) Like a good teacher who leads a student from the side (Yiddish) until the student finds the truth and thinks it was all his own. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the hidden hand under every step you think is yours. ⇒ RETURN: Return: walk as if it is your own effort (it is), knowing the Father holds you; use the days when He is near. - SV2:SV2-C19 [Why can't I see G-d? Is He hiding?] (M1) G-d did not change by making the world. He is not like a craftsman whose strength gets caught in his work. → (M10) The worlds come from His 'name' only. A person's powers show who he is and he enjoys them; but his name gives life to his body, and he would feel no difference if he were called Avraham or Yitzchak. → (M4) From the light you cannot know Him at all. You cannot even say He is 'a source of light'. → (M6) Only from creation out of nothing do we know: His existence is from Himself. → (M2) When something else hides a thing, the hiding is forced. When a thing hides itself, the hiding is by will, and a willed hiding is not a hiding. → (M12) Only the Infinite can hide itself. So His hiding is only toward the worlds, not toward Himself: there He shines fully, and the worlds still remain. → (M11) Even our sins only seem to hide Him, because they are hateful and opposite. 'Your sins separate between you and your G-d', between you, not on His side. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: He is fully present and hidden by His own will; the hiding is not real distance. ⇒ RETURN: Return: before prayer, 'heaviness of head' (bitterness over the crookedness that made the screen) lifts the soul 'on eagles' wings... to Me' (5824). - SV2:SV2-C20 [Why did my soul have to come down into this body?] (M1) The soul did not want to come down. 'Against your will you live.' → (M9) Even good Jews feel love and awe only at prayer; the rest of the day the mind is small. That is a great fall from a high roof. → (M4) But the soul's true root shines only when it comes down. Above, it is 'standing'; below, it can 'walk'. → (M10) Taking a step needs only your measured strength. Leaping from a high mountain to a far place needs every hidden strength you have. → (M9) In a moment of great fear a person lifts a load many cannot. The hidden strength is there, but only a true strongman brings it out at will. → (M5) Your body was made separately, from dust, lower than the animals; your soul is higher than all. To join them, He 'blew from within'. → (M10) To lift something with a lever you grip its lowest end. That is why the soul was put in the lowest body: to lift everything. → (M15) Below, love can leave all vessels and want to be included in the Essence: 'with all your might'. And it often comes from the narrow place itself. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the soul's root, which shows only in the leap down into the lowest body. ⇒ RETURN: Return: the daily refinement of the body and the world, holding the lowest end of the lever. - SV2:SV2-C21 [Is my soul really pure if I have done wrong?] (M2) 'The soul You gave me is pure.' Pure is not the same as holy. Holy is set apart in essence. Pure means of the same kind as others, only nothing covers it. → (M4) Purity is only said of something that came out of impurity. → (M10) Letters written in ink are another substance laid on the parchment. They cover what is under them. → (M10) Letters engraved in a gem are the gem itself. Your soul's root is engraved, not written. → (M12) Where the gem is engraved it looks a little darker. That is only less brilliance, not a covering. → (M3) Before prayer your soul is still 'in your nostrils', not yet spread into your body; crookedness weighs on the head like a heavy load. → (M15) The holy heaviness is bitterness over the distance and pity on your own soul. Then 'I carried you on eagles' wings and brought you to Me'. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the engraved root of the soul, never covered, only less bright. ⇒ RETURN: Return: before prayer, a moment of bitterness and self-compassion, and charity; then prayer lifts to the root. - SV2:SV2-C22 [Is there goodness in me even when I don't feel kind?] (M8) Every person has the root powers in full, including essential goodness, simply because the soul is whole. → (M1) Yet someone can still be cruel or foolish, because the root power is not drawn out at all. → (M9) Even a stingy, cruel man becomes generous at the wedding of his only son: joy reaches the root powers. → (M7) A 'good person' is called good even when not doing kindness, because the goodness in his heart is ready to wake at any moment. → (M15) You cannot force a feeling or a new insight; revelations cannot be forced. But you can force yourself to bind your mind to the matter, and then the will appears by itself. → (M2) Because only revelations cannot be compelled; the essence can. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: the root goodness and bond present in everyone, not dependent on feeling. ⇒ RETURN: Return: when no feeling comes, bind the mind by choice; gladness and great occasions wake the root. - SV2:SV2-C23 [Why does a small mitzvah matter so much?] (M7) Some wants are for a purpose: you want business for the profit, a house because you need somewhere to live. Such wants can change. → (M2) An inner want is the purpose itself. It cannot be explained, only 'this is what I want'. → (M4) G-d's will in the mitzvos is His inner will. It is not from lack: He is complete, so His will is above reason. You do it with your reasoned will, and He makes your will like His. → (M13) That is why part of a mitzvah can count as all of it. The Baal Shem Tov: when you grasp a part of the essence, you grasp all of it. → (M15) And that is why the mitzvos come in physical things: anything spiritual is already a vessel and a mixture; the physical is not a vessel at all, so the bare will can rest in it. ⇒ BOTTOM: Bottom: His inner will, undivided, wholly present in any part. ⇒ RETURN: Return: do the small physical mitzvah fully; it holds all of Him.

## SV3 — Samach Vav III

This slice is the end of Samach Vav and a key to the whole of it. It holds the close of the last maamar (Vaera 5668) and its completion on Rosh Hashana, Yom Kippur and Shabbos; the Rashab's own notes; the Rebbe Rayatz's explanations of the opening; and a printed summary of all 61 maamarim (the publishers' 'content, chapter heads and summary'; its author is not named in the slice). So it shows the Hemshech's whole climb in small: why there is a world at all (a longing with no reason), why no rise ever reaches Him ('up to, and not including'), and where He is reached instead: the plain deed, the yoke of Heaven, the simple servant, teshuva and the toil of Torah. It also shows how this is revealed (through Torah, through the 'sons'), and that the goal is His home down here. (stated)

What it adds to the full depth: (1) The deepest delight cannot be felt, and the texts place the touch of it in what feels like the opposite of pleasure. That means the contracted fear of missing His will, the bitterness before prayer, His hidden face, the dry walk, the craftsman bent into his work. DESCENT's bottom (D8, the bond) is right. SV3 adds that the way into it often feels tight or dark, and that this is a door, not a failure. (2) The lowest-looking service reaches highest. Son, faithful servant and simple servant are ranked, and the simple servant 'whose whole existence is the master's existence' reaches the 'truth of truth': 'there is nothing else', which is beyond even 'all before Him is as nothing'. (3) The return is deeper than the ascent. The 'return' is more your own than the 'run', and it is rooted in the Essence. The end of a deed holds more of the beginning than the head does. In the future the body comes first. (4) The Rayatz's three knowings: knowing what it is, knowing it is beyond that, and grasping it, where 'he loses his whole existence', broken and lifted at once. The worker who feels it in a deed knows it better than the greatest thinker. (stated, each point; the arrangement is ours)

For the guide (ours): this slice gives taps for people DESCENT serves less well. For the dry and dark there is SV3-D4 (keep walking; held from behind). For the scrupulous scholar there is SV3-D1 (the tightness of caring is a door). For the returner there is SV3-D3 (a run with no goal). For the simple doer there is SV3-D5 and SV3-D6 (the deed, the low job, done as His). For the one who fears not being enough there is SV3-D7 (wanted 'even if he is not as he should be'). Guards are written into each depth. Never use contraction or bitterness with someone anxious, depressed or self-hating. Never use 'nothing for yourself' as a reason for anyone to accept mistreatment.

## Depths

- **SV3:SV3-D1 Tightness is a door** (extends Sharpens D7 and D8 (and corrects any rule that the deep place must feel sweet)): The deepest delight is not felt, and the text says you can touch it while feeling the opposite of pleasure: the tight, afraid, contracted heart of someone who only wants to get G-d's will right. Felt joy is a lower level; the narrowing that comes from caring about His truth reaches higher. In prayer too, light comes 'in the measure of the bitterness'. | Inside: The text's images: fear and contraction of soul (יראה וכיווץ); the craftsman whose powers are bent into the work and feel no joy while working; bitterness before nearness. | Lands: In the body: the tight chest at the moment of trying. Now: during the task, not after. Deed: finishing the task without waiting for it to feel good. | Q: ואדרבה, הוא בבחי' יראה וכיווץ בנפשו, היפך העונג — on the contrary, he is in fear and contraction of soul, the opposite of pleasure (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:6241)
- **SV3:SV3-D2 The knower is lost: broken and lifted at once** (extends Extends D5 (where knowing ends) and D3/D4): There are three ways of knowing something deep. Knowing what it is. Knowing that it is beyond all that, which lifts and widens you, though 'you are still there'. And grasping the thing itself, where you 'lose your whole existence'. From inside, the third feels like confusion: knowing and not knowing, broken and lifted, all at once. That is what 'love in delights' is. And you cannot jump to it: the deeper you understand plainly, the deeper the 'not this' can go. | Inside: Yiddish images in the text: 'he will dig deeper, to where the definition does not apply'; 'he loses his whole existence'; brokenness and upliftedness together; short points, wondrous ideas. | Lands: The text lands it in the deed: what one feels in a deed holds more than the idea (6537), and the worker who feels it knows it better than the greatest thinker (6529). | Q: ער ועט אוף גראבין טיפער ויא דער ענין דער גדר איז דארטין ניט שייך — he will dig deeper, to where the definition of the matter does not apply (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:6552)
- **SV3:SV3-D3 A run with no goal** (extends Extends D2 (no one left to watch) and D8; new route through teshuva): Teshuva's run is unlike the run of great love. It does not want to reach a level or melt into light. It wants only to leave the old place, and it gives itself over 'without any goal'. Being near G-d at all is good enough. Out of that narrow place comes the strongest leap, 'to the Essence itself'. | Inside: 'He does feel a G-dly light, but as how bad and bitter'; 'the general of G-dliness is good for him'; the narrow strait and pressure; a leap and a jump. | Lands: In the body: the turning away from the old thing. Now: the moment of leaving. Deed: one thing not done today that you used to do. | Q: והרצוא שלו הוא הנתינה והמסירה שמוסר א"ע לאלקות, אן קיין שום מבוקש ותכלית להגיע לידי איזה מדרי' שתהיי — his running is the handing over of himself to G-dliness, without any aim of reaching any level (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:6570)
- **SV3:SV3-D4 When He hides His face, keep walking** (extends New (between D8 and D9; a depth for dry times)): Prayer is called 'behind': a son walking after his father who does not turn around. When no light shines in your soul and you still keep going in Torah and mitzvos, the text says the father's love doubles. Under every forgetting is a point that never forgets: 'my heart is awake'. And even the walking you think is your own is held from behind, hidden on purpose so the step is yours. | Inside: The father who hides his face and kisses double; the infant looking for his father; the child learning to walk, pushed from behind and thinking he walks alone; 'my heart is awake'. | Lands: In the body: walking, standing for the Amida. Now: the dry moment itself. Deed: the next mitzvah in the dark. | Q: כמשל האב המסתיר פניו מבנו, ואעפ"כ מתאמץ הבן לרוץ אחרי אביו, שעי"ז ניתוסף אהבת האב לנשקו בכפלים — like a father who hides his face from his son, and still the son strains to run after him, and through this the father's love grows to kiss him doubly (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:7276)
- **SV3:SV3-D5 Up to, and not including** (extends Sharpens D9 (the bottom sends you back) and D12): Every rise, even in Gan Eden, is a jump that costs your former self, and still no rise reaches Him. The higher you go, the more wondrous He is. Climbing to Him is 'up to and not including'. What reaches the Essence is not the top of the ladder but the plain deed, which was first in His thought before any world. The builder delights in his very self only when the house stands. | Inside: The river of fire between levels; Yerushalmi that needs you to forget Bavli; 'the end holds more of the beginning than the head'; the seed that rots before the earth grows. | Lands: Hands, now, one mitzvah. | Q: ובכל העליות לא יגיעו לעצמות התענוג, ואדרבה, שנפלא מהם יותר — in all the rises they do not reach the essence of delight; on the contrary, it is more wondrous from them (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:7033)
- **SV3:SV3-D6 Nothing for yourself, not even light** (extends Extends D11 (called into being: the command) and D2): The simple servant has no joy of his own, yet that his master's will was done by him is 'all his life'. He keeps nothing: not physical things and not spiritual ones, not even the glow of his own service. His pleasure is the master's, his work counts as the master's own handiwork, and chopping wood is the same to him as handing over a precious cup. Through him the truest truth appears: 'his whole existence is only the existence of the master'. | Inside: An ox under the yoke; chopping wood and handing a precious cup; 'my soul like dust to all'; 'gladden the soul of Your servant, for to You I lift my soul'. | Lands: The low job, now, with the hands. | Q: ועבד כזה אין לו שמחה ונח"ר לעצמו, ומ"מ, מה שנתמלא רצון האדון מעבודתו זוהי כל חיותו ומציאותו — such a servant has no joy for himself, yet that the master's will was fulfilled by his work is his whole life and existence (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:7347)
- **SV3:SV3-D7 Wanted in your being** (extends Sharpens D6 (under all your names) and D8 (the bond)): Joy holds on to how things look; will holds on to what a thing is. The sign: a father wants his son 'even if he is not as he should be'. The mitzvah is that kind of will, His wanting of you, with a hidden joy inside it. Your own deepest will, the yechida's, is one and undivided, 'a strength of will that cannot be separated from G-d'; the other side has strong wills too, but many, and they weaken each other. | Inside: A father and a son who is not as he should be; will that turns pain into joy; many wills that weaken each other against one will that does not divide. | Lands: Doing one mitzvah as His wanting you; now; with the body. | Q: כמו הרצון במהות הבן גם אם אינו כדבעי — like the will in the very being of the son, even if he is not as he should be (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:7096)
- **SV3:SV3-D8 Rest in itself** (extends Extends D13 (plainness is the deepest)): The craftsman feels no joy while working. When he finishes, he is glad at the good work; then glad in his very self; then glad with a gladness that no longer hangs on anything. Shabbos climbs the same way, to a delight 'that has no hold in anything', and at the root it is 'rest in itself', not rest from something. | Inside: The craftsman; Friday night, Shabbos day, the third meal; 'rest after toil, and rest in itself'. | Lands: After a finished task; on Shabbos afternoon. | Q: שמתענג בעצם, כמו בכל תענוג עצמי שאינו בא בסיבת איזה דבר — delighting in itself, like any essential delight that does not come because of anything (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:6435)
- **SV3:SV3-D9 There is nothing else, beyond 'as nothing'** (extends Extends D3 and D4): There is 'none besides You' (apart from Him nothing, yet with Him there is light) and 'nothing besides You' (less even than 'none'). There is the higher unity, 'all before Him is as nothing', and above it 'truth of truth: there is nothing else', which is beyond even that bittul. It is not reached by thinking but by stillness: 'I composed and quieted myself', the silence of the yechida; and the humble, not the sharp, reach it. | Inside: Standing before a king and unable to speak; streams that run dry; David's stillness; 'my soul like dust to all'. | Lands: The silent Amida; a gentle answer to someone (Beis Hillel). | Q: ואמת לאמיתו - אין עוד שלמע' גם מהביטול דכולא קמי' כל''ח — and truth of truth: 'there is none else', above even the bittul of 'all before Him is as nothing' (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:7493)
- **SV3:SV3-D10 The return is the deeper half** (extends Sharpens D9 (the bottom sends you back) and D14): The pull upward comes as a gift each morning and passes fast. The coming back down into the day is more your own than the rising, and it is rooted in His Essence, His wish for a home below. Going up is like taking off a coat; coming back is putting it on, and that is harder. Even one who never had great moments has this: he finds no rest without Torah and mitzvos all day. And through prayer as 'behind', especially the return, the essential hiddenness is drawn. | Inside: A flame that needs air to stay on the wick; Aaron's sons who rose and did not return; taking off is easier than putting on. | Lands: The first ordinary task after prayer. | Q: ואדרבה, השוב הוא בכח עצמו יותר מהרצוא — on the contrary, the return is more by one's own power than the run (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:7161)
- **SV3:SV3-D11 Your being is where only He works** (extends Extends D1 and D10): What you feel of yourself is your powers: thinking, feeling, wanting. The plain fact that you exist is hidden, and making something from nothing belongs to the Essence alone. G-d's life in a thing is not like sight in an eye (a blind eye is still an eye); without that life the thing would not be there at all, not even its 'vessel'. In the future the body will come first. | Inside: The mother's hidden part from which the child's body comes; the eye that can be blind and still an eye, unlike a creature without G-d's life. | Lands: The body, now. | Q: מה שמאיר בו עתה בגילוי הוא בחי' הכחות הנפשיים שבו, לא גוף ההתהוות שלו — what shines in him openly now is his soul-powers, not his very coming-into-being (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:6263)
- **SV3:SV3-D12 The soundless cry** (extends Extends D6 and D8; the shofar route): What reaches the Essence on Rosh Hashana is not a word but a cry of the heart with no sound, which 'moves the very essence of the soul', together with giving oneself over not by reason, clever as a person and following like an animal. Even the animal soul's own cry is part of it. On Rosh Hashana service is from the essence of the soul, 'the torch brought near the spark'. | Inside: The ram's horn; the cry no one hears; the torch and the spark. | Lands: Chest and throat; the moment before a request in prayer. | Q: צעקת הלב בבחי' קלא פנימאה דלא אשתמע, שזהו הזזת עצם הנפש ממש — the cry of the heart, an inner voice not heard, the moving of the very essence of the soul (Chassidus-txt/R. Shalom Dovber Schneersohn (Rebbe Rashab) (1860-1920)/Hemshech Samach Vav (scans, OCR).txt:6400)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- SV3:SV3-C01 [6201] (M3) Every limit you run into, every 'this far and no further', starts at one place: G-d's first holding-back, when He said to His world 'enough'. → (M6) The Torah comes from before that holding-back. It is not a message about Him from far away; it is one with Him. → (M1) So here is the puzzle: something that high came down into a voice you could hear with your ears, and into deeds done with your hands. That is not a step down; it is a jump. → (M2) A leap never happens by steps. It happens only by a hidden strength, the kind that is not used up in ordinary showing. → (M5) That hidden strength is His own delight. Torah is called His 'plaything'; the deepest thing in Him, His pleasure, is what reaches all the way down. → (M15) Because it is His delight, it does not stop halfway. It comes 'from the highest of high to the lowest of low, even to us, souls in bodies'. When you say 'asher kideshanu' over a mitzvah, you are naming that delight. ⇒ BOTTOM: His essential delight: the one thing strong enough to leap all the way down. ⇒ RETURN: So by Torah and mitzvos, done here in a body, a person is lifted into His inner essence (6214). The return is the blessing over the next mitzvah: 'who made us holy' is said by a body, about a deed. - SV3:SV3-C02 [6216] (M2) There are two kinds of simple joy: the kind you can feel, and the kind that is too simple to be felt at all. → (M7) Joy that sits in a feeling is mixed with the feeler. The simplest joy is the light that has no hold on any vessel. → (M5) Even the very core of the light is still 'light', something given off. Deeper is His own name held inside Him, the hidden Eden no eye has seen. → (M4) And the surprise: you do not reach that unfelt joy by understanding. You reach it by doing a mitzvah that was 'not given for enjoyment'. → (M15) The same is true of the plain work of the day: making your own animal side and the low things around you into vessels for G-d. That is His home below. ⇒ BOTTOM: The unfelt delight: His name inside Him, hidden Eden. ⇒ RETURN: Back to a deed whose pleasure you do not feel, done because He asked. The text says this is exactly where the deepest delight lives. - SV3:SV3-C03 [6237] (M17) Torah also has two layers: clear light, and truth buried under questions that you have to dig out. → (M5) If you dig for the reason, you reach the hidden root of wisdom. If you dig because you must know what He truly wants, you reach Him beyond wisdom. → (M12) Here is the turn: the one who studies this way often feels no pleasure at all. His heart tightens with fear of getting it wrong. That tightness, the opposite of pleasure, is what touches the pleasure that cannot be felt. → (M15) This only happens down here. Torah up above is clear, with nothing to dig. Only Torah in a world of doubt lets you reach the root. → (M6) Why you and not an angel? Angels are made of His 'speech', things that stand on their own. Your soul is cut into Him like letters engraved in stone, not ink laid on top. ⇒ BOTTOM: The tight, unpleasant fear of missing His truth is a touch of the delight no one can feel. ⇒ RETURN: Open the page you are stuck on. The struggle with the question is the contact, not the obstacle to it. - SV3:SV3-C04 [6254] (M2) The lowest level in G-d's order, Malchus, the receiver, has the highest root of all. → (M9) Look at birth. The father's part is visible and stirs things up. But the child's very body comes from the mother, from the hidden side. → (M6) Making something from nothing belongs only to the Essence. Nothing else can do it. → (M9) Notice this in yourself: what you feel of yourself is your powers, your thinking and feeling. Not the plain fact that you exist. That fact is the hidden part, and it is the deepest. → (M12) So the deed and the study need each other. The deed wakes the Essence. The study lets it be seen. → (M15) In the end the body comes first. The text says that in the future the soul will receive its life from the body. ⇒ BOTTOM: Your plain being, not your powers, is the place where only the Essence works. ⇒ RETURN: Do one plain deed with the body. It wakes what study will later light up. - SV3:SV3-C05 [6276] (M2) 'We will hear' does not mean hearing with the ears. It means the heart accepts it and finds it sweet. → (M7) 'We will do' is the opposite: doing it when you have no wish for it at all, the way a forced document is still binding. → (M5) Putting 'do' before 'hear' was not just obedience. It was laying down their very selves. → (M6) And that is why it reached so high: a self laid down meets His own hiddenness. ⇒ BOTTOM: A self laid down, doing before feeling, meets His hidden Essence. ⇒ RETURN: Do the next mitzvah before the heart agrees. The heart's 'yes' may come after; it is the second crown, not the first. - SV3:SV3-C06 [6318] (M5) The very hidden Essence is reached only by the plain servant who serves because he was told. → (M12) But what he reaches, he cannot show. The returner draws the Essence up; the steady righteous one lets it shine down. Each needs the other. → (M4) So the one who looks lower is truly higher. Yehuda, the shepherd who works from below, is above Yosef the ruler, even if Yosef shines now. → (M8) Higher still: a rich man who calls out like a poor man. A scholar who sets his whole mind aside to learn only what G-d wants. → (M12) But the text holds back: it is hard to say scholars get more, since 'all Israel have a share'. The son-made-servant is higher only because he has both: he wakes the Essence and also reveals it. ⇒ BOTTOM: The simple servant reaches what the bright son cannot; the bright son reveals what the servant reached. ⇒ RETURN: Whatever your level, add the servant's part: one thing today done only because He asked, without judging it. - SV3:SV3-C07 [6363] (M2) G-d's life is inside every single thing, to the last blade of grass. Each one has a force over it saying: grow. → (M1) Then how can He say 'I have not changed'? If He is inside all of it, giving it life, surely something in Him moved. → (M10) Like a king whose name is called over many lands. His name rules there; he himself is not spread out in them. → (M4) That is why Rosh Hashana says 'remembrance'. You only need to remember something far and of no account. To Him, the whole world's life is that small. → (M12) This does not mean He looks away. His care for each detail never stops for a moment. Remembrance asks for more: that what surrounds the world should shine inside it. → (M13) Two opposites, the G-d beyond the world and the G-d in it, can be one only where neither takes up any place: in Him Himself. → (M8) What reaches there is not a word. It is the cry of the heart that makes no sound, the shofar, which moves the very essence of the soul. → (M15) And the horn is a ram's: people clever as humans who follow You like animals. Even the animal soul's own cry is part of the shofar. ⇒ BOTTOM: The soundless cry that moves the essence of the soul meets the Essence, where beyond-the-world and in-the-world are one. ⇒ RETURN: Afterward come the shofar verses: after you have done all you can, what is above all doing is revealed ('You were revealed'), 6405; then Yom Kippur. - SV3:SV3-C08 [6405] (M15) The deepest gift cannot be earned. And still it only comes after you have done everything you could. → (M2) Yom Kippur is that level now. No food, no drink: a joy that is not felt. → (M5) It is called 'Sabbath of Sabbaths', a rest even from the rest of Shabbos. Shabbos still has felt joy; this is beyond it. → (M6) That is why forgiveness comes then. 'With You is forgiveness': from what is with You, Yourself, not from anything You send out. → (M16) 'And we will live before Him': before Him, truly. Not near a light from Him; before Him. ⇒ BOTTOM: Forgiveness from 'with You', a joy too simple to be felt. ⇒ RETURN: The fast is the body's share: not eating is how the body takes part in the unfelt delight. - SV3:SV3-C09 [6427] (M9) Think of a craftsman at work. While he works, all his powers are bent down into the task. He feels no joy then; the bending is the opposite of joy. → (M3) When he finishes and rests, his powers come back up. He looks at the work, sees it came out right, and enjoys it. → (M8) Then the joy climbs. He is glad not just at the beauty of the thing, but in his very self, at the whole of it. → (M5) And then a joy that hangs on nothing. It came because of the thing, but it is no longer about the thing. He is simply glad, in himself. → (M10) So it is above: six days of making, then Shabbos. Friday night the joy of the details; Shabbos day the joy itself; the third meal, a joy 'that has no hold on anything'. → (M14) At the root, this is 'rest in itself'. Not rest from something. Rest. ⇒ BOTTOM: A joy that hangs on nothing: rest in itself. ⇒ RETURN: 'Whoever toiled on Friday eats on Shabbos' (7158). The ladder starts with real work done well. - SV3:SV3-C10 [6541] (M2) To know what love is: love is nearness and feeling, and nearness wants to become one with the one you love. → (M4) There is a second knowing: knowing that it is beyond all those definitions. This knowing does not shrink you; it lifts and widens you. → (M17) The way down is through the first knowing, not around it. The deeper you understand plainly, the deeper the 'not this' goes. → (M5) A third knowing grasps the thing itself. Here you do not rise. You lose your whole existence in it. → (M12) From inside it feels like confusion: knowing and not knowing at once, broken and lifted at once. → (M8) That is what 'love with delights' is: everything is open before you, and you are not there. → (M15) And it is not only for great souls. Even a little of it, in a simple soul, is truly a piece of the Essence. ⇒ BOTTOM: Knowing that loses the knower: broken and lifted at once. ⇒ RETURN: The worker who feels it in a deed knows it better than the greatest thinker (6529); feeling is fuller in the deed than in the idea (6537). - SV3:SV3-C11 [6565] (M2) Teshuva is not first a wish to climb. It is a wish to get out: to leave the old place and pull out every old wanting. → (M9) And the returner does feel G-d's light. He feels it as bitterness: how bad it was to leave Him. → (M13) 'We sinned before You' comes first, then each sin. The 'before You' makes every detail sharper. → (M5) So his running has no goal. He is not trying to reach a level or be swallowed in light. He just gives himself over. Being near G-d at all is good for him. → (M5) And this is the one that leaps furthest. Out of the narrow place, with more force, a jump, to the Essence itself. → (M12) Even the loud road of teshuva has a quiet center: a cry inside that makes no sound. ⇒ BOTTOM: A giving-over with no goal, from the narrow place, leaps to the Essence. ⇒ RETURN: Name one thing to leave behind today, and do not ask what level it buys. - SV3:SV3-C12 [7023] (M1) Why make a world? Not to show what He can do. Up there, His power never lacks its act; nothing is missing. → (M1) And not so He would be known. Knowing could happen in the high worlds. Why make this world, where you cannot feel Him at all? → (M14) The answer is: He wanted it. A longing with no reason that the mind can give. → (M6) So the deed came first in His thought, before the first thought of any world. That is why a plain deed reaches His very delight. → (M10) Like someone building a house: the wish, the plan, the feelings, then the hands. Only when the house stands does he delight in his very self, back to before he ever wanted it. → (M15) The earth shows it. A seed must rot first. Then the ground grows far more than was planted. The end holds more of the beginning than the head does. ⇒ BOTTOM: A longing with no reason, which the deed fulfils. ⇒ RETURN: The next ordinary deed is where His first wanting comes home. - SV3:SV3-C13 [7028] (M4) Even in Gan Eden, every rise is a jump with no ladder. To go up, the soul has to stop being what it was. → (M9) You know this from prayer. Nearness comes after bitterness and lowliness, and the light comes in the measure of the bitterness. → (M11) Every rise needs a river in between, something that cuts you off, so you come to the end of your being, and only then go up. → (M10) And yet: all those rises never reach Him. In fact He becomes more wondrous, further, the higher you go. → (M2) There are two kinds of 'up to'. One includes the place you aim for. The other never includes it. Climbing to Him is the second. → (M15) So the Essence is not at the top of the ladder. It is in the deed. 'Your command is very wide': every other joy has an edge; only the mitzvah's does not. ⇒ BOTTOM: No rise reaches Him; He is wider than every height. ⇒ RETURN: Stop climbing for a moment and do the mitzvah in front of you. It has no edge. - SV3:SV3-C14 [7086] (M2) Joy is deeper than wanting. You only want what you would enjoy. → (M12) But wanting is also deeper. A strong will can turn pain into joy, as when someone gives his life for G-d's name. → (M4) In truth both come from one root in the self. Joy is the inner side, hidden; will is the outer side, shown, and runs with all its strength. → (M9) Here is the sign. A father wants his son even when the son is not as he should be. Joy holds on to how things look; will holds on to who you are. → (M15) The mitzvah is that will: His wanting of you, with a hidden joy inside it. And 'He will do for the one who waits for Him': you hope, and you bow. ⇒ BOTTOM: You are wanted in your being, even not as you should be. ⇒ RETURN: Do the mitzvah as His wanting, and wait; the joy inside it is His, and it is hidden for now. - SV3:SV3-C15 [7125] (M2) Your soul is a flame. It wants to go up. But what it is made of is breath, the breath G-d blew in. → (M10) A flame needs air. Without air it goes out; and air also keeps it on the wick so it does not fly off. → (M11) The pull upward is not even yours by nature. Your soul came down. The pull is a wake-up from above, the one that comes every morning. → (M15) That wake-up passes fast. What you do with it is yours: the slow work of thinking and loving, by your own effort. → (M4) And coming back down, the 'return', is more yours than the rising. In the rising you feel the help from above; all day long, when you are not near, it is only you. → (M5) The return is rooted in His Essence: He wanted a home down here. Going up is easy, like taking off a coat. Putting it back on is harder. → (M16) If you never had the great moments, the return is still yours: you keep at Torah and mitzvos all day, and you find no rest without them. ⇒ BOTTOM: The return is rooted in His wanting a home here. ⇒ RETURN: Come back to the day's tasks on purpose. That coming back is the deeper half. - SV3:SV3-C16 [7148] (M2) Your soul did not come down to fix itself. It came to refine the body and the animal soul. → (M10) Think of sunlight. It always needs the sun; if the sun were hidden it would be gone. So every created thing needs, every moment, the One who makes it from nothing. → (M10) But the picture breaks. Sunlight is like the sun. You are not like what makes you. So you are even less than the ray is. → (M5) Then the Shema: thinking of His Essence beyond being any source. Not 'I am small', but there is nothing besides. → (M15) And in that fire even the animal soul is consumed. Then all day there is more refining, in thought and in action. ⇒ BOTTOM: Not like the ray, less than the ray: nullified in being before the Essence. ⇒ RETURN: The second Temple had no fire from heaven, and the offering was still kosher, 'for the main thing is the person's own toil' (7154). - SV3:SV3-C17 [7345] (M2) A son serves his father by nature. His father's will is his will. It is no burden; it is joy. → (M7) A simple servant works against his own will, like an ox under the yoke, until it becomes his nature. He gave himself to be a servant and let go of all his own existence. → (M5) He has no joy of his own. And still, that his master's will got done by him: that is his whole life. → (M4) The servant who works from love is higher in light. But in truth, work from love is not even 'work'. A lover would do as much for his beloved. → (M13) For the simple servant, chopping wood and handing over a precious cup are the same. He is not in it, so nothing in it changes. → (M5) Even his pleasure is his master's. So his work counts as the master's own, as if the master did it with his own hands, and that new thing is the master's greatest pleasure. → (M8) He keeps nothing for himself: not the physical, and not the spiritual either. Not even the light of his own service. → (M15) So his work is in plain things: the low jobs no master would do himself. That is where the newest light comes from. ⇒ BOTTOM: Nothing for himself, not even spiritual light: his life is that the Master's will is done. ⇒ RETURN: Pick the lowliest task of the day and do it as His. The text says that is what He enjoys as if He did it with His own hands. - SV3:SV3-C18 [7270] (M2) Prayer is called 'behind'. Like a son walking behind his father, even when the father does not turn to show his face. → (M11) There is not-remembering, and there is forgetting. Not remembering is from not knowing. Forgetting is because something else pulls you away, like pleasures that make Torah taste of nothing. → (M5) But at the deepest point there is no forgetting at all. 'My heart is awake.' The point of the heart above reason shines always, without change. → (M10) A father hides his face from his son. The son still strains to run after him. Then the father loves him double and kisses him. → (M9) Before facing Him, you start as a little child looking for his father and not seeing him. That bitterness is the beginning. → (M16) And then, in the Amida, the King's face shines. You are not there; there is only love toward Him. ⇒ BOTTOM: The heart's point is awake even when His face is hidden; walking after Him in the dark is itself the bond. ⇒ RETURN: When prayer feels empty, keep walking: say the words, do the mitzvah. The text says this is what brings the face. - SV3:SV3-C19 [7406] (M2) Wisdom makes you humble the way a grown-up's feelings are milder than a child's: you take up less room. That is real, but you are still there. → (M7) Deeper inside: standing before the King and unable to speak. That is the Amida. Not noisy like the Shema, but silent, and you set yourself down. → (M5) There is 'there is none besides You', and there is 'there is nothing besides You'. The first leaves room for the light. The second is less even than nothing. → (M8) At the top is silence. Not the silence of having nothing to say, but 'I made myself still and quiet': the bittul of the yechida. → (M15) And this silence lives in the deed. Torah is like food, given to each limb by its role. In doing a mitzvah, there are no differences between people. ⇒ BOTTOM: Silence of the yechida: less than nothing besides Him. ⇒ RETURN: Say 'L-rd, open my lips' slowly before the Amida, and then let the lips be His. - SV3:SV3-C20 [7434] (M2) There are two ways to clean up the inner mess. A great light can push the darkness away from above. Or you can work the body itself, from below, until it becomes a vessel. → (M4) When the animal soul is just overwhelmed by a great light, it is quiet, but it was not refined. It did not wake up by itself. → (M9) Think of a small child learning to walk. The father holds him from behind, hidden. The child thinks he walks by himself. → (M6) That is you each morning. A light around you holds you from behind and does not let you fall. Because the help is hidden, it feels like your own walking. → (M12) That is not like being picked up and carried on His shoulders, as at the Exodus. Now He hides His help so the walking can be truly yours. That is the guarding of exile. → (M16) Another picture: seeing your teacher's face. It is not part of the argument, but you understand better. The help is from his very self; the understanding is yours. ⇒ BOTTOM: Hidden help, so the step is truly yours. ⇒ RETURN: Take today's step yourself. The hand behind you is there; it is hidden on purpose. - SV3:SV3-C21 [7422] (M1) A son who runs his father's house well still lives on what he was given. The sages call it 'bread of shame'. → (M5) The real fullness is when he goes far away, learns a trade, and lives by his own hands. → (M15) Yosef went so far from his father that he became a slave, and that is how he became a king. → (M16) 'No one is wise like the one with experience.' Even after it is clear in your head, when you come to do it, doubts come up. You work harder, and only then is it truly clear. ⇒ BOTTOM: What you earn far from the Father, by your own hands, reaches His hidden Essence. ⇒ RETURN: The doubt you meet when you try to live it is not failure; it is the trade you are learning. - SV3:SV3-C22 [7480] (M4) Everything that is, is true, but not 'true of truth'. If the Creator wished, it would stop, like a stream that runs dry. → (M13) Kindness and strictness each have an opposite. Mercy has none, because in it shines the Essence, which carries all opposites. → (M5) There are three truths. The lower unity. The higher unity, 'all before Him is as nothing'. And truth of truth: 'there is nothing else', beyond even 'as nothing'. → (M15) You reach it not by thinking but by serving: when your whole existence is only your Master's existence. → (M8) David found the true ruling because he was utterly still. A broken heart, 'my soul like dust to all', and then 'open my heart to Your Torah'. → (M16) That is why the law follows Hillel's school. Shammai's were sharper. Hillel's were gentle and humble, and truth of truth comes only by bittul. ⇒ BOTTOM: 'There is nothing else': truth of truth, beyond even 'all is as nothing'. ⇒ RETURN: Be gentle with someone today. The text ties the truest ruling to the humblest people. - SV3:SV3-C23 [7306] (M9) Watch your own will. You decide to lift your hand, and it moves at once. No organ holds your will the way your brain holds your thinking. → (M2) Your limbs are moved by your will, and your will is not moved by them. Like a garment: you hold the garment; the garment does not hold you. → (M12) Still, your will changes your life. When someone does what you want, you feel more alive; when someone angers you, you feel less. The will is moved by nothing, yet doing it or not doing it matters. → (M13) And your will is the same in your head and in your foot. It is not more in the thinking part than in the walking part. → (M6) So with Him. He holds all things; nothing holds Him. He is the same in all of them, the same will in every one. ⇒ BOTTOM: He holds you like you hold a garment: closer than anything, and never held. ⇒ RETURN: Move your hand on purpose once, slowly, and notice what moved it. - SV3:SV3-C24 [7546] (M1) Up above, a soul is always in love and awe. Down here, even good people are not, and their love is tiny next to the love above. So why come down? → (M2) 'He blew into him': blowing comes from deep inside and takes force. To put something so high into a body is a leap, and leaps take strength. → (M10) A person was made with the lowest body and the highest soul, like a lever, which must lift from the very bottom. → (M5) So only down here does the soul's root show. The force it took to put you here is the force of the Essence, and it is in you. → (M15) That is why only here you can 'walk'. Angels stand. You can love 'with all your might', with no measure. ⇒ BOTTOM: The force that put you in a body is the force of the Essence. ⇒ RETURN: Your body is the lever's bottom end. Lift something today with it. - SV3:SV3-C25 [6640] (M9) You can argue with a hard-hearted simple man for hours and not move him. Then a great sage says something very deep, far above him, and it breaks his hard heart to pieces. → (M2) The rule: to move the lowest place, you need a power from higher up. A light at your own level cannot move you. → (M4) And when the high thing is hidden from you, there are two kinds of hidden. One is your own blocking. The other is the Giver hiding on purpose, like a great teacher who says deep things in a few clear words. → (M5) What is truly simple is not tied to any level. Sometimes it chooses to show itself in the low place and passes over the high one. ⇒ BOTTOM: The highest is free to choose the lowest place. ⇒ RETURN: Do not water down the deep truth for a simple heart; the text says that is what breaks it open. - SV3:SV3-C26 [7623] (M2) 'Waiting for Him' means hope, and hope is for something completely beyond you. → (M7) Real hope has bowing inside it. You lay yourself down, and you wait. → (M15) And that is how the future comes: 'on the day G-d binds the break of His people', by accepting the yoke and hoping, with the self laid down. → (M16) And this is not only for scholars. Simple people with little learning reach that hidden Eden too, through deeds done because He wants them. ⇒ BOTTOM: Hope with the self laid down meets what is beyond comparison. ⇒ RETURN: Wait well today: do the mitzvah, and hope.

## TZ — the Tzemach Tzedek

The Tzemach Tzedek adds three things to the full depth. First, he takes the ladder into the act of addressing G-d: no name reaches the Essence; only 'You' (Atah), said face to face, does; we pray to Him, never to His attributes; of the Essence the right word is not even 'One' but 'Alone', while 'One' is His oneness inside a world that is really there. Second, he shows that the bottom is not calm. Where knowing ends, faith is a separate sense (you cannot see with your foot), and the true response to not-grasping is love-sickness and longing. The deepest love, 'with all your might', is a love that breaks YOUR own limit, and its fuel is distance: the body that hides G-d is what makes the love burst like dammed water; the soul came down to stop 'standing' and start 'walking'. It wants not Gan Eden but Him alone ('I want nothing; not Your Gan Eden; only You'). Third, he grounds the return in the lowest: matter comes straight from the Infinite, so a fly's wing shows His power more than the sefiros; His greatness is His humility, bending equally to heaven and earth; the highest is 'dry land inside the sea', separate and pointing 'this is my G-d', which only the Essence can do; newness comes through shrinking like the moon; today alone is the place of turning.

## Depths

- **TZ:TZ-D1 Only 'You' reaches Him** (extends Extends D5 (where knowing ends) and D6 (under all your names): under all His names, too, there is no name, only 'You'. new.): No name reaches the Essence. Every name of G-d names Him as He acts through some attribute. The only word that reaches Him as He is, before any attribute, is 'You', said face to face. (stated: DM 222-223) | Inside: Not an idea of G-d but a turning toward Him. The mind stops describing; the whole person faces. (ours: description; the text gives the grammar: 'to the face', lenochach.) | Lands: Lands in the first two words of every blessing, many times a day. | Q: דאתה היינו למהו"ע כמו שהוא טרם התלבשותו במדותיו, שאין שם המורה עליו רק באמרו אתה לנוכח — 'You' means His Essence before He clothes Himself in attributes; no name points to Him except saying 'You', face to face (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Derekh Mitzvotekha (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:223)
- **TZ:TZ-D2 Your own 'too much'** (extends Extends D8 (the bottom is a bond) and D9 (the turn): the distance itself is the fuel for the deepest love. Corrects any reading of D2 that makes the self only something to dissolve: here the self's limit is the place where the Infinite is woken.): 'With all your might' (me'odecha) is not a love that is truly infinite. It is a love that goes past your own limit, a 'very much' that is yours. Because it breaks your measure, it wakes the true Infinite. And it is powered by distance: the body and animal soul that hide G-d are what make the love break its banks. (stated: DM 232, 258-259) | Inside: Water held back by a board bursting through; a soul that 'stood' starting to 'walk'. (stated images) | Lands: Lands in money: 'with all your might' means 'with all your property'; give charity from your own toil (DM 258-259). | Q: ולפיכך נק' מאדך מאד שלך שאצלך הוא בלי גבול — so it is called 'your might': yours, which for you is without limit (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Derekh Mitzvotekha (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:232)
- **TZ:TZ-D3 Love-sickness: when knowing ends, longing begins** (extends Corrects and deepens D5 (where knowing ends): the end of knowing is not only that 'the soul's root knows', but an ache of longing that keeps the person moving. Extends D14.): When the mind really sees that the power making things from nothing cannot be grasped, the right response is not calm. It is love-sickness: a longing that cannot be filled, the way an animal cries when its food is held back. Longing (cheshek) is higher than love: the longer's thought never leaves the one he longs for. (stated: CH 597-619) | Inside: Like a sick person who takes pleasure in nothing and cannot keep food down; like a deer crying for water. (stated images) | Lands: Lands in prayer (prayer is 'chilu', sickness), and in plain study of halachah as the support. | Q: יחלה מזה ויתאוה תאוה להשיג על בורי' את כח השופע מאין — he will grow sick and long to grasp clearly the power that flows from nothing (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Sefer HaChakirah (Lubavitch, c. 1840s).txt:606)
- **TZ:TZ-D4 Alone, not only One** (extends Sharpens D3 and D4: 'nothing' and 'one' apply only where there is a world; the Essence is beyond both. Guards against D3's danger: the world is not unreal.): Of the Essence itself, 'One' says nothing: there is no second to deny. The right word is 'Alone' (Yachid). 'One' (Echad) is said of Him inside a world that is really there, which is like the ray inside the sun. He is Alone now just as before the world. (stated: DM 229, 235) | Inside: Before Him darkness and light are alike, up and down equal. (stated image) | Lands: Lands in the first verse of Shema each day, which ends in 'a dwelling below'. | Q: אמנם לגבי עצם מהות הא"ס ב"ה אין שייך לקרותו אחד רק יחיד — of the very Essence one cannot say One, only Alone (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Derekh Mitzvotekha (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:235)
- **TZ:TZ-D5 Not Your Gan Eden: only You** (extends Extends D8 (what you cannot let go) and corrects a hidden counterfeit of the whole descent: seeking depth as an experience. new.): The deepest love does not want any pleasure, not even spiritual ones, not even Gan Eden, which is 'for those who love being something'. It wants Him alone. The Alter Rebbe said it in Yiddish in his deveikus. (stated: DM 255-256) | Inside: Better to be nullified and touched by Him than to remain whole and enjoy His light. (stated: 'better to take shelter in G-d than trust in man') | Lands: Lands in plain service with joy, even when one feels 'barren' of arousal (DM 256). | Q: איך וויל זע גאר ניסט איך וויל ניט דאיין ג"ע איך וויל ניט דאיין עוה"ב — I want nothing at all; not Your Gan Eden; not Your World to Come (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Derekh Mitzvotekha (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:256)
- **TZ:TZ-D6 Dry land inside the sea** (extends Extends D10-D12 (the return): the return is not a compromise but the one thing only the Essence can do.): The highest is not vanishing like a fish in the sea, nor living apart like a land creature. It is both at once: still separate, with an evil inclination, and pointing with the finger 'This is my G-d'. Two opposites in one subject can come only from His Essence; that is why the Sea, and every true marriage, is 'hard'. (stated: MA 247-270) | Inside: Each one pointing with his finger 'this'. (stated) | Lands: Lands in a marriage, a body, a weekday: fully yourself and His. | Q: וכדי שיהי' שני הפכים אלו בנושא אחד ממש — for these two opposites to be in one subject (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Maamarei Admur HaTzemach Tzedek (Lubavitch, 1855).txt:268)
- **TZ:TZ-D7 The moon goes dark before it is new** (extends Extends D2 (stillness given) and D14 (no bottom: each return renews): the newness is the fruit of the shrinking.): A truly new light, not the daily renewal of the old, comes only through becoming nothing: the moon shrinks to a dot and disappears; the seed rots before it sprouts. (stated: MA 271-279) | Inside: A seed rotting in the ground; the moon black. (stated images) | Lands: Lands in learning each day 'as new', and in Shabbos. | Q: שהתבואה אם לא ירקב מקודם אזי לא יצמיח כלל — grain that does not rot first will not sprout (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Maamarei Admur HaTzemach Tzedek (Lubavitch, 1855).txt:275)
- **TZ:TZ-D8 Salt water made sweet by the earth** (extends Extends D9 (the bottom sends you back) and D11: the descent is what makes the water living. new image.): The way to the deepest will is 'from the narrow place', and each person knows his own sore. The soul's passage through the body is like salty deep water breaking up through thick earth: by passing through, it becomes living water. But the well must be dug with a heavy head and a broken heart. (stated: OH 4092-4098) | Inside: Bitter water turning sweet as it rises through soil. (stated image) | Lands: Lands in prayer first, then Torah, then deeds (OH 4095). | Q: וענין המיצר מה שידוע לכאו"א נגעו — the narrow place is what each knows as his own sore (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Or HaTorah (Lubavitch, c. 1828-1866).txt:4093)
- **TZ:TZ-D9 Faith is a sense** (extends Sharpens D7 (simple faith): faith is a distinct sense, and it does not replace da'as; both are needed.): Faith is not weak knowing. It is the organ for what is beyond mind, as the eye is for light: you cannot see with your foot. Before Him, even 'graspable/ungraspable' does not apply, any more than 'touchable'. (stated: DM 96-103) | Inside: Like a rich man glad of the pearls in his pouch though he is not looking at them. (stated image, DM 97) | Lands: Lands in steadiness: faith does not move feelings by itself (the thief), so keep the knowing working too. | Q: וכמו שלא יוכל לראו' ברגלו כך לא יוכל לתפוס בשכלו — as one cannot see with his foot, so one cannot grasp with the mind (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Derekh Mitzvotekha (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:103)
- **TZ:TZ-D10 Today only** (extends Extends D12 (the deed) and D14 (begin again): the possibility of beginning again is a property of this world.): Only here, where good flows even where evil is, can a person change from one end to the other. In the world to come each thing is in its place. One true thought of return turns a life. (stated: DM 261-264) | Inside: Mixed ground, where change is still possible. (ours: image) | Lands: Lands every morning (DM 264: 'every day'). | Q: לכן זאת ישים אדם על לבו בכל יום איך שהיום בעוה"ז דוקא הוא יכול לשנות טעמו מהקצה אל הקצה — let a person put on his heart each day that today, in this world specifically, he can change his taste from end to end (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Derekh Mitzvotekha (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:264)
- **TZ:TZ-D11 His greatness is His humility** (extends Extends D11 (called into being: the command): being commanded is His humility, and our yielding draws His kingship. new.): For Him to be King over creatures who are nothing before Him is like a man showing off before a fly: a huge lowering. His greatness is itself humility, and He bends to see the earth exactly as the heavens. What moves Him to it is our giving up of our will. (stated: DM 110, 114, 338-340) | Inside: A king who bends his head; a king's son crying from captivity. (stated images) | Lands: Lands in small surrenders of will in the day, and in Baruch Shem after Shema. | Q: וכמו שאין שייך כלל לומר באדם שיתפאר לפני זבוב — as a person would not glory before a fly (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Derekh Mitzvotekha (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:338)
- **TZ:TZ-D12 After vanishing, a lover is given back** (extends Extends D9-D11: the return gives back a lover, not just a world.): There are two kinds of giving oneself: 'kallah' as consuming (nothing left, in 'One') and 'kallah' as pining (someone still loves). The lower comes after the higher because 'Baruch Shem' is said between, giving back a world and a self. (stated: DM 257) | Inside: A bride: once consumed, once pining. (stated image) | Lands: Lands in the words of Shema each day, and in the day that follows. | Q: הא' מלשון כליון שמכלה ומבלה הכל — the first from 'consumption', consuming everything (Chassidus-txt/R. Menachem Mendel Schneersohn (Tzemach Tzedek) (1789-1866)/Derekh Mitzvotekha (Lubavitch, 1813-1827).txt:257)

## Chains (start → steps → bottom → return)

- TZ:TZ-C01 [What exactly are we commanded to believe? That G-d exists?] (M9) You do not need faith to know that G-d is there. You can see it the way you know your own soul is in your body: the body by itself is as still as meat in a pot, yet it lives. → (M6) The whole world is like one great body. Look at the turning sky, the growing grass, the feeling animals: that is the life of G-d inside it. Look this way and you 'see' Him. → (M16) So what is commanded is not to know it once. It is to go deep into it and not let your mind slip away. A passing thought does nothing. → (M10) Knowing that is felt (da'as) is the key to the heart. Faith on its own sits above you like a crown and does not move your feelings: the thief at the tunnel prays to G-d for success and still breaks in. → (M15) So go as far as knowing can go, and you become a different person, because your heart opens. → (M4) Where knowing ends, faith begins, and faith is not a weaker knowing. It is the only organ that fits Him, as only the eye fits light: you cannot see with your foot. ⇒ BOTTOM: What can be known, know; what cannot be known, believe (DM 99). Faith is a sense of its own, the organ for the Essence. ⇒ RETURN: The return is to da'as again: deepen what can be known so the heart opens and the deed follows (DM 98-99). - TZ:TZ-C02 [Is G-d the greatest mind, the Knower who is also the Known (Rambam)?] (M1) Calling G-d 'pure intellect' quietly gives Him a definition, and anything defined has an outside. → (M4) Because He has no definition at all, there is nothing outside Him. A defined thing is separated from what is not it; He is not defined, so nothing is separate from Him. → (M2) So 'I' in 'I am the L-rd your G-d' means 'I who am I', the Essence itself, which no thought holds; and faith is how we hold it. → (M5) Even 'unknowable' is too small a word. Before Him, 'known' and 'unknown' are like 'touchable': the category does not reach Him. → (M12) And yet both Rambam and Maharal are right: in the sefiros, where He clothes Himself, He truly is Knower and Known; in Himself He is beyond. ⇒ BOTTOM: The Essence is not something, not even 'the unknowable something'. It is simple being, and because of that, nothing stands outside it. ⇒ RETURN: Back down: since nothing is outside Him, every creature is known by Him in particular, with personal providence (DM 108). - TZ:TZ-C03 [If G-d does everything, why does He not change, the way a craftsman changes when he works?] (M1) A worker who pours himself into his work is pulled out of himself: the hand tires, the mind tires. That is 'flow' (shefa). → (M3) Light is different. The sun shines and loses nothing; cover it with clouds for a thousand years and it gains nothing. → (M10) But the sun cannot hold back its light; a giver chooses. G-d has both perfections: He shines like light and gives by choice. → (M12) So the fullness of faith holds two things at once: every single thing that happens comes from Him, personally; and He does not change at all. ⇒ BOTTOM: He is fully present to each creature and fully unmoved. Neither cancels the other. ⇒ RETURN: In practice: what happens to you today is from Him, addressed to you, and He is not tired of you. - TZ:TZ-C04 [How can the Infinite 'withdraw' (tzimtzum) without changing?] (M9) Close your eyes. Sight does not leave you; it folds back into you. → (M7) Even a blind man has sight inside him: that is why he can father a child who sees. → (M3) Or think of a tractate you know by heart. While you learn something else, it is all there, whole, 'as if in a box', only not shining in words. → (M4) So the withdrawal is only toward us. What is left showing is a trace, like the mark left when writing is erased. ⇒ BOTTOM: Nothing of Him ever left. What we call hiding is light folded into its source. ⇒ RETURN: The person in darkness is not abandoned: what is not shining is whole, as in the closed eye (ours: applied to a person's own dark times). - TZ:TZ-C05 [Why do we praise G-d, if everything is nothing before Him?] (M1) The question is real: does He need our praise? Everything on earth counts as nothing before Him. → (M4) For Him to put on 'greatness' and 'kindness' at all is a lowering. His greatness is itself His humility. → (M12) He is so high that heaven and earth are the same distance below Him. So He bends equally to see the earth, and does not leave it to angels. → (M5) So spiritual is not closer to Him than physical. → (M9) That is why praise matters: praising a wise man for his wisdom wakes his wisdom. Our praise draws Him into the attributes that act for us. ⇒ BOTTOM: His height is the reason for His nearness to the lowest. Greatness and humility are one act. ⇒ RETURN: Praise in prayer is not flattery; it draws Him down. And the earth, your physical day, is where He bends to look (DM 114: 'in the midst of the land'). - TZ:TZ-C06 [If nothing is outside Him, how can He be moved by us, glad or angry? And how can a prayer change His will?] (M4) In His Essence He is not moved at all. 'If you were righteous, what do you give Him?' → (M12) But in the contracted light He puts out, we do take up some place, and there gladness and anger are real. → (M3) His will is like a king's robe. Changing from red to white does not change the king. → (M7) Only someone close to the king can ask him to change his robe. When we say 'May it be Your will', we are that close. → (M6) Where does that closeness come from? From the soul's readiness to give itself up for Him, which comes from its root in His Essence. → (M5) And what reaches the deepest will of all, beyond the will written in the Torah, is a cry from the inside of the heart. ⇒ BOTTOM: Prayer works not because He changes, but because a person close enough, from his own depth, asks Him to put on another garment. ⇒ RETURN: So pray from the inside of the heart, as one who belongs in the king's room (ours: the wording of the practice). - TZ:TZ-C07 [How can love be commanded?] (M1) Love between people cannot be ordered: either it does not help or it is not needed. And chasing it makes it run, like honor. → (M2) Faith does not move feelings; only understanding does. You love a treasure only when you think about it. → (M4) So 'You shall love' is a promise as much as a command: think, and love will come by itself. Do not chase the feeling. → (M9) Admiring is not loving. A pretty picture pleases you; your friend's gold does not move you. Love comes when you feel it can be yours. → (M16) The generation that saw G-d at the Sea did not need this command. We do: 'Hear' means understand, so that by thought we come near to their seeing. → (M6) If the scraps of the upper pleasure, given to the outside, made Rome so loud and rich, how much more is the pleasure of the source. So choose life: choose Him who is the source of all pleasure. ⇒ BOTTOM: Love is not made; it is let. The work is thinking, and love is what follows. ⇒ RETURN: Do the contemplation; then let the love go into Torah and mitzvos, which make 'Havayah your G-d' in the open (DM 417, 420). - TZ:TZ-C08 [Whom do we pray to: G-d, or His attributes?] (M3) A king gives with his right hand or his left; either way we say 'the king gave'. The names of G-d name Him, not the attribute He works through. → (M7) So never pray to an attribute, even to ask it to plead for you. Better: 'Father of mercy, roll Your mercy onto us.' → (M5) Then what word reaches Him as He is, before any attribute? Only 'You'. → (M13) Some great men simply prayed 'with the mind of a small child', without the Kabbalistic intentions. ⇒ BOTTOM: The deepest address in prayer is the second person. No name reaches the Essence; 'You' does. ⇒ RETURN: Every blessing begins 'Baruch Atah': each time, the person stands face to face, and only then names how he needs Him (ours: the practice; stated: the reading of 'Atah'). - TZ:TZ-C09 [What happens in the first verse of Shema?] (M6) Each morning is a small Rosh Hashanah: life goes back up to its first source and comes down new. → (M5) What comes down is truly new, a light that was not there yesterday, because it comes from the Infinite itself. → (M1) But why say 'One'? Of the Essence itself, 'one' says nothing: from whom would you deny a second? → (M4) Of the Essence the right word is 'Yachid', Alone. 'Echad', One, is said of Him inside the seven heavens, the earth and the four directions. → (M12) This does not mean the world is not there. It means it is like the sun's ray inside the sun. → (M15) Before Him darkness and light are alike, and up and down are equal: His Essence is below exactly as it is above. ⇒ BOTTOM: Yachid: He is alone now as before creation. 'One' is His oneness as it shines inside a world that is really there. ⇒ RETURN: The aim of the verse is 'a dwelling below' (DM 229): the new light goes down into thought, speech and deed. - TZ:TZ-C10 [How can a limited person's love awaken the truly Infinite?] (M2) There are two loves. One settles in the heart. The other is too big for the heart: it wants to leave the body and be swallowed into the King. → (M9) You know this from ordinary life: sometimes one matter touches a person to the inner point of his heart, and he does and says things beyond reason. → (M10) It is not truly infinite: a greater tzaddik would hold it easily. But because it is beyond YOUR limit, it calls up the true Infinite. → (M11) And where does this 'too much' come from? From distance. The godly soul alone loves in measure. The body and animal soul, which hide G-d, are what make the love break its banks. → (M3) Like water held back by a board: when it breaks through, it rushes far stronger than it ever flowed. → (M14) That is why the soul came down. Above it 'stood' like an angel, always at one level. Here, through the distance, it becomes one who walks, rung after rung. → (M15) The bitter half is part of it: to see how all our days are spent on livelihood and on leaving things for our children, and to ask, is this why my soul came down? ⇒ BOTTOM: The bottom is not a calm state but a breaking through your own limit, powered by the very distance you feel. ⇒ RETURN: It lands in money: 'with all your might' means 'with all your property'. Charity from your own toil turns 'something into nothing', which is His laughter and delight (DM 258-259). - TZ:TZ-C11 [If the world is nothing before Him, why does it feel like something, and what do we do with that?] (M12) There are two true knowings. From above: He is the real 'something' and the worlds are nothing. From below: the world is the something and He is the hidden 'nothing'. → (M16) We do not see the upper truth. We acknowledge it. 'Modim' is acknowledging, not seeing. → (M14) Why make beings who feel separate at all? Because there is no king without a people: kingship means separate beings who take Him on by their own will. → (M13) There are two kinds of giving yourself in Shema. One consumes everything: 'kallah' as in 'consuming'. The other longs: 'kallah' as in 'my soul pines'. → (M15) Between them we whisper 'Blessed be the name...' It brings back a world and a someone who can love. ⇒ BOTTOM: Total vanishing in 'One' is real, and it is not the end: after it, a lover is given back. ⇒ RETURN: From 'Baruch Shem' comes 'You shall love': a self again, now to love and act (DM 257). - TZ:TZ-C12 [What is the full contemplation that leads a person to want only Him?] (M17) Go down the ladder: all life is a ray of Him; a contraction leaves a trace; a thin line; worlds; then the 'hairs', a life so thin that cutting hair does not hurt; then wisdom, speech, the screen, the lower worlds, the physical. → (M5) Then climb back up. All the pleasures of this world are the waste of the lower Garden of Eden.
