A soul must forget this world to enter there, and forget that garden in a river of fire to enter the higher one. → (M15) From this comes a love that wants no pleasure, physical or spiritual, not even Gan Eden: only Him. → (M4) Gan Eden is where the soul stays itself and enjoys: it is 'for those who love being something'. Better to be nullified and touched by Him. → (M16) The Alter Rebbe said it in Yiddish, in his deveikus: I want nothing at all. I don't want Your Garden of Eden, I don't want Your World to Come. I want only You Yourself. → (M11) And even when we have no arousal of our own, like a barren woman, joy in a mitzvah breaks the measure and brings this great love. ⇒ BOTTOM: Wanting Him, not His gifts, not even spiritual ones. ⇒ RETURN: This love is reached through joy in plain mitzvos, even by those who feel barren (DM 256). - TZ:TZ-C13 [Where is the Infinite's power seen most: in the high worlds or in a stone?] (M1) Spiritual things come from spiritual causes, step by step. But no chain of causes, however long, can turn spirit into a physical body. → (M6) So matter comes straight from the Infinite's own will. No creature, however high, could make even a fly's wing from nothing. → (M4) So the plain world shows His power more than the sefiros do. That is why David, in 'Bless the L-rd, my soul', praises the mountains for the ibex, and not the sefiros. → (M3) The sun is warmer near the ground than in the middle air: where its light ends, it bounces back up. → (M15) This is why physical mitzvos are the purpose of the soul's coming down: they reach the Essence itself. ⇒ BOTTOM: The lowest thing carries the highest signature: existence from nothing. ⇒ RETURN: Do the physical mitzvah with a physical thing; that is where the Essence is touched. - TZ:TZ-C14 [Why can a person change only in this world?] (M2) In the world to come everything is in its place: angels here, souls there, the harmful forces apart. Nothing is mixed, so nothing can turn. → (M5) Here the light that is beyond all levels shines, the one before which darkness is like light. So even the one who fell lowest can rise. → (M16) One true thought of return is enough to turn a whole life. A man who betroths 'on condition that I am righteous', even a total sinner, the betrothal holds: perhaps he thought of teshuvah. ⇒ BOTTOM: Today is the only place of turning. ⇒ RETURN: Say it each day: today I can change from end to end (DM 264). - TZ:TZ-C15 [What is the love-sickness of the Song of Songs, 'for I am sick with love'?] (M6) Look at the flow that never stops: six thousand years and the giving of life has not paused once. → (M12) And yet everything it makes is counted and separate. No two blades of grass grow joined; no two stars touch. The Infinite makes limits too: that is His might. → (M5) When a person thinks this through in prayer, he wants to grasp the power that makes things from nothing, and he cannot. And from wanting and not reaching, he becomes lovesick. → (M9) An animal screams bitterly when its food is taken. A person with da'as should be sick with this. → (M5) This is more than love. It is longing (cheshek), where the lover's thought never leaves the beloved. → (M2) This is why the secrets of the Torah are given only to one whose heart worries inside him: the worry is this not-reaching. → (M15) The cure is in the future: 'you will call Me my Husband'. Until then: 'sustain me' with the outer Torah, with halachah. ⇒ BOTTOM: The end of knowing is not peace; it is love-sickness, a longing that does not let go. ⇒ RETURN: Hold on through plain Torah study and halachah until the full seeing (CH 619). - TZ:TZ-C16 [How far is the finite from the Infinite?] (M4) Add and add: a hundred, a thousand, a million. You are no closer to infinite than you were at one. → (M10) A million is again 'one million'. Count up and the count starts over. → (M12) So before the Infinite, the highest wisdom and a lump of earth are exactly equal. → (M9) And you feel this in your own family: my grandfather in his day was just as I am in mine. ⇒ BOTTOM: No amount of more brings you nearer. Nearness cannot be built from quantity. ⇒ RETURN: So the small deed of one person is not 'too little': before Him, the great and the small are equal (ours: application; DM 119 'He makes small and great equal'). - TZ:TZ-C17 [Why is making a match 'as hard for G-d as splitting the sea', when creating the world from nothing was effortless?] (M1) Creating the world took no effort: 'not with labor, not with toil', only His word, because it comes from His ray, not from Himself. → (M3) 'With You is the source of life', not 'You are': even the source of life is only something beside Him. Like hair: cut it and nothing hurts; pull it and it hurts, so a little life is in it. → (M3) Fish cannot live a moment out of the sea; land creatures live apart from the earth that feeds them. So the hidden world is like the sea (all nullified), and our world like dry land (all feels separate). → (M11) Yet dry land has its own advantage: from the dark comes a greater light. The earth 'is never sated with water': it wants His Essence, not Gan Eden. → (M12) At the Sea both came together: 'Israel walked on dry land inside the sea'. They were still separate people, with an evil inclination, and each one pointed with his finger: 'This is my G-d'. → (M5) Two opposites in one subject cannot come from the chain of levels. Only His Essence can do that. That is why it is 'hard'. → (M15) A marriage is the same: two that are opposite become one under a canopy that surrounds both. So it too is 'as hard as the Sea'. ⇒ BOTTOM: The highest is not vanishing into the sea; it is being fully yourself and fully His at once, which only the Essence can give. ⇒ RETURN: In a marriage, in a body, in an ordinary day: separate, and pointing 'this' (ours: application to the person). - TZ:TZ-C18 [Why does Israel count by the moon and not the sun?] (M2) The sun renews the world each day, but that is renewing the old. Nature is a sunken seal: the letters are there and cannot be seen. → (M4) Even the sun that grows the grain knows nothing of it: it is an axe in the woodcutter's hand. → (M9) The moon gets a truly new light, and only by shrinking until it disappears: 'I am black'. → (M3) A seed must rot before it sprouts. Food renews an old body; birth is new. → (M15) Begin with awe, the foundation: with a good foundation you can build many buildings. ⇒ BOTTOM: Newness comes from becoming small. The moon's dark night is the start of its month. ⇒ RETURN: Each day, learning Torah 'as new', and on that base the Shabbos, where both unities are joined (MA 280-281). - TZ:TZ-C19 [Why are the mitzvos called 'the limbs of the King'?] (M9) Your will is in your limbs: you want to move a foot and it moves at once. It can even hold a foot in hot water against nature. → (M10) But your stomach and inner organs do not obey your will, though they live from you. Their life is there, hidden. → (M7) So nature is like the inner organs: G-d gives it life without showing His will in it. A mitzvah is a limb: His will is there openly. → (M5) And yet the person is higher than the mitzvah: tefillin lying on a table are not a mitzvah. → (M15) So when you make the mitzvah, He counts it 'as if you made Me'. ⇒ BOTTOM: The deed is where His will is a living limb, and you are the one who moves it. ⇒ RETURN: Do the mitzvah with your body: your hand is where He becomes present (ours: the phrasing). - TZ:TZ-C20 [Why does it say 'the glory of G-d WILL fill the earth', if it is already full?] (M4) For Him to be a king over creatures who are nothing before Him is a huge lowering, the way a man would not show off before a fly. → (M2) What brings Him to want this? Our giving up our own will. 'Soul' means will: to give the soul is to give up what you want. → (M12) When we put our will aside, He as it were puts aside His, and wants to be King here. → (M11) And mercy is woken by crying. A king's son taken captive who does not care that he is far, the king may forget him too. If his heart is bitter and he cries to his father, the father's mercy wakes. → (M15) The spies' error was wanting Torah and mitzvos only in the spiritual. But the end is wedged in the beginning: the glory is to fill the earth. ⇒ BOTTOM: His becoming King here hangs on our giving up our will, and on our tears over distance. ⇒ RETURN: The glory fills the land, not the clouds: here, through physical life (DM 351). - TZ:TZ-C21 [How does a soul reach the deepest will, above what Torah and mitzvos draw?] (M5) Even Gan Eden is only His radiance. A dwelling below means His Essence itself shows here. → (M11) The way is 'from the narrow place'. And what is that? Each person knows his own sore. → (M3) The deep water under the earth is salty. When it breaks up through the thick soil it is cleaned at once and becomes 'living water'. → (M2) But a well does not dig itself. It needs a heavy head and a broken heart. → (M15) A heart that is blocked and hard as stone gives living water through studying halachah, after first striking fire from it. ⇒ BOTTOM: The bitter water is the same water that becomes living, by going through you. ⇒ RETURN: Dig: a broken heart in prayer, then a page of halachah (OH 4095, 4098). - TZ:TZ-C22 [What does 'holy' (kadosh) mean, if G-d already fills heaven and earth?] (M3) His filling and His surrounding are both 'luminaries', ways of giving light. → (M5) 'Holy' is beyond both: a darkness above light, His hidden Essence that is not in the category of giving at all. → (M1) If He fills everything, how may we work at all? One who walks too erect 'pushes the feet of the Shechinah'. Before a king you do not lift your hand. → (M15) But 'six days you shall work' is also a command: to work the animal soul like tanning a hide, until it wants only His Essence. ⇒ BOTTOM: Holiness is the dark above light, the Essence beyond giving. ⇒ RETURN: Its holiness is drawn down by working the weekday self: food, prayer, charity from one's toil (MA 799). - TZ:TZ-C23 [What is prayer, at its root?] (M2) Before prayer the soul is only 'in the nostrils', not yet spread through the body. That is why we do not greet a friend before praying. → (M8) 'The soul You gave me is pure' comes before 'You created it': the root of the soul is there before it was created at all. → (M7) Your root is above your mind. 'Though he does not see, his mazal sees': the part of you above you sees what you do not. → (M15) Prayer is a ladder joining that root to the soul in your body, until there is one will in you, even in the animal soul. → (M17) So we start with a heavy head, the lower teshuvah; the prayer itself is the higher teshuvah. ⇒ BOTTOM: In prayer the root of the soul, which was never not pure, enters the body. ⇒ RETURN: Do not start the day with greetings and business; first let the soul spread into the body through prayer (MA 741).
